# v2 decision log

Append-only. Each entry: date, decision, reason, consequence. When a later entry changes an earlier one, it says so; earlier entries are not rewritten. Blueprint = `architecture/02d-greenfield-blueprint.md`. Facts carried from v1 are in `carried-forward-facts.md` (CF-nn).

## D-001 · 2026-10-19 · Programme authority

The owner's implementation request of 2026-10-19 authorises building the blueprint's architecture and the complete v2.1 UI in this repository, with deployment to dev and stage. It supersedes the "documentation only" scope of the architecture documents and of #72. Product, privacy and reliability rules are unchanged. PIO is not used; the Software Factory skill and its gates are retired and are not reinstated. The blueprint governs architecture and behaviour; the v2.1 export governs visuals; conflicts are resolved in this log.

## D-002 · 2026-10-19 · Branches and promotion

- Integration branch `codex/release-knowscroll-v2` (from `dev@106c4701`). Feature branches `codex/<issue>-<purpose>` reach it by squash-merged PRs (the repository allows squash merges only).
- A tested integration revision reaches `dev` by a squash-merged PR. Immediately afterwards `dev` is merged back into the integration branch (a plain merge pushed to the integration branch) so the next promotion has a fresh merge base and does not re-conflict with its own squash.
- `dev` deploys the dev world on push. `stage` follows by `scripts/promote.sh stage` (fast-forward to the tested `dev` commit; same release artifact, no rebuild).
- Agents never update `main` or deploy production. The main-targeted PR is prepared for the owner and not merged.
- The coordinator may merge feature PRs into the integration branch after green CI on the exact head and an independent review with no blocking finding, and may merge integration → `dev` after the same plus hands-on verification. Owner-only approvals that remain owner-only: reel-script approval and spend (CF-62), main/production.

## D-003 · 2026-10-19 · The v1 implementation and docs leave this line of history

Tag `legacy/v1-final` → `106c4701` marks the last v1 revision on `dev`. The integration branch removes the v1 apps, packages, tests, scripts, docs, ADRs and agent instructions so future sessions cannot mistake them for current rules. Retained, because v2 needs them:

| Kept                                                                                                        | Why                                                                                                      |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/hooks/refuse-main-update.mjs`                                             | Enforces "agents never update main" (strengthened, D-012)                                                |
| `ops/vps/**`                                                                                                | The live VPS provisioning and `ks` deploy tool; v2 extends it (S1.5)                                     |
| `ops/pool/migrations/` (was `packages/db/pool-migrations/`)                                                 | Schema of the live `knowscroll_pool` spend database (CF-30)                                              |
| `packages/backend/src/modules/content/adapters/cutroom/wire-v1/` (was `packages/contracts/src/cutroom-v1/`) | Byte-for-byte vendored Cutroom wire schema pinned to `94ee04a1` (CF-61)                                  |
| `packages/design-tokens/fonts/`                                                                             | Bricolage Grotesque, Instrument Sans, DM Mono with their OFL licences                                    |
| `content/**`                                                                                                | Frozen v1 data: editorial and model-written Scrolls, approved reel scripts bound to paid history (D-005) |
| `scripts/promote.sh`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`                                                        | Promotion and the v1 deploy path; deploy is replaced for v2 in S1.5                                      |

## D-004 · 2026-10-19 · Where v2 runs

v2 runs in the **existing dev and stage worlds** (`app.dev`/`backend.dev`, `app.stage`/`backend.stage`), replacing v1 in a world when a v2 revision is promoted there. It uses new databases `knowscroll_v2_dev` and `knowscroll_v2_stage`, new roles and new media directories. The v1 databases `knowscroll_test_dev|stage`, their media and the shared `knowscroll_pool` record are left untouched and are not dropped. The owner's Mac database `knowscroll` is not read, migrated or changed.

Reason: the owner asked for promotion to dev then stage; separate `v2dev`/`v2stage` worlds (the discovery alternative) would need new DNS records in hPanel and new environments, and would leave dev/stage showing a product that is being replaced. Consequence: stage keeps running v1 until the first v2 promotion to stage (planned once the Phase 2 journeys pass on dev). Live remains untouched.

## D-005 · 2026-10-19 · Legacy content and generated content

- `content/` is frozen v1 data. Its 14 reel scripts are bound to paid history by request id (CF-63); never edit them.
- v1 Scrolls (23 editorial, 70 model-written) are **not** imported into the v2 inventory: their format differs and v2 must demonstrate its own generation. Importing them later is a separate decision.
- Reels already produced by Cutroom from owner-approved scripts (greenhouse, seasons-v5, tides-v4) may be admitted into v2 through the v2 Cutroom adapter's lookup/import path, with provenance (run id, script, approval ref). This is a real Cutroom result reused at no new cost; it does not count as proof of a new commission.
- v2 model-generated content lives in the database and media store, not in Git. Test fixtures are labelled as fixtures.
- One authored editorial starter Scroll may seed the Phase 1 thin journey; it is labelled `authored_editorial` provenance and never presented as generated.

A pressed Like, Save or other two-state control uses the filled cobalt treatment on the poster register (cobalt fill, filled cream glyph) and the filled cream treatment on dark and media surfaces (opaque cream, filled ink glyph); never yellow. ## D-007 · 2026-10-19 · Identity (to be proven in S1.3)

Use better-auth (MIT) with its email magic-link plugin and Expo plugin, sessions stored in PostgreSQL (`identity` schema). Web uses same-origin secure cookies with origin/CSRF checks; native uses a bearer session in platform secure storage. Mail is sent through the existing AgentMail inbox (CF-40) by the API, one send, no retry, no address oracle. Disposable test accounts on dev/stage can additionally be signed in with an operator-minted one-time link created from a server shell; this path is disabled in production by configuration and refused at startup if enabled there. If the S1.3 proof shows better-auth cannot meet these constraints with Nest/Express and Expo, the fallback is a small first-party magic-link implementation following the same rules, recorded here.

## D-008 · 2026-10-19 · One origin per world

The Expo web export is served statically by Caddy on `app.<world>`. The same host proxies `/v1/*`, `/auth/*`, `/socket.io/*` (WebSocket upgrade), `/media/*`, `/s/*` share previews and `/health` to the Nest API, so web needs no CORS and cookies stay first-party. Native clients call the same origin. `backend.<world>` keeps proxying everything to the API.

## D-009 · 2026-10-19 · Phase order adjusted

The reasoning harness, Scroll DSL/renderer and Scroll production move into Phase 2, ahead of Planner/Cartographer, so real model-generated interactive Scrolls exist before journeys that depend on inventory. Cutroom **reuse/import** of already-produced Reels also lands in Phase 2; a new paid **commission** is Phase 4 and needs an owner budget. Reason: authored fixtures cannot prove generation quality, and every later journey needs real admitted content.

## D-010 · 2026-10-19 · Where tests run

Automated suites and release checks run in GitHub Actions (owner rule 2026-10-19). Local runs are only for starting and using the product by hand, with data on the SSD. Required checks are never weakened or skipped to obtain a green run.

## D-011 · 2026-10-19 · The main-update hook was strengthened

`.claude/hooks/refuse-main-update.mjs` now also refuses `git -C <dir> push … main`, quoted refs, a bare `git push` from a `main` checkout, `gh pr merge` whose base is `main` (or cannot be confirmed), `gh api … base=main` and `gh repo sync --branch main`. `gh pr create --base main` stays allowed: preparing the owner's PR is in scope, merging it is not. The hook only loads for Claude sessions started inside a checkout of this repository; sessions started elsewhere rely on discipline.

## D-012 · 2026-10-19 · Scroll document DSL `scroll-document.v1`

The blueprint names a ScrollDocument but defines no schema; against the v2.1 screens it covers 4 of 37 needed capabilities (discovery `scroll-dsl.md`). v2 adopts the proposed declarative DSL with these rulings: 17 authored block types (paragraph with passage offsets, subheading, list, quote, callout, disclosure, code, table, figure/media, comparison, timeline, sequence, diagram with authored frames, chart, simulation with a closed set of formula kinds and views) plus a server-emitted `fallback`; no `predict_reveal` in v1 (it risks reading as a quiz); no expression grammar in v1; three separate artefacts (model draft, consumer document, internal provenance) with the client forbidden from importing provenance; code, not the model, sets reading time, coverage weights, gestures and the integrity hash; personal simulation parameters never enter telemetry and enter a snippet only when explicitly included; limits `scroll-limits.v1` as listed in the package README; client capability negotiation with per-block fallbacks. No model-generated JavaScript is ever executed.

## D-013 · 2026-10-19 · Research sources for generated Scrolls

v1 allowed only NASA, NOAA and USGS pages, which cannot support ML, history or football Scrolls. v2's research allowlist adds, per host and reviewed in code: English Wikipedia, arXiv abstract pages, US government `.gov` sites, Our World in Data, and official technical documentation (e.g. MDN, language/framework docs). OpenStax stays excluded (owner, 2026-09-24). The writer produces original prose: a copy check refuses any run of more than eight consecutive words from the material, quotes are kept internally for claim checking, and readers never see sources (owner rule) while provenance stays correctable. Pages are fetched by code (https only, size-bounded, redirects re-checked, no cookies), never by the model.

## D-014 · 2026-10-19 · Workspace, toolchain and release-tree conventions (S1.1 #224)

Status: accepted (coordinator, 2026-10-19), amended after the independent review of #252.

- **Pinned matrix.** Every third-party version is pinned exactly in the pnpm catalog (`catalogMode: strict`, `savePrefix: ''`, `strictPeerDependencies: true`) and recorded with dates, peers, licences and what CI proves in `operations/dependency-matrix.md`. Node 22.23.x (engines `>=22.23.0 <23`), pnpm 10.34.7, TypeScript 6.0.3, Nest 12.1.3 (not 12.2.0, released the same day with behaviour changes), oRPC 1.15.7, Expo SDK 58 (`expo@58.0.14` and its bundled module versions), PostgreSQL 16. pnpm 11/12 are not adopted: 11 renames build-script settings and adds release-age gates, 12 is moving fast (14 minors in 7 weeks); neither gives this workspace something it needs yet.
- **Sources inside, `dist/` outside.** Library packages (`contracts`, `backend`, `migrations`) export their TypeScript sources under the custom condition `@knowscroll/source` (type-checking, Vitest through `vitest.workspace-sources.ts`, dependency-cruiser) and their compiled `dist/` otherwise (builds, Node at runtime). A build never compiles another package's sources.
- **Release trees come from the lockfile.** After building, `pnpm --config.inject-workspace-packages=true --filter @knowscroll/api deploy --prod <release>/api` (contains `dist/main.js` and `dist/migrate.js`), the same for the worker, and the Expo web export for `web/`. Injecting workspace packages (on the command line only; ordinary installs keep linked packages) makes `pnpm deploy` install from the shared `pnpm-lock.yaml`; `--legacy` re-resolved instead and is no longer used (`scripts/release/build.mjs` uses the same command). The CI `build` job checks every installed version in both trees against the lockfile and boots the trees against PostgreSQL. The `.pnpm` folder names of injected workspace packages embed a hash of the build directory, so two checkouts produce different folder names (never different versions). Layout and variables: `engineering/workspace.md`, `engineering/runtime-configuration.md`.
- **Migrations** are the workspace package `@knowscroll/migrations`: reviewed `NNNN_name.sql` files at `migrations/` and a static registry with each file's SHA-256. `dist/migrate.js` refuses a file whose text does not match its recorded hash and a database whose applied migrations were applied with different text (hashes recorded in `migrator.migration_checksum` in the migration's own transaction), applies the pending batch forward-only in one transaction (a failure applies nothing), then installs pg-boss and applies every declared queue policy.
- **One tool per job.** Prettier (formatter), typescript-eslint typed rules (linter; a `default:` branch never counts as handling a union member), dependency-cruiser (boundaries, blueprint §22, with a CI probe proving each rule rejects a forbidden import and a manifest check for workspace dependencies), knip (unused code), squawk (migration hazards), Vitest (backend and pure code), jest-expo + React Native Testing Library (client). Automated suites run in GitHub Actions only (D-010).
- **Packages are created when code needs them.** `client-core`, `ui`, `scroll-model`, `scene-core` and `design-tokens` (as a package) arrive with their first code; the boundary rules for client packages already name them.

## D-015 · 2026-10-19 · @orpc/nest 1.15.7 on Nest 12.1.3 accepted; no OpenAPI-first fallback (S1.2 #225)

Status: accepted (coordinator, 2026-10-19).

The compatibility spike passed in CI through the deployed release trees: contract-first routes with `@Implement`/`implement()` (GET and JSON POST), request-derived callers through Nest's `@Req()` inside the handler closure, input validation, `customErrorResponseBodyEncoder` plus a `sendResponseInterceptors` entry that answers every error as `application/problem+json` (RFC 9457) with stable codes, a Nest exception filter and an Express error middleware for failures outside oRPC (unknown routes, body parser 413/400, non-JSON 415), the typed `@orpc/openapi-client` link from `@knowscroll/contracts`, and OpenAPI generation (`@orpc/openapi` + `@orpc/zod/zod4`). oRPC errors are not the public error format: oRPC's own 4xx codes map to their problem codes (any other 4xx stays a client error), and lock waits, statement timeouts, deadlocks and serialisation failures anywhere in the cause chain answer a retryable 503 with `Retry-After` (logged once as `http.retryable_failure` with their code, since a timeout or deadlock is usually a real fault) instead of a 500. oRPC 2.x (beta, tested upstream on Nest ^12.1.3) is not adopted; revisit when it is stable. OpenAPI-first remains the fallback only if a later oRPC release breaks this test.

## D-016 · 2026-10-19 · Command receipts are an infrastructure facility with their own schema (S1.2 #225)

Status: accepted (coordinator, 2026-10-19), amended after the independent review and re-review of #253.

Blueprint §5 lists "command receipts" under Experience, but every module's commands need them (social, scenarios, privacy), and modules write only their own data. Receipts therefore live in `packages/backend/src/infrastructure/receipts` with the infrastructure-owned schema `receipts`, erased per universe through the erasure registry:

- `personal_commands` keyed `(universe_id, command_id)`, read and written only inside the locked personal scope; the request hash covers the operation and the canonical validated request (keys in code-point order; non-plain data refused) including the revisions it relied on.
- A replay returns the stored outcome only when the receipt's privacy epoch equals the caller's and its `outcome_version` is the one the caller reads. A receipt from before a privacy reset is never replayed; the caller is already at the current epoch, so it answers `COMMAND_KEY_REUSED` (mint a new ID) rather than a reload-and-retry problem that would loop. Outcome versions: a release that writes version N also reads N-1; a receipt written by a newer release (rolling deploy) answers a retryable 503 and is never re-applied; one in a retired older shape answers `COMMAND_KEY_REUSED`.
- `shared_commands` keyed by scope kind and ID, actor, operation and command ID, usable only through a `SharedScopeTx`, which `enterSharedRoot` hands out at most once per personal scope after the personal root is locked (blueprint §19 lock order).
- **Refusals.** Retryable refusals (lock busy, stale epoch, missing or stale asset) write no receipt and are evaluated again on retry. A future command with a _terminal_ refusal (for example, sending to a room the person has left) must either store the refusal as its outcome or require the client to mint a new command ID; each such command states which.

## D-017 · 2026-10-19 · Diagnostics carry no free text (S1.2 #225)

Status: accepted (coordinator, 2026-10-19), amended after the independent review and re-review of #253.

`logEvent` fields are IDs, codes, route templates, counts, durations, configuration problems as `VARIABLE: kind_code`, and sanitised errors (class, code, shortened frames; never messages). Framework messages are reduced to recognised lifecycle codes; pg-boss warnings to their kind; plain-object errors keep only their code. The journal projection withholds personal-linkable IDs (blueprint §26 "Logging retention and erasure must agree"). Secret-shaped values are refused even in identifier-shaped fields (the canary test found a token that passed an ID pattern). The same rule covers job state: a failed job's stored output (`pgboss.job.output`, kept for days, dead letters for two weeks) is a code-only record (`{failure, errorClass, code}`), never the error's message, stack or driver detail; a payload that does not match its queue is dead-lettered at once. Consequence: some debugging detail (exception messages) is unavailable in the journal; rich, personal detail belongs in erasable, access-controlled decision records when they arrive.

## D-018 · 2026-10-19 · Owner: Cutroom budget for v2 Reels — up to $2

The owner allows v2 to spend **up to $2 (200¢)** on new Cutroom Reels. The shared `knowscroll_pool` record stays the single money record: its cap moves from 700¢ to 900¢ ($7 + $2) through a new pool migration (never editing an applied one), and v2 holds one grant of at most 200¢; Cutroom's own per-Reel cap stays 75¢. The owner still approves each reel script in its PR (CF-62). Before the first paid order the fal balance is read server-side and must cover the order. Applies from S4.2 (Phase 4).

## D-019 · 2026-10-19 · Owner: Android builds and native journeys run in GitHub Actions

GitHub-hosted runners may build the Expo Android app (prebuild + Gradle) and run emulator/Maestro journeys; this supersedes the v1 "Android builds only on the owner's Mac" rule (CF-31) for v2. The owner tests on a real phone later; until then native acceptance is labelled emulator evidence, never physical-device evidence. No self-hosted runner, no signing secrets in CI until release work needs them.

## D-020 · 2026-10-19 · Owner: `checks` is required on `dev`

Ruleset `25423111` ("dev: v2 checks must pass") requires the GitHub Actions checks `integrity`, `static`, `backend`, `build`, `client` and `release` on `dev`, with no bypass actors. The two existing rulesets (no deletion/force-push; only promoters update main and stage) are unchanged.

## D-021 · 2026-10-19 · Owner: personal-context model roles may use MiniMax for real users

Planner, Cartographer, Scenario Planner and room AI roles may send bounded personal-context-derived inputs to MiniMax for real users, not only test accounts (owner, 2026-10-19), knowing MiniMax's terms allow inputs to improve its services. The blueprint's minimisation still applies: each role sends only its prepared, bounded context (topic/unit references and authorised thread excerpts), never secrets, raw observation streams or another person's private data; requests are not logged; the privacy screen (P02) states that a third-party model processes exploration context.

## D-022 · 2026-10-19 · Identity: first-party magic links and sessions, not better-auth (S1.3 #226)

Status: accepted (coordinator, 2026-10-20; proposed by the S1.3 worker 2026-10-19, revised after the independent review and re-review of PR #257, merged as `d467f664`, applied and verified on dev 2026-10-20). Applies D-007's fallback clause.

D-007 asked S1.3 to prove better-auth first. The spike ran better-auth **1.7.10** (MIT; magic-link and bearer plugins, Kysely adapter with `schemaName: 'identity'` and snake_case field maps, `generateId` → UUIDs) on Nest 12.1.3 / Express 5.2.1 against a throwaway PostgreSQL 16 (hands-on, real database; script and output in `/Volumes/Mrigesh SSD/knowscroll-dev/v2/evidence/s13/spike-better-auth/`, and in branch history at `13f738f6`). What worked: mounting under `/auth/*` before Nest's JSON parser (other routes kept their body parsing), storage in the `identity` schema, hashed single-use tokens (`storeToken: 'hashed'`), cookie and bearer session lookup, an origin check on its own routes. What it cannot satisfy without being taken apart:

1. **A GET signs in.** The link points at `GET /magic-link/verify`; a request with no cookie and no origin — what a mail scanner or prefetcher sends — answered 302 with a session cookie and created a session. The rule is "consumed by POST; a prefetched GET cannot sign in". Keeping better-auth would mean disabling its verify route and calling its server API from a POST of our own.
2. **No account and universe root in one transaction.** `databaseHooks.user.create.after` runs after the user insert (queued after its transaction by design); a failure there left an account without a universe and answered 500. The rule is one transaction with a receipt.
3. **Its error and log formats are not ours.** Errors are `application/json {message, code}`, not RFC 9457 problem details (D-015); it logs free-text messages and stacks (D-017) unless its logger is replaced.
4. **Operator sign-in would couple to its internals** (its verification identifier and value format), and it needs a signing secret per world that we would have to provision.

What remained after the necessary workarounds was a session table and token hashing behind a framework that owns its tables, routes and error shapes. The first-party implementation (`packages/backend/src/modules/identity`, README there) has exactly D-007's properties:

- Links: 32 random bytes (`ksl_…`), only the SHA-256 stored, at most 15 minutes (database check), single use, consumed only by `POST /auth/redeem`. The token rides in the URL fragment; the redeem page first asks `POST /auth/peek` whose account the link opens (the full address for a web link) and redeems only after the person taps "Continue as <address>" (login forgery by link shows itself). Addresses: a strict RFC 5321 subset (printable ASCII, dot-atom local part, LDH domain; anything else `422`). Budgets: 10 requests per client (an IPv6 client is its /64; an in-memory flood brake, not a source of truth) before anything else; then, durably and counting only links actually issued, 3 per address, 60 per recipient domain and 300 per world per 15 minutes (domain and world each under an advisory lock), each answering `429` with `Retry-After`, a filled domain or world budget logged once per window (`identity.sign_in_budget_full`). `202` for every acceptable address and no account created before redemption (no address oracle); one AgentMail send per link, not awaited, never retried. Expired links and long-ended sessions are removed by a scheduled pg-boss job (`identity.sweep-sign-in-records`, every 15 minutes), never in a request.
- Native links are bound to the device (PKCE-style, RFC 7636 S256): the app keeps a verifier in its secure store and asks for the link with its challenge; only the verifier redeems it (and peeks at it). A link asked for without a challenge is redeemed by the web only. This is the device binding §19 asks for; OAuth itself stays out of scope. **Off on every world** (`KS_NATIVE_SIGN_IN_BINDING`) until the native app ships, because as built anyone could bind a person's links to their own challenge and lock the address out of its budget; before enabling it, count the address budget per binding or let the web redeem a bound link with a code from the mail body (re-review N1).
- Redemption: one transaction — address advisory lock → link row lock → find or create account + address + universe root (epoch 1) → `WithResolvedPersonalScope` locks and rechecks the root → receipt `(universe, commandId)` → session. Every refusal rolls the whole transaction back, so a refused redemption consumes nothing. Retrying the same command replays only while the session that redemption issued is still live, and replaces it; concurrent retries and two links for one address never create a second root.
- Sessions: 32 random bytes (`kss_…`), hash stored, 30 days, revocable; web gets an HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax, `__Host-` Secure cookie and cookie-authenticated mutations and socket handshakes must carry the world's `Origin`; native gets a bearer token once for its secure store; a web token is never accepted as a bearer token or vice versa. Authority (account, universe, current epoch) is re-read on every request; revoked or expired sessions are 401.
- The scope claim (one guard in front of every `/v1` route; opt-outs only via the reviewed `UNSCOPED_V1_ROUTES`): every account request carries `X-KS-Scope: <accountId>:<privacyEpoch>` and every socket handshake the same in `auth.scope`; the API serves it only when it names the presented session's account and current epoch (`409 SCOPE_MISMATCH` otherwise, nothing read or written). The client's AccountScope owns its own credential and ends before a switching redemption is sent; on `SCOPE_MISMATCH` it rereads the session. So an account switch in another tab, or a privacy reset whose revocation notice was lost, never serves one scope another's answers.
- Operator sign-in for disposable accounts (`dist/operator.js issue-sign-in`): only in the allow-listed worlds dev, stage, ci and local (refused elsewhere, live included whatever it is called), only with `KS_ALLOW_OPERATOR_SIGN_IN=1`, and only for addresses matching the world's `KS_OPERATOR_SIGN_IN_PATTERN` (dev and stage: `*@test.knowscroll.space`), checked at API startup, at command startup, at run time, when the link is issued and again when it is redeemed. An account whose address proved itself by mail is never opened by an operator link. Each issue writes a durable audit row (`ops.operator_sign_ins`: world, time, `cli:<operator>`, account) that survives "clear history" and becomes a tombstone on account deletion.

Consequences: `KS_AUTH_ADAPTER` defaults to `session` (`none` is gone; `test-bearer` stays a CI fixture, and it too must send the scope claim). Production refuses to start unless `KS_MAIL_SENDER=agentmail` is fully configured; the v2 API unit reads `/etc/knowscroll/secrets/shared/agentmail.env` and the v2 settings gain `KS_MAIL_SENDER=agentmail`, `KS_ALLOW_OPERATOR_SIGN_IN=1` and `KS_OPERATOR_SIGN_IN_PATTERN` (Ansible `roles/worlds_v2`; apply before deploying a revision with S1.3). The v2 programs run as their own users `ks2-<world>` with `/etc/knowscroll` invisible to them, and the v2 web app gets a Content-Security-Policy and `X-Frame-Options: DENY` from Caddy (both applied by Ansible). No signing secret is needed. Open for the owner before live (re-review N2): valid addresses still spend the domain and world budgets, so a few dozen client addresses can switch sign-in off for a domain or the world; the options are in the identity README ("Before live"). OAuth does not apply until a third-party provider is added ("other sign-in options" stays a placeholder); when one is, revisit this entry rather than growing the first-party code into an OAuth server.

## D-025 · 2026-10-20 · Coordinator: navigation lineage and mutable encounter context

Implementation interpretation of blueprint §§8–9 and N01/J1/J5: platform Back restores a parked
AccountScope entry without a command, activation or new evidence/return bonus. A later deliberate
navigation may name any previously activated, still-authorized member offer/exposure of that same
session and epoch. It must carry the latest server intent revision, not the parked snapshot's old
revision. Cross-session, stale, withdrawn or otherwise ineligible lineage is refused. The new move
commits its new intent atomically; restoring Back does not manufacture a server encounter.

An authorized exposure-context read supplies current action state, latest Composer session revision
when applicable, and bounded existing series/related/format availability. Mutable context is not
stored in immutable offers/receipts. A ready continuation descriptor is not activation authority; a
card-selected expected link/revision is revalidated rather than silently substituted. Pending always
requires existing durable authorized work; no invented series total, future part or generated supply.

Explicit actions may carry an exposure ID. The consumer rechecks ownership, activation, epoch and
exact revision before using it for encounter attribution. An action without this lineage remains an
asset preference only; never guess the latest exposure or award encounter qualification/bonuses.
Saved keeps its original immutable revision; a current revision may appear only with corrected
availability and must differ. Neither Saved nor a context read bypasses withdrawal.

New command branches stay strict. Existing legacy unknown-field stripping remains compatible
except newly reserved discriminators (`target` for activation, `status` for open responses and
`sessionId` for activation responses); malformed new branches cannot downgrade into legacy effects.
Interim consumers refuse unsupported new commands before any effect. Shared contracts land and
pass independent review/Actions before feature consumers.

## D-026 · 2026-10-20 · Coordinator: initial layered 2D Universe renderer

Use the existing react-native-svg dependency for the initial layered 2D Universe renderer, with ordinary accessible RN/Web controls and lists. The [source/design study](discovery/scene-renderer-study.md) evaluates committed U01–U08/D02, blueprint§13/§18 and current scene-core/Scroll runtime evidence; U07 explicitly permits layered SVG without 3D. This is an initial implementation choice, not populated-scene or physical-device performance acceptance. Keep descriptor/formation/identity independent of rendering and revisit from actual scene/device evidence if needed.

The server persists meaning-selected family/template, opaque personal seed, generator version and anchors; rendering never rerolls identity from sibling order or activity. Full/low/reduced-motion keep the same semantic targets and framing. No arbitrary model SVG/scripts/URLs, placeholder growth, evidence-from-map-visits or client-invented region offers. Exact scoped camera return, supported Composer Open and durable per-account shown/skipped transition acknowledgement remain explicit acceptance work; origin IDs or client memory alone do not establish cross-device once-only presentation.

Initial finite scene convention (coordinator refinement 2026-10-20, clarified 2026-10-21): the established Earth system and its star retain distinct object kinds, and both use the existing terraced.v1 family/template fields. Star names an object kind and renderer semantic, not a fourth SceneFamily; the earlier phrase “star family” was ambiguous. Seasons uses terraced.v1, Tides archipelago.v1, and Greenhouse ridged.v1. Regions inherit their canonical parent's selected family. These are cosmetic rendering conventions, not scientific classifications, formation evidence or new meaning authority. Persist family/template, seed, generator and anchors before variation; unsupported/ambiguous meaning labels fail closed and alias resolution remains Geography-owned. The initial projector does not bootstrap nonempty historical worlds: empty worlds remain empty, and any later bootstrap requires an explicit reviewed maintenance path.

## D-027 · 2026-10-20 · Coordinator: public social view revisions do not expose private transitions

Sharing contracts distinguish caller-visible view revisions from internal pair/authority revisions. Silent decline leaves the sender's pending view, expiry and visible revision unchanged until the fixed public expiry; revision staleness/refusals cannot disclose a recipient-private transition. Future consumers still lock/recheck internal authority before replay or effects. Hidden, expired and unknown presence share one constant unknown wire branch with no event-bearing revision, expiry or TTL; authorized visible availability may carry bounded validity. Public visitor IDs are publication-scoped identities, never serialized private Geography/Scene/universe IDs. The initial visitor wire may support an explicit semantic/list fallback; this does not implement the U06 visual renderer or server projection.

## D-029 · 2026-10-21 · Coordinator: initial Friends request window

The first backend Friends slice uses one fixed seven-day public request window. Its sender resubmission cooldown ends at that same public expiry. A private recipient decline changes neither the sender's pending view nor this deadline, revision, no-op/replay behavior or notices (D027). Reads and commands evaluate expiry from server time even if cleanup stalls; expiry materializes its public revision at most once. Reciprocal request is not acceptance. ## D-030 · 2026-10-23 · Owner: the Codex run ends; new working flow and budgets

The Codex coordinator was stopped on 2026-10-23 after an audit found the foundation sound but every completion requirement open: no generated Scroll accepted, dev not promoted, stage on v1, and most time spent on review and evidence ceremony. A Claude coordinator (Opus 5.5) continues with Sonnet 5.5 subagents and workflows. The owner decided:

- **Working flow:** `AGENTS.md` "How we work" replaces the previous working rules and `docs/CHECKPOINT.md`. Status lives in `docs/STATUS.md` (one page, plain language) and tracker #222. New branches are `claude/<issue>-<purpose>`; the integration branch keeps its name `codex/release-knowscroll-v2`.
- **Tests (supersedes D-010):** unit and integration suites may run locally, against a disposable PostgreSQL on the SSD, while building. GitHub Actions `checks` remains the required gate before any merge. Browser, Android and end-to-end journeys run in Actions or against dev.
- **Model budget (supersedes the call ledgers of D-024 and D-028):** MiniMax may be used as much as needed until the token plan's 5-hour window limit is reached; work then continues on other tasks and generation resumes after the window resets. No per-call reservation or ledger beyond the product's own attempt ledger. Scroll writing uses `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash`. Target: 50–60 generated Scrolls through the real production pipeline, at least 20 with interactive blocks.
- **Cutroom (supersedes D-018's $2 and, for v2 Reels, CF-62's per-script approval):** up to **$4 (400¢)** of new fal spend for v2 Reels. The coordinator may write, check and order Reel scripts itself; each script and its settled cost is logged in #240. The pool spend record changes only through its own functions and migrations.
- **Dev database:** `knowscroll_v2_dev` may be dumped to the SSD and recreated so testing starts from a known state. The v1 databases and `knowscroll_pool` are not touched.
- **Dev and stage:** the coordinator may promote integration to `dev` (D-002) whenever testing needs it, and promote to `stage` with `scripts/promote.sh stage` for the release candidate. `main` and production stay owner-only.
- **Existing code:** keep what follows the blueprint. Code that contradicts the blueprint is corrected to it; nothing is replaced with quick shortcuts. Quality and strict conformance to the agreed architecture come before speed.

## D-031 · 2026-10-23 · MiniMax model naming

`/v1/models` lists `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash`, `MiniMax-M3`, `MiniMax-M2.7`, `MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed`, `MiniMax-M2.5(-highspeed)`, `MiniMax-M2.1(-highspeed)` and `MiniMax-M2`. A request for an unlisted name such as `MiniMax-M3.1` or `MiniMax-M3.1-Pro` is served by `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` without an error, so the adapter must check the response's `model` field against the configured model. `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` returns a `thinking` content block before its text by default; `MiniMax-M3` does not. `MiniMax-M3` is the verified vision model; whether `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` accepts images is unverified.

## D-032 · 2026-10-24 · Scroll writer: one call, a flat authored shape, plain-text JSON

Every earlier writer attempt (about 12, on `MiniMax-M3`) failed, and the failures were blind because refused answers were discarded. A reproduction on the old pipeline (claims call, then a writer call that returned the draft as a JSON string inside a tool call) showed the cause: the model dropped a quote and left a bracket unbalanced while writing a long, deeply nested document inside an escaped string. The harness now keeps what it refuses, and the writer was rebuilt around what the model does reliably.

- **Visible failures.** A refused answer of a nonpersonal production attempt is kept 30 days, redacted and cut at 64 KiB, with the validator's own findings (`reasoning.production_rejected_answers`, `reasoning rejected` operator command). Personal roles keep nothing: only the production ledger implements the recording port.
- **Carrier.** The answer is one JSON object in plain text, parsed strictly. The `submit_output` and JSON-string tool carriers, the structural schema sent to the provider and their fixtures are deleted.
- **Authored shape.** The model writes `scroll-authoring.v1`: a flat list of blocks (a heading block starts a section), plain strings for rich text, ids it picks for diagram nodes, comparison as a table of rows. Code converts it to the canonical `scroll-draft.v1`, derives accessibility and fallback text, aligns simulation grids and marks every simulation illustrative. Every canonical check still runs. Cosmetic text up to twice its limit is shortened at a word boundary; prose keeps its strict limit.
- **One call instead of claims then writer.** The writer receives the frozen research as numbered passages (`e1`…) and cites them per block. Code maps each citation to the exact stored passage, so a claim is still a substring of fetched research, and derives the bindings: a figure in any reader-facing text must appear in a passage the block cites, a chart plots only such figures, and at least four distinct passages are cited. This keeps the claims stage's guarantee (exact source text, no invented figures) and removes a call, a stage and a failure point. A job is two requests at most.
- **Repair.** One repair per job, carrying fixed findings located at the authored block. It also carries the refused answer, escaped and delimited as data in the user message (never in the instructions), so the model revises instead of rewriting from nothing. Only roles that opt in and only production, where the answer is kept, can do this.
- **`MiniMax-M3.1-Flash`.** Sent `thinking: adaptive` (it refuses `disabled`); thinking blocks are dropped and thinking tokens recorded. Left alone it once thought for 15,000 tokens and never answered, so the writer asks for `output_config.effort: medium` (honoured by the route; `budget_tokens` is not) with a 16,000-token ceiling. The reply's `model` must equal the configured model or the attempt fails as `MODEL_MISMATCH` with its usage charged. It is the default model for every role; a role that names no effort gets `low`, so thinking cannot eat the small output ceilings of decision roles. `thinking: disabled` is sent only to `MiniMax-M3`, where it is verified; the M2.7 models get no `thinking` parameter. A request may take two minutes or more, so the default request timeout of every worker is 240 s (at most 300 s): a timeout after the request left makes the attempt `unknown`, never resent, and burns the topic's job.
- **Allowed blocks and source names.** Code refuses a block type outside the prose blocks and the recipe's own format block (`block_type_not_allowed`), not only the prompt. A reader must not see the organisation or the web address of a source the Scroll rests on; ordinary words of a host name stay allowed, and the repair hint names the word to remove from a fixed list.
- **Yield addendum (24 Oct).** Dev showed most refusals were `copied_passage` in both attempts, plus misspelt diagram styles and one nested operand. Changes without weakening a rule: the repair names the copied words (`repairNotes`, a delimited `<repair-notes>` block cut from the research text; the nine-word rule of D-013 stays); the instructions say never to use more than five words in a row from a passage and to paraphrase technical definitions; a diagram's layout or node kind that is not an allowed value becomes the default and is recorded in the provenance as `authoring-normalisation`, because it changes no meaning; the simulation reference says an operand is a number or a key and shows a ratio over a constant. The writer prompt is `scroll-writer.v13`; the recipe `scroll-production.v14` is unchanged, so no published Scroll or job is touched.
- **One recipe version.** The version history is collapsed: `scroll-production.v14` with writer `scroll-writer.v12` (`v13` after the yield addendum). Terminal jobs of earlier versions are not carried; migration 0050 drops the claims run column and the `claims` state.

Not decided here: the quality bar for generated Scrolls. Numbers and quotations are checked mechanically and do not prove entailment; human sampling stays.

## D-033 · 2026-10-24 · Generated Scroll coverage is decided by code from the recipe

The coverage approval list was empty, so no generated Scroll could carry topic or facet coverage and no planet could ever form. Content now holds a library plan, and code decides coverage when a generated Scroll is published.

- **Library plan.** The topic systems, planets, facets and explanation recipes are code-owned data in `content/domain/library/`, one file per system. The concept catalogue `content-concepts.v1` is derived from it, so nothing is written twice. A recipe names its planet, one facet (and optionally a supporting facet), a brief, and one to three research pages. Every planet has at least three recipes over at least two facets, as Cartographer's formation rule needs.
- **Coverage is decided by code.** In the same production transaction that publishes the revision, the hook attributes the recipe's planet (one primary topic, weight 1) and appends a coverage head. Rule `scroll-recipe-coverage.v1`: the primary facet needs the first, middle and last eligible body block; a supporting facet needs the last two. A person or a model never approves coverage. The rule is structural, not semantic: the recipe brief and the writer checks govern whether the prose teaches the facet. A document with fewer than three eligible blocks publishes without coverage and the job's outcomes say so.
- **Kept.** Exact asset revision, immutable history, a correction appends a new head, no personal data. The standalone reviewed-command path stays as a default-deny seam for authored content.
- **Catalogue changes.** Reads do not compare the catalogue digest. Each coverage binding pins its facet's meaning; a binding whose facet vanished or changed is left out of the read mapping and never throws. `content readmit-coverage [--dry-run]` decides coverage again for published generated Scrolls through `admitRecipeCoverage`, appending corrected heads and keeping history.
- **Scenes.** Each system (and optionally planet) in the plan names its Scene family, from the three the renderer has. The Scene projection reads it from the plan, so new systems and planets draw. Earth keeps its assignments, and a drawn object never changes family.
- **Recipe keys.** SQL checks only the shape of a recipe key (migration 0045). The code-owned plan decides which keys exist, so adding a recipe needs no migration.
- **Research pages.** Wikipedia was unusable through the research extractor: footnote markers hid sentence ends from the quote-candidate splitter, which then refused the page. The extractor now drops footnote markers, typeset maths and reference lists, keeps an arXiv abstract, and the fetcher identifies itself with a contact URL as Wikimedia's user-agent policy asks. The quote-candidate pool still holds at most 32 candidates, so a recipe names one long page or two short ones; `scripts/check-library-sources.mjs` checks every page.

## D-034 · 2026-10-24 · Composer buffers and Jev (#234)

The blueprint (§9) leaves these open; this is what the code does.

- **When a buffer is prepared:** when an offer is activated, in the activation transaction, not when the navigation that produced the offer commits. Only then is the encounter really the person's current one and in the recent-repetition input, so the worker ranks what the person will actually move on from. The job is IDs only (`composer.prepare-buffer`).
- **Key and coalescing:** one buffer per person and mode (`vertical`), bound to the session, its intent revision and the source offer and exposure. The job carries the revision; a job whose session moved on does nothing, so a newer activation supersedes older jobs. Preparing replaces the previous buffer and deletes its decision.
- **What is stored:** a normal Composer decision (bound to revision + 1, the revision the serving navigation commits) and a pointer row `composer.buffers` with up to three selected offers and their catalogue watermarks. Serving deletes the row in the committing transaction; a buffer serves at most once.
- **Lifetimes:** a buffer is served for 10 minutes. Stored Jev assessments are valid for 30 minutes and live in the buffer row. After the buffer's life the HTTP path ranks deterministically, using the assessments while they are valid.
- **Serving checks:** transaction 2 rechecks each selected offer exactly as for a fresh ranking (admission at the exact revision, catalogue watermark, Hide, link). Drift deletes the buffer and its decision and allows the existing single rerank.
- **Jev:** role `composer.jev`, version `jev.1`. Criteria: relevance, appropriateness, redundancy, each in [0,1]. `SemanticFit = 0.4 relevance + 0.4 appropriateness + 0.2 (1 - redundancy)`, combined by code. Shortlist: the 20 best eligible candidates by the deterministic baseline. One call per key (no repair); the harness run ID is the queue job ID, so a redelivery cannot pay twice. Failure, refusal or an unusable answer is the absence of an assessment, never a negative judgement. Switch `KS_JEV_ENABLED`, default 0, set to 1 on dev. Model: the harness's configured model; `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` once #232 lands its adaptive-thinking handling. Limit: the generic personal harness runs only while recording is at its initial generation, so a person who used Pause has no Jev until the recording-aware harness accepts a Composer purpose.
- **Exploration:** unchanged. 10% of vertical first slots go to the exploration pool; the branch and the realised probability are in the stored decision, for buffers too.
- **A subject move never offers the explanation the person is on** (hard eligibility `current_explanation`, in either format). Dev showed "Next subject" repeating the watched Reel. With nothing else to offer the move is exhausted.
- **Registry digest (removed by D-050):** `composer.buffers` and the queue payload store change the fresh-recording registry, so its digest and the places that repeat it in SQL change (migration 0047). Schema 44 is live on dev and its accounts hold witnesses stamped with the old digest, which the application reads as an unavailable lifecycle (every recording-aware command fails for them). 0045 therefore re-stamps existing witnesses with the new digest, with the guard trigger off for that one statement. That is truthful because the new stores are empty for every existing universe, and an integration test applies 0045 over a 0044-era account and then opens, activates and navigates. A binary still running the old digest sees the lifecycle as unavailable until it is replaced. This step recurs for every new personal store until the manifest is simplified.
- **Unassessed candidates are neutral:** when Jev assessed part of a shortlist, an unassessed candidate's SemanticFit is 0.5 (the lexical overlap sits on a much lower scale). With no assessment in play all candidates use the lexical baseline as before.
- **Cleanup:** a navigation deletes the person's buffer once it is past its life with no valid assessment; the worker's replacement deletes the previous one; `pruneComposerDecisions` deletes expired buffers before decisions. No separate sweep.

## D-035 · 2026-10-24 · Coordinator: how the Planner's plan reaches Composer and supply (#236)

The blueprint draws Planner → Composer and Planner → Quartermaster (§3) but does not fix the numbers. Choices:

- **Planner on.** `KS_PLANNER_ENABLED` defaults to `1` in the `local` world and to `0` elsewhere. The dev world sets `1` in its environment file (`ks_v2_planner_enabled_worlds`). The switch stays. It spends the same MiniMax token plan and daily caps as every other role.
- **Role budget.** Role `planner2` allows 12,288 output tokens. `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` thinks before it answers and thinking counts against the ceiling; 4,096 was truncated twice on a real run. Real answers took 2,700–12,288 output tokens and 15–225 seconds per call, so the worker needs `KS_MINIMAX_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS=300000`, the largest it accepts (the default is 60 s and would leave the run `unknown`). A call that spends the whole ceiling on thinking returns no text; the single repair usually recovers it.
- **Which plan Composer reads.** The latest accepted revision, until each opportunity expires (the Planner already caps expiry at seven days). A run that is waiting for newer evidence does not hide the plan. Composer only reorders candidates it has already admitted, so an older plan cannot add content or evidence.
- **How much a plan can move a score.** The base Intent (vertical) or Continuity (horizontal) of every eligible candidate is computed first, from the current intent and links alone. When the plan has an unexpired opportunity suited to the move (`vertical` for a vertical move, `related` for a horizontal one), a candidate that ties for the best base value (within 0.01) and matches an opportunity gets `+0.15 × agreement`, capped at 1. Agreement is 1 for the same explanation in a format the opportunity lists when its target is admitted content, and 0.5 for a shared non-incidental topic in a listed format. Nothing is scaled down and no candidate below the best is lifted, so an old plan can only decide between candidates the intent or link does not already separate. It never overtakes a better match. With a source whose topics already give every same-topic candidate Intent 1, the plan changes nothing among them.
- **Record.** A matched candidate's evaluation in the Composer decision carries `plan` (revision, opportunity position, match, feature, before, after). Titles, purposes and briefs never enter the decision.
- **Stopped runs start again.** New evidence puts a run that ended `blocked` (disabled, no provider, quota, budget, a refusal: nothing was sent) back in the queue by the same transition as an explicit resume, so a paid answer already kept in the ledger is reused. An `unknown` run (a request may have been sent) is woken, and retired with a new business run as successor once its attempt is settled or the run is two hours old. The attempt is never resent and stays in the ledger as a liability.
- **Existing targets are unread.** Code refuses an `existing` target whose explanation the person has already met (qualified evidence or retained history). Missing supply may be built on a read unit.
- **Missing supply.** Each accepted `missing_supply` opportunity becomes one private demand row (`supply.requests`, origin `planner`) in the same transaction as the accepted plan, unless the person already has an open planned demand for the same brief (a unique index per person and shared group backs this). Supply builds the brief the same way as for a person's catalogue ask. Reuse applies only to admitted content the person has not met (the opportunity asks for something new), giving state `ready`. Otherwise the demand is `deferred`: it joins or creates the shared matching group. No resolver job is queued and nothing is produced; a later production path binds deferred demand. Only code-admitted catalogue topics in English become demand; others are not stored.

## D-036 · 2026-10-24 · Coordinator: deploys stay on the push path; one-use deploy admission reverted

#390 made every dev or stage deploy wait for a root-prepared, one-use intent and a separate `ks-deploy@` service. It was never installed on the server, and it contradicts the owner's flow (AGENTS.md: `dev` deploys on push; `stage` moves by `scripts/promote.sh stage`). Installing it with the next Ansible run would have blocked every deploy. It is reverted. The repository's `ops/vps/tool` now matches what the server runs. The deploy lock, the pre-migrate dump, the exact schema check before activation and the rollback guards stay as they were.

## D-037 · 2026-10-24 · Quartermaster Create, bounded takes and free-text matching (#239, #232)

Scroll production now runs as worker jobs, and a person's request for a topic with no admitted content ends in a produced Scroll. The blueprint (§11) names Create without numbers; these are the choices.

- **Create produces a library recipe, nothing else.** The catalogue is derived from the library plan, and a library test proves every facet has at least one recipe, so a valid catalogue brief always has a recipe. Code builds nothing from catalogue meanings and does no research lookup of its own: the recipe's brief and allowlisted pages are used as they are. A brief with no producible recipe left (all published, or none can be taken again) closes as `could_not_prepare`.
- **Which recipe.** For a brief, the candidates are the recipes that teach its facet, then those that cover it as a supporting facet (every recipe of the planet for a brief without a facet). Prefer one already running (the request joins that work), then one not started, then a rejected one that may be taken again. Published and stopped recipes are never chosen.
- **Only Scrolls, only catalogue meanings.** A Reel, or a variant of an existing explanation, has no production path and closes at once as `budget_unavailable`. A contextual free-text ask closes as `unsupported_request`.
- **One transaction.** Create commits the shared group (`supply.work.production_recipe`), the production job and the request together. A global advisory lock (last in the lock order) serialises Create decisions so the day's count and the group agree. A group whose production ended is closed instead of joined.
- **Daily caps, one per origin.** `KS_SUPPLY_CREATE_DAILY_CAP` productions per UTC day for people's asks, and `KS_SUPPLY_PLANNER_CREATE_DAILY_CAP` (default 5; dev: 5) for the Planner's demand, each counted from `supply.work` rows with a recipe by who commissioned them (`commissioned_by`). The Planner's effective cap is never above the people's cap, and it cannot use what an explicit ask needs. People's default is 20 in `local`, 0 elsewhere (dev: 10); 0 turns Create off for both. Joining a running group is always allowed. API and worker read the same variables.
- **A join revives the job.** Every join sends the group's production job again for its current take (same `singletonKey`, `duplicate: ignore`), so a job that paused or was dead-lettered resumes when another person asks, and a queued job is never doubled. A group with no commissioned production (it predates Create) is closed and recreated by `commissionSupply`, not joined.
- **Too many open asks.** A person may have at most 10 asks open (`requested` or `matching`); an eleventh is refused as `too_many_open` (HTTP 429, no receipt) until one ends or is cancelled. Each open ask polls through its own jobs, so the number is bounded. The Planner's demand is not counted.
- **Planned demand is for something new.** When a planned demand resolves, content binds only if the person has not met its explanation, re-derived at that moment under their personal scope from their exploration snapshot. Without proof of what they met, nothing binds. An ask the person made themselves has no such filter: reading a Scroll does not make it unavailable to them.
- **Research blips are not verdicts.** A transient research failure (`dns_timeout`, `request_timeout`, `network_failed`, `http_refused`, or an error that is not a refusal) leaves the job at `research` with no take spent and fails the delivery so the queue retries. Only a verdict about the sources (`soft_404`, refused host, unusable page) or the answer rejects a take.
- **Unsent attempts are not lineage.** A paused run leaves attempts that sent nothing (`not_sent`). They stay in the ledger and in the job's receipt but are left out of the proof a published Scroll must show, so a Scroll published after a pause is attributed to its topic and bound to its facets. Four unsent attempts exhaust a step (`attempts_exhausted`) and reject the take.
- **Jobs.** `content.produce-scroll` payload is the recipe, the recipe version and the take. The queue is pg-boss `exclusive` with `singletonKey = recipe:version:take`: one job per take in flight, and a finished or failed job never blocks a new send. Quartermaster sends at priority 10, the operator at 0. The consumer resumes the same business run on every delivery; an attempt of unknown outcome is never resent. A budget or quota stop pauses a run (job open, delivery completes) instead of rejecting the recipe.
- **Bounded takes.** A rejected job is final for its take, but a recipe may be taken again: the job identity is (recipe, version, take) with take 1 to 3. Take N starts only when takes 1 to N-1 all exist and were rejected, never after a published job or an unknown attempt. Each take is a new business run with its own writer run. `produce-library` and Create send the next take of a retryable recipe.
- **Waiting.** Each waiter polls through its own `supply.resolve-request` job every 20 seconds (a delayed job whose ID is per request and slot, so polls never fork) and closes after 30 minutes. Polling reads the recipe's standing, not a take, so a waiter benefits from a later take. The wait ends at once when production ended without a Scroll. A waiter whose group commissioned nothing commissions as if it had just asked. The 30 minutes also applies at read time: an open request past it reads as `could_not_prepare` whether or not a job closed it, and the next write freezes that outcome.
- **Recovering open demand.** `supply recover-open-demand [--dry-run]` sends one resolver job (stable ID per request) for every open ask, so asks that predate Create or lost their poll advance. It prints counts by state, queued and already queued, and calls no provider.
- **Free-text matching.** A free-text ask is stored as `matching` and decided by one role call, `supply.match-request` (`MiniMax-M3.1-Flash`, low effort, one repair), on the personal harness with the request ID as the business run. The answer must be an exact catalogue planet and optionally one of its facets (the schema refuses anything else); `null` closes the ask as unsupported. A match is then treated as the person's own catalogue ask. Limit: the generic personal harness runs only while recording is at its initial generation, as for Jev.
- **Facets by coverage.** A catalogue facet is established by admitted coverage (`content.region_bindings`), not by the discovery projection's own facet labels, which hold the planet ID and never matched a facet ID.
- **Production caps.** The production scope has its own daily caps (`KS_REASONING_PRODUCTION_REQUESTS_PER_DAY` 200, `KS_REASONING_PRODUCTION_TOKENS_PER_DAY` 3,000,000) in place of the per-person scope caps. The global and role caps still apply.
- **Requests list.** `GET /v1/content/requests` returns the person's own asks (not the Planner's), newest 20, each with its current status and a short title (their words, the catalogue topic or the ready Scroll's title). The client shows it at `/account/requests`; the same component can sit in Keep's Requested view.
- **Limits.** A closed request can be tried again with `request.retry{requestId}` only when it closed as could-not-prepare, at most three times (D-054, #454); otherwise a new ask is the way. No nearest-options list on a closed request; Pause recording blocks free-text matching; Reel production waits for Cutroom.

## D-038 · 2026-10-24 · Cartographer: code forms the places, the model proposes labels and routes

Reading now forms places on a running system (#237). A local drive with a real sign-in, the release API and worker, three Scrolls from the production path (authored CI fixture writer) and a real MiniMax-M3 role run showed dust after the first Scroll, a system and star after the second, and the planet with three regions after the third.

- **Rules decide formation.** `requiredChanges` lists every place the policy `geography-passive.v1` entitles (two distinct units: system and star; three units over two facets: planet; each supported facet of a formed planet: region) and every activity change that the latest support implies (quiet after 30 days). It is pure, parents first, with the canonical catalogue label, and it passes the same `proposalIssue` check as a model proposal.
- **The model still proposes.** A valid proposal is reconciled with the rules (`reconcileWithRules`): required changes it left out are added (`applied_with_rules`), and when the two conflict or do not fit one delta the rules alone are applied. When the model gives nothing usable (invalid output after the repair, provider failure, quota or budget, the role's failure brake, or a refusal before admission) the rules alone are applied (`applied_by_rules`). `geography.runs.outcome` records which. What only the model adds is the choice between canonical labels and routes between formed planets.
- **`unknown` never falls back.** The attempt may still be reconciled, so the run stays `unknown` until an operator resumes it. It is the only state that holds a run.
- **Nothing strands a person.** A refusal before any ledger run exists (no provider, request too large, role or context refused) applies the rules under the claim's authority, or releases the run when nothing is due. A role that is switched off releases its claimed run as `insufficient/disabled`. Unusable answers with nothing due settle the run. Runs that earlier code parked as `blocked/disabled` or `blocked/provider_unavailable` restart on the next material change. The next qualified encounter always starts a new run.
- **One switch.** `KS_CARTOGRAPHER_ENABLED=1` on the worker (default 0). The separate economic-permit callback is gone; the shared reasoning budgets and the failure brake bound spend. The worker also needs `KS_REASONING_PROVIDER=minimax` and `MINIMAX_API_KEY`.
- **Dust is a read, not a place.** `POST /v1/universe/emerging/read` returns one opaque, unlabelled cue per catalogue system that Personal Context holds a qualified unit for and that is not established yet. It has no label, topic or count, a stable ID derived from the universe, epoch and system, and no store of its own, so Clear removes it with the evidence. The Universe client draws it; no scene object is created.
- **A heavy reader degrades, never stops.** Trusted coverage is read in batches of 100 probes, split when a batch reaches the trusted read's own bound (it refuses past 128 KiB of output, which real certificates reach within a few dozen Scrolls). When the supports do not fit one frozen context (100 supports, about 360 KB), `compactSupports` keeps the newest support per facet of every planet, then further distinct units up to a planet's need, then the newest rest. That changes no place the rules form and no activity they set; past the budget the oldest support goes first. Candidate discovery refuses only past 1000 raw references.
- **Limits.** A run applies at most 32 operations; the rest is due at the next run. Only Scroll coverage is trusted facet support, so a Reel-only reader gets dust but no system until Reel coverage exists; a Reel and its Scroll twin share one explanation unit and count once. Labels are the catalogue's, never a personal rewrite.

## D-050 · 2026-10-24 · Plain erasure registry; recording witnesses carry no registry digest (#246)

Every slice that added a personal store had to swap a SHA-256 in the identity code, a CHECK and the birth trigger, and re-stamp every existing witness. Missing it stopped every recording-aware command for existing accounts (#396). The digest proved nothing about erasure.

- **The registry is a plain list.** Each module declares its stores in `adapters/erasure.ts`; `src/erasure-registry.ts` lists the declarations in eraser order. The 700-line manifest, its import-time hash and the frozen-declaration loop are gone. Social now declares its own Clear dispositions (all `preserve`) instead of the registry file doing it.
- **Completeness is a schema check.** `checkErasureInventory` also finds `person_id` and `*_person_id` columns, any foreign key to `identity.accounts` or `identity.universes` under any column name, and tables that reach a root only through other tables. It runs in CI and inside every Clear, as before. Shared tables pass by having none of these links; the Cutroom and Region shared inventories keep their own proofs.
- **The witness keeps what fencing needs.** Mode, epoch, account, origin and clear operation stay. Migration 0051 drops `registry_sha256` (and with it the CHECK) and rewrites the birth trigger. Existing 0044 and 0047 witnesses stay valid; an integration test upgrades both through the real migrator and then opens, activates, navigates, pauses, resumes and clears.
- **The current-registry proof stays.** `eraseCurrentExploration` is still the only runner that lets Identity install a witness at Clear, so a test registry cannot certify a Clear. It no longer compares a digest.
- **Rollout.** Apply 0051 with the new binary; a process of the previous recipe fails closed on the dropped column until replaced.

## D-039 · 2026-10-24 · Universe screens: how the map is drawn from persisted anchors (#238)

The Universe follows U01 to U08 and D02 with real scene data. Choices where the blueprint and the artboards leave room:

- **Anchors are a lattice, not a drawing.** The projector gives every object the nearest free slot on a 300-unit lattice, so siblings crowd onto whichever side of their parent was free. The client therefore uses anchors only for what they say reliably: a system's place on the map, and a planet's direction and distance from its star (distance picks one of three orbits, direction the spot on it). Two planets never share a spot because two objects never share a slot.
- **Region pins ignore anchors.** The first region takes a spot from its own seed; each later one takes the free spot farthest from those placed, in creation order (IDs are UUIDv7). A placed region never moves when another arrives.
- **One camera model.** A frame is relative to how the current view first draws itself: pan in screen pixels, zoom about the centre. A fresh frame always shows the whole view on any screen size.
- **Level of detail by size on screen.** Far (below 0.2 px per composition unit): star and name. Mid: orbits and planet dots. Near: planets. The same 48 px targets exist at every level. Far away a first tap selects a system and shows its card; closer in a tap enters it.
- **Palette from the scene.** The projector's palette picks the colour set (sage: teal with green land, cobalt: pale strata, ochre: amber dunes); the family picks the silhouette. This is cosmetic and says nothing about reading. Calm (quiet and Lately on) pulls colours 35 % toward grey and removes glows.
- **Memory per device.** Camera, Lately and the ship's place persist in the account scope's storage, never sent anywhere. After three days away the camera starts framed on everything and a "Return to ..." chip appears. `?focus=last` flies to the ship's place.
- **The avatar stays (D-006 c).** The map's root header keeps the account avatar beside the title. U01 and U08 draw search and Lately in that row, which has no room for the avatar at 390 px, so they sit on a second row under the title. System and planet views have a Back pill instead and no avatar, as on other tabs' detail screens. On desktop the rail carries it.
- **List view is the outline by system.** The artboards group by topic family, which the client has no data for. The sheet on phones adds no history entry of its own; from 1024 px the outline is a permanent panel.
- **Routes are the Cartographer's, nothing more.** A route joins two planets of one system, so the system view draws it with a 48 px target ("Route from A to B") and a planet offers "Route to B". Regions have no routes and no line joins them. The region card has no "More in ..." link until a read for more encounters from a region exists.
- **Not built:** the friend's universe (U06; there is no visit backend), room stations (the scene has no room objects), and named sky regions that merge quiet systems at far zoom (U08; no family data). A native persistent store waits for the platform work.

## D-041 · 2026-10-24 · Reels from Cutroom: one short operator path, with the money rules kept (#240)

The connected commissioning built for #240 (about 9,800 lines, with its tests) was never used and
could not be: it needed a root-owned approval packet, per-script human approvals, a corpus manifest,
funds and sole-writer attestations with expiry, a pool role mapped by OID, a function-definition hash,
and a pool grant capped at 200 cents, which cannot carry D-030's 400. It is removed. What replaces it is
short, and keeps every money rule.

- **Operator commands, no jobs.** `reel check | order | follow | receive | status | write-off | settle`
  in the worker operator. A Reel takes 13 to 41 minutes and the coordinator watches each paid step, so
  each step is a command that can be repeated; nothing waits in a queue.
- **Claim before order.** A paid attempt records the exact request bytes, then claims the whole
  ceiling in the shared pool, then commits a dispatch intent, then sends one POST. The table refuses a
  paid attempt that leaves `reserved` without a claim. The pool's own functions are used as v1 used them
  (`pool_claim`, `pool_settle`, `pool_release`); the pool cap is the one limit on total spend.
- **Never resent.** An uncertain POST is only looked up by request id. A run that exists is adopted.
  When none is found the attempt stays `unknown`; it can be written off (the claim stays open and
  counted) or, with proof (Cutroom answers, has no run, the POST is over two minutes old), written off
  with its claim given back. A retry is a new take and a new request id.
- **Settle once, at the real cost.** The cost is the sum over the chain (an original and at most one
  continuation Cutroom makes itself). A failed run that Cutroom is continuing is not the end. A
  completed Reel is published first and settled second; a Reel that is made but cannot be published
  stays held with its claim open until a person settles it (`reel settle`), except for the engine's own
  failed checks, a media file that is not a Reel and a repeat, which are settled as spent.
- **Budget.** At most 400 cents of new spend (D-030). Pool migration `0005` raises the cap to 1017, the
  617 cents already settled plus 400, after locking the claim table, and refuses a pool unless the cap
  is exactly 700 and exactly 617 is committed. Every order asks for exactly 75 cents, Cutroom's own cap
  for one Reel: Cutroom stops a run at its ceiling after it was paid for, so a lower ceiling can only buy no
  Reel. A paid order also needs a finished rehearsal here whose estimate plus 20 cents (the most real runs
  have run over) fits in 75. Settled cost above a ceiling is recorded as it was.
- **Approval.** A script carries `approvedBy: coordinator (delegated by owner, D-030)`. The owner does not
  approve each script in a pull request any more (D-030); the pool records the same text with the claim.
- **The twin.** A script names the library recipe whose Scroll is its twin. Every claim rests on that
  Scroll at one revision; the order refuses unless that revision is admitted in the ordering world. The
  Reel is admitted in the Scroll's explanation unit and planet, so Explore offers both formats.
- **Repetition.** The three fingerprints of CF-63 are computed from the script (the template fingerprint
  matches v1's `3d8d42d5`). A new script may not share one with the fourteen v1 scripts or with any Reel in
  the ordering world.
- **Receive.** The second world rebuilds the request from the same script, compares it byte for byte
  with the pool's record, reads Cutroom's runs and publishes the same Reel. It claims, sends and settles
  nothing.
- **Kept as it was:** the byte-pinned wire schemas (CF-61), the pinned engine, the GET-only read client,
  the media store and probe, and the importer of the three v1 Reels.
- **Removed:** migrations 0030, 0031, 0033, 0036 and 0039's tables (never populated; dropped by 0053),
  pool migrations 0003 and 0004 (never applied), the Cutroom queues, scopes and settings of the worker, and
  the `cutroom` operator commands.
- **Limits.** Nothing here has placed a real order. The record check is derived from the three Reels v1
  imported; no completed continuation run has been observed, so a Reel from one is the first real test of
  that branch (it is held, not refused, when the record and result disagree). Run ids must be UUIDs, as
  every real run has been.

## D-042 · 2026-10-24 · Conversations and share links (#242)

Choices where blueprint §14 and §17 and the C01, C02, H01 and H02 artboards leave room:

- **Two people per conversation.** This slice builds direct conversations only. Group threads come with "Explore with friends" (#243), which the share sheet already has a clean place for: it shows the option only when the caller passes a handler.
- **A conversation lives as long as the friendship.** A conversation is tied to the friendship's lineage. Remove and Block end it for both people with no answer that says why. After a person leaves, the other keeps the history, sees "This conversation has ended" and cannot send (`ended` on the snapshot, the inbox row and the changes page).
- **A conversation shows in Talk once it has a message.** Starting one is silent. Sending a Reel or Scroll starts it if needed.
- **One lock, in the usual order.** A command takes the personal root, then both accounts' fences, then the friendship row, then the conversation rows. Counters (`last_sequence`, `last_change_sequence`) are incremented in SQL, so deleting an account can tombstone its messages without taking the friendship lock.
- **Receipts as friends do.** Conversation commands use shared receipts keyed by the friendship. The same command id and body replays the same answer; a retry after an unknown outcome reuses the same id. A card names an exact asset revision, and a withdrawn or unavailable asset cannot be sent.
- **Live in two layers.** A committed message, read or leave bumps `social.inbox_heads` for each member and sends one NOTIFY with the scope `inbox`, named by the person's own universe id. The socket is a hint. The client then reads the changes page from its cursor, so a missed hint costs nothing. Talk also polls every 30 s and refreshes on focus.
- **Stateless cursors.** Inbox, history and changes cursors are base64url of a version, a number and an id. They are not signed, because each is only a position, and every read still checks that the caller is a member. The Friends roster cursor used a process-random AES key, so it died on restart and could not work across instances. It is now the same kind of plain keyset record (filter, limit, account, universe, epoch, head revision, last person), checked against the caller on use.
- **Share links last 30 days.** A link names an exact asset revision. Only the SHA-256 of its token is stored. The token is returned once with the first answer. If that answer is lost, the retry says `unavailable`; the person stops that link and makes a new one. A link has an audience: public (anyone with the link sees public metadata) or one conversation (only its members).
- **The landing is a page from the API.** `GET /s/<token>` returns HTML with no script, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` and a strict CSP. D-008 already sends `/s/*` to the API, so the edge needs no change. A person who is signed in sees "Reply in Talk" for a conversation link. A native deep link and a React Native `/s/[token]` screen wait for the native work.
- **Unavailable looks the same.** Unknown, revoked, expired, withdrawn and "private to you" all show "This link is no longer available", name nobody and answer 200 on the same page (D-006 h). The preview shows only public metadata (title, kind, summary). It never shows who shared it, a note or private reading state.
- **No typing indicator.** It would be a private behaviour signal for little value. Read state stays private: each person's `read_through_sequence` is theirs, and the other person sees only "Sent".
- **Receiving is not reading.** Sending, receiving, opening a card and following a link add no exposure, observation, Keep or evidence row. A card's "Open Reel" starts an ordinary encounter.
- **Deleted accounts (D-043).** `eraseAccount` deletes the person's share links, tombstones their messages with new change sequences and closes their conversations. The friendship row goes, so `conversations.pair_id` is nullable (`on delete set null`). The other person reads the closed conversation through their own scope, without a friendship lock, and sees "Deleted account" (`DELETED_ACCOUNT_LABEL`). Their own receipts apply. Export lists the person's own words, their shared links without tokens, and counts.
- **Clear keeps conversations.** Clear exploration is about what a person explored. It never removes messages, cards or share links.
- **Share is a sheet, not a screen.** The share sheet pauses playback while it is open and restores it, and is never in Back history (D-006 k). Copy link creates a public link the first time and offers "Stop sharing this link". P02 lists every live link with a Stop button.
