- **Not built:** group threads, a Report entry (P03), moderator and AI participants, read receipts, message edit and delete, a "new message" button in Talk, starting a conversation from a friend's row, a native `/s` deep link and Open Graph previews for private links.

## D-043 · 2026-10-24 · Account deletion, export and sessions (#246)

Choices where the blueprint (§5, §19, §26) and the P01, P02 and D05 artboards leave room:

- **One lifecycle for the person, driven by the registry.** Each module's `adapters/erasure.ts` now also declares `eraseAccount` (account-keyed rows and links, after every `erase`) and `export` (what the file holds). Every process refuses to start when a module has account-keyed stores and no `eraseAccount`, no `export`, or an export that does not account for exactly its declared stores (`accountLifecycleRegistry`). The integration test reads the registry, fills every store through real paths and authored fixtures, deletes, and asserts each declared store is empty; it fails when a store is never filled.
- **Fence by status, not by a new table of checks.** A deleting account and its universe take the status `deleting` (migration 0055) and the epoch advances once. Every guard in the code already tests `status = 'active'`, so requests, workers, sockets, sign-in and exports refuse it without a change. Sessions are deleted, not revoked. A Clear that still holds its barrier makes the request answer `unavailable`.
- **Two transactions, no model or network wait in either.** The request fences, records the operation and enqueues `identity.delete-account` together. The worker runs the erasers and removes the roots in one transaction under address lock → root → account → operation row, so a crash rolls everything back and a redelivery starts over; every eraser is idempotent. A foreign key from an undeclared store fails the final `delete` and rolls back. A paid attempt with an unknown outcome (reasoning reports `pending`) is never refunded or resent: the rest is erased and committed, the receipt stays `in_progress`, and the recovery cron (every 5 minutes, 25 per pass, 15 minutes between hand-backs) retries until accounting settles. Nothing is deleted from `identity.universes` while any row still references it.
- **The receipt is the client's own id.** The client chooses the deletion's UUID and keeps it before sending; it names no one and is no credential. After deletion every session is gone, so `GET /auth/deletion/{id}` and `POST /auth/deletion/retry` need none and answer only `in_progress`, `needs_retry` or `completed` (rate-limited per client; unknown id is 404). A lost answer is recovered by asking: unknown means the request never arrived. When the work completes the operation row loses its account and universe and stays as the audit row: id, two timestamps and a state. `ops.operator_sign_ins.account_id` becomes null (its tombstone, D-022). Native keeps the id in memory until the platform has a persistent store.
- **Placeholder rule for shared history.** Social's friendship rows end for the other person silently; nothing else there outlives them. Where shared history remains (conversations, threads, rooms: #242), the module's `eraseAccount` removes authored bodies and attachments, leaves a minimal unavailable-message marker where continuity needs it, and every surface shows `DELETED_ACCOUNT_LABEL` ("Deleted account", `@knowscroll/contracts`) instead of a name, handle or ID. Other people's messages, evidence and kept items stay theirs; a shared conversation is not deleted; deleted private text appears in no summary (blueprint §19).
- **Export is a file of your own records, in words.** `POST /v1/account/exports` records the request and enqueues the build; the worker reads each module's `export.read` inside the person's locked scope and stores one JSON file (at most 8 MiB, lists cut at 2,000 newest with `truncated`) on the export row. The file holds titles, times, requests, the places of the universe, the sharing profile and counts of connections, with a Keep place in words ("at 0:31", "part 2 of 5"); modules with nothing to say are not in the file; never IDs, hashes, tokens, scores, prompts, stored positions or another person's data (Social reports counts; Experience resolves titles through a read-only join of Content's immutable revisions). The file is downloadable once from a link of 60 seconds that is only a hash in the database, is served without a session under `/auth/exports/{token}` (that prefix is already proxied by every world; the link is the credential), and is removed from the row in the same transaction; only GET redeems it (HEAD and every other method answer 405, so a link prefetcher cannot spend it). A ready export expires after seven days, a stuck build fails after 30 minutes, receipts go after 30 days, five are kept per person. Clear removes the person's exports (an export copies the history Clear removes); deletion removes them and their queue jobs. Readable HTML is not built.
- **Sessions are listed by coarse label.** Account shows each live session as "Chrome on macOS" or "KnowScroll on Android" (computed once from the `User-Agent` at redemption, never stored raw) with its last use (written at most every ten minutes, in the statement that resolves the session). The person can sign one other session out, or all others; the presented session is ended only by sign-out (which clears the cookie). Sign-out asks once, in the row.
- **P02 as drawn, D05 as one page.** Phones and tablets get the hub (recording, History & export, Clear exploration, Delete account) with a page for each control; desktops get the settings list beside one card per control, and the sub-page addresses send them to it. The artboards' "[N days]" for backups is not shown because no number is known: the copy says backups are not used to rebuild anything and expire on their own schedule. P03 (Feedback & report) is a different feature. Other slices add privacy controls through `features/privacy/privacy-extensions.ts` (the share slice's revocation goes between Clear and Delete).
- **Not built:** readable HTML export, notifications when an export is ready (the page polls while one prepares), the profile Edit row and the settings P01 draws that have no backend, native persistence of the receipt id, a way to cancel a deletion once accepted.

## D-044 · 2026-10-24 · Operator visibility: ops views, the journey inspector and the Operator screen (#248)

Choices where blueprint §26, D-006 l and the Operator artboard leave room:

- **Views over the owners' tables, never copies.** Migration 0057 creates the `ops` views. Each lists its columns by name and shows IDs, states, times, counts and codes. Free-text columns that are really codes pass through `ops.safe_code`, which returns null for anything not code-shaped. A row that is cleared or erased is gone from a view at once, so nothing needs its own erasure. A test lists the exact columns of every view and plants private text in every table that holds some (an ask's words, a refused answer, a Reel script, an operator's note, a job payload).
- **pg-boss's views are not migrations.** pg-boss installs its schema after the SQL migrations and changes it when it upgrades, and a view blocks PostgreSQL from changing the columns it reads. `migrate.js` drops `ops.jobs`, `ops.queue_backlog` and `ops.recent_errors` before anything else, creates them after pg-boss has started and grants them again. A migration that changes a column an `ops` view reads drops and recreates that view in the same file.
- **One reader role, granted on every migrate.** `ks2_ops_reader` (SELECT only) reads every view except `ops.journey_events`, which carries scoped IDs. It is granted CONNECT on the world's database too, because that database refuses PUBLIC. The role carries a 30 s statement timeout, and `migrate.js` drops the job views with a short lock timeout and retries, so a dashboard query cannot stall a deploy. The migration role cannot create roles, so the owner creates it once per cluster; a missing role is a warning, and the next `migrate.js` grants it.
- **Who is an operator is an address list.** `KS_OPERATOR_ACCOUNTS`: addresses or one-`*` patterns. Unset: the sign-in pattern on `dev` with operator sign-in on, nobody elsewhere. A wildcard is refused on live. The API reads the caller's address on every call, so removing an entry takes effect on the next request. Every other account, signed out included, gets the same 404 as an unknown route whatever it sends (the journey route takes any string and parses the UUID after the operator check), and the refusal is logged as a code. The panels are read three at a time so they cannot take the API's connection pool.
- **A journey is a closure over keys, bounded by scope.** The inspector finds the steps that carry an ID, then follows the keys they name (request to group, group to the production runs of its recipe, run to attempts and refused answers, run to asset, exposure to asset) for at most four rounds and 300 steps. After the first step it reads only that person's records and shared records. An asset ID answers with shared steps only, and a production run shows no request, so several requests that waited on one group stay apart. Production runs link to groups by recipe and time (a group commissions its run when it is made), because no row names the group.
- **Gaps are shown, not guessed.** An attempt with an unknown outcome is a gap that says it is never sent again. A started step with no end after 30 minutes is a gap. Nothing is inferred from time order.
- **Account search is a server step.** `operator.js journey --account <id>` resolves the account through Identity's read port and opens that person's journey. The API and the screen answer record IDs only: a universe ID is refused there as unknown.
- **The screen has its own flat grey look**, desktop first, one to three columns by width. It is not linked from the app. Panels with no Tier 0 source (service health, request latency, media disk, realtime delivery, dropped diagnostics) are unavailable by construction, not by a failed read.
- **Not built:** Grafana dashboards (the role and views are ready; dashboards belong in `ops/observability/`), trace links, saved queries, per-hint realtime history, an account search in the screen, and retention of operator reads beyond the journal's counts.

## D-045 · 2026-10-24 · Native persistent storage and the lane's journeys (#249)

- **One synchronous port, encrypted underneath.** The account scope's `KeyValueStore` stays synchronous. On native it is now `createSecureKeyValueStore` over `expo-secure-store` (Keystore on Android, Keychain on iOS): memory is the working copy, a restart reads persisted keys back, and changes reach the backend through one ordered queue. The earlier work-in-progress branches rebuilt the scope around asynchronous "prepared" stores (about 800 lines plus changes to `AccountScope`); nothing in the product needs that, so it was not reused.
- **Bounded, and honest about durability.** At most 256 keys and 1 MiB of values; the oldest written go first, and a value larger than the whole budget is not stored. A change is durable a moment after the call, not at it. A backend that fails costs persistence, never correctness. A new key joins the index before its value is written and a value is deleted before its key leaves the index, so a kill in between never leaves a value that purge cannot find. After a failed index read or write the store writes no values, serves from memory, and retries the index at its next read or change, so an unreadable index is never overwritten.
- **Purge.** A scope still ends and purges its prefix (sign-out, account switch, Clear, account deletion). Because a native store outlives the process, mounting a scope also removes every other scope's keys (`purgeOtherScopes`), which cleans up a run that was killed mid-scope. The web keeps its shared `localStorage` untouched: other tabs may hold a scope. The deletion receipt id lives in the same store outside any scope, so it survives a restart and a sign-out.
- **Backups.** `expo-secure-store`'s config plugin is now listed in `app.json`, which excludes its preferences from Android backup (restored ciphertext without its Keystore key is unreadable).
- **Native sign-in binding in the lane, not on dev.** The test API sets `KS_NATIVE_SIGN_IN_BINDING=1`; dev and stage stay unset until the native app ships (the identity README's N1 conditions still hold). The native client already binds its links; the journey uses a mailed link read from the fixture mail file, not an operator link: a native link needs its verifier, and operator links carry no binding.
- **A test API in the emulator job.** The default `api_origin` of `native.yml` is `http://127.0.0.1:3399`, reached through `adb reverse`. A build for that origin sets `EXPO_PUBLIC_KS_TEST_API=1`, which lets the client use plain HTTP (still only to localhost, 127.0.0.1 and 10.0.2.2) and makes the manifest allow cleartext; the APK step checks both ways (allowed in a test build, absent otherwise). The API is the built release trees over a disposable PostgreSQL service, with the authored starter Scroll, one Reel from generated video through a fake Cutroom (labelled "[Fixture]"), and mail recorded in a file. Pull requests carrying `run-native` now build against it instead of the dev origin; an https `api_origin` still builds a normal app and runs only the signed-out flows.
- **Limits.** Emulator evidence only, one AVD. The journey proves restart persistence of the session and of one scope key (the dismissed hint), not every feature that uses storage. iOS is not built. Reading a Scroll gives the Reel/Scroll pair no topics, so no planet forms in the journey.

## D-046 · 2026-10-24 · Content threads, episodes and anchored snippets (#243)

Choices where blueprint §17 and the C04, H01, H03 and X03 artboards leave room:

- **A thread is a conversation of kind `thread`.** It has a root (the exact revision it started from) and a starter. Direct conversations are untouched. A thread never shows in the direct inbox; Talk lists both, newest activity first, from two reads (`conversations/inbox`, `threads/inbox`).
- **Friends are invited, not added.** "Create thread · N friends" (one to eight, all current friends) makes the thread and one pending invitation each. An invited person sees only the invitation (who asked and what it is about) until they join. After joining they read the whole history, including messages from before. An invitation is dated and ordered by when the thread began, never by when the others last talked. Declining ends the invitation.
- **An invitation rests on the friendship that carried it.** The member row keeps that friendship's lineage. Remove, Block from either side, or a new friendship later, end the member's place for them and drop them from the people list; nothing says why. The starter's place has no such dependency. If the person who invited someone leaves or deletes their account, those whose friendship still stands keep their place on their own membership, and anyone whose place had already ended stays out (their membership is closed, so releasing the link cannot give it back). Blocks between two invited members are not looked at: they need not be friends.
- **The thread is its own shared root** (`social_thread`, the conversation row): FOR UPDATE for commands, FOR SHARE for reads, after the personal root and the caller's own account gate. Counters stay atomic SQL, so account deletion tombstones without this lock; it takes the thread rows first, in id order, before touching any inbox head, so it never waits on a send while holding one. Inbox hints go only to members whose place stands and whose account is active.
- **Create is idempotent through the thread's own ID.** It is derived from the starter and the command, so a retry finds the same thread, and receipts live on that root. A refused create writes nothing.
- **Episodes are the declared series that contains the root revision** (`content.series_units`, latest series revision), by part, or the root alone. Corrected and withdrawn units are shown as such and never replaced. "Preparing" and "Suggested" are not produced until What if (#244) exists. "Visited" is the caller's own activated exposure of that asset: their own history, removed by Clear, never a grade, never another person's. There are no thumbnails: the Reel and Scroll have no poster source, so the card is a typographic tile.
- **Which episode is selected, and whether the player is folded, stay on the device** (account scope storage). The server holds no position, selection or seen marker of any member, and no answer has a field for one.
- **Snippets are anchors into the thread's own sequence**, checked against the exact current revision at send. A Reel range is at least one second and inside the duration. A passage is a stable block, a range on grapheme boundaries and the hash of the quote (scroll-model's locators). A block names its id, type and version and may carry a snapshot built by scroll-model; a value the sender typed themselves travels only with `confirmPersonal` (D-006 g). The server copies the quote, the block label and the settings in words when it stores the snippet, and shows them only while the content is not withdrawn.
- **A corrected source is never followed silently.** The card says "Updated". Opening it asks Content: a Scroll passage or block is re-pointed through the explicit anchor map of the two revisions and says so; a Reel has no time mapping, so the card says the moment changed and offers the current Reel, and the old range is never played.
- **Opening or expanding a snippet is not an encounter.** The viewer shows a poster or an introduction with an explicit Watch or Read; only that starts an ordinary encounter of the original content, with its own recorder. "Open as set" shows the sender's settings in memory only and never overwrites the reader's own saved settings.
- **A passage is picked by sentence** (a run of sentences of one block, at most 600 characters), which also works with a keyboard and a screen reader. Mapping a native text selection to offsets is not built.
- **Not built:** What if, perspectives and branches (#244), "Keep listening" (audio only), renaming a thread, adding people later, mute, Like and Save inside the thread's encounter area (they stay on the full-screen encounter), the multi-pane desktop thread (D03), a snippet's own link, and reporting from a thread.

## D-047 · 2026-10-24 · Sharing & visibility, availability and the friend visit (#241)

Choices where blueprint §17 to §19, D-006 e, D-027 and the F01, F02 and U06 artboards leave room:

- **Everything starts off.** Nobody may visit, no system is shared and availability is hidden. A person who never opens F02 has no row at all, and reading the settings writes nothing.
- **Visitors see systems and their planets, nothing else.** A shared system shows its name and its factual planets' names. Stars, regions, routes, hypothetical places, activity, positions, sizes and any count of what is hidden are never published. A hypothetical system is not even offered. Labels are the person's own words and must pass the public-label rule.
- **Public identities are random and short-lived.** Each shared system and planet gets a random ID that stays while it stays shared and is new when it is shared again, so two shares cannot be linked. The list is sorted by label, so its order says nothing about when anything formed. The visitor's device invents the map's positions, family, colours and seeds from the public IDs alone (`visitor-scene.ts`); the owner's anchors, seeds and families never leave.
- **A published copy, not a read of the owner's Geography.** A visitor's request never enters the owner's personal root (lock order, blueprint §19). The owner's own command (`visibility.set`) draws the copy from their Geography in the same transaction. The worker redraws it after each scene projection (system actor, its own transaction; a failure retries the job, which re-projects nothing). A universe that shares nothing is a no-op.
- **Revocation is the very next read.** Every visit read checks, under the friendship's lock, that both people are friends and neither blocks, that the owner's audience includes the visitor, and then reads the copy. The client rereads every 15 s while the screen is open and drops the list after two minutes without an answer, so going offline cannot hide a revocation. There is no socket hint for it.
- **One refusal.** Never shared, no longer shared, an ended friendship, a block, an unknown person and yourself all give the same `unavailable` answer. The screen says "stopped sharing" only from the visitor's own memory of having seen the list in this visit, and then promises that nothing is kept: the list lives in memory and goes with the screen.
- **The contract changed where it was only declared.** The visitor answer carries the visitor's `pairRevision` and the copy's `publishedProjectionRevision`, which is a random token that changes when the list does and counts nothing; the owner's settings revision never leaves the owner (the declared `visibilityRevision` and the `expected` input are gone). The answer lists planets under each system. The owner's own read lists their systems with a `shared` flag instead of `sharedSystemIds`, so one read draws F02.
- **A choice of friends lasts as long as the friendship.** The chosen-friend rows are deleted when a friendship begins or ends (accept, remove, block), under the friendship's lock, so an old "may visit" cannot return with a new friendship. "Friends I choose" with nobody ticked is saved as nobody and stays selected on the page.
- **Availability is a heartbeat that lapses.** The app sends `presence_heartbeat` over the socket every 20 s while connected and visible. The answer is always `{ ok: true }`. A beat is stored only for a person whose preference is not hidden, and hiding deletes the row. "Online" lasts 60 s (three missed beats), "Recently active" up to 15 minutes, and the preference caps the word (someone who chose "Recently active" is never shown "Online"). The answer carries a constant lease (30 s or 60 s), never the time left, so the last beat cannot be worked out. Beats are stamped, and their age measured, by the database clock. `expiryRevision` is a random token, new for each stretch of activity, so a friend cannot learn how often someone has been around. Hidden, expired and unknown share one wire shape. Friends read it on the friends page and the pair read (no new route); the client rereads every 20 s while Friends is open and keeps only words, each for its lease counted from when the read started.
- **Visiting is only a read.** It writes no row anywhere, tells the owner nothing and is no evidence for either person. A test compares the row counts of every table before and after.
- **Erasure.** The published copy is universe-keyed: Clear and full erasure delete it, so a friend who visits after a Clear sees an empty universe (P02) while the person's choices stay. The settings, chosen friends and heartbeat are account-keyed: Clear keeps them and deleting the account removes them (the chosen-friend rows on either side). The export says in words who may visit, how many friends were chosen (never who), the availability word and the titles of shared systems; never a heartbeat, a public ID or another person.
- **Not built:** F02's "Preview what Ravi sees", entering a room from a visit (there are no rooms), availability on Talk rows and in the share sheet, a live revocation hint, and region cards on a visit.

## D-048 · 2026-10-24 · Coordinator: Cutroom orders are bounded by real fal money, not pool cents

On 24 Oct five v2 orders were counted by `knowscroll_pool` as 183¢ (48 + 40 + 50 + 40 + 5), but the fal balance fell from $11.99 to −$0.09: about $12.08 of real spend. On these runs the pool's counted cents **under**count real fal money about 6.6×. CF-62's statement that counted cost overcounts real money does not hold for v2 Reels. The owner's D-030 budget ($4 of new fal spend) was exceeded by about $8 before the fal account locked; the coordinator stopped all ordering and reported it on #222. Two Reels were produced.

From now on a paid order is bounded by real money: the operator reads the fal balance (server side, with Cutroom's environment) before and after every order, and stops when real new spend reaches the amount the owner set. The pool cap stays as a second limit. Nothing is ordered without a new owner decision on the amount.

## D-051 · 2026-10-24 · Idea rooms with a moderator and labelled AI inhabitants (#245)

Choices where blueprint §10, §15 to §17 and the R01, R02, R03 and D04 artboards leave room:

- **Rooms own their threads.** A room's members, discussions, messages and moderation are the `rooms` module's (blueprint §10: "room messages use the equivalent room-owned shape"). A discussion with a root is a content thread: it reuses the thread contract's shapes (episodes, snippet cards, message bodies), Content's anchor checks, and the client's episode strip, player and snippet cards. It does not share Social's thread tables, whose members are invited friends with a friendship behind each place. Social gives Rooms only its public reads: people by account, the caller's friends, and the snippet card rule.
- **A room belongs to a topic, not to a map.** The operator seeds one room for each library system (`rooms seed`, idempotent by slug). It has no member, no message and no moderator until people come and the operator names one (`rooms moderator`). People cannot create rooms yet. A room is `open`, `paused` or `closed`; the join rule is `open`, `friends_of_members` or `invite_only`; joining needs a claimed sharing profile because members show by name.
- **A moderator is a person.** The blueprint's model-proposed "Moderator" role is not built. A moderator pins and unpins a discussion, hides and restores a message (it keeps its place and gets a fresh change number), pauses and resumes the room, and removes an ordinary member, who cannot join again. A pin organises the list and nothing else: no player moves, no universe changes, no evidence is made.
- **AI inhabitants speak only when asked.** Two exist (Explainer, Skeptic), with fixed labels that the contract carries as a separate author kind. The ask is a command by a member, bounded to one prepared at a time per discussion, 12 per room and 4 per person per UTC day, and 12 of any outcome per person and day, under the room lock so the bounds are exact. An ask is refunded only if it never reached the provider: one whose answer is dropped because the asker left, the room paused or a Clear came is still counted, so leaving and rejoining buys no extra model call. The worker answers it with the personal harness as the asker, then rechecks membership and the room's status before it stores the message, so a member who left or a pause cancels it. Its words, kind, role and prompt versions, model and attempt are kept with the ask. `nothing_to_add` is a valid answer and posts nothing. A reply with a link or a claim to be a person is refused with one fixed repair hint.
- **Blocks.** A room is not a friendship, so a block does not remove anyone from it. It is the blocker's own wish not to read someone: for the blocker, a message by a person they blocked reads as "A message from someone you blocked" (in the discussion, in catch-up and in history), and a shared moment of theirs does not open. Everyone else, including the blocked person, sees the room as before, so nothing tells the blocked person; unblocking restores the words. A moderator who blocked an author must still be able to moderate them, so they get the words flagged `blockedByYou`; the client keeps them collapsed behind "Show for moderation" and the usual message options stay. A block changes no message's change number, so catch-up cannot carry it: an open discussion reads afresh (a snapshot that replaces what was loaded) when the person returns to it and when the Friends page announces an accepted block or unblock. The room's moderators remain the way to remove a person from a room. Social's `READ_BLOCKED_AMONG` answers which of some accounts the caller blocked and never who blocked the caller.
- **A Scroll made for a thread is not a room's to quote.** Rooms gates snippet checks and opens with the same audience check as Social (D-049): a Scroll ordered for a friend thread is unavailable to the room.
- **Asking an AI model sends the discussion's recent words.** The inhabitant bar says so before the first ask: "Asking sends this discussion's recent messages, without names, to an AI model."
- **The moderator role is the operator's to give.** Naming one needs a claimed profile and never undoes a moderator's removal; taking it back only touches a current member; a moderator who leaves and returns is an ordinary member until named again.
- **The model reads only the room.** Context: the room's name, topic and guidelines, the discussion's title, the content it is about, and its newest 20 messages, with speakers as "Member N". No name, handle, ID, position or hidden message. Friend conversations and threads are never read, whatever the topic. An inhabitant never starts production: the "Explore this" button in the artboards waits for What-if (#244).
- **Routes `/v1/rooms/*`, Social's problem set.** Refusals are Social's closed answers (`SHARING_UNAVAILABLE` for every "no", `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` for the daily bound). Access is checked on every call; a stranger, a person who left or was removed, and a missing room look the same.
- **Updates are read, not pushed.** A discussion catches up from its cursor on focus, on reconnect and every 20 seconds, and reads afresh while an inhabitant is preparing. No socket hint is sent for rooms yet. A queued ask older than 15 minutes stops holding its discussion.
- **Erasure.** Clear keeps rooms (explicit communication, D-042) and ends only the asks still queued, with their jobs (they stay counted against the day). Account deletion removes the person's membership and invitations, tombstones their messages with fresh change numbers, deletes their snippets, clears the starter link and the asker link; an AI contribution the person asked for stays with the room. The export holds the person's rooms, discussions started and own words, and no other person.
- **Talk lists rooms.** A "Rooms" section under conversations shows joined rooms and invitations by name and topic, with no count or activity (D-006 o).
- **Evidence (24 Oct, a local stack with disposable accounts, not dev).** Two accounts joined the Machine learning room; one asked the Skeptic and one real `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` call returned an assumption to check, stored with role `inhabitant.1` and prompt `hp2` and shown with the AI label on both phones. A real Scroll ("Seasons follow a tilted Earth, not a closer Sun", two model calls to write it) was kept, shared into the room and opened as a content thread. The moderator pinned a discussion and removed a message; the other account saw the pin and only the removal marker. On the second slice the moderator started a poll and a quiz, both accounts voted and read, the Scroll thread opened beside the room at desktop width with a labelled Explainer answer on it, and the Universe drew a station beside each system of the (script-authored) maps. Four real model calls in all (two to write the Scroll, a Skeptic and an Explainer).
- **A station is a read, not a stored object.** Blueprint §10 puts personal placement in Geography. A persisted placement would need a new Scene kind (the canonical parsers and the capability versions are closed) and a Geography store, a breaking change for every client. `rooms.stations` instead reads the caller's own map through Geography's public scene input and answers, for each non-closed room whose topic is a factual system or planet that map holds, the IDs of that scene object; the client draws the station beside it. The placement is therefore always consistent with the map, differs between maps, is never stored, and says nothing about membership. A hypothetical place never carries a room. Persisting placements, and Geography owning them, waits for a Scene capability.
- **R03 is optional and unscored.** A moderator's poll or quiz exists only inside its room. Votes are visible to the room and can be changed until the moderator closes the poll; results are held back by the server, not only the client: a view has no tally, no voter and no `yourVote` until the person has voted or the poll is closed (a moderator waits like any member). A quiz is served whole and answered on the device: the server has no command for an answer, so it never holds an answer, score or position (the artboard says "Nothing is kept as a result"). Bounds: five open and twenty started per room and UTC day, exact because starting one locks the room FOR UPDATE. Neither is consumption evidence or an input to formation, and an inhabitant cannot start one. The artboard's observations `room.activity_opened` and `skipped` are not recorded: nothing is observed.
- **D04 is the same screens side by side.** From 1024 px a member sees the room, the open discussion and the extras at once; nothing is read that a phone does not read. The navigation rail is not drawn beside rooms.
- **Not built:** What-if in a room (#244 owns it), a focused snippet viewer (opening a card offers "Watch from here" or "Read the Scroll"), sharing a Scroll passage back from the room (the Scroll opens in the ordinary reader), reports, "Move into its own thread", "Mark as helpful", requesting an invitation from inside a room, "Pin exploration" in D04's moderator tools, persisted placements and socket hints.

## D-049 · 2026-10-24 · What if, Another perspective and the shared Scenario Planner (#244)

Choices where blueprint §14, §16, §17 and the X01–X04 artboards leave room (D-006 a, i, s; D-037; D-046):

- **A new module, `scenarios`, owns the exploration.** It has `scenarios.nodes` (the branches of one thread) and `scenarios.drafts` (a member's request while directions are proposed). Social still decides who may take part in a thread: scenarios enters a thread only through Social's `THREAD_GUEST` (enter as a member, read a bounded window, keep receipts, announce), and workers read standing through `READ_THREAD_STANDING`. Content produces the Scroll and Quartermaster commissions it; neither reads scenarios' tables.
- **A branch is a node in a tree, one parent each.** The parent is the thread's root revision or another node at a revision. A **What if** carries explicit changes (one or two) and what stays the same (up to three); the premises that hold are the parent's plus these, at most four, and a node that changes a subject its parent already assumes replaces it. Code checks this with one pure rule (`planStep`), used for the model's proposals and for a member's own words, so anything the model proposes can be chosen. A line the step keeps may not name a subject an earlier branch assumes in other words (`kept_contradicts_premise`); one that restates it in the same words adds nothing, and is listed once, among the assumptions. The member's own form lists what already holds and lets each changed line either add a premise or name the one it replaces, and an edited suggestion keeps its subjects, so a new line never silently stands beside the one it contradicts. **Another perspective** has a named lens and changes no assumption; it keeps the parent's. A branch is hypothetical if its parent is or if it is a What if, and that status is inherited by every descendant. A perspective on the unchanged root is not hypothetical.
- **The Planner only proposes; the member's choice is the only thing that commissions.** Asking (`draft`) stores a private draft and sends one job; it never starts production. The role `scenarios.planner` (version `planner.1`, MiniMax-M3.1-Flash, one call and one repair, low effort) sees the member's words, the subject, the premises that hold and the last 12 messages of the thread, clipped, with authors as "you" or "member": no names, ids or other threads. It answers one clear idea (`direct`, one option) or two to three directions (`options`), each marked if it needs confirming (a speculative or unclear premise, X01's third state). The run is the draft's ID, so a redelivery never pays twice. A member may instead write a complete request of their own (also while the directions are still being thought of); that needs no model. At most 20 drafts per member per UTC day; a draft lives two hours.
- **Choose is one idempotent command.** In one transaction under the thread's lock it checks membership and the parent's revision, makes the node (or finds the live one with the same meaning, that is the same parent, kind and change or lens, which is reused and says so: two members asking for the same idea share one branch and one Scroll), and commissions exactly one order through Quartermaster. That order has its own ledger (`supply.scenario_orders`) and does not go through ContentDemand's compatible-supply matching: matching exists to join a request to supply for the same brief, and a branch is the opposite, a Scroll made for one thread and its premises, never joined or reused. A retry, or two identical commands at once, make one node, one order and one job. A late model result for a member who has left, or a draft of a member whose place ended, is dropped. A parent that changed since the member looked is `stale_revision`; a removed parent cannot be continued.
- **Quartermaster's Create for a branch is a made-to-order Scroll.** The order is its own work (never joined, never reused for another thread): recipe key `order-<node>`, a code-built brief, and up to three research pages chosen by code from the root Scroll's own provenance (falling back to the same explanation's other Scroll, then the library recipe's pages). It uses the same `content.produce-scroll` job, take and stop rules as a library recipe. The brief holds the direction's title and explanation, the premises and what stays the same (a premise may be a sentence the member wrote themselves), and the public title and summary of the subject; the free text they typed to ask is not in it. The writer is told the mode (`scenario-writer.v1`): a thought experiment that opens by stating the premise ("Suppose …"), marks every consequence as reasoning, writes premise quantities in words, sets the changed world beside the original and ends with a limit; a perspective keeps the same facts and changes the emphasis. The kicker and the framing (a **Hypothetical** label with the premises, or the lens) are copied by code, never written by the model, so a branch below a what-if carries the label and the whole premise.
- **A hypothetical Scroll never grows real-world coverage.** An ordered Scroll is published with no topic attribution and no coverage (`attribution: none`), so it adds no planet, no region, no discovery lane and no progress. It is excluded from every discovery candidate lane, cannot be shared as a link card, and is reachable only by members of its thread. Content asks the ordering module on every way in by ID: open, document read, save, like and hide, navigation (a `return` to it, an offer of it, activating an offer, an exposure's context), a snippet that names it (sent or opened), the saved list and saved page (an entry the caller may not see reads as withdrawn, with no title or summary, and can still be removed) and the person's data export (it keeps no title, place or part: the line reads "Withdrawn"). IDs compare in lower case, so a shouted ID is the same ID. Every ask reads on the caller's own open transaction (`ReadOn`), never a second connection, because the pool is small and a second connection per concurrent open would exhaust it. So a member who leaves loses the Scroll everywhere, while its owner's other Scrolls are untouched.
- **Preparing is honest, ready is a fact.** A node is `preparing` until its Scroll is admitted, then `ready` and bound to that asset revision; `failed` when production stopped or 30 minutes passed. A worker sweep (`scenarios.resolve-branches`, every minute, idempotent) binds, sends the next take of a rejected recipe (at most three), closes, and tells the thread's inboxes. The cards poll every 5 seconds while one is preparing, and a hint also refreshes them.
- **Two caps bound the spend**, counted from Quartermaster's own ledger of orders: `KS_SUPPLY_SCENARIO_DAILY_CAP` branches per UTC day for the world (default 20 in `local`, otherwise 0; dev 6) and `KS_SUPPLY_SCENARIO_THREAD_CAP` per thread over its life (default 6; dev 6). A refusal is `budget_unavailable`, writes no node and tells the member plainly. Only the API reads them.
- **Removing a branch** is allowed to the member who started it, at the revision they saw. The card stays in the chat where it was, as a quiet "removed" note, so the tree has no hole, and its Scroll is closed to everyone; the Scroll is not deleted and a production in flight is not recalled. The same idea can be asked for again later. Descendants keep their premises.
- **Erasure, exactly.** A draft (the member's own request text and the directions proposed) is private: erased on Clear, on universe erasure and on account deletion. A node is shared work of the thread, like a message that others have answered: kept on Clear. On account deletion only the link to the person (`started_by_account_id`) is cleared. What stays, without any account ID, is what the others read: the card's title and premises, the order's brief and framing, and the Scroll; a premise the member typed themselves is among it. (Messages are tombstoned on deletion, D-043; a branch's Scroll has been read by others and cannot be unwritten, so it is treated as thread content, not as the person's message.) The member's receipts for these commands are deleted with them on account deletion and kept on Clear. The export lists their requests and the branches they started with their premises. The planner's run and its protected bytes follow the reasoning harness's own retention.
- **What this changes.** D-046 said the episode states Preparing and Suggested wait for What if. Branches appear as cards in the chat; the episode navigator is unchanged; the branch view and the map follow in the addendum below.
- **Not built:** Reel branches (a branch is a Scroll), a retry command for a failed branch (remove it and ask again), branching from idea rooms (#245), branches whose root is a corrected revision (the member asks again from the current one), and a push to someone who has not opened the thread. A member whose recording is paused gets "no directions this time" from the Planner and can still write their own.

## D-049 · addendum · 2026-10-24 · The branch view and the map of a thread (X03, X04, #244 PR 2)

What the artboards leave open, and what this build shows only because it is true:

- **A branch is opened at its own address** (`/threads/<id>/b/<node>`), replacing the thread's reader without a share control. The screen shows the thread's title, "What if · <idea>" or "Another perspective · <lens>", a yellow way back that names where it goes (the part the member had selected, "Part 2", or the thread's name when it has no parts) and the branch's one item. It is the member's own encounter, so where they are in it is theirs on the device.
- **A branch has exactly one item.** X03 draws a second, dashed item; no such item exists, so none is drawn. Nothing is prepared "next" in a branch. Until its Scroll exists the item says "1 · preparing".
- **Speculative is words, not a label.** D-006 a allows only Hypothetical and Corrected/Updated as status labels. A branch whose premise the member confirmed as speculative says "Speculative scenario · not established science" as plain text on its card, over its Scroll and in the map; no tag or pill.
- **Speculative is its own fact, not "unclear".** Part 1 took the Planner's one `needsConfirmation` ("speculative, contradictory, or unclear") as the flag, so a merely unclear idea would carry "not established science" for good. A direction now has `speculative` (its premise contradicts established knowledge; always false for a perspective) and `needsConfirmation` (unclear or speculative: the member confirms what is meant before anything is made). The sheet's confirm step says "Speculative scenario · not established science" only for a speculative direction, and "Is this what you mean?" for one that is only unclear. The flag (`scenarios.nodes.speculative`) is set when a member confirms a speculative direction, and inherited by every descendant. It is a fact about the premise, so it is part of the branch's meaning: an ordinary branch is never reused for a speculative twin, or the reverse. A speculative branch is hypothetical (a check on the table). The meaning's hash now includes the flag and the premises taken over, and the keys of branches made before are not recomputed: asking again for an idea that already has such a branch may make one extra Create, once, instead of reusing it.
- **An edit keeps the flag; a request from scratch has none.** Editing a direction sends `own` with `fromOptionId`, which the server resolves from the member's own ready draft; the branch keeps that direction's `speculative`. A request typed from scratch is never speculative, because nothing but the Planner says so. The flag is never taken from the request, so changing a word of a speculative idea does not make it established; an unknown `fromOptionId`, another member's draft or a draft not yet answered is refused.
- **A change names the premise it replaces.** Part 1 replaced a premise only when the new change used its exact subject, so a Planner restatement under another subject stood beside the old premise and both reached the writer. A direction's change now carries `replaces: <subject of a premise that holds> | null` (the Planner role is `planner.2`, which also lists each premise as "subject: statement"). Code checks the name against the parent's effective premises and applies it: the change takes that premise's place whatever its own subject says. When the Planner names none and a change says a held premise again in other words (nearly all of the shorter statement's content words are the premise's, at least four), the answer is not accepted: it goes to the one repair with a fixed hint, and the draft fails honestly if the repair does not fix it. A member's own request is not checked this way: its form already lets each changed line replace a named premise, and a line added beside one is the member's choice. A change that names a premise under another subject must share at least a content word with it, or it goes to the repair too: a premise is not dropped by a change that has nothing to do with it. The sheet shows each premise an option would take out, in the premise's own words ("Replaces: …"), on the option card, the summary and the confirm step, so the member agrees to what goes before choosing; a request the member edits shows the same through the form's own "Replaces" choice. A kept line may not name a subject a change replaces. A premise replaced under another subject is part of the branch's meaning.
- **A removed branch is a bare placeholder.** Part 1 kept sending a removed branch's title, changes, lens and premises to every member. A removed card now carries where it stood in the tree, its kind and when it was made, and nothing of what it said (title "Removed", no premises, lens or starter), also in a replayed answer.
- **Existing branches are backfilled, best effort.** The migration marks a what-if as speculative when the draft that made it holds an option marked `needsConfirmation` whose changes equal the node's, then everything below such a branch. A draft erased by Clear (or by deleting the account) cannot be recovered, so a branch made from it stays unmarked; an edited direction whose changes were left as they were is marked although part 1 did not mark it.
- **The map is real state only.** It is offered once the thread has two or more branches that were not removed, as an overlay (Back closes it; it is not a page), a nested list and never a canvas. Branches nest under the thread's root episode: a branch's parent is the root or another branch, so a branch under another part does not exist. A removed branch shows as "Removed" with no title and never matches a search. "You" marks only the member's own place (the branch they have open, or the part they have selected), kept on the device and never sent. The filter (Mine, Everyone, Suggestions) and the groups the member opened are remembered per thread on the device. Search and the filter appear once the map has six or more branches and suggestions; a smaller one is just its outline. A group of five or more folds after three, "More from <parent> · Show" (a fold never hides a single line). The Map button is in the thread's header and in a branch's, so "You" can mark the branch the member has open.
- **A suggestion is an offer the member has not made.** It is dashed, labelled "SUGGESTION · NOT MADE" and has no number (D-006 s). It is a direction from the member's own ready draft that they have not chosen, so it is private: no one else's offers appear, and `list` returns them only to the asker. Pressing one opens the sheet on that draft's directions. A draft expires after two hours, and an expired offer says it is not available any more.
- **The same audience rule.** A branch the member cannot reach never shows its title anywhere: the list is only for members of the thread, and a removed branch sends no title at all.
- **The branch's address tells only what is true of the read.** "This branch was removed" is shown only for a branch that is there and removed. A thread that is not the member's (or no longer is) shows the thread's own "This thread isn't available"; a read that did not arrive shows "Can't open this branch" and Try again; a branch missing among the ones read shows "This branch isn't available" and does not say why (unknown, or not theirs).
- **Not built:** a discussion scoped to one branch ("In this branch / Whole thread", needs a scope on messages, which Social and idea rooms (#245) own); a position time in the way back ("Part 2 · 0:18": positions are not stored); "Resume at branch 1" and "Opened by you" on cards (a visit to a branch is not recorded); a branch under a specific part; a retry for a failed branch; loading a folded group lazily (the list is read whole); the "map opened" observation.

## D-040 · 2026-10-24 · Explore, the Reel and Scroll as designed (#234)

What the v2.1 artboards (E01–E09, N01, A01, S01) leave open, and the few places where this build departs from them.

- **Reel.** The video fills the screen (`contain`, ink behind it). Over it: format switch and ⋯ (top), the four-action rail, the title, the Part chip, the progress row, the continuation edge tabs. A tap pauses and plays (the whole stage is one real button named Play or Pause, and the Play disc shows while paused). Swipe up is the next subject, sideways continues. The chrome is a sibling of the stage's gesture wrapper, so a button never starts a swipe. No ±10 s buttons and no captions or transcript sheet: the media has captions burned in and no transcript exists.
- **⋯ is in the top bar, for both formats** (D-006 b lists it in the action set; the artboards draw only four actions). The rail and strip carry Like, Save, Share, Ask. ⋯ holds Back, the ready Parts, Next and Previous subject, Keep this place (only while saved), Hide, Account and settings, and the person's own path.
- **Account.** There is no avatar over a Reel or Scroll (E01 and E02 draw none). It is in ⋯.
- **Sound.** A mute switch sits at the end of the progress row. Sound is the person's choice, never a side effect of a tap.
- **No topic chip.** The offer carries no topic, so none is drawn. API gap: `EncounterMetadata` would need a `topic` field. Likewise a Part is labelled "Part 2", not "Part 2 of 3": the series total is not in the context.
- **Edge tabs are buttons, not links.** The artboards call them real links; they have no address of their own (a continuation is an in-session move with an expected link, not a route), so they are named buttons with the destination in the name. Keyboard and screen readers reach them like any control.
- **Continuation.** A ready next Part is the yellow edge tab; a ready related move is the dashed tab; the previous step is the ink tab on the start edge. A Scroll has no edge tab: its continuation is the end-of-article card and ⋯. Blocks own horizontal drags there, and vertical reading never navigates.
- **Format switch.** A missing sibling shows its plain name and opens the request sheet (E06); a requested one shows "preparing". The ready notice offers Later and Open and never switches by itself.
- **Ending (E08).** A stop from the thread or from an exhausted direction is the yellow card: Keep a way back, Something different, Done for now. Keep a way back asks for a save and then confirms, with See it in Keep and Undo (Undo is the existing five-second removal). "Something different" opens a fresh subject: there are no suggestion cards because the Composer offers one subject at a time. Done for now is a calm Explore, nothing playing.
- **Share (H01 so far).** Copy link and the system share sheet, for the encounter's own address (`/reel/<id>`, `/scroll/<id>`: admitted shared content only). No friends, conversations or moments until those exist.
- **Keep.** One card per item: tile, format tag, title, one line of place ("Paused at 0:31", "Your place is kept", "Not started"). A visible Remove stands in for the swipe. The Requested filter is not drawn: there is no durable request list. Paging keeps its own buttons.
- **Dev evidence panel** only with `EXPO_PUBLIC_KS_DEV_EVIDENCE=1`, behind a conditional `require` like the gallery: a build that does not set it does not contain the panel's code, and CI greps the production export for it (the gallery export, built with the flag, is the positive control).
- **Entry.** "Continue with email" is cobalt and "I have a sign-in link" the quiet outline (A01 as drawn); the placeholder row stays out.
- **Overlays on the web** are drawn into the document body so they sit above the tab bar and rail.
- **Sheets are not departures (D-006 k).** Closing a sheet over a playing Reel plays it on from where it was (`entry.resumePlaying`, read once by the remounted player); Back, a trail jump or a tab detour still return paused. A sheet action that navigates (Account, a conversation) waits for the browser Back that removes the sheet's history marker (`afterPendingBack`; the encounter marks that Back as pending), and the marker is popped once however many state changes land first.
- **Direct opens read the exposure context.** `/reel/<id>` and `/scroll/<id>` show the Reel/Scroll switch from `GET /v1/experience/exposures/{id}/context`, so an existing twin is available (E06); a missing one stays unavailable there, because a direct route has no request flow.
- **The Reel player fills its parent** and takes its chrome as `children`; it no longer measures a bounded height (`stageMaxHeight` is gone). A screen that wants a smaller player gives the player's parent a height.

## D-052 · 2026-10-24 · Personal model runs: one paced run, a valid answer applies, budget ends a run (#231)

On dev, one test account that read every 15 s started 229 Cartographer runs in five hours. 228 were cancelled and one completed. 94 made a model call (about 940k tokens): 41 got a valid answer that was then discarded, and 53 failed `OUTPUT_SCHEMA_MISMATCH`; 46 of 51 first answers failed validation. Once the person's day of tokens was spent, 138 admissions an hour were refused. A Planner run stayed `admitted` in the ledger with no attempt, revived by every encounter. This changes D-035 ("Stopped runs start again", the budget part) and D-038 ("one run and one model call per material change"). Rules:

- **One pending run, paced.** Evidence already coalesced into one active run per person and role. A run that settled used to start its successor at once, and evidence after it started a run at once. Now a settled run sets `next_run_not_before` on the role's state row (Cartographer 30 s, Planner 5 minutes, migration 0060) and the next run's wake waits for it. Evidence meanwhile joins that run, which freezes its context when it starts, so a newer input supersedes only before dispatch.
- **A valid answer applies unless authority changed.** An answer in flight is not voided because more reading arrived. Cartographer: every operation is checked against today's authority; withdrawn, corrected or retracted support still makes it `source_changed`. Planner: every opportunity is checked against today's evidence and inventory; a new explicit intent still makes the plan stale. The other reasons stay: epoch or recording revoked, the geography revision moved, the role switched off. A valid answer that concludes nothing needs changing completes its ledger run; only a voided one is cancelled.
- **No model call without a decision for it.** Rules form places (D-038). A reading-triggered Cartographer run with no place due asks no model: the run settles `insufficient/no_changes` with no ledger run, and activity changes alone apply by the rules. Routes and labels are the model's to propose with the places that form.
- **First answers validate.** The role never named the `version` it must return, the repair printed the allowed literal as `?` (a model answered `"version": "?"`), and a planet needed supports the model had to work out. The role now shows the reply shape, lists what the rules entitle as ready `due` operations, and a repair names the operation and the rule it broke. The model is asked only for what it adds: another canonical label for a due place, a route, a label revision. Code applies every due operation whatever the model returns (`reconcileWithRules`), so the model does not copy every 72-character `supportRef`. The role judges the model's list with the due operations merged in (`mergeWithRules`), so a route to a planet that is only due is valid; a finding on a code-owned operation is not the model's to repair, and the rules are then applied alone. For a reader owed many places that copy could exceed the 8,192 output tokens and fail the repair the same way. An empty answer is the normal reply and is recorded `applied_with_rules` (a valid proposal plus the rules); `applied_by_rules` stays for no usable answer. Real calls: first answers invalid 3 of 3 before, 0 of 11 after (0 of 5 with the final wording, three of them empty).
- **A context that no longer hashes the same retires its run.** The `due` items change the model-facing context, so a run claimed before a deploy no longer matches its recorded hashes and could never send. The claim used to return silently and the run held the person's scope. It now retires such a run (`stale/context_changed`) when accounting shows no live attempt, and starts an unpaced successor. A run with an attempt in flight is left to the ledger's own recovery.
- **Budget ends a run.** A Planner run refused for budget or the failure brake closes its ledger run `cancelled`, ends `insufficient/budget_exhausted` and is not revived; the next evidence starts a new run, paced. The Cartographer already applied the rules and closed its ledger run. A run whose ledger run was closed under it ends the same way and a new business run takes over.
- **Idle admitted runs are closed.** The five-minute sweep also closes personal runs left `admitted` for six hours with no live attempt (`reasoning.idle_runs_closed`). Their owner finds the run closed and starts another. `reserve` refuses a run that is no longer `admitted`, because the sweep takes no personal root lock and can close a run between admission and reservation.
- **Explicit requests are a latent operator path.** `REQUEST_CARTOGRAPHY` and `REQUEST_PLANNER` have no product caller today; only tests use them. A request is not paced, it asks the Cartographer's model even with nothing due, and it joins a pending run instead of starting a second. The Cartographer reads the explicit mark from the person's latest desired source when it claims the run, so reading that arrives before the run starts overwrites it and the run may skip the model. A plain request accepted while a Planner run works does not void that plan; it gets a paced successor.
- **Budgets unchanged.** The per-person day stays 60 requests and 600k tokens. A Cartographer call costs about 10k tokens and a Planner call about 6k; with the rules above a 30-minute session uses about 70k. The dev exhaustion came from the churn, not from the cap.

## D-053 · 2026-10-24 · Desktop layouts and accessibility checks (#247)

- **One thread aside.** From 768 px a Reel or Scroll in Explore has the thread beside it: 212 px on tablets (V01), 368 px on desktops (D01). It is a `complementary` landmark named "This thread" and lists only moves the server already admitted for this exposure: the step before, the open part, the next part, another angle, and the other format ("Open Scroll", "Ask for a Scroll" or "being prepared"). The exposure context carries no list of a thread's parts, no thread name and no conversation, so D01's full part list, thread title and "Talk · Ravi" card are not drawn; the contract would need them first.
- **A desktop Reel is a card.** From 1024 px the Reel is a 9:16 card on the paper ground (at most 480 px wide, height follows the window) with Previous subject, the Reel/Scroll switch and ⋯ above it, the action rail beside it and Next subject with the key hints below (D01). The edge tabs and the swipe hint are not drawn there; the aside and the buttons do the same moves. The card and the full-bleed column are one element tree, so resizing across 1024 px never remounts the player.
- **Keys act on the focused stage.** ↑ ↓ change subject, ← → move along the thread, Space plays or pauses, with the Reel stage focused (Tab or a click). No key works on the whole page, so no single-character shortcut can fire by accident (WCAG 2.1.4). The hint row says so.
- **axe in the web smoke.** `@axe-core/playwright` 4.13.0 runs over the main routes at 390, 834 and 1440 px, signed out and signed in, plus a Reel, a Scroll, the More sheet and the Universe; any serious or critical WCAG 2.x A/AA violation fails the job, and every route must have one `main`. Nothing is baselined.
- **What axe found and this PR fixed.** `accessibilityState` is not passed to the DOM by React Native Web, so checkboxes had no `aria-checked` (and others no `aria-expanded`/`aria-busy`/current): the ARIA attribute is now set beside it. Desktop Privacy's settings list was a `menu` of links: it is a labelled `navigation`. Explore and the Universe had no `main`. The Universe list sheet and the formation view were dialogs without a name. Route targets between close planets overlapped the planets' targets: they drop below the pair.
- **Talk panes (D03, part 2).** From 1024 px a content thread is two panes, the thread (episodes, player) and "Conversation" (messages, asks, composer), as D03 draws; a direct conversation keeps the inbox beside it and the Talk tab shows the list beside an empty pane. Tablets keep one column with the rail. Conversations and threads keep their addresses; a pane is a layout of the same screen, not a nested route. The rail now also frames a thread and a conversation (`TalkFrame`), which are not tabs. Presence is not drawn.
- **A keyboard map without global keys.** `KEYBOARD_MAP` lists every key the app listens to and where it works. It is a sheet opened from Account ("Keyboard shortcuts") and from the desktop Explore footer ("All shortcuts"), so it needs no key of its own. A new key is added to the list in the same change.
- **Space on checkbox, radio and switch.** React Native Web leaves Space to button roles, so a keyboard user could not tick the delete or clear acknowledgement. `spaceActivates` fixes it for the acknowledgement, the friend and sentence pickers, the request choices and the text-version switch.
- **Headings break long words.** On the web a heading uses `overflow-wrap: anywhere`, so a long title at a large text size on a 320 px phone does not run past the edge. Body text and labels keep the default, because `anywhere` would let a flex item shrink to one letter.
- **Not claimed.** VoiceOver was not run by an agent (it needs a person at the machine). Axe covers names, roles, contrast of text on paper and landmarks; contrast of captions over video is not machine-checkable.

- **One element path across 1024 px.** A resize must not remount what holds state: the Reel card and the phone column are one tree (`ReelFrame`), the thread's panes and a conversation's wrapper are one tree with the inbox as a sibling, and roles stay constant because React Native Web draws a different element per role. Tests resize across 1024 px with a playing Reel and a typed draft and require the same video, no second exposure and no second read.
- **Focus follows the stage.** Each encounter is its own stage element, so a key that moves on would drop focus to the page. A stage that held focus when it went away hands focus to the next stage within three seconds, so the next key lands. The arrows ignore Alt, Ctrl and Cmd (browser Back, the person's own shortcuts).
- **The web has a text size.** The app reads the browser's text size (root font size over 16 px) as its system text size, so the 200 % type rules apply on the web too. `reflow-smoke` doubles it and checks the right edge and overlaps.
- **A modal sheet makes the page inert** (`usePageInert`), and axe checks an open dialog on its own.

## D-054 · 2026-10-24 · Request states as drawn in E07, and what E09 cannot show (F5 fidelity sweep)

- **Every request state has a word and, where one exists, one action.** Requested, Preparing, Ready · Open, Couldn't prepare, Can't be made and Cancelled are chips in the list, in the request sheet and over the encounter. "Can't be made" (unsupported, no compatible supply, not permitted, source gone, and `budget_unavailable`, which a Reel ask, a variant ask or the daily cap closes with) offers nothing to retry; "Couldn't prepare" (`could_not_prepare` only: the work failed) does.
- **Try again retries the same request (`request.retry{requestId}`, #454).** The first cut sent the ask again as a fresh request because nothing could reopen a closed one. `RetryContentRequest` now re-drives the same request: same ID and words, a new wait (`wait_started_at`, `retries`; migration 0067), the same Reuse, Join or Create decision, so it respects the day's Create cap, the open-request limit and the recipes left, and it resends no paid attempt. A free-text ask that was never matched has no brief and is not retried: unknown outcomes are never resent. Three retries at most per request. The answer is an immutable receipt: `retrying`, `already_open` or `not_retryable` (cancelled, closed for another reason, retries used, nothing can start now), and a request that cannot go again says so and is left as it was. Both the request sheet's Try again and the list's Retry send it. It is offered only for `could_not_prepare`.
- **Requested lives in Keep.** `/saved?view=requested` and the Requested filter show the list; `/account/requests` shows the same list. The notices stay where the request was made, under the top bar.
- **The opened request says so.** Opening a ready request from its notice or the sheet shows "Ready — the Scroll you asked for" on that encounter, and only there.
- **Nothing more ready is not the end of a thread.** A move with nothing eligible and no declared series says "You've seen what's ready for now" with Another subject and Something you kept, on paper. A declared series that ended, or "Done", keeps the E08 ending on yellow. "Ask for more on …" is not drawn there: the composer needs an active encounter.
- **Captions cannot follow narration in the client.** A Reel document carries `captions: "burned_in"` and nothing else: no caption track, transcript or timing. The client draws no captions, no CC toggle and no transcript sheet (E01 paused, E09 history and football Reels); the words on screen are in the video's pixels. A track needs a contract change on the Cutroom document and the media grant. E09's narration styling, kinetic captions and illustrated scenes are content the asset brings, not screen chrome.
- **Corrected notes on an encounter** (E01, E05) need a correction field on the exposure context; threads and share links already carry one. Not built.

## D-055 · 2026-10-24 · Feedback: Not interested and reports (#250, P03)

Choices where blueprint §8, §9 and §19 and the P03 artboard leave room. Migration `0068_feedback`.

- **Two owners.** "Not interested" shapes what Experience offers, so Experience owns it (`experience.not_interested`). Reports go to the safety team, so a new Feedback module owns them (`feedback.reports`). The API groups both under `/v1/feedback/*`.
- **Not interested is a short-lived routing constraint, never a fact about the person.** One row per person and asset, for 14 days or until Undo. It is read through its own port (`readNotInterested`), apart from Hide (`readHidden`, Hide only). Composer leaves a marked asset out of what it picks for the person (a subject move, a ranked buffer, the pin's first pick, and the commit recheck, so a buffer that holds one drifts and is ranked again). It never blocks a move that names its target: Saved, a finished request's open, a variant, a series or related link, a pin opened on a chosen encounter, a return inside a session. A person who marks a saved Scroll can still open it, and activating an offer that was issued rechecks Hide only. Hide stays total for all of them. It is no evidence: no action row, no observation, no job, and Personal Context, the Planner and the Cartographer never read it (they are handed Hide only). It lowers nothing about similar content and gives no topic a negative weight. A person holds at most 500; a write drops lapsed rows and the oldest past 500. Clear and full erasure delete the rows; the export lists the title and when it lapses.
- **It replaces "Hide this encounter" in ⋯.** The `hide` action stays in `/v1/actions` for its tests and recorded history; the menu row is gone. "Hide this subject for a while" and "Copy link" from the artboard are not built (Share has the link).
- **Undo is a command, and the move is undone too.** Tapping Not interested saves, then moves to the next subject, with a dark acknowledgement that offers Undo for 8 seconds. Undo removes the row and returns the person to the Reel they left, at the place they left it, if it is still in their trail. Marking an asset that is already marked restarts the 14 days and keeps the earlier mark (`prior_*` columns), so Undo of the second tap goes back to the first instead of dropping it; if the first has lapsed, Undo removes the row. The acknowledgement lives in a store of the account scope, so it survives the move and a closed sheet.
- **Departure from the P03 artboard: the acknowledgement says "Got it — we won’t show this for a while."** The drawn words, "fewer like this", promise a lowered weight for similar content, which D-055 does not do: it hides this one asset for 14 days and nothing else. The copy says what happens.
- **Reports.** A content error names the exact revision, a reason from the form (Something's wrong, Out of date, Misleading, Other), the moment in a Reel or the block of a Scroll, and an optional note. An abuse report names a Reel or Scroll, a message in a conversation or thread, a room message or a person, with a reason the person must pick (harassment, hate, sexual content, violence, spam, self-harm, other) and an optional note. A note is plain text, at most 500 characters. The server checks that the caller can see the target (member of the conversation or room, claimed profile, audience of the asset) and answers one `NOT_FOUND` for a missing, hidden or own target. At most 25 reports per account in 24 hours. Every command ID is its own report with its own receipt, also about a target the same account reported before: a second report may carry another reason or a note about something new, and it must reach the safety team, so a repeat is stored and counts against the 25. Only the same command ID replays its first receipt. Moderators and the room list count different people, so reporting twice does not raise what they see.
- **Nobody a report concerns can see it.** No route returns a report to a consumer; the receipt holds an ID and a time. The reported person's data, export and screens are unchanged (a test compares them before and after). Operators read the newest reports on the Operator screen through `ops.feedback_reports`: ids, kind, reason, target kind, whether a note exists and when. The note, the sender and the person reported stay out of the view, because the `ops` views never carry text or accounts (D-044). The person acting on a report reads the note from the table.
- **Room moderators see reports on their room's messages.** `POST /v1/rooms/reports`, moderators only: which message, the reasons, how many different people reported it, and on which day (the start of the UTC day of the newest report, never the time). Never who reported, the note or the author, and never a report about the moderator's own words. Moderators are named by the operator (D-051). Anyone else gets the same refusal as a room that is not there.
- **Erasure follows who the data is about; deleting an account must not erase what others told us about it (coordinator policy, 24 Oct).** Reports are account-keyed: Clear keeps them (an explicit message to us, like rooms and conversations, D-042). Deleting the _reporter_ removes every report they sent. Deleting the _reported_ account keeps the reports about it for **90 days** from the deletion, without a pointer to the person: `reported_account_id` becomes null, a person target becomes `deleted_account`, and `retain_until` is set. A message id stays because Social and Rooms tombstone the message in the same deletion, so it names no one (the report's note stays as its reporter wrote it). A content error names an asset, never an account, so it is never touched by the reported side of a deletion; it goes with its reporter. The scheduled job `feedback.purge-reports` (pg-boss cron, daily at 03:17 UTC, in the queue registry and the Operator's retention jobs) deletes reports whose `retain_until` has passed, 500 at a time, skipping rows another transaction holds. The reporter's export lists their own reports in words (kind, reason, what it was about, their note, when, and until when for a report about a person who has since deleted their account) with no identifier and no name of the person reported. The delete-account page lists these reports under "What stays".
- **Offline.** A report is saved in the scope's storage (at most ten) before it is sent. With no answer it is "queued", the sheet says "Will send when online", and it is sent again with the same command ID after 1, 2, 4 ... 60 seconds, and whenever a feedback surface opens after a restart. A server error shows Retry with the same command ID; any answer that settles it (target gone, too many) removes it. Changing the form after a failure makes a new report with a new ID. Sign-out and a privacy reset discard the queue with the rest of the scope.
- **After a report: "Also block".** For a message or person whose author is known, the receipt offers the existing block command. The message is not hidden for the reporter; the receipt says only what happens next.
- **Entries.** ⋯ in the encounter (Not interested, Report a content error, Report abuse); long-press (500 ms), right-click, Shift+F10 or the menu key on the Reel opens the P03 menu; the conversation's ⋯ ("Report {name}"); a long-press, right-click or screen-reader action on another person's message in a conversation; "Report this message" in a room's message options (AI inhabitants included); "Report" on the friend visit bar. A Scroll's reading surface keeps text selection and the browser's menu, so its only entry is ⋯. A message bubble opens the report sheet on long-press (a held mouse button on the web) or a screen-reader action; the browser's right-click on a bubble stays the browser's, so the text can be copied (the Reel's right-click is kept: a video has nothing to copy). Content-thread messages have no report entry yet.
- **Evidence (24 Oct, local, CI fixtures, no provider).** Integration tests against PostgreSQL cover receipts, Undo (and Undo of a second tap), Composer exclusion (live and from a prepared buffer), that an explicit open of a marked asset still works (a return with no session, the activation after it, a return inside a session), the lapse, the bound, repeat reports, access to every target, who can see a report, the moderator list (different reporters, the day), the operator view, Clear, deleting the reported account (de-identified for 90 days, message tombstones), the purge after 90 days, deleting the reporter, and export. Client tests cover every state of the sheet and the notice. Screenshots at 390 and 1440 are under `/Volumes/Mrigesh SSD/knowscroll-dev/v2/tmp/p250/shots/`.

## D-057 · 2026-10-24 · Account settings kept on the device; recording as a switch (#250, fidelity round 2)

- **Device preferences, not account settings.** Notifications and Accessibility & playback (P01) are choices about how this device looks, moves and plays: motion (follow the system or always reduce), text size (system, larger, largest), Reels open with sound, and the "Ready" notice over an encounter. They are stored in the platform's own storage (`SecureStore` on native, `localStorage` on the web) under one key, read once at the root above the account scope, and survive signing out, switching account and a privacy clear. They hold no account data, so no scope purges them and nothing is sent to the server. A damaged or unknown value falls back to its default.
- **Never more motion than the system asks.** "Follow my device" is the default; the person's choice can only reduce motion further, and text size multiplies the system's size.
- **Only settings with behaviour.** No push, streak or "you missed" setting exists because no such message exists. Captions and transcript stay with the player's own Text version toggle; there is no second preference for them.
- **Recording is a switch.** The same two commands as before (`set(false)`, `set(true)`), now one switch row plus the paused card with its Resume button. The page still shows no state it has not read back.

## D-058 · 2026-10-24 · Background roles keep to a share of the model budgets, and Jev reuses its assessments (#231)

This amends D-034: "one call per key" becomes at most one call per subject per ten minutes, and "stored Jev assessments live in the buffer row" becomes "they live in the person's pool", while the buffer row still carries the ones that informed its ranking. The rest of D-034 stands.

On dev in 24 hours Jev made 408 runs, the Cartographer 239, the Planner 36, What-if 9, request matching 2 and a room 1. The world's 300 requests a day were gone, 200 of them Jev's. A person's own 60 requests ran out from Jev alone while they were swiping, and their What-if drafts and room asks then failed as `budget_unavailable`. D-034 prepares a buffer, and so runs Jev, on every activation, and nothing told the roles a person asks for from the roles their reading triggers.
