- **Jev asks once per subject, not once per swipe.** Each judgement is kept for 30 minutes in the person's pool (`composer.jev_assessments`, migration 0066: one row per person, at most 60 entries, newest kept). A preparation sends Jev only the shortlisted candidates that have no usable entry, and no call when there are none. A call also waits 10 minutes after the subject's newest judgement. The candidates left out rank as unassessed (neutral 0.5, D-034), never as bad. What a call returned is kept even when its buffer is skipped, because the session moved on while the model thought. The purge that deletes expired buffers also deletes a pool with no entry left, so an idle person leaves no row behind.
- **What "the same" means.** An entry is about a subject: the language and the primary topic of the step the person was on. A step without a topic is its own subject. It is reused only by the same role version, harness framing version and model. Jev scores relevance and redundancy against the step, so a reused score is an approximation: the person is still on the subject, and Composer recomputes repetition for every ranking (the current explanation is excluded, recent encounters and established history are inputs). This is the simplest rule that gives the order of magnitude; an exact-step key would not reuse anything while someone swipes on.
- **Roles have a class.** `ReasoningRole.budgetClass` is `background` or `interactive` (the default, so a role is never squeezed by forgetting to say). Jev, the Cartographer and the Planner are background. The Scenario Planner (What-if), room inhabitants, request matching and the Scroll writer (Quartermaster Create) are interactive.
- **A background role also fits a share of the caps.** `KS_REASONING_BACKGROUND_SHARE_PERCENT` (0 to 100, default 50, 50 on dev in Ansible) is the part of the global cap and of each person's cap, in requests and tokens, that background roles may use together. Background spend can never pass it, so the rest of every cap is always left for what a person asks for. Interactive roles see only the old three caps. The hard caps are checked first and keep their codes. A refusal by the share is `budget_exhausted` with dimension `background_global` or `background_scope`. Background spend is summed from the receipts (`usage_receipts.background`, migration 0065, set when the attempt is reserved) under the same advisory lock as the other sums, so the share is exact under concurrency.
- **Refusal is quiet.** Jev falls back to the stored judgements, or to the deterministic ranking when there are none, and its run ends `cancelled`. The Cartographer applies the rules alone (D-038). The Planner ends the run `insufficient/budget_exhausted` and the next evidence starts a new one (D-052). No screen shows an error.
- **The recording-aware harness carries the class.** Planner and Cartographer calls pass through a facade that accepts only a closed list of role fields; `budgetClass` is now on it. Without that the facade would have refused both roles.
- **Numbers.** The caps stay 300 requests a day for the world and 60 per person. At 50 percent a person's reading can use 30 requests and 300,000 tokens a day, and 30 requests and 300,000 tokens stay for what they ask for. Reservations count at their full size until settled, so a busy afternoon can reach the share earlier than the tokens suggest.
- **Measured.** CI (fixture model): 100 activations on one subject make 1 Jev call. Local, real `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash`, one disposable person, 12 activations over 12 explanations on one subject: 12 calls and about 15,900 tokens before, 1 call and 1,469 tokens after. Each preparation waited 2 to 9 seconds for its call before; after the first (4 seconds) none waited.
- **Limits.** A person who reads heavily reaches their background share in a day and then gets the deterministic ranking and the rules. The share is a ceiling for background roles, not a minimum for them. Entries of an old role version are not reused after a deploy that changes Jev's instructions, so the first preparation after one asks again.

## D-056 · 2026-10-24 · Speculative is a status tag; "Opened by you" is the caller's own list (#250, fidelity round 2)

- **Speculative joins Hypothetical and Corrected as a status label (amends D-006 a and D-049, which said a speculative premise is words, not a label).** X02 draws a "Speculative" tag on a what-if the Planner flagged and the member confirmed. The card shows it in place of "Hypothetical" (a speculative branch is hypothetical, and the card's spoken name still says so), and keeps the line "Speculative scenario · not established science". Nothing else is labelled.
- **"Opened by you" comes from the caller's own exposures.** The scenario list answers `opened`: the node IDs whose Scroll the caller activated, read through `ReadVisitedAssets` (the caller's own history at their current epoch, the same read that marks a thread's visited episodes). A card never carries it and no member's list names another member's visit, so a visit of someone else cannot be learned from the list. A branch that is still preparing has no Scroll and is never in it.

## D-059 · 2026-10-24 · The library plan declares series and related moves; an operator command admits them (#232)

Explore's Part tabs and dashed Related tabs never appeared on dev because the 54 library Scrolls had no `content.series_units` or `content.continuity_links`, and nothing produced them. The content model already supports both (`admitContinuity`); what was missing was a source of truth and a way to admit it.

- **Declared next to the recipes.** `domain/library/continuity.ts` lists 11 series (2 to 4 parts, one planet, every system has at least one) and 25 related moves, all by recipe key. Each series carries a comment naming what in the briefs supports the order; where no order is supported (the black holes, the exoplanets, the efficiency recipes) there is no series, only related moves. Tests check that the keys exist, a series stays on one planet, a recipe is in one series only, a recipe is the source of at most one related move and never points to a series neighbour.
- **One related slot, not three.** The brief allowed up to three related moves per source. The model has one active slot per source revision and direction (`continuity_active_slot_idx`), so a source holds at most previous, next and one related. The declaration therefore has one related move per source.
- **`content admit-continuity [--dry-run]`.** It resolves each recipe key to the Scroll admitted now and calls `admitContinuity` under one fixed authority, `library-plan.continuity.v1`. A series is one transaction, all related moves one more. A series with an unpublished part is skipped whole (a series revision is a complete manifest); a related move with an unpublished end is skipped; both are listed.
- **Idempotent without a new mechanism.** A link the store already holds exactly is left out of the admission. Link ids are derived from the link's content, so a repeat or a concurrent run is the same link. A changed declaration or a republished Scroll becomes a new series revision and, where a slot is held by a different link, a replacement with the next link revision. History stays.
- **No new tables, no migration.** The command reuses `admitContinuity` unchanged.
- **Series total is not invented.** The context response carries a part number and no total, so the client keeps showing "Part 2".
- **Verified.** Unit tests of the declarations and the planner; an integration test on real PostgreSQL (admits, skips unpublished, idempotent, republish replaces, and `readContext` then offers previous, next and related); the worker operator command from the built release tree. Local browser check (fixture Scrolls, real API and database): the yellow Part tab and dashed Related tab in "This thread" on a wide window and on the end card on a phone.
- **Limits.** The order of some series (training, web requests, algorithms) is a reading of the briefs, not something a brief states; a reviewer who disagrees changes the declaration and re-runs the command. The command admits against the revision admitted when it runs, so it must be run again after a Scroll is republished.

## D-060 · 2026-10-30 · Owner: the v3 designs are final, and the newer one wins (#463)

On 29 Oct 2026 the owner ruled that the New Universe file, World internals 05, Simple views, Entering a system, V3 Scrolls, R3 Rooms and T3 Talk are final: "all are final". Every "MY PICK", "My recommendation" and "LEANING" is decided, and each PDF's "Questions for you to react to" page is a page of decisions. On 30 Oct the owner added the animations for the first open, entering a planet, entering a ringed planet and entering a world as final ("Never change it"), with the New Universe file updated to match.

Where two designs overlap, the newer one wins. T3 Talk (29 Oct) beats R3 Rooms (27 Oct): room AI is 2 or 3 named residents, not R3's four assigned roles. The Universe files (28 to 30 Oct) govern anything drawn on the Universe map, including room stations. T3's colour, chrome and look apply to Talk and to rooms.

- **Amends D-001** ("the v2.1 export governs visuals"). The v3 designs now govern the Universe, Scrolls, rooms and Talk. The v2.1 export still governs everything else.
- **Where they are.** `docs/v2/design/v3/` holds the files; its README gives the reading rules. CI checks them against `SHA256SUMS`. The PDFs stay out of Git.
- **Authored examples.** The people, messages, content and worlds in the designs are never product data. Their code is a reference for the scene builders, not code to paste.
- **The blueprint is not edited.** Where a design changes a rule, the amendment is a numbered decision below (D-064 onward) and the product rules in `AGENTS.md`.

## D-061 · 2026-10-30 · Owner: access for the v3 build session (#463)

The session that builds v3 works for the owner with GitHub Write on `KnowScroll/knowscroll` and root SSH to the VPS. The machine it runs on holds no provider keys. Real model calls run only on the server, through the dev worker or its operator commands, never from the build machine.

- **Why.** Keys stay on the server (AGENTS: no provider credentials in the client, Git, logs or evidence).
- **Dev, within D-030.** The session may change dev's feature flags and caps in `/etc/knowscroll/dev.v2.env` after a backup, and may dump and recreate the v2 dev database.
- **Unchanged.** The never-list in AGENTS: `main`, live, `stage`, the v1 databases, the owner's Mac database, `knowscroll_pool` rows and self-hosted runners. MiniMax use follows D-030 and D-031. Cutroom and fal spend needs the owner's approval on the tracker (D-048).

## D-062 · 2026-10-30 · Owner: the 26 Oct rulings still bind, Scrolls are game-like and rooms are alive (#463)

The owner gave two rulings on 26 Oct that this log never recorded. D-051 (24 Oct) and D-006 o (19 Oct) are older. They still bind, and the final designs build on them.

- **Scrolls are game-like.** Streaks, scores and progress are allowed for Scrolls. Any new block must be fillable by the LLM writer from JSON. Nothing is model-written HTML. Blueprint line 43 stands: no streak is required for growth.
- **Rooms are alive.** The Explainer and Skeptic speak unprompted. The data notice shows once. General is the default channel. Each room has its own art. Game feel is allowed. AI is always labelled and never poses as a person. The ban on counts and presence in rooms (D-051, D-006 o) is lifted, within the honesty rules of D-069.

Consequence: they change D-051 and the E08 note, which D-068, D-069 and D-072 amend.

## D-063 · 2026-10-30 · Play results never feed ranking, planning, evidence, formation or the Universe (#463)

This is a rule of the product that the v3 designs do not change, recorded because they add a play layer. Picks, correctness, sparks and tiers are play results. Composer, Jev, Planner, Evidence, Personal Context, Geography and Scenes never read which option was picked, whether it was right, sparks or tiers.

- **Why.** Behaviour is evidence, not proof of learning (AGENTS). A game must not teach the product to rank, plan or form places from how someone played.
- **What still counts.** Evidence comes only from the encounter, as before. A call that was locked, or a Scroll that was finished, may count as engagement with the Scroll. The option picked does not.
- **The Universe.** It may show a landmark as a read-only overlay (D-089). A play result never forms, moves or changes a place.
- **Enforced** by a dependency-cruiser rule that stops those modules importing the `play` module. It is added with the module.

## D-064 · 2026-10-30 · Game points are play features, not scores (#463)

Owner ruling (29 Oct): the final designs show sparks, combos, "3 calls", the Final call, landmarks, stars and a podium.

**Amends** AGENTS, "Consumer UI never shows internal prompts, model reasoning, scores, sources panels or operational machinery". It now bans internal ranking and model or evidence scores. Sparks, landmarks, stars and the podium are play-layer features and may be shown.

- **Why.** The rule keeps inference and ranking machinery out of sight. A game point is something the person earned in a visible game.
- **Limits that stay.** Blueprint line 34 (no compulsory tests, no scoring of learning, no announcing what a person believes) and line 1257 (no topic scores or mastery levels). A point belongs to one Scroll, thread or room act and is never summed into a level for a topic. A podium is per Launch only. There is no weekly leaderboard, karma total or level anywhere, and agents cannot vote or earn flair (R3 Q9, T3 Q13).
- **Wording.** "You learned" and mastery words stay out of the UI (D-072).

## D-065 · 2026-10-30 · A thread shows a coarse part-level place to its own members (#463)

Owner ruling (T3 Q6: "Yes inside a thread you joined on purpose, with a per-person 'hide where I am'"). A member sees which Part each other member of a thread they joined is on, or that they finished ("Ravi is on Part 2", "all finished").

**Amends** AGENTS, "Peer playback positions, seen markers and current branches are private"; blueprint lines 1045, 1152 and 1170 (seen markers, selected episodes and playback positions stay private; the UI must not imply "Ravi is currently watching Part 1"); D-006 f; and D-046, "The server holds no position, selection or seen marker of any member".

- **Coarse.** A Part, `on` or `finished`, and a day. Never a playhead, a branch, a selected episode, a seen marker or a time finer than a day.
- **Stored** as `social.thread_places(thread, account, part, on|finished, day)`, written only by the member's own client. The server never reads another member's Experience.
- **Members only.** Only people who joined that thread can read it. Each person has a "hide where I am" switch. Places are shown by default.
- **Removed** on leave, block, Clear and account deletion. Exported as the person's own data.
- **Wording.** "On Part 2" or "finished", never "watching".
- **No clock times.** T3 draws party lines with a time ("Ravi finished Part 1 · 13:02"). They are not built: a place carries a day, so the line shows at most the day. Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.
- **Unchanged.** Availability (D-047) still carries no place. Branches, playheads and seen markers stay private. The J2 check holds for everything outside places.

## D-066 · 2026-10-30 · SEEN receipts in direct conversations are mutual and on by default (#463)

Owner ruling (T3 Q16: "On, with a privacy switch that works both ways: if you hide yours, you don't see theirs.").

**Amends** D-042, "Read state stays private: each person's `read_through_sequence` is theirs, and the other person sees only 'Sent'".

- **Rule.** In a direct conversation the sender sees SEEN under their last message once the other person has read it. The switch is per person. A person who hides theirs does not see the other's.
- **Built for direct conversations only** (T3 Q16 asks about DMs). T3's thread and room screens also draw SEEN under your last message. It is not built there: a group has no single reader, and a per-member list would show each member's read state. Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.
- **Meaning.** AGENTS "seen markers" means what a person has seen of content. SEEN here is message read state in a DM. Content seen markers stay private.
- **Consequence.** A `social` change with a stored per-person setting, included in the export.

## D-067 · 2026-10-30 · Typing is an ephemeral signal that only holds back AI (#463)

R3 says a resident never answers while someone is typing.

**Amends** D-042, "No typing indicator". The indicator stays banned: no person is ever shown that someone is typing. The blueprint allows ephemeral typing signals (lines 269 and 1180). R3 and the T3 kit also draw a typing indicator between people; it is not built, because D-042 still holds for display. Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

- **Rule.** The client sends a typing signal over the socket. The server uses it only to hold back an AI turn. It is never stored, never shown and never sent to another person.
- **Re-check at fire time.** Before an AI turn posts, the room checks again that no new human message or typing signal has arrived.
- **Why.** The signal is a private behaviour signal (D-042), so it must do no more than prevent a collision with AI.

## D-068 · 2026-10-30 · Residents speak unprompted, under a room scope with caps (#463)

Owner ruling of 26 Oct (D-062) and T3 Q10 (2 or 3 residents per room).

**Amends** D-051, "AI inhabitants speak only when asked", and the rule in the rooms module that an AI message always has an asker.

- **Residents.** 2 or 3 per room, each with its own name: an Explainer and a Skeptic always, plus a Quizmaster that hosts the Launch. They may speak without being asked. They are labelled and never pose as a person.
- **A room reasoning scope.** Unprompted turns run under a nonpersonal scope with its own enable switch and daily caps. Today's nonpersonal scope is hard-wired to Scroll supply, and every inhabitant call runs as the person who asked.
- **A new budget class `ambient`** (beside D-058's two) can never take what a person asked for.
- **A ledger.** Every AI turn has an `ai_turns` row naming its trigger and the cap that decided it.
- **Caps** (R3 Q3: start here, tune after two weeks of cost data). Unprompted: 1 per 3 human messages, 8 an hour and 40 a day per room; the Explainer 4 an hour; the Skeptic 3 an hour and 1 per person per hour. Asked turns: 10 per person per hour and 80 per room per day, which replace D-051's 4 per person and 12 per room per day.
- **Quiet.** A cap refusal on an unprompted turn posts nothing. An idle room makes no model call.
- **Consequence.** Dev's model budgets must be raised before this ships.

## D-069 · 2026-10-30 · Room presence and counts follow R3's honesty rules (#463)

The owner lifted the ban on counts on 26 Oct (D-062).

**Amends** D-051, "Talk lists rooms … with no count or activity (D-006 o)", and the "no member, online or activity field" comments in the rooms contract.

- **Faces** of people who have the room open, from room presence on a room-scoped lease. It is separate from friends' availability, which stays off by default (D-047).
- **A number shows only at 3 or more.** Never "0 online" or "1 online". The AI cast is never counted.
- **The member total** shows in the room menu (R3 Q7).
- **An explicit allow-list** in the contract names every count and activity field a room may carry.
- **Never fabricated** (AGENTS).

## D-070 · 2026-10-30 · The Launch is a scored room activity; moderator polls and quizzes stay unscored (#463)

R3's Launch is a live quiz with a podium per Launch, a replay against real recorded answers, today's question and Spot the flaw.

**Amends** D-051 on R03: "R03 is optional and unscored" and "the server has no command for an answer, so it never holds an answer, score or position". Both stay true for a moderator's poll or quiz.

- **A new activity kind.** The Launch records answers and scores as game state. They are never evidence (D-063).
- **Questions** come from a nightly batch over admitted library Scrolls, checked by code and approved by an operator on dev. They are never generated live. Templates cost no model call.
- **Replays** are labelled and use real recorded answers.
- **Consequence.** Built after the room scope exists (D-068).

## D-071 · 2026-10-30 · Swipe moves between Parts in a Scroll, from text and stop surfaces only (#463)

V3 Scrolls: swipe left is Part 2 of the same topic.

**Amends** D-040: "A Scroll has no edge tab … Blocks own horizontal drags there".

- **Rule.** Swipe left or right moves between Parts from text and stop surfaces only. The stage and any block that takes a drag (a slider, code, a wide table, a sort) keep their drags. Native edge zones stay platform Back.
- **Equivalents.** Every swipe has a button and a key (blueprint line 1293).
- **Unchanged.** Vertical reading never navigates (blueprint line 1293). Pulling up past the end and the top "Next ↑" are explicit commands for the next topic (V3 Q3, Q16), not reading. Reels keep their edge tabs.

## D-072 · 2026-10-30 · Counts, minutes and progress are allowed for Scrolls (#463)

Owner ruling (26 Oct, D-062), carried into the final V3 design. The design shows a dots spine, "4 of 10", "3 min · 3 calls", sparks and a weekly Orbit (3 days a week).

**Amends** the E08 note ("Titles only — no counts, minutes, 'you learned' or progress meters", `discovery/design-screens.md`) and D-040's ending, for Scrolls.

- **Allowed for Scrolls.** Counts, minutes, the spine, "3 min · 3 calls" and the weekly Orbit.
- **Still banned.** "You learned", "you now know" and mastery wording. "Called it" speaks about the call, not the person.
- **Plain.** Reels and the E07 "nothing more ready" card stay plain.
- **No pressure.** Nothing is lost for missing a day, and the Orbit is required for nothing (blueprint line 43).
- **Real numbers.** A count is always a real one. A series total comes from the admitted `series_units`, never an invented number (D-059).

## D-073 · 2026-10-30 · The Universe's space views use three.js (#463)

The New Universe design needs 3D suns, haze, revolving planets and a fly-in.

**Amends** D-026, "Use the existing react-native-svg dependency for the initial layered 2D Universe renderer". D-026 called itself an initial choice to revisit from device evidence. The blueprint leaves 2D or 3D open (line 1326).

- **Space views** (the overview, a system, a planet) are drawn with three.js: a DOM canvas on the web, `expo-gl` on native. One render loop at most every 33 ms, a pixel ratio of at most 1.5, paused off screen or in the background, and a single frame under reduced motion.
- **Labels and tap targets** stay React Native views placed from projected points.
- **SVG** stays for the landed map. **Plain lists** are the Simple drawing, switched on automatically when WebGL is missing, the context is lost or memory is low.
- **Unchanged from D-026.** Identity, formation and anchors are independent of rendering.
- **Gate.** After the first spike, one scene must hold 30 fps on the web and in the Android emulator lane. If it fails, decide and record before building more.

## D-074 · 2026-10-30 · No orbit or route lines are drawn; the route is a chip (#463)

The New Universe rules: no orbit lines, yellow lines or dashed route lines on any screen.

**Amends** D-039, "Routes are the Cartographer's … the system view draws it with a 48 px target", and its orbits at mid detail.

- **Rule.** The line drawing and its tests are deleted. A route stays a Cartographer relation and shows as a "Route to <planet>" chip on the planet view and as a row in lists.
- **Unchanged.** D-039's anchors, camera model and 48 px planet targets.

## D-075 · 2026-10-30 · Watch together in rooms is a shared clock (#463)

R3 has a synced Watch together and a Stage mode while something is live.

**Amends** blueprint line 1152: "There is no synchronised co-watching or shared playback cursor in the initial version."

- **Rule.** A room can run a shared-clock session. The server holds only the start time. Each device works out the position from it.
- **Count.** "N watching" shows only at 3 or more, and only among people who joined.
- **Rooms only.** A DM's Watch together keeps the DM and starts a thread for the same two people, with no sync (T3 Q5).
- **Still private.** Peer playback positions and seen markers (D-065). Nothing here is evidence.
- **Consequence.** Built after the Launch exists.

## D-076 · 2026-10-30 · What-if branches are capped at 5 per thread per day (#463)

T3 Q8: any member may ask, up to 5 per thread per day.

**Amends** D-049, "`KS_SUPPLY_SCENARIO_THREAD_CAP` per thread over its life (default 6; dev 6)".

- **Rule.** The per-thread cap is 5 per UTC day, counted from Quartermaster's order ledger. It no longer counts over the thread's life.
- **The world's daily cap** (`KS_SUPPLY_SCENARIO_DAILY_CAP`) is unchanged.
- **Refusal** stays `budget_unavailable`, writes no node and tells the member plainly.
- **Consequence.** The config default and the dev value change with the slice that builds it. The short What-if answer card is a new worker role and gets its own decision when it lands.

## D-077 · 2026-10-30 · Planet types are unique while possible, and the look is assigned once (#463)

The New Universe rules say "Never repeat a planet type inside one universe", yet there are 6 types (lush, ocean, frozen, scorched, barren, gas) and up to 17 planets.

- **Decision.** Types are unique while possible. A type is chosen least-used first, preferring one not already in the same system. A repeated type gets a different hue shift and layout.
- **Assigned once.** Type, rings, moon and each system's haze family are persisted look data. They are never recomputed from what is in use now. Rings and moon come from the seed. The look is stored in a sibling table `scenes.looks`, because `scenes.guard_object` freezes the v1 payload.
- **Backfill.** Existing dev worlds are backfilled without moving any slot, seed or anchor.
- **Related.** D-026: persist family, seed, generator and anchors before variation.

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-078 · 2026-10-30 · "Not yet reached" land uses the exact facet sites plus decorative grey (#463)

The landed map (U04) shows grey land that is not reached yet. The globe (U03) shows every continent in full colour under clouds and never grey land (New Universe rules: "uncharted land uses its own greys on the map only"). Here, "a site" is one planet's room for one region, one per facet.

- **Decision.** Each planet has a fixed table of sites, one per facet. The planet descriptor carries `siteCount` and each region carries its `site`. The client lays out every site from the planet seed, so a cell keeps its shape when a region forms. On the map, unformed sites are grey, and decorative grey continents are added so the visible grey count never equals the unformed count.
- **Never** labelled, counted, interactive, or present in lists, search or the accessibility tree. Grey cells are not offers (D-026).
- **Maths.** The generators live in `scene-core` with pinned maths and a generator version on the descriptor. The design's `buildWorld` is not copied: it seeds from the region count, which would move every border when a region forms.

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-079 · 2026-10-30 · Lately keeps its 30-day window and gains a rules-only quiet sweep (#463)

In the design, quiet systems go calm without anything new being read.

- **Why.** `set_activity` is computed only inside a Cartographer run, and runs start only on new evidence. A person who stops reading would never see a place go quiet.
- **Decision.** The 30-day quiet window stays. `geography.state` stores `next_quiet_at`, and a scheduled job settles states that are due, with no model call.
- **Unchanged.** Inactivity changes activity, never existence (AGENTS). "Everything is where you left it" stays on the device (`seenAt`).

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-080 · 2026-10-30 · The ship marks the planet of the last place and stays on the device (#463)

The design parks a ship at the last place and offers "Return to <planet>".

- **Decision.** The ship sits on the planet of the last place. Where it is stays on the device (`universe-memory.ts`, `universe-here-marker.ts`) and is never sent. An optional per-planet last-region map is added the same way.
- **Related.** D-039: camera, Lately and the ship's place stay on the device.
- **Map browsing writes no evidence.** The J3 check is extended to the 3D renderer.

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-081 · 2026-10-30 · The room "Stage manager" is code, not a model (#463)

R3's behaviour table has a Stage manager that decides who speaks.

- **Decision.** It is a code policy in the rooms domain, run under the room lock. Caps, ratio, timers, no-repeat, levels and the single AI-to-AI reply are arithmetic.
- **Two semantic judgements.** "Is it a question?" and "is this claim overstated?" use a lexical pre-filter and the speaking role's own `nothing_to_add`. A classifier that only proposes may be added later.
- **Why.** Models propose and code validates, with no autonomous loops (AGENTS). It also removes about half the cost of the table, and keeps its behaviour.

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-082 · 2026-10-30 · Play data is erased by Clear and by account deletion, and exported (#463)

- **Decision.** Calls, results and landmarks are erased by Clear and by deleting the account. They are included in the export. The `play` module is in the erasure registry (D-050).
- **Why.** They describe what the person did in a Scroll, so they follow the same rule as the rest of their reading data.
- **Not included.** Stars and constellations belong to a room, not to a person, and follow the rooms module (D-051).

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-083 · 2026-10-30 · While recording is paused, play saves nothing on the server (#463)

Pause recording (D-057) means the server keeps no record of reading.

- **Decision.** While recording is paused, play works on the device and saves nothing on the server. The screen says so.
- **Consequence.** A landmark, a spark or an Orbit day earned while paused is not stored, and does not appear later.

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-084 · 2026-10-30 · A landmark earned before its planet forms waits in a tray (#463)

- **Decision.** A landmark earned before its planet exists waits in a tray, then attaches when the planet forms.
- **No formation linkage.** A landmark never makes a planet form (D-063; V3 Q7).

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-085 · 2026-10-30 · "Simpler" is written when the Scroll is produced (#463)

V3 D2 offers Simpler on a selected passage.

- **Decision.** The simpler text is authored per stop at production and checked with the Scroll. Showing it is instant and free, and calls no model.
- **Why.** No model call on the HTTP navigation path (AGENTS).

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-086 · 2026-10-30 · "Quick answer" is a capped worker job (#463)

V3 D2 offers a Quick answer on a selected passage.

- **Decision.** It is a worker job: an HTTP command, then pg-boss, then MiniMax, then a socket hint and a poll. It has a daily cap. It is labelled "Quick answer · not a checked Scroll", can be reported, is never evidence and never appears in the Universe.
- **Why.** No model call on the HTTP path or inside a transaction (AGENTS).

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-087 · 2026-10-30 · The weekly Orbit is shown as text, not as a ring on the map (#463)

V3 draws a weekly Orbit ring around the star. The New Universe file says no orbit lines and no sparkles. The Universe file governs the map (D-060).

- **Decision.** The Orbit shows in the Scroll recap, the play summary and the Universe panel or list text. It is not a ring on the map.

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-088 · 2026-10-30 · Clear skies do not thin as Scrolls are finished (#463)

V3 says clear skies thin as you finish Scrolls. In the New Universe, clouds mean "not reached yet". The Universe file governs the map (D-060).

- **Decision.** Cloud thinning is not built.

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-089 · 2026-10-30 · Landmarks are small painted markers on the globe and the map (#463)

Landmarks stand on planets. The Universe file governs how they look there (D-060).

- **Decision.** A landmark is a small painted marker on the planet's globe and map, in the Universe style. It is a read-only overlay from the `play` read API and has no effect on formation (D-063).

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-090 · 2026-10-30 · A room's constellation is drawn with its station, in the Universe style (#463)

A room earns stars, up to a 7-star constellation the room names, and its station lights on every member's map. The Universe file governs the map (D-060).

- **Decision.** The constellation is drawn with the station in the Universe style, with no sparkles. No Cartographer or Scenes change is needed: `rooms.stations` carries the sky state (D-051).

R3 and T3 show painted room backdrops. The MiniMax key is text-only, and fal belongs to Cutroom.

- **Decision.** Ship the drawn kit: a role returns closed-enum JSON and the app draws it. No painted backdrop is built until the owner approves an image provider and a budget.

## D-092 · 2026-10-30 · First open for an account that already has places (#463)

The first-open animation plays once in the lifetime of the account and is drawn ending on U01 Empty. The design does not say what happens when the account already has places.

- **Decision.** It plays up to the moment the empty-state title, line and "Go to Explore" would rise in, then a 0.3 s cross-fade shows the real overview. It never claims the account is empty.
- **Unchanged.** The seen flag lives on the account and is set when the animation starts. Skip and finishing both count as seen.

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-093 · 2026-10-30 · Going back up uses a 0.3 s cross-fade (#463)

No reverse animations were designed.

- **Decision.** Back goes up one level with a 0.3 s cross-fade, not the forward animation backwards. Long jumps, such as search to a region, go straight to the destination.

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-100 · 2026-10-30 · The three.js gate: both platforms draw, with two native fixes and four things not verified (v3 U-0, #464)

Records the gate that D-073 asked for. D-073 (the owner's ruling) chose three.js for the space views, a DOM canvas on the web and `expo-gl` on native, and amends D-026. D-074 says no orbit or route lines: none are drawn here. Everything below builds on D-073 and does not change it.

**Verdict: accept three.js.** One scene (orange sun with haze, a ringed planet, a planet with a moon, nebula dust) draws on the web and in the Android emulator lane. The gate's "30 fps" is met on the web and not on the emulator: see the evidence.

- **Where it runs.** `features/universe/space/`. The web draws on a DOM `<canvas>` with `THREE.WebGLRenderer`. Native draws on an `expo-gl` `GLView` and calls `gl.endFrameEXP()` after each frame. Everything above the canvas host is shared. `three` is loaded on first use (`import()`), so other screens do not carry it. Versions are in the dependency matrix.
- **Pacing.** A frame is drawn at most every 33 ms (30 fps cap). One frame advances the scene by at most 0.1 s. The drawing buffer uses at most 1.5 pixels per layout pixel; on native the view is laid out smaller and scaled up, because a `GLView` buffer has one pixel per device pixel.
- **When it runs.** The loop is cancelled, not just skipped, when the route is unfocused, `AppState` is not active (on the web, the tab is hidden) or, on the web only, the canvas is off screen. It resumes at the same scene time.
- **Reduced motion.** One frame at a fixed calm time and no loop. A resize redraws that one frame, or, if the view could not draw at the time, when it next can.
- **A scene is a pure function of time.** Every motion is computed from the time value `t`, never accumulated, so one frame is a complete picture and the fly-ins can be scrubbed.
- **No canvas for textures.** Textures are plain pixel arrays made in code, because Hermes has no canvas. The same seed paints the same pixels.
- **Labels and targets are views.** Names and tap targets are React Native views placed at projected points (44 px at least). Picking is by those 2D regions, never by raycasting the scene. The canvas itself is hidden from assistive technology.
- **Fallback is the Simple views, never a blank.** WebGL missing, a context that cannot be created, a lost context (web), a frame that throws, or a low-memory warning replaces the drawing with the two plain lists (systems as headings, planets as rows), with the same names in the same order. This is what the Simple drawing switch will also show.
- **One surface at a time.** `expo-gl` can make a new GL context when the app returns from the background, and the canvas then reports a new surface. The view releases the old renderer and loop, and starts again on the new surface. A view that unmounts stops its loop and releases the renderer (and, on the web, the browser's context).
- **Where it can be reached.** Only the developer gallery page `/gallery-space` (builds made with `EXPO_PUBLIC_KS_GALLERY=1`, not in a production export). The Universe tab does not use it until the U-2 slice.
- **Gate evidence.** Web, Chromium with software WebGL (Playwright, `e2e/space-gallery.spec.ts`): frames 33.3 ms apart on average (minimum 33.2 ms), 0.4 ms of CPU per draw, pixels read back and changing, reduced motion draws 1 frame in 2 s, the loop stops off screen and in a hidden tab, and the plain lists replace the scene without WebGL and on a lost context. Android emulator (`maestro/space.yaml` in `native.yml`, API 35, swiftshader): the `expo-gl` scene draws and a label tap works; with system animations off (reduced motion) exactly 1 frame; with the loop on, 876 frames, never faster than 32.1 ms apart, 8.1 ms of CPU per draw, **68.5 ms apart on average (about 15 fps)**. The software GL of the emulator cannot reach 30 fps, so its timing proves the path and the cap, not phone speed. Whether a phone holds 30 fps is open: U-2 must measure it on a physical phone before any planet view is built on this.
- **Two native fixes the gate found.** The release build's R8 renamed the Expo `GLView` and the app crashed on creating it: `plugins/keep-expo-gl-views.js` adds a keep rule for the expo-gl classes. three.js refuses any context that is `instanceof WebGLRenderingContext` and the `expo-gl` WebGL 2 context inherits from it: the native host hands three.js a proxy that hides that inheritance, answers `getContextAttributes()` and ignores colour-space hints the context refuses.
- **Not verified, and why it is acceptable for now.**
  - _Native off-screen pause._ The canvas reports leaving the screen on the web only (`IntersectionObserver`). On native the loop follows route focus and `AppState`; a canvas scrolled out of view inside a focused screen keeps drawing. No U-2 screen scrolls its map, and this is not driven on a device.
  - _Low memory._ The trigger is the `AppState` `memoryWarning` event. React Native raises it on iOS; on Android it is not known to be raised, and nothing was driven on a device. A device-memory class check that starts in the lists belongs with the Simple drawing switch (findings Slice 5) and is deferred to it.
  - _Native context loss._ `expo-gl` raises no loss event. A context made anew after a resume arrives as a new surface, which the view handles (unit-tested, not driven on a device).
  - _Phones and iOS._ Nothing was run on a physical phone or on iOS, and no long run was checked for memory.

## D-102 · 2026-10-30 · The look lives in `scenes.looks` and reaches a client on the manifest read, under a capability (v3 U-1, #465)

D-077 said the look is a sibling table and D-078 said sites ride on the planet and region. This records where the look lives, what it holds and how a client asks for it. The scene descriptor, chunk and manifest stay `scene-descriptor.v1`, `scene-chunk.v1` and `scene-manifest.v1`.

- **A sibling table, not a new descriptor version.** `scenes.looks` has one row per system, planet and region, keyed like `scenes.objects` and referencing it. An update is refused by the database, so a look cannot be recomputed. `scenes.guard_object` and every stored chunk are untouched, so a client that predates looks parses exactly what it parsed. Stars have no look.
- **What a look holds** (`scene-look.v1`, pinning generator `worldmap.v1`). System: `haze`. Planet: `type`, `rings`, `moon`, `hueShift`, `variant`, `siteCount`. Region: `site`. There is no activity, count, support, time or score in it, and `ReadGeographySceneInput` gained nothing.
- **Haze is in the library plan.** `LibrarySystem.haze` is one of the six families, different for each system: Earth amber, Space violet, Machine learning teal, Human body coral, Computing cobalt, History pink. Like `sceneFamily`, edit it for new systems only. All six are used, so a seventh system has to share a family: the person who adds it chooses one in the plan, and the plan test (no two alike while there are six or fewer systems) allows it from then on. Nothing picks a haze by itself.
- **Delivered on the manifest read, not the chunk read.** A chunk is immutable for its revision and a backfill adds looks later without a new chunk, so a look in a chunk would go stale in a cache. The manifest is what a client reads again. `looks` lists the looks of the objects in the manifest read, for the selected universe or system.
- **The capability.** `SceneClientCapabilities.looks` is a new optional list. A client that sends `scene-look.v1` and declares the `worldmap.v1` generator gets `looks` on `empty`, `ready` and `lagging` reads; the response schema requires it exactly then. Any other client gets the read it always got, byte for byte. A world with fewer looks than objects (not yet backfilled) lists only those that exist, and a client draws the rest as it always did.
- **Assigned in the projection.** The projector gives a look to every system, planet and region that has none, in the transaction that stores the object. Rollback removes both. The rule is D-103.
- **Backfill.** `scenes backfill-looks [--dry-run] [--limit n]` (operator) sends one `scenes.backfill-looks` job per universe that has a projected place without a look. The worker, under that universe's lock and after its own owner check, inserts the missing rows and nothing else: no slot, seed, family, anchor, chunk or manifest changes. It advances the personal root once and sends the disposable notice, only when it assigned something. A second run assigns nothing.
- **Clear and erasure** delete `scenes.looks` before `scenes.objects`, and the new queue's payloads with them. A look exports nothing, like the objects it describes.
- **The plan grows by appending facets, and a look must survive it.** A planet's look is drawn once, so a facet appended later (the Seasons planet has a fourth, "elsewhere", that the plan added after its first three) must still find a site. Each planet is therefore drawn with spare sites (D-103). A region past even those is left without a look: the scene stays current, the object is drawn as before, and `scenes.look_refused` is logged with the reason `site_beyond_capacity`. Only a structural disagreement between the plan and the stored objects (a look with no object, an object with no meaning, a facet not on its planet) still makes the scene unavailable, and that is logged with its reason too.
- **A world with such a region stays in the backfill's list.** The command finds it again, its job assigns nothing and logs the same event. That is the signal that the planet needs more spare sites, which is a new look version, not an edit.

Amends D-077 (it named the table; this adds the delivery and the capability) and D-078's "one site per facet" (spare sites, D-103). Confirmed by: `tests/integration/modules/scenes.test.ts` (served only to a declaring client; stored once; backfill moves nothing; rollback; erasure), `packages/contracts/src/scenes.test.ts`, `privacy-clear.test.ts` and `erasure.test.ts` (the new table is in the inventory).

## D-103 · 2026-10-30 · How a planet gets its type, hue, rings, moon and sites (v3 U-1, #465)

D-077 gave the rule in one line: least-used first, preferring a type not in the same system. This is the exact rule, so that every process gives the same answer.

- **Order.** Places that need a look are taken by kind (system, planet, region) and then by object id. Object ids are UUIDv7 minted when the Cartographer's context is built, not when the place is stored, so this is close to creation order but not exactly it. That is enough: the answer is the same on every run, whatever order Geography lists the places in and across restarts, and a look once given is never given again.
- **Type.** The least-used of the six types in the whole universe. Among equals, one the planet's system does not hold yet. Among those, the first in an order that depends only on the planet's seed (a seeded shuffle). Hypothetical planets count like factual ones. The first six planets get six types; after that the counts differ by at most one.
- **A repeat.** `variant` is the number of planets of that type before it. The first planet of a type leans toward its system's haze (the design's shift per family) with a seeded jitter of 15 degrees either way. A repeat turns its hue 75 degrees one way, then the other, a step further each time, with the same jitter, so it cannot be mistaken for the first. `variant` also tells the generator to lay out a different world.
- **Rings and moon** come from the planet's seed after its type: a gas world always has rings, a frozen one has them when its seed says so, no other type does. Any type has a moon when its seed says so.
- **Sites.** A planet's `siteCount` is the number of facets of its catalogue entry plus 4 spare sites (`RESERVED_SITES`), at most 32. A region's `site` is the position of its facet in the plan's list, so a facet appended later takes the next site and no earlier border moves; spare sites draw as the decorative grey of D-078. Nothing about which other regions have formed is used, so a region never moves when another forms. **Amends D-078**, "one site per facet".
- **Facets are append-only.** A site is a position, so the plan may only append a facet to a planet, never insert one or reorder them, or two regions would share a cell. A pinned table of every planet's facet order in `assign-looks.test.ts` fails the change that breaks it.
- **One source of numbers.** Every seeded number is `seededUnit(seed, channel)` from `scene-core`, the same integer mixing as `silhouette.v1`. Channels 16 and above belong to looks. Changing a channel, a constant or this order is a new look version, not an edit.
- **Not guaranteed.** A friend's visit derives its own look on the visitor's device from public ids (D-047) and may repeat a type.

Amends D-077. Confirmed by: `packages/backend/src/modules/scenes/application/assign-looks.test.ts` (six types, least-used afterwards, system preference, stability across passes and orders, hue gap, rings, sites from the pinned facet order, a facet appended inside the spare sites, a region past them skipped, refusals) and `scenes.test.ts` (the scene stays `ready` in both cases and the event is logged).

## D-106 · 2026-10-30 · The space views draw the server's look, place bodies from anchors and seed, and draw no lines (v3 U-2, #466)

The Overview, Far, a System and the Empty, Emerging and Offline skies are drawn in 3D (D-073). This says what they draw from and what they never do.

- **The look is the server's.** The client declares `scene-look.v1` and the `worldmap.v1` generator on the manifest read (D-102) and draws type, rings, moon, hue turn and variant of each planet and the haze of each system exactly as given. A planet's size, tilt and spin come from its seed and never encode activity or reading (D-006 m). The client never picks a type or a haze.
- **A place with no look is drawn plainly**, so the map is never blank: a colour set from its palette (sage lush, ochre barren, cobalt ocean), teal haze, no rings, no moon. This is a friend's visit (D-047 keeps its list wire; its own look from public ids is a later slice) and a world not backfilled yet. It is not a second look system: it carries no variety and nothing is stored.
- **Positions.** A system sits at its persisted anchor (300-unit slots become 3.2 scene units) with a small seeded wander. A planet's orbit radius is its lattice band plus a seeded wobble, and its start angle is the direction its anchor has from the star. A place keeps its spot when others arrive (tested). Planets revolve; inner ones faster.
- **No lines.** No orbit, route, dashed or yellow line is drawn on these views (D-074). The route line drawing, its 48 px target and its tests are deleted; "Route to <planet>" is a chip on the planet view. The SVG orbit ring of the formation story (U05) goes too; its other beats are unchanged.
- **Stations** are drawn in the Universe style beside their system (just outside its outermost orbit, or just outside the orbit of the planet they name), at an angle taken from the room's ID, from `/v1/rooms/stations`. A station whose system is not on the map is not drawn. Constellations (D-090) come later.
- **The ship** is a small mark parked on the planet of the last place (a region's planet; nothing for a system) and is hidden under Lately (D-080). Where it is stays on the device.
- **Dust** is a toon nebula, never named or counted: one large cloud at the centre when nothing else exists (U01 Emerging), small ones past the systems otherwise.
- **Lately** (30 days, D-079): quiet systems and planets draw grey with no bloom, and labels dim. Nothing leaves the map.
- **Labels and targets** stay views (D-100): a 48 px button and a label at each projected point, in the scene's own order. Where bodies crowd each other a target may shrink, as D-107 records. Where 3D cannot run, `SpaceSimpleLists` shows the same names in the same order and every place still opens, with room kept under the last row for the chips and the tab bar.
- **Browsing writes nothing** (D-063). The web smoke (`e2e/universe-space-smoke.spec.ts`, real WebGL) pans, zooms, flies in and out, toggles Lately, searches, opens the Far card and the dust note, with a station, a dust cue and a visited planet on the map, and checks that only the Universe's reads left the page. The ship itself is drawn in the canvas: the smoke sees it only as the "Return to <planet>" chip, and the renderer and screen tests (`build-scene.test.ts`, `universe-screen.test.tsx`) pin where it is parked.

Amends D-039, "Routes are the Cartographer's … drawn with a 48 px target", and its orbit bands at mid detail (D-074 already supersedes the lines). Confirmed by: `space-model.test.ts` (look used as given, plain look, stability, calm only under Lately, ship, stations, dust), `build-scene.test.ts` (no lines, ship on its planet), `universe-screen.test.tsx` (the capability is sent, the looks reach the renderer, Route chip, plain lists), `universe-space-smoke.spec.ts` (draws, reads only).

## D-107 · 2026-10-30 · The camera is the level: Overview, Far and System are distances of one camera, and a fly is a pure function of time (v3 U-2, #466)

The design has three space levels. They are one scene seen from different places, not three drawings.

- **One scene, one rig** (`camera-rig.ts`, pure maths). The Overview frames every system: the distance fits the widest and deepest extent for the canvas shape, with the depth foreshortened by the camera's elevation (26.6 degrees; 33 for a System). Far is the same universe farther away, past 68 scene units. A System is the camera close over one sun, framed to its outermost orbit and its stations.
- **Zoom and pan** are the person's frame: pixels panned and a zoom about the centre. The universe zoom is 0.4 to 2.5 times and a System's 0.7 to 2.2; the session stops the frame there, so the next pinch or key press moves the camera at once. A pan may carry the target 0.9 of the framed radius past the middle. The planet view keeps its own range.
- **Far picks first.** Past the Far distance a first tap on a system picks it (a warm glow and its card, "Open system"); a second tap or Open enters. Nearer, a tap enters.
- **Fly.** Entering a system flies the camera 2.4 s with the field of view widening by up to 9 degrees; stepping up takes 0.3 s (D-093; not the forward animation reversed). The other systems leave the scene when the fly is 85 % done. The first fly is not the entering rocket of the design (a later slice). Under reduced motion there is no loop and a change of place is a jump with one redrawn frame.
- **Rebuilding.** A new definition (Lately, a formed place, the ship) rebuilds the scene in place; the camera, the glow and a fly in flight are kept. The pixels of a painted planet are kept (64), so the lens does not repaint every world.
- **Frame timing, measured in the real scene.** The cap stays at 33 ms. Web, Chromium with software WebGL, 390 x 844, the Universe tab over the authored fixture: 121 frames in 4 s, 33.3 ms apart on average (31.9 ms at the least), and 0.9 ms of CPU per draw on the tab's own layout of three systems, a station and the ship (`?scene=universe`). The Android emulator's number is in the PR; software GL on the emulator cannot reach 30 fps (D-100), and a physical phone is still not measured.
- **Crowded targets may shrink, to a floor of 24 px.** Bodies orbit and pass close by each other, and a tap target that another one covers is not a target (axe found one). A normal target is 48 px, never below the 44 px of the design. Where two targets would overlap, `separateTargets` shrinks both only as far as needed for them to touch, never below 24 px, the minimum of WCAG 2.2 AA 2.5.8 (Target Size, Minimum). If two bodies are closer than 24 px, the hit areas, not the drawing, move apart: 12 px at most for a pair, and 24 px in the tested crowd of four. A heavier crowd is not bounded. This is an exception to "targets 44 minimum" and to D-100's 44 px. The List view and the plain lists give every place a row of 44 px or more, so nothing is reachable only by a small target.
- **Not built here** (listed in the PR): the entering rocket, planet views in 3D, the orbit dial, search flying to a place, the friend's own look, landmark markers, constellations.

Confirmed by: `camera-rig.test.ts` (levels, limits, pan reach, pure time, fly endpoints), `universe-session.test.ts` (zoom limits), `separate-targets.test.ts` (targets touch, never below 24 px, how far hit areas move), `universe-space-smoke.spec.ts` (real WebGL: cadence, hidden tab, reduced motion), `universe-screen.test.tsx` (a fly inside one scene, Far picks first).

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

## D-120 · 2026-10-30 · scroll-document.v2 is written as one flat list of items (S-0, #471)

The V3 Scrolls need stops, calls, asides, a recap and a pinned stage. D-032 found that a model writing long, nested JSON loses count of its brackets, and the V3 block table nests: `stage.states[{stop, changes}]`, a call with its options and common guess, an aside with `anchor_stop`. This entry settles how the model writes a v2 Scroll. It changes nothing in v1: `scroll-document.v1`, `scroll-authoring.v1` and writer `scroll-writer.v13` still publish the library.

- **One flat list.** `scroll-authoring.v2` is `{title, dek, items[], recap, askSuggestions[]}`. Each item is a `stop`, a `call_it`, the `final_call` or an `aside`, in reading order. The model writes no id, no index and no count.
- **Position replaces references.** An aside belongs to the item before it. The Final call is the last item. A stage change belongs to the item that carries it. There is nothing to number and nothing to point at.
- **The stage is a small object of words.** An item's `stage` names only what changes, from closed word lists of the Earth & sky kit (view, month, axis, Ray, orbit, two pins, Moon, focus). The model writes no number, coordinate or place name. Code carries the unnamed values over and builds the full `stage.states[]`.
- **Code writes what the model could get wrong.** Ids; the description of every stage state (the text version and accessible name, derived from the state, so it cannot disagree with the picture; this replaces the design's `alt_per_state` written by the writer); the order of a call's options (the model writes the correct one first, code shuffles them by a stable seed, so the right answer is not always first); the misconception and its source; the common guess's source; the measures and the hash.
- **Claims cite like any other.** Every item and the recap cite passages. The answer, explanation and twist of a call and the text of an aside are claims. A figure in any reader text must be in a passage that item cites. Nine words in a row from the research fail the Scroll.
- **Findings sit at the writer's item** (`items[4]`, `recap`), so the one repair can fix them all together.
- **Two validators, one set of findings.** The authoring rules check what only the authored list can show (citations, copying, where an aside sits, the recipe's myth and variant). `validateDocumentV2` checks what the document can prove (limits, structure, stage, reading time, measures, hash). Their findings are merged.
- **Not decided here.** The limits and measures (D-121), the spike command (D-122), the reader, play, coverage v2 and admission of v2 documents (S-1, S-2).
- **Verified.** Unit tests of every rule, each shown failing on a broken input; property tests of the option shuffle and the stage descriptions; the recorded fixture answer through the real harness over PostgreSQL.

## D-121 · 2026-10-30 · scroll-limits.v2 and scroll-measures.v2: what counts and what is refused (S-0, #471)

The V3 design gives the numbers (at most one stage, four calls, two other interactives; 230 words a minute and ten seconds a call; three to six minutes; a stop of at most 60 words) and leaves the rules for applying them open. These are the rules the code applies. v1 limits and measures (200 words a minute, 2 to 8 minutes, 8 interactive blocks) are untouched.

- **Words.** A stop's text and its line together are at most 60 words, and the line at most 12. The explanation, the twist, an aside and a `simple` text are each at most 60 words.
- **Reading time.** Words at 230 a minute plus ten seconds for every call, the Final call included, must come to 180 to 360 seconds. Counted: stops, calls (prompt, options, explanation, twist), asides and recap captions. Not counted: `simple` texts. The twist is counted although only a wrong pick reads it, so the estimate is for the longer path. A call also weighs 38 word-equivalents (ten seconds) in the stop weights that coverage will use.
- **Budget.** At most 1 stage, 4 calls (the Final call included) and 2 other interactives (none is built, so this is a constant only); 6 to 14 stops and calls; at most 4 asides; at most 3 ask suggestions; 2 or 3 options per call; 128 KiB.
- **Structure.** Exactly one Final call, and it is the last stop. At least one Call it before it. At least one aside of kind `limits`, anchored before the Final call (never last), and the recap links to it. Only the first Call it of a myth Scroll has a common guess, and it carries the recipe's source. A stage has its first state on the first stop, states in stop order, and at least three states; a Scroll without a stage has none.
- **Notices, not refusals.** A myth whose source label has not been reviewed does not refuse the document; `validateDocumentV2` returns it as a notice, and the library must clear it before admission.
- **Interpretation.** The design says "stops about 14"; the code takes 6 as the least a 3-minute Scroll can have.

## D-122 · 2026-10-30 · The v2 writer spike: one operator command, nothing stored (S-0, #471)

The V3 design asks to run 20 topics through the new authoring shape before any engine is built. `content writer-spike-v2 --topics <keys> [--out <file>] [--concurrency <1-4>]` does that on the dev worker. It amends nothing: it is an operator tool, not product code.

- **Migration-free, no table, nothing admitted.** The command fetches each recipe's public research, runs writer prompt `scroll-writer.v14` through the production harness, assembles and validates the answer, and reports. The only records are the harness's own (run, attempts, kept refused answers). The branch can be deployed to dev by manual dispatch for the spike.
- **The same bounds as production.** One writer request and at most one repair per topic. Every paid attempt goes through the attempt ledger and the existing caps. An unknown attempt is never resent and its run stays open. No model call or network wait is inside a transaction. The batch starts no new topic after a spent budget, an unknown attempt, a refused harness, a model mismatch or two provider failures in a row.
- **Model.** Only `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` (D-031). The command refuses to start on another configured model, and the transport already fails an attempt whose reply names another model. Each line reports the models of its paid attempts.
- **What a line reports.** Outcome (accepted first take, accepted after repair, rejected, or why not), the findings of each refused answer (codes and paths, no text), words, measured minutes, counts of stops, calls and stage states, tokens in and out with thinking tokens (reported by the provider; thinking is part of output), wall time, and the run and attempt ids.
- **Recipe data the spike carries.** The library plan has no variant or misconception, so the spike keeps a table for its 20 recipes: stage or plain, and the misconception. Only the seasons myth has a reviewed source label, taken from the V3 design. Every other label is a placeholder marked `reviewed: false`, so no spike document can pass for a library Scroll. In the product these become recipe fields (S-6).
- **Output files.** `--out` appends each accepted topic's document and citations as a JSON line. It holds the model's own text about public sources: read it on the server, never paste it into an issue.
- **Results.** The numbers (acceptance within two requests, repair rate, minutes, tokens, the top findings) are measured on dev and recorded in #471 and in the entry that follows, not here.

## D-123 · 2026-10-30 · The v2 writer spike passed; four things change before v2 Scrolls are published (S-0, #471)

The spike ran on dev on 30 Oct through the dev worker with real `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` (D-122): 20 recipes, nothing admitted. Results are in #471.

- **Gate passed.** 17 of 20 were accepted within the writer and one repair (85 %), 8 of 9 stage recipes (89 %; the gate was 70 %), 11 on the first take. Every paid request reported `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash`. About 11k tokens and 75 s per Scroll; the run used about 2 % of the five-hour window. Reading times were 3.0 to 4.4 minutes.
- **So the flat shape (D-120) stays,** and the library waves can be sized at about 11k tokens a Scroll.

A read of six documents found errors the validator cannot see. These change the next slices:

1. **Stage states are derived by code from a few choices.** The writer set Earth's axis "sideways" in every Seasons state and drew hemisphere pins that contradict the month. The kit fixes Earth's tilt at 23.4° and derives the pins and Ray's angle from the month and the view; the writer chooses only the view, the month and the focus (S-1).
2. **A recipe gets a stage only when a kit draws its subject.** Tides and stars-fusion got pictures that show neither bulges nor a star's interior. Stage assignment is recipe data, per kit (S-1, S-6).
3. **Every wave gets a factual read, and the validator refuses a Final call that restates an earlier call.** Two correct options were wrong or evasive, and one Final call repeated a call (S-6).
4. **The common-guess rule and the prompt are brought into line** before the waves; two recipes were refused twice on it (S-1).

## D-124 · 2026-10-30 · A stage state holds four choices; code derives the physics, and a call never shows its answer (S-1a, #472)

D-123 found two stage errors the validator could not see: Earth's axis set "sideways" in every Seasons state, and hemisphere pins that contradicted the month. This entry amends D-120 so that such errors cannot be written. It also settles where a call's picture shows.

- **The writer chooses four things.** `view`, `earthAt` (the month), `moonAt` (the phase, only in the Moon view) and `focus`. The old `axis`, `ray`, `orbit`, `northPin` and `southPin` words are gone. The orbit is two views: `orbit` (to scale) and `orbit-stretched`. `night-sky` is gone: the kit draws no stars.
- **Code derives everything physical.** Earth's tilt is 23.4 degrees in every picture. A lookup of the lean toward the Sun by month (the sine of the year) says which half leans toward the Sun. That half has a high Sun, a warm pin and a steep Ray; the other half the opposite; at the March and September equinoxes neither leans and both are neutral. The two halves can never both be warm or both be cool.
- **Only what a view draws is stored.** A view lists its parts. A month where the view has no seasons, a phase where it has no Moon, and a focus on a part that is not drawn are set aside by `settleStageState` before a state is stored. Settling is idempotent. `validateDocumentV2` refuses a stored state that is not settled (`stage_state_unsettled`).
- **The text version is derived from the state.** `describeStageState` settles first and mentions only drawn parts. It states the fixed tilt, which half leans toward the Sun, the pins and Ray for each half. It never says the axis is on its side. A stored `alt` must equal it.
- **Properties tested.** For every month and every view: the pins and Ray agree with the season of each hemisphere, checked against the noon Sun height at 40 degrees north and south computed in the test from the declination; the halves mirror six months apart; no description of any state says the axis is on its side.
- **A call never changes the picture.** A stage state sits on a read stop, never on a `call_it` or the `final_call` (`stage_state_on_call`). While a call is open the picture is the one the stop before it set up. The answer's picture belongs on the stop after the call, where the reader has already locked in. The Final call is the last stop, so no picture shows its answer; the recap keeps the last picture the stops set. The prompt and the validator say the same thing.
- **Earth's axis is never "sideways".** A real-model re-measure (#472) still found "Earth's sideways axis" in the prose. The prompt says Earth's axis is tilted by a fixed angle, and the authoring rules refuse a sentence of a stage Scroll that joins "sideways" or "on its side" to the axis, the lean or the tilt (`earth_axis_sideways`). A plain Scroll may say it: Uranus does spin sideways.
- **Not decided here.** The stage engine, the drawing itself and the stage's text-version UI (S-1b).

## D-125 · 2026-10-30 · A stage needs a kit that draws the subject, and "Seasons elsewhere" is Part 3 (S-1a, #472)

D-123 found that tides and stars-fusion got Sun, Earth and orbit pictures that show neither a bulge nor a star. This entry amends D-120 and D-059.

- **Recipe data, not a system default.** A library recipe may name a `stageKit`. Only a recipe with one gets a pinned picture; every other recipe is a plain Scroll with calls and no picture. Today one kit exists, Earth & sky (`earth-sky.v1`). The spike's own variant table is gone: it reads the recipe.
- **Three recipes have a stage:** `seasons`, `seasons-hemispheres` and `seasons-limits`. Tides, stars, exoplanets, the greenhouse recipes and the new `seasons-elsewhere` are plain Scrolls with calls until a kit draws them. A new kit is a new id and a new decision.
- **New recipe `seasons-elsewhere` and facet `earth.seasons.elsewhere`.** "Seasons elsewhere" is seasons on Mars and Uranus. Its pages are the NASA Mars and Uranus fact pages (54 passages), which state Mars's tilt of 25 degrees and its longer seasons, and Uranus's tilt of nearly a right angle and its extreme seasons. No passage is about a world without a tilt, so neither the brief nor the facet asks for one. The earth.seasons planet now has four facets. `seasons-limits` and its facet `earth.seasons.limits` are unchanged, because coverage pins the facet's meaning.
- **No picture for other worlds.** The kit draws only Earth. A stage on `seasons-elsewhere` would have the screen-reader text describe Earth in June while the stop talks about Uranus, so the recipe is plain. A kit for other worlds can come in S-8 (a new kit id and a new decision). The brief says nothing of a picture, because the v1 writer reads it too.
- **Series.** `library.earth.seasons` is now seasons, seasons-hemispheres, seasons-elsewhere (a new series revision, admitted by `content admit-continuity`). `seasons-limits` is in no series, so a related move `seasons-elsewhere` to `seasons-limits` ("Where the simple tilt picture breaks down") keeps it reachable. All series rules still hold: two to four parts, one planet, a recipe in one series, one related move per source.
- **After deploy.** The new facet changes the catalogue digest. Stored coverage stays readable. Run `content admit-continuity` for the new series revision, and `content readmit-coverage` once Scrolls of the new recipe are published. After `admit-continuity`, `seasons-limits` keeps a `previous` link into series revision 1 (the old Part 3): decide at admission whether to retire that link or republish it. Nothing is produced or published by this change.
- **Facts still to be settled by a person.** The misconception for `seasons-elsewhere` in the spike table is a placeholder with an unreviewed source, like every one except the seasons myth.

## D-126 · 2026-10-30 · The common guess is a place in the options, and the Final call may not restate a call (S-1a, #472)

D-123 found two Scrolls refused twice on `common_guess_invalid` and `common_guess_missing`. This entry amends D-120 and D-121.

- **Why it failed.** The v14 prompt asked for `"commonGuess"`: a number, "1 or 2", counted from the correct option at 0. A call may have only two options, so 2 was out of range. The same mistake raised both codes: the authoring rule said `common_guess_invalid`, and the assembler then had no guess to set, so the document said `common_guess_missing`. The repair hint repeated "1 or 2". The refused answers stay on the dev server and were not read for this entry; the cause follows from the two rules.
- **The fix is the shape, not a better hint.** The writer writes no number. In the first `call_it` of a myth Scroll the correct option is first and the wrong option that states the myth is second. Code marks the second option as the common guess before it shuffles. There is nothing to count and nothing to forget. The key `commonGuess` is no longer part of `scroll-authoring.v2`. The document's own rules (a wrong option, the recipe's source, only the first call of a myth) are unchanged and still checked on stored documents.
- **Tested with an answer that follows the prompt literally:** a two-option myth call passes, and the prompt text names the second option and no number.
- **A Final call may not restate a call.** `final_call_restates_call` refuses a Final call whose prompt, or whose correct option, is near-identical to an earlier call's. The measure is the share of content words two texts have in common (letters and digits, lower case, filler words dropped, plurals cut back), compared as sets. The pinned threshold is 0.6. A text with fewer than three content words is not judged. The tests use a near-copy (0.83) and different questions on the same subject (0.1 at most). Heavy paraphrase is not caught; a person's read of each wave (D-123) still is needed.
- **Prompt `scroll-writer.v15`** carries all of this: the second-option rule, the four stage choices, no stage on calls, and a Final call that is a new case.

## D-127 · 2026-10-30 · Coverage v2: a v2 Scroll binds its facets to read stops (S-1b, #472)

A planet forms from facet coverage (D-033). Coverage v1 binds a facet to top-level paragraphs, quotes and callouts, and a v2 document has none of those, so no planet could form from a v2 Scroll. This adds `scroll-recipe-coverage.v2`. Coverage v1 is unchanged and still decides every v1 Scroll.

**Amends** D-033 for documents whose reading facts are v3 (D-128).

- **Same shape, new units.** The eligible units are the read stops that cite at least one documented claim. The primary facet needs the first, middle and last of them; a supporting facet needs the last two. Fewer than three eligible stops claim no coverage, the same bar as v1.
- **Calls bind nothing.** A call is play, not explanation, and D-063 keeps play out of formation. A binding that names a call is refused at admission (`CONTENT_COVERAGE_LOCATOR_INVALID`). Calls do not weigh in coverage either (D-128).
- **The bar is checked before publication.** The v2 document validator asks for at least three read stops (`read_stops_too_few`, `LIMITS_V2.minReadStops`, kept equal to the coverage bar by a test), so a published v2 Scroll can never be refused coverage for too few stops, and a writer that falls short gets its one repair.
- **Same mapping, one more version number.** The stored mapping keeps `scroll-static-coverage.v1` as its policy, so the region bindings and every reader of a mapping keep working. Its `readingFactsVersion` is 3 and the review reference says `scroll-recipe-coverage.v2`.
- **Decided by code, at publication.** The rule runs in the publication transaction like v1. `content readmit-coverage` applies the rule that matches each stored revision.
- **Verified.** Unit tests of the rule, and an integration test on real PostgreSQL that publishes a labelled v2 fixture over a v1 revision and reads back both heads. Shown failing when the eligible unit is changed.

## D-128 · 2026-10-30 · Reading facts v3: what counts as reading a v2 stop (S-1b, #472)

The Reader qualifies a reading by `q = 0.5 · coverage + 0.5 · dwell` (blueprint §8). A v2 Scroll is read stop by stop, and a reader can jump to any stop from the spine, so a stop must count as read by more than being on screen for half a second.

**Amends** the qualification input for v2 documents. The policy (`scroll-qualification.v0`), its weights and its thresholds (q at least 0.6, coverage at least 0.5) do not change.

- **Facts v3.** Content stores, with each v2 revision, one unit per stop and call: a rule for when it counts, a weight, and the reading estimate (the measures' reading time, calls included). **Only read stops weigh**, with the weight the document's own measures give them (an aside weighing with the stop it opens beside); the total is the sum of those weights. A call is a unit so that its `locked` or `revealed` is accepted at intake and stored as engagement, but it weighs 0. The recap is the payoff screen and is not a unit.
- **A read stop** counts after it was visible, while the article was active, for 40 % of the time its own words take at 230 words a minute, and never less than the policy's half second. A reader up to two and a half times faster than the pace still reads it.
- **A call** is seen when it was reported as locked in or revealed (never by being on screen). Seeing it adds nothing to coverage, so **playing never stands in for reading and not playing never blocks it**: a reader who plays every call but skims the prose does not qualify, and one who reads all the prose and locks no call does. Calls reach q only through the reading estimate, which counts ten seconds for each (findings §5a: no change to q). The option picked is not a reportable interaction at all, so nothing about it reaches evidence (D-063). The new value-free kinds are `locked` and `revealed`, declared for `call_it` and `final_call`.
- **Tapping through the spine does not qualify.** Without time on each stop the read stops are not seen, so coverage stays low; the 40 % rule closes the gap v1's half second would leave for a skimming tapper (coverage 1 with a fifth of the dwell would reach 0.6).
- **Evidence carries the rule, not content.** A unit without a rule counts as v1 always has, so v1 facts and Reels are untouched. The two hash the same way in coverage certificates; a mapping names the facts version it was decided for.
- **Verified.** A pure test with 500 generated tapping traces and the hardest tap (every dot, each read stop just under 40 %, every call locked and on screen for two minutes), none qualifying; 500 traces with all calls locked and the prose skimmed, none qualifying; full prose with no call locked qualifies; a walk at the full pace and a fast reader qualify. Shown failing when the share is 0, when stops count after half a second, and (the call traces) when calls carry the weight of their words.

## D-129 · 2026-10-30 · v2 Scrolls are admitted by the production job and served by schema (S-1b, #472)

The production job published only v1 Scrolls. A v2 Scroll is now produced, admitted and served through the same job, the same attempt ledger and the same coverage rule, and a client that cannot render it is not given it.

**Amends** D-037 and D-120 (the writer ran only in the spike).

- **The plan marks a recipe v2.** `LibraryRecipe.scrollV2` carries the variant and the misconception. Every recipe stays v1 until the plan marks it. This entry marks none; marking the Seasons recipes is the next step.
- **A job per version.** A v2 recipe's jobs use `scroll-production.v15`, a v1 recipe's `.v14`. A recipe already published as v1 is therefore a new job and its Scroll a new revision of the same asset (the identity never changes, D-033). A v2 recipe counts as published only when its current revision is a v2 document, so `produce-library` and `production-status` treat a v1 Scroll as not yet regenerated. A queued job whose version does not match the plan is dropped.
- **Same bounds.** One writer request and at most one repair, in a worker, through the attempt ledger. No model call or network wait is inside a transaction or on HTTP. The writer role is the S-0 v2 writer at its current version (`writer-v2.ts`); the stage rules belong to S-1a. The citations a Scroll makes must fit its provenance (at most 48 different passages): that is checked while the answer is validated, so the one repair can fix it.
- **Refused by the plan, before any request.** A recipe whose misconception source label no person has reviewed (`reviewed: false`) is refused at dispatch (`plan_refused`, `misconception_source_unreviewed`): no job is reserved, nothing is sent, and `production-status` shows it as `stopped`, so neither `produce-library` nor Quartermaster picks it. The document validator still reports the label as a notice and the stored compile still refuses it, should the plan change while a job runs.
- **Provenance for citations.** `scroll-provenance.v2` records the claims a v2 Scroll cites (the exact source passages), their sources, the production record, and for each stop, aside and the recap the claims it cites. It is stored beside the revision and never served.
