- **Served by schema.** The document endpoint serves a v2 document whole to a client that declares `scroll-document.v2`, and answers `cannot_render` to any other. A v2 document has no blocks to substitute. `parseDocumentV2` is the client path (it skips measures and hash unless asked, like v1). `renderableFormats`, `canRender`, region candidates and the Planner's capabilities accept either schema.
- **A v1 revision stands in, or nothing does.** Discovery offers a client that cannot render a v2 current revision the latest earlier v1 revision of the same asset, if one exists; otherwise the asset is not offered to it. An exact revision pinned by an offer or a link is never swapped. **Limit:** this assumes every v2 revision regenerates a v1 one. A later correction of a v2 revision (v3) would be offered to a v1-only client as the oldest v1 revision, which may be older than the one it first saw; the rule has to be revisited the first time a v2 Scroll is corrected.
- **Not in this entry.** Snippet anchors for v2 passages, the reader (S-1c), play (S-2).
- **Verified.** Integration tests on real PostgreSQL: regeneration over a v1 revision, the rejected source, the status, the served schemas, the fallback; unit tests of the versions and the stored compile.

## D-130 · 2026-10-30 · The context carries a series total counted from admitted units (S-1b, #472)

**Amends** D-059: "Series total is not invented. The context response carries a part number and no total." D-072 allows counts for Scrolls, and a count must be real.

- `series.total` in the exposure context is the number of `content.series_units` of the series revision the Scroll is in. It is never declared, estimated or taken from the library plan.
- It appears for any unit that is in an admitted series, Scroll or Reel, and nowhere else. A series with no counted units has no `series` at all. Every other field of the context is unchanged.
- The Composer's snapshot does not carry it: ranking never reads the total.

## D-133 · 2026-10-30 · The stage engine and the Earth & sky kit: what is drawn, how it moves, where it sits (S-1c, #472)

D-120 and D-124 fixed what a stage state may say and what code derives from it. This entry decides how the reader draws it.

**Builds on** D-124. The physics stays in `scroll-model` (`stagePhysics`); the drawing only places things.

- **One scene, from the derived state.** `sceneOf(state)` turns a settled state and its physics into positions and angles. It decides no physics: which half leans, how high the Sun stands and each pin's mark come from `stagePhysics`.
- **Where Earth is.** June is at the right, September at the top, December at the left, March at the bottom. The orbit views show the four of them as pale Earths with the same axis, so the one fixed lean can be seen. A pale Earth that would sit on the real one is not drawn.
- **Two orbits.** `orbit` is nearly a circle with the Sun in the middle. `orbit-stretched` is flatter with the Sun off-centre toward the near side. It is an illustration and says so in its words.
- **Side views.** The Sun is at the left and its light arrives in parallel. Each hemisphere's pin sits at a distance from the point that faces the Sun that follows its Sun height (22, 50 or 76 degrees), so Ray's angle and the size of the patch the beam covers come out of the geometry: a low Sun spreads over more ground. The axis leans toward the Sun for a leaning north, away for a leaning south, and is upright when neither leans (a lean toward the viewer cannot be drawn flat).
- **Drawing agrees with physics, by test.** For every month, the half that leans in the orbit picture is the half `stagePhysics` names; the side-view lean matches; the higher Sun covers less ground and has the warmer pin. Shown failing when the orbit's axis turn was changed.
- **The stage at a stop** is the last state set at or before it. A call sets none, so a call never shows its own answer.
- **How it moves.** A move inside one view, or between the two orbits, glides over the subject motion token's 320 ms (Earth by the short way round, the axis and the pins blended). A move to a different view fades one picture into the other. Under reduced motion every move is a 160 ms cross-fade and nothing between two states is drawn. A move that starts while another is under way starts from the picture on screen.
- **Its name.** The picture is one image whose accessible name is the state's description from the document. "Text" (key T) swaps the picture for those sentences.
- **Where it sits.** On a phone the stage is a third of the screen (at least 176, at most 300 px) pinned above the text, with the part label on it. From a tablet the top bar carries the title and the part. There is no V3 frame for a Scroll on a desktop, so a desktop is a centred column about 750 px wide with the stage pinned at its top and the thread beside it, as for any Scroll.
- **Calls are played on the device.** Pick, lock in, then the answer and why; a wrong pick adds the Plot twist, and the reviewed source of the common guess shows when that was the pick. Nothing is saved or sent but the reveal (D-135); locking, sparks and the Plot twist's effects are S-2.
- **Not decided here.** Parallax, an animated Ray, other kits, the stage's own drags (the stage takes none in this version).

Confirmed by: `earth-sky-scene.test.ts`, `use-stage-pictures.test.tsx`, `stage-reader.test.tsx`, `stage-reader-smoke.spec.ts` (picture follows stops, tween and cross-fade, axe at 390 and 1440).

## D-134 · 2026-10-30 · Gestures and keys of the Stage + Spine reader (S-1c, #472)

**Builds on** D-071 (swipe between Parts from text and stop surfaces only) and D-053 (keys act on the focused part, never the page).

- **Swipe, by finger only.** A mouse or pen drag across the text is selecting it (the way to "Simpler" and "Ask about this"), so it never moves a Part or asks for a new topic; on a desktop every move is a button and a key. A sideways drag of a finger of at least 64 px that is clearly more sideways than vertical moves between Parts: left is the next Part, right the one before. It is read only on the text. The stage, the spine and the dock are outside it, so the stage keeps its own drags. A drag that starts in the 24 px edge zone of a phone is the platform's Back. A drag that starts on a field or slider is that control's.
- **Vertical reading never navigates.** Only a pull up from the very end of the text, at least 96 px, asks for a new topic. A fling that ends at the end is not that: the pull must start at the end. On iOS the scroll view's overscroll past the end (72 px) is the same ask, and only when the drag began at the end; Android uses the button.
- **One move, one path.** Swipe, key and button call the same thing. A Part is the series continuation the server admitted for this Scroll, sent with its displayed link. A new topic is the vertical move in the same format. Where there is no admitted Part the button is absent and the swipe and key do nothing.
- **Keys.** They work while the reader itself has focus (Tab to it or click it) and nowhere else: down or Space the next stop, up the one before, right the next Part, left the one before, N a new topic, U your Universe, A Ask, T the text version, F the other format, 1 to 4 pick in a call, Enter lock in. A control inside keeps its own keys. The keyboard map lists them, and a test fails when a key is added without a row.
- **Quick presses add up.** A key pressed while the last is still scrolling continues from where that one was heading.
- **Soft snap.** Native uses the scroll view's snap offsets. The web settles on a stop's top when scrolling has been still for 140 ms and the text is within 48 px of it, and never at the very end. CSS scroll snapping pulled a long last section back to the first stop, so it is not used.
- **Not decided here.** The Part 2 entry animation and a swipe that works at once mid-read (S-5; this reader does not credit it).

Confirmed by: `part-gesture.test.ts`, `reader-keys.test.ts`, `keyboard-map.test.tsx`, `stage-reader-smoke.spec.ts` (swipe left, a short nudge and a stage drag move nothing, N, a pull up in the middle and at the end). Shown failing when the swipe distance was 8 px (the nudge swiped), when an unlisted key was added to the reader, and when the iOS pull ignored where the drag began.

## D-135 · 2026-10-30 · What the stage reader reports, and which screens read a v2 Scroll (S-1c, #472)

**Builds on** D-063 and D-128.

- **The stop on screen is the one visible stop.** The reader tells the recorder that stop is wholly visible and every other is not. The stage, the spine, an aside and the end tray are not units. Going to another stop, leaving the screen or replacing the observer closes the stop. Activity is the article's own rule, so time with a sheet over the reader never counts.
- **A jump gives only the time it was on screen.** A dot tap or key scrolls without animation, so the stops passed on the way get no time. Reading every stop for 50 % of its own words' time plus 0.3 s reports above the 40 % rule for each; tapping through every dot reports none.
- **A call is reported once, as revealed.** The event has no field for the option picked. `locked` is not reported until locking exists (S-2).
- **A place is a stop.** Keep and resume store the stop id as the block id with offset 0. Opening at a place scrolls to that stop.
- **Who reads v2.** Explore and `/scroll/<id>` declare `scroll-document.v2` and draw it. Threads, snippets and everything else still declare v1 only, so the server sends them an earlier v1 revision or nothing. A v2 document reaching a screen that did not declare it shows the update state and is not activated.

Confirmed by: `stage-reader.test.tsx`, `scroll-session.test.ts`, `scroll-v2-reader-trace.test.ts` (what the reader reports, qualified by the server's own rules: a full read qualifies, tapping every dot does not), and the two reading tests of `stage-reader-smoke.spec.ts`, which check every observation against the intake schema. Shown failing when the stop on screen was reported as not visible.

## D-137 · 2026-10-30 · The four Seasons recipes become v2, and which of them test a misconception (S-1e, #472)

**Amends** D-129, which marked no recipe, and D-125, which named the three Seasons recipes that get a picture. **Builds on** D-123.

- **Four recipes are marked.** `seasons`, `seasons-hemispheres` and `seasons-limits` get a picture (they name the Earth & sky kit). `seasons-elsewhere` is a plain Scroll, because the kit draws only Earth. A library test pins the list and keeps the picture choice equal to `stageKit`, so marking a fifth recipe is a visible, deliberate edit.
- **The misconception is a person's choice, one recipe at a time.** A myth needs a source label that a person has reviewed, and the library refuses a recipe whose label is not (D-129).
  - `seasons` keeps the distance myth ("closer to the Sun in summer") with the label `A Private Universe (1987 documentary)`, reviewed, as the V3 design names it (pages 3 and 6). Its page, the NASA Space Place article, also says that many people believe it.
  - `seasons-hemispheres`, `seasons-limits` and `seasons-elsewhere` have **none**. On 30 Oct I fetched the five pages the recipes cite (Wikipedia "Season" and "Earth's orbit", NASA Mars and Uranus facts, NASA Space Place) and searched them for a statement that people hold a wrong idea (misconception, popular or common belief, "many people believe", myth). Only the Space Place page has one, and it is the distance myth that `seasons` already carries. "Earth's orbit" states that Earth is nearest the Sun in January while the north is coldest, which shows the idea is wrong, but never says people believe it. The spike's placeholder myths for the other three ("summer comes to the whole Earth at once", "the same four seasons of equal length", "every planet has four long seasons") are stated as a belief in none of the pages, so no label could be reviewed for them. A Scroll with no myth is a narrative Scroll with calls; its first call has no common guess.
  - The spike no longer keeps its own table for these four recipes. It runs them with the plan's decisions, so a spike measures what the library will publish.
- **What marking changes.** The four recipes are now jobs of `scroll-production.v15`. The v1 Scrolls already published are not "published" for the plan, so `production-status` lists the three as `not_started`, and a v2 production writes the next revision of the same asset. `seasons-elsewhere` has no Scroll yet. Nothing is produced by this entry: the coordinator produces, reads and releases them on dev (D-138, the runbook).
- **Create does not write them.** A person's ask never starts a v2 recipe (D-138), so asks about Seasons are answered from the library until the v2 Scrolls are released.
- **Not decided here.** A kit for other worlds (S-8), and the misconceptions of the other library recipes (S-6).

Confirmed by: `library.test.ts` (the list, the picture rule, reviewed sources only, the one myth and its label), `topics.test.ts` of the spike (it runs a recipe exactly as the plan marks it), and `scroll-v2-admission.test.ts` (a v1 Seasons revision followed by the v2 regeneration of the same asset, then released). Shown failing when `seasons-limits` was given an unreviewed myth, when `seasons-elsewhere` was given a picture the kit does not draw for it, and when the spike table was allowed to override the plan.

## D-138 · 2026-10-30 · An operator reads a v2 library Scroll before anyone is served it: the review gate (S-1e, #472)

**Builds on** D-123 item 3 (every wave gets a factual read) and D-129. **Amends** D-037 (Create never writes a v2 recipe).

- **What there was.** Nothing between "admitted" and "served". `content.assets.status` is `admitted` or `withdrawn`, and every read follows the asset's current revision. The gate uses that pointer instead of a new state machine.
- **Hold.** Production of a v2 library recipe stores the revision, its provenance, its topic and its coverage in the usual transaction, but does not move the current revision. A new asset is born `held`. A row in `content.revision_reviews` says `held` (migration 0072, which also allows the asset status `held`). The job is `published` and records `held_for_review`; its result carries `held: true`. The rule sits where the revision is stored, so the operator's library run, a direct run and a retry cannot skip it. v1 recipes and made-to-order Scrolls publish as before.
- **What cannot see it.** Reads that follow the current revision (discovery, navigation, series and related moves, region candidates and the Universe, opening a Scroll) never reach a revision newer than it or an asset that is `held`. Reads that take an exact asset and revision from their caller refuse a revision whose review state is not `released`: exact encounters, the stored document for rendering, shareable and saved metadata, topics and attribution, the source pages a what-if may draw on (`readScrollSourcePages`), the coverage mapping that formation reads, and the check that a report names a revision a person could have met (`findAssetForAction` with a revision, used by `file-report`, so a rejected revision cannot be reported and its title cannot reach the reporter's export). Coverage is still decided for a held revision at publication and by `readmit-coverage`, so the operator sees whether the planet would form. Not guarded, because they take coordinates only from rows made for a served revision: snippet anchors, Keep position targets (also checked against the current revision), an exposure's reading facts, and series admission (which reads current revisions).
- **Standing.** `production-status` shows `held` and the held revision, never `published`. `produce-library` skips a held recipe, and a second take is refused while the first is held. Quartermaster never picks a v2 recipe for a person's ask: the ask could not be answered by a Scroll that waits for a read, and the tokens would be spent for nothing. The operator's library run is the only way a v2 recipe is written.
- **Create, now and at the end.** While some recipes are v1, Create still writes those, and asks about a v2 meaning are answered from the library as it stands. A group that named a recipe before the plan made it v2 is not joined and its job is not sent again: `readScrollProduction` reads such a recipe as stopped until its Scroll is served, so the join closes the old group and the ask starts on a recipe Create may write, and its waiters close with `could_not_prepare` (tested). Once every recipe is v2 (S-6, the waves), `chooseScrollRecipe` returns none for every catalogue ask, and an ask for a meaning with no served Scroll closes `could_not_prepare` honestly. Nothing in this slice replaces Create: S-6 owns the waves that fill the library, and S-7 (Ask from here, with ask orders) owns the path by which a person's question becomes a made-to-order Scroll.
- **Four commands, shared database only.** `content review-list [--all]` (recipe, revision, title, words, minutes, picture, coverage; no body text; `--all` adds who, when and why), `content review-show --asset <id> [--revision <n>] [--sources]` (the Scroll as plain text on the terminal, with the cited passages on request), `content release --asset <id> --revision <n>` and `content reject --asset <id> --revision <n> --reason <text>`. Both decisions record `cli:<operator>` and the time in `content.revision_reviews`, are safe to repeat, and each writes one `content.review_decided` line to the event log (recipe key, outcome, revision and, for a refusal, its code; never the Scroll or the reason given).
- **Release** makes exactly that revision the current one (a `held` asset becomes `admitted`), never backwards, and refuses a rejected revision. **Reject** is final: the take is spent, the earlier revision (if any) stays served, and the next take writes the next revision number. Nothing edits a held revision: a wording to correct is a reject and a new take.
- **After release** the coordinator runs `content admit-continuity` (a series is admitted from the revisions served at that moment, and a series with a part still held is skipped whole) and `content readmit-coverage`.
- **Limit.** A recipe has three takes. If all three are rejected the recipe is `stopped`, and its brief or pages need a code change and a new production version.

Confirmed by: `review-gate.test.ts` on real PostgreSQL (a new asset and an asset that serves a v1 revision are produced held; discovery, exact reads, rendering, coverage, regions, sharing, saved metadata, opening and a series reach nothing of the held revision; release makes it visible once and again changes nothing; reject keeps the earlier revision served, is final, frees the next take and the next revision is released; `produce-library` skips it and a second take is refused; a v1 recipe publishes straight to served; topics, attribution and source pages of a held revision are unreachable and a report may not name it), `feedback-reports.test.ts` and `file-report.test.ts` (a rejected revision cannot be reported), `supply-create.test.ts` (a group that named a now-v2 recipe is not joined and its job is not sent), `review.test.ts`, `review-text.test.ts`, `release/review-operator.test.ts` (the commands and their event-log line from the built release), and the runbook's commands. Shown failing when a held revision advanced the current revision, when rendering, exact reads and coverage reads ignored the review state, when a rejection did not free the next take, when production skipped the hold, when `production-status` counted a held recipe as published, when a release could move a served asset backwards, when Quartermaster could pick a v2 recipe, when topics, attribution or source pages ignored the review state, when a report ignored it, and when a group naming a v2 recipe still read as coming.

## D-139 · 2026-10-30 · Keys in a Scroll act on the stop the reader is heading to (S-1c follow-up, #472)

**Amends** D-134 ("Quick presses add up" now covers the call keys too, and a jump follows a section that grows).

- **The race.** The reader learns which stop is on screen from the scroll report, and the web can hold that report back (a slow device, or a scroll end that has not fired yet). A 1 to 4 or Enter pressed in that gap acted on the stop the reader had just left, and the pick was lost for good.
- **The rule.** Every key acts on the stop the reader is on or travelling to: where the last jump was heading while it is under way (800 ms), otherwise the stop on screen. Arrows and Space already worked this way; the call keys now do too.
- **A section that grows.** Showing a call's answer makes its section taller, and the new height is reported a frame later. A jump made in that frame (Enter, then Down at once) used the old tops and landed inside the answer, short of the next stop. A jump under way (800 ms) is repeated to the new top when the heights arrive; a layout change later leaves the reader's place alone.

Confirmed by: `reader-actions.test.ts`, `stage-reader-smoke.spec.ts` (a key pressed before the scroll to a call is reported). `use-reader-scroll.test.tsx`, and the full-read test (it failed about 1 run in 20 before). Shown failing with the call keys on the stop on screen (e2e 10 of 10, unit test red) and with the jump left on the old top (unit test red).

## D-140 · 2026-10-30 · A thread's header is a 72 px strip and the conversation keeps over 60 % of a phone (T-A, #481)

Amends D-046 and D-053 (the thread pane of D03). Client only: no contract, SQL or server change.

- **A strip, not a card.** A thread opens with Back, a line saying what it is ("A THREAD · 3 PARTS"), the title, Series and options. The fixed series card and the horizontal part strip are gone from the phone. Rooms keep their own header.
- **The conversation keeps the screen.** At 390 x 844 with the player docked the messages are 65.9 % of the viewport and the whole conversation region (asks and message box included) is 84.6 %. A test measures it. With the player opened in place it is 47 %: that is the person's choice.

Confirmed by: `apps/client/e2e/thread-phone-smoke.spec.ts` (the 60 % measure).

## D-141 · 2026-10-30 · Series is a sheet pulled down from the header, and its arrow keys only move the selection (T-A, #481)

Amends D-006 k and D-053.

- **An overlay, never a route.** Series lists the parts and the people. Back, Esc, Close and a tap on the scrim close only the sheet. The header stays in view and only what is below is dimmed (`PullDownSheet`).
- **Pressing a row chooses it and closes the sheet.** The arrow keys (up, down, left, right) only move the selection to the neighbouring part; the sheet stays open. The keyboard map says so.
- **The sheet exists below 1024 px only.** Resizing to a desktop with it open closes it and does not leave the player paused.

Confirmed by: `thread-phone-smoke.spec.ts` (sheet, Back, Esc, arrows, resize), `episode-list.test.tsx`, `pull-down-sheet.test.tsx`.

## D-142 · 2026-10-30 · The thread's player starts docked on a phone and the person's own choice wins (T-A, #481)

Amends D-046 (selection and fold stay on the device).

- **Docked is a 56 px bar** naming the open part, with Updated when the revision is corrected. Pressing it opens the player in place; folding docks it again.
- **A default, then the person's choice.** Until the person folds or unfolds, a phone is docked and a desktop is open. Pressing Watch counts as unfolding, so a resize across 1024 px never docks a playing Reel.
- **Nothing is sent.** Selection and fold stay in the account scope's storage on this device.

Confirmed by: `own-place.test.tsx`, `thread-phone-smoke.spec.ts`, the resize test in `talk-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-143 · 2026-10-30 · Desktop has a series panel beside the conversation (T-A, #481)

Amends D-053 (Talk panes).

- **From 1024 px the conversation fills the left and a 400 px panel on the right** holds the parts, the player and the people. The panel is first in reading order and last on screen.
- **The inbox is not beside a thread yet.** The thread has the rail only.
- **No place.** Nothing says where another member is. A coarse place is T-D (D-065).

Confirmed by: `thread-phone-smoke.spec.ts` (panel), `talk-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-144 · 2026-10-30 · A time under each run of messages in a thread (T-A, #481)

- **A run** is one author's messages, each within five minutes of the one before and on one day. The last message of a run shows its clock time in the reader's own time zone; the day capsule above shows the date. Both come from `recordedAt`.
- **Threads only.** The direct conversation list gets its times in T-B.

Confirmed by: `talk-format.test.ts`, `thread-phone-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-160 · 2026-10-30 · Who is here: a one-minute lease, on a member's own view, with the reader always in it (#487)

D-069 says faces always, a number only from three, no "0 online" or "1 online", the cast never counted, and an allow-list in the contract. This is how the room module keeps it. It **builds on** D-051 and D-069 and changes neither.

- **The lease.** `rooms.room_presence` holds one row per member who has the room open. Only the member's own device writes it, through `rooms/presence` (`here` or `away`), never derived from messages or another module. A row counts for 60 seconds; an open, visible room renews it every 25 seconds. Leaving, removal and account deletion delete it. Clear keeps it, as it keeps the membership: it is a lease of the account, not exploration, and ends by itself.
- **The allow-list.** A member's own view of a room carries `here` (faces, and `count` only from three), `memberTotal` and `lastMessageAt`. A list, a preview and a station carry none of them. The contract refuses a `count` below three and a `count` smaller than the faces shown.
- **The reader is always a face.** A person who reads the room has it open, so their own face is in `here` even before their first beat. Others appear only through a lease.
- **A blocked person is left out, not drawn as a placeholder.** For the blocker, the person's face and the count are both computed without them, so the count cannot give them away. Everyone else reads the room as before (D-051: a block is the reader's choice).
- **Hints.** A room scope on the socket is subscribed by current members only. A hint goes out when a lease starts or ends, when someone leaves or is removed, and when a message is sent. A steady beat sends none. The hint's revision is the commit time in milliseconds and is not a position to catch up from, so a client coalesces a burst of hints with a 300 ms quiet window instead of skipping any; the hint names the room and nothing else. The worker's stored AI reply sends the same hint, and a room scope is never held in the gateway's authorisation cache, so a removed member hears nothing at once. Expiry has no event, so a room is read again on the slow poll and on focus as before.
- **Quiet.** "Quiet right now · last message 3 h ago" is worked out on the device from `lastMessageAt` and the device clock: after ten minutes without a message, and "no messages yet" for a room nobody has written in. A clock that runs behind never makes a busy room look quiet.

## D-161 · 2026-10-30 · Keeping messages out takes effect with the setting, and the AI notice says what is true today (#487)

R3 question 6 (keep my messages out of what AI reads) and question 5 (default level) are final through D-060. The R-1 issue said the substitution would come with R-2; it is built now, so the switch is never a promise.

- **Substitution before items are built.** The asked-turn context builder replaces each message of a member who keeps their messages out with `[a member's message]` before any context item is created: their text, the title of what they shared and their note are not read, so neither the model nor the stored request bytes carry them. The message keeps its place and its speaker number. A discussion's title is the starter's own typing, so when the starter keeps out it becomes `[a member's topic]`; the room's name and guidelines are the operator's, the content's title and summary the library's, and polls and pins are never read. This also holds for the member's own asks. An AI message that was written earlier stays, because it is the room's.
- **Nothing cached to unpick yet.** No summary or memory exists, so there is nothing to mark stale. A summary built later must use the same builder and add the stale mark in the change that creates the cache.
- **Defaults.** A new member starts at Lively in an open room and Quieter in a room that is not open to everyone. A person who comes back keeps their own level, keep-out choice and notice mark. The level is stored now and matters once residents speak on their own; until then asked turns are unchanged.
- **The notice.** It is shown once, in General, until the member taps "Got it" (`notice_seen_at` keeps the first time). Afterwards the same words live in the room menu under AI here. Its words describe today: an inhabitant reads recent messages, without names, only when someone asks. The per-visit line in the inhabitant bar is gone. The wording changes when residents speak unprompted.
- **General.** Every room has one General (`discussions.kind = 'general'`, one per room by a partial unique index), made with the room and backfilled for rooms that exist. Seeding makes one for a room that has none. It cannot be pinned and is not in the topics list; `today` is reserved and nothing creates it yet.

## D-131 · 2026-10-30 · A vertical move never offers another part of the series the person is in (S-1d, #472)

In the V3 grammar swipe left is Part 2 and pull up is a new topic. Until now the best "next subject" after Seasons Part 1 was Part 2 or Part 3, because they share the planet and rank first. That made the two moves say the same thing.

**Amends** D-034 (the list of hard exclusions on a subject move).

- **Rule.** A vertical move excludes every candidate of the source's series, in any revision of that series (`same_series`). The series is compared by its id only. With nothing else to offer the move is exhausted (`no_eligible_content`), never Part 2.
- **A Reel is not a series unit, but it is still a part.** Only Scrolls have a `series_units` row. So the series comes from the source, or else from a candidate that shares the source's explanation unit (its Scroll twin), and a candidate whose explanation unit is that of a part is excluded too. The Reel of Part 2 is Part 2, from a Scroll and from a Reel. This looks only at the candidates already retrieved, so it adds no read.
- **Where.** Composer's pure policy decides, in `domain/vertical-rules.ts`. Experience names the source's series in the snapshot (`intent.currentSeriesId`) for a fresh ranking, and the worker does the same for a buffer. The reason is stored in `composer.decisions` beside the other exclusions.
- **Unchanged.** Series, related, variant and return moves. The Part move is how a series is reached.
- **A move inside a region is left alone.** A person who opened a place to browse it can reach its parts there. Only the open-ended "next subject" leaves the series.
- **Not done.** The related move of the thread is left to D-136. If the Scroll twin of a Reel is not among the candidates (a catalogue past the retrieval cap), the series is not known.
- **The policy label stays `composer-policy.v0`.** Migration 0017 allows only that label, and the rule applies only when the snapshot names a series, so every stored decision replays as before. S-5 changes the weights and moves the label with a migration.
- **Verified.** Pure and property tests (a ranked-first Part 2 is never returned, for any seed, order or format) and integration tests on real PostgreSQL from Seasons Part 1, with the Reel of Part 2 as the only other format, and from the Reel of Part 1. Each fails when the rule is removed.

## D-132 · 2026-10-30 · Next stays in the format the person is in, and falls back only when none is left (S-1d, #472)

A person reading a Scroll who pulls up for a new topic expects a Scroll; a person watching a Reel expects a Reel. Today a vertical move carries no format, so a Scroll can be followed by a Reel.

**Amends** D-034 (what a buffer is ranked for).

- **Contract.** The vertical navigation intent has an optional `format` (`reel` or `scroll`). The client fills it with the format of the encounter in front of the person. A request without it is ranked with no format preference, as before.
- **Rule.** Composer reads that format first. While anything of it is eligible, every candidate of the other format is excluded (`format_sticky`). Eligible means after every other exclusion, so a hidden item or another part of the series does not keep the format alive. When none is eligible nothing is excluded and the other format is offered.
- **Recorded.** The decision stores `stickyFormat: { preferred, outcome }`, with `outcome` either `kept` or `fell_back`.
- **Equal status.** The rule follows the format the person is in now. It is the same for Reel and Scroll and stores nothing. The per-topic format learning (`format` feature) is unchanged.
- **Retrieval.** With a format, up to 100 candidates of that format are read first and the other format fills what is left, so a large catalogue of one format cannot crowd the person's format out of the cap.
- **Buffers.** The worker ranks a buffer for the source's own format. Experience serves it only to a move that names that format. A move that names another format, or none, is ranked fresh and the buffer waits for its own end of life.
- **Not here.** Anything finer than the format. Play level is S-5 (#476).
- **Verified.** Pure and property tests (stay in the format exactly when something of it is eligible; the same inputs give the same decision), an integration test on real PostgreSQL from a Scroll and from a Reel, with and without an eligible candidate of the format, and the buffer cases. Each fails when the rule is removed.

## D-136 · 2026-10-30 · A vertical move does not offer what the thread's Related tab reaches (S-1d, #472)

After D-131 the Seasons thread still led to "Seasons limits": it shares the planet, so it ranks first. It is the related move of Part 3 (D-125), another perspective on the thread, and a related perspective is one of the four distinct relationships. It is reached from its own tab, not by pulling up.

**Amends** D-131 (which left the related target out).

- **Rule.** A vertical move excludes the assets the thread's Related tab reaches (`related_target`), by asset id in any revision, and the format twin of each. These are the related targets of the source, of its format twins and of every other part of the source's series found among the candidates. The tab sits on one part (Part 3 for Seasons), but the thread owns it, so Part 1 leaves it too.
- **A Reel.** The continuity of the Reel and of its Scroll twin counts, and the Reel takes the series of its twin among the candidates (D-131), so the related move of any part of that series is left too.
- **Steps aside rather than leave nothing.** If excluding would leave nothing eligible in either format, none are excluded and the move offers what is there, as it did before. The series rule stays hard (D-131). The decision stores `relatedTarget: { outcome, matched }`, with `outcome` either `applied` or `fell_back`.
- **Before sticky format.** When a new topic and the person's format cannot both be had, the new topic wins: the related target in the same format is passed over and the other format is offered (D-132 records `fell_back`).
- **Where.** The pure policy decides (`applyRelatedExclusion`). `readRelatedAssetIds` reads the admitted links, a few small reads of shared data inside the transaction already open; Experience and the buffer worker both use it. A move inside a region takes no part in it.
- **Unchanged.** The Related tab and the `related` move still open the target.
- **Verified.** Pure and property tests, a unit test of the reads, and an integration test on real PostgreSQL from Seasons Part 1 and Part 3, with the fall back and the Related tab. Each fails when the rule or the read of the other parts is removed.

## D-104 · 2026-10-30 · A scheduled sweep settles quiet places by rules, with no model (v3 U-3, #467)

D-079 decided the sweep. This records how it works, so that a place goes quiet 30 days after the last reading whether or not the person comes back.

**Amends** D-038 and D-052 ("activity changes alone apply by the rules" only inside a reading-triggered run).

- **Stored time.** `geography.state.next_quiet_at` is the earliest the sweep needs to look at a universe: when the newest support under an active place turns 30 days old, or now when a change is already due. It is scheduling state, never evidence, and a lower bound for later reading, which only moves the true time forward. Every Cartographer apply rewrites it from the places as they are after the delta. A sweep pass that finds nothing due stores the corrected time. Support that is withdrawn, corrected or retracted can move the true time earlier; the place then goes quiet at the stored time or at the next apply, whichever comes first. Null means no place can go quiet.
- **The rule is shared.** `activityChanges` (`domain/quiet.ts`) is the Cartographer's own rule, moved out of `requiredChanges`. A place is quiet when its newest support is more than 30 days old (`quietAfterDays` is unchanged), active otherwise, and keeps its state with no support. The sweep and a reading-triggered run call the same function, so they cannot disagree.
- **Two jobs.** `geography.sweep-quiet` runs every 15 minutes (pg-boss cron, worker). It makes one indexed query for the oldest 100 states whose time has passed, and enqueues one `geography.settle-quiet` for each that can be settled, in the same transaction. It reads no support. A pass reads at most 100 rows however many universes are refused (D-105). The settle enters the personal scope as a system actor, rechecks the account, epoch and recording generation, reads current support, and applies only the activity changes. No model call, no ledger run, no network wait.
- **No repeat work.** Handing a universe over pushes its `next_quiet_at` 30 minutes ahead (a lease), so the next pass does not queue it again while its settle waits. The settle rewrites the time. If the job is lost, the sweep finds the universe when the lease ends. A second delivery of a settle finds nothing to change.
- **At most one delta of 32.** A person with more than 32 places going quiet at once settles over the following passes; the settle leaves the time at now while any change is still due.
- **A delta without a run.** `geography.deltas.run_id` is now nullable. A quiet settle writes the same immutable delta, revision stamps, scene projection job and publish job as any apply, with no Cartographer run behind it. Only `activityRevision` moves, so Scenes redraw activity and nothing else, and a settle with nothing due moves no revision and no personal revision.
- **Existence never changes.** The ids, labels and origin transitions of the places are untouched (AGENTS: inactivity changes activity, never existence). A new encounter after quiet behaves as it did: the next Cartographer run sets the places it supports active again.
- **Unchanged.** "Everything is where you left it" stays on the device (`seenAt`, D-079).
- **Existing worlds.** The migration sets `next_quiet_at` to now for every state that has places, so the first passes read each one's support and store its true time.
- **Verified.** Pure tests of the rule and the time. An integration test on real PostgreSQL ages a world 31 days with no reading and sees every place quiet, with the same ids, labels and origins, one delta, only the activity stamps moved, a projection job, and nothing added to the reasoning ledger. It fails when the sweep hands nothing over.

## D-105 · 2026-10-30 · When the quiet sweep stands aside (v3 U-3, #467)

D-104 says what the sweep does. This says when it does nothing, so that it cannot change what a person has not allowed, and cannot void a Cartographer run.

- **Recording paused.** A universe with recording off is not handed over, and a settle that was already queued writes nothing (`fenceRecording` against the generation the sweep saw). Pause means geography does not change, as it does for Cartographer runs. The sweep touches only its own `next_quiet_at` (see Parked). The places settle within a day of recording being on again, under the new generation.
- **Paused time counts.** The 30 days run on the calendar. While recording is paused the server keeps no record of reading (D-057, D-083), so a person who reads on the device during a pause has no new support, and a place whose last recorded support is over 30 days old is quiet once recording is on again. Pausing neither stops nor extends the window.
- **Parked.** A due universe the sweep cannot settle now (paused, a run holds it, a Clear in progress, account not active) moves its `next_quiet_at` a day ahead, in the same pass that looked at it. Without that, refused universes would stay at the head of the index and each pass would walk them all before it found one it could settle. Every row a pass looks at leaves the head, so a pass costs at most 100 rows, and a refused universe is looked at again a day later. A run's apply rewrites the time sooner.
- **Clear and deletion.** Clear and account deletion remove the state row, so there is nothing due. A queued settle is erased with the universe's other geography jobs, and a payload from an earlier epoch is refused by the scope.
- **A run holds the universe.** A Cartographer run freezes its context at the current revision, and a delta committed under it would void its answer. The sweep parks a universe with an active run and a settle stands aside (`run_active`). The run applies the same activity rule itself, and its apply rewrites the time.
- **The role is off.** Both jobs follow `KS_CARTOGRAPHER_ENABLED`, the one switch for geography. With it off, nothing is handed over and a settle does nothing, as a run does. No new setting.
- **Support unreadable.** If current support cannot be read, no place changes. The settle stores `next_quiet_at` a day ahead, so one universe does not occupy every pass.
- **Lock order.** The sweep takes no personal root. The settle takes the root first, then the owned rows, as a Cartographer run does, so the two serialise on the root and never wait on each other in the other order.
- **Verified.** Integration tests on real PostgreSQL: a paused universe is left exactly as it was and settles after resume, the old generation's payload is refused, a universe with a pending run is parked and the run still applies, refused universes at the head of the index are parked so the next pass reaches one that can be settled, a lease keeps a universe from being handed over twice, a settle waits for a held root and then applies once, nothing happens with the role off, and queued settles are erased with the universe.

## D-162 · 2026-10-30 · Residents are paid by a room scope and an `ambient` budget class (R-2, #488)

**Amends** D-051, "every inhabitant call runs as the person who asked", and the reasoning README, "the nonpersonal scope is Scroll supply". **Builds on** D-058 and D-068.

- **A second nonpersonal root.** `reasoning.production_scopes` gains `rooms` beside `scroll-supply`. It is the same engine, tables and attempt ledger; a harness is bound to one root and refuses the other's authority. It has no account, no universe and no epoch.
- **Its own switch and caps.** `KS_REASONING_ROOM_ENABLED` (default 0), `KS_REASONING_ROOM_GENERATION` and the daily caps `KS_REASONING_ROOM_REQUESTS_PER_DAY` / `KS_REASONING_ROOM_TOKENS_PER_DAY` (defaults 100 and 1,000,000). The root row is enabled by the migration; the environment switch is the gate, and an operator can still disable the row or raise its generation to fence queued work.
- **A third budget class.** A role may be `ambient`: it speaks because a room's resident chose to, not because anyone asked or read. An ambient reservation must fit the room scope's cap and also `KS_REASONING_AMBIENT_SHARE_PERCENT` (default 30) of the global cap, counted over ambient receipts only (`usage_receipts.ambient`). It can never take what a person asked for. The resident roles have their own ids (`rooms.ambient-explainer`, `rooms.ambient-skeptic`), so the role cap and the failure brake of asked turns are not shared with them.
- **Asked turns do not change.** They stay `interactive` on the asker's personal scope. When the room scope or the ambient share is spent, an ask still works.
- **What it keeps.** The model call's request bytes are kept 48 hours in `production_requests`, like every request. They hold the room's recent messages after keep-out, with no names. They belong to no person, so no one's Clear or deletion erases them; they expire.

Confirmed by: `budgets.test.ts` (the share), `reasoning-ambient-share.test.ts` (exactly the share is admitted, also under concurrency; the next is refused as `ambient_global`; a person's ask is still admitted; the test fails if either ledger's ambient sum is broken), `rooms-residents.test.ts` ("lets asked turns work when the room scope is spent").

## D-163 · 2026-10-30 · A room's cast is a table of named residents (R-2, #488)

**Amends** D-051, where a room has `inhabitants text[]` of at most two, and the contract's `max(2)`. **Builds on** D-068 and T3 question 10 (2 or 3 residents per room), which wins over R3's four.

- **The table.** `rooms.room_cast(room_id, inhabitant, name, position)`: one row per resident, positions 1 to 4, one row per role. A room has an Explainer and a Skeptic always, so two rows at least. The migration gives every existing room the residents its old array named. The old column is no longer read; a later migration drops it.
- **Names.** From a fixed catalogue, never from a model: Nova (Explainer) and Halley (Skeptic), the names T3 gives them. Every room has the same pair until a room gets a face of its own (R-4), which changes rows, not code.
- **Always labelled.** The contract's AI author carries the resident's `name` beside the label that cannot be removed (`AI · Explainer`). A resident never has a person ID, handle or profile, and is never counted among the people here.
- **Not yet.** The Quizmaster (Pixel) comes with the Launch in R-6, with its behaviour. The role enum and the table's check stay at two roles until then; the table's shape already holds four.

Confirmed by: `cast.test.ts`, `rooms-residents.test.ts` ("gives every seeded room a named Explainer and Skeptic"), `rooms-residents-migration.test.ts` (existing rooms get their residents).

## D-164 · 2026-10-30 · The router decides who speaks, and every turn is a ledger row (R-2, #488)

**Implements** D-081. **Amends** D-051, "AI inhabitants speak only when asked", and the migration 0062 rule that an AI message always has an asker. **Replaces** D-051's 4 asks per person and 12 per room a day with D-068's 10 per person an hour and 80 per room a day.

- **Triggers.** A person's text that reads as a question (a `?` at the end, or an opening interrogative of five words or more) asks the Explainer. A text with a strong-claim word (proven, always, obviously, 100%, ...) asks the Skeptic. Both are lexical pre-filters in `rooms/domain/triggers.ts`; the resident's own `nothing_to_add` is the real judgement. An Explainer answer that itself reads as overstated may draw one Skeptic reply, then nothing until a person speaks.
- **Waits.** People answer first: 4 minutes for a question, 2 for a claim, 90 seconds when only the author has the room open, 1 minute for the one reply. The wait is measured from the message and checked again at fire time against who is here then. A trigger older than 30 minutes is let go.
- **Let go without a record.** A person other than the author, or an AI, posted after the message. The room is paused, the author left, the author keeps messages out of what AI reads (checked again just before the call), every member has the AI Off, or the message is a newcomer's first and would draw the Skeptic.
- **Caps** (D-068; start here, tune after two weeks of ledger data). One unprompted turn per 3 human messages since the last one (not counted for the Skeptic's one reply), 8 an hour and 40 a day per room, the Explainer 4 an hour, the Skeptic 3 an hour and 1 per person an hour. A `nothing_to_add` and a call that failed after it reached the provider count for the hour and day, never for the ratio. A call that failed before it reached the provider is handed back. Once a call has reached the provider the turn stays counted whatever happens next: `dispatched` is only ever raised, never lowered, and a redelivery that finds its request changed (a person answered, the author followed up) counts as sent.
- **A refusal is a row, not a message.** Every decision is an `ai_turns` row, written under the room lock: allowed (`queued`, `caps_clear`) or refused (`refused`, naming the cap), with the counts it compared. A refusal posts nothing, shows nothing and queues no call. Each message and trigger gets at most one row (a unique index).
- **Who it runs as.** `rooms.cast-due` and `rooms.cast-pass` act as a system actor for the universe of the person whose message set them off, only to take the room lock in the blueprint's order and to stop when that person is gone (a deletion or a privacy reset ends the check). The model call is paid by the room scope (D-162).
- **What a reader sees.** Lively shows a resident's unprompted message, Quieter marks it `folded` (one line, the words on request), Off receives a `muted` marker with no words. The one-line why is a fixed phrase for the trigger the server names, never model text. An asked contribution always shows in full.
- **Asks are not blocked by residents.** A resident preparing an unprompted answer is not "an ask in progress": the gate that shows an inhabitant as preparing, and refuses a second ask, counts queued asks only.
- **Idle.** A message that is neither a question nor a strong claim queues nothing. An idle room makes no call.

Confirmed by: `router.test.ts`, `triggers.test.ts`, `rooms-residents.test.ts` (idle, the wait, a human answer, the Skeptic and the newcomer, the reply chain, the ratio and hour and day caps, levels, kept-out bytes, a quiet refusal, the room scope spent, a paid answer that stays counted, keep-out turned on before the call, an ask accepted while a resident prepares).

## D-165 · 2026-10-30 · Typing is a ten-second hold on a discussion, with no person in it (R-2, #488)

**Amends** D-067: the typing signal is held ten seconds in an unlogged row with no account. D-067 says it is never stored; this keeps a discussion and a time for ten seconds and nothing else. Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462.

- **What the client says.** While a member types in a discussion, the device sends `room_typing {roomId, discussionId}` over the hint socket at most every 4 seconds. Nothing is sent when the draft is empty.
- **What the server keeps.** One row in `rooms.typing_holds (discussion_id, held_until)`, rewritten in place to ten seconds ahead. The table is unlogged, holds no account and no text, is read by no API route and is never sent to anyone. A hint from a non-member, with a bad body or a stale session writes nothing, and the answer is the same `{ ok: true }` in every case, so it tells no one anything.
- **What it does.** `cast-due` looks again after the hold lapses instead of deciding (at most 24 times). `cast-pass` puts posting off until the hold lapses (at most 3 times), then posts. A person's new message while a model call runs already ends the turn without posting.
- **A paid answer is kept, not asked for again.** When posting is put off, the answer is stored on the turn (`held_kind`, `held_text`) and the turn is marked as having reached the provider in the same transaction. The next delivery posts the held words after the usual checks, without building the context or calling the model, so a person's answer or the author's follow-up in between cannot make the ledger refuse a changed request. If the words are dropped (someone answered, the author keeps messages out), the turn ends `failed` and still counts. The held words are cleared when the turn settles.
- **Deletion and Clear.** A queued unprompted turn whose job is erased with a universe is settled `failed` and counted in the same transaction, so it neither blocks a discussion nor waits for a call nobody will make.
- **What stays.** D-042: no one is ever shown that someone is typing.

Confirmed by: `rooms-residents.test.ts` ("holds back a turn while someone types", "stores a discussion and a time, never a person", "an answer that was paid for"), `rooms-erasure.test.ts` (Clear and account deletion end a queued resident turn), `account-scope-socket.test.ts`.

## D-167 · 2026-10-31 · A pointed ask or an @name is an asked turn of its own kind (R-3, #489)

**Builds on** D-068 and D-164. **Amends** the rooms rule that an ask names a resident: a member may also point an ask at one message.

- **Five kinds.** `ask` (the plain ask of a discussion), `challenge` (the Skeptic tests one message), `other_side` (the Skeptic argues the opposite), `about` (the Explainer answers it) and `mention` (an @Nova or @Halley in a message; the first name in the text is the one asked, once). The kind decides the resident, so a pointed ask sends no resident name.
- **The ledger names it.** An asked turn keeps `trigger = asked`, its kind in `ask_kind` and the message it is about in `cause_message_id` (none for a plain ask). A database check holds that every asked turn has a kind and every other turn a cause. The old one-turn-per-message index now covers unprompted turns only, so several members can challenge one message; a message that names a resident asks once.
- **Never against the unprompted caps.** The caps count unprompted rows only, so no kind of ask uses up the 1-in-3 ratio, the hour, the day, a resident's hour or Halley's one-per-person. The asked bounds of D-068 count every kind together: 10 asks per person per hour, 80 per room per day, 20 requests of any outcome per person per hour, one reply prepared per discussion at a time.
- **What a pointed ask may name.** A message of that discussion that is shown to the asker, written by someone the asker has not blocked. Otherwise the answer is the same `unavailable` as for a stranger. A resident's own answer can be challenged.
- **A kept-out author's message is accepted and ends without a call.** The asker is not told why, so a member's keep-out choice is not revealed. The turn is handed back (`dispatched` stays false). The same happens when the message was removed after the ask. The model never reads the words.
- **An old message is still answered.** The context holds the newest 20 messages of the discussion. The message a turn is about (a pointed ask, or the message that named a resident) is read by its ID when it is older than that, and goes through the same reducer as the rest, so a removal or a keep-out applies to it. The same holds when more than a window of talk lands before the job runs.
- **A mention never fails the message.** The text always posts. The ask is made only when the bounds allow; the room read says when a resident is resting. A message that names a resident is addressed to it, so the router does not also decide whether to answer it. A send whose text holds an @ takes the room lock FOR UPDATE, so the bounds stay exact.
- **What a reader sees.** The answer carries the kind, who asked (left out when the reader blocked them, or their account is gone) and the message it is about. The screen says "Asked by Lena · The other side" from fixed words, never from model text. An asked answer always shows in full, at any AI level.
- **Limits are visible.** The member's view of a room carries `limits`, built from the same constants the bounds and the router use. The room menu's "AI here" states them.
- **Role.** `inhabitant.2` adds one fixed line per kind to the instructions. The role ids and the personal, interactive budget are as before.

Confirmed by: `rooms-asks.test.ts` ("a question about one message" including the message older than the newest twenty, "naming a resident in a message", "asked turns and the unprompted caps"), `asks.test.ts`, `room-messages.test.tsx`, `asks-and-topics.test.tsx`, `rooms-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-168 · 2026-10-31 · Topics begin from a message, and Useful is one mark that is never your own (R-3, #489)

**Builds on** D-051 and D-069. The tag list and the ordering are choices the designs leave open (R3 B2).

- **A topic may begin from any message with words.** The message is the topic's first item: it is read through `origin_message_id`, so a removal or a block applies to it as it does where it was said, and an AI asked in the topic reads it first. The message stays where it was. A card message (`topic_discussion_id`, no words of its own) is left there, authored by the person who started the topic. Its figures come from the room's list, so it keeps up without a write. A Reel or Scroll card still starts a content thread, as before.
- **The title.** The first words of the message, which the starter may edit (at most 80 characters). A title left as the message's own words (or given as exactly those words) is flagged `title_derived`: it is another person's words. It is shown to a reader only while that message is shown to them. If the message was removed, its author's account is gone, or the reader blocked the author, the reader sees "A topic from a message" instead. Restoring the message restores the title. When the author deletes their account the stored title is rewritten to the neutral one, and another person's file never holds those words (the export says the same neutral title). A title the starter changed is the starter's own and is always shown. Every place that serves a title does this: the list, the room view, the topic card, the topic's header, the pinned title of a preview and the moderator's reports. For AI it is withheld when the starter keeps messages out, when the author of the message it began from does, and when that message was removed.
- **Tags are a closed list of four,** Question, Debate, Explainer and Share, and optional. The room's own topic is the room, so it is not a tag. Debate is a label until R-5 gives it a vote and a close date.
- **The list.** Pinned topics lead. Then Hot, New (by when the topic began) or Unanswered (no person but the starter has spoken). Hot is a code formula, `(replies + 2 × useful + 1) / (hours since the last message + 2)^1.5`; no model and no score on screen. Ties fall to the newest activity and then the ID.
- **Counts are on an allow-list.** A reply count sits on a discussion's summary (a card reads it) and a Useful count only on a topic of the list (D-069). So do who spoke last (`lastReplyBy`, left out for a blocked person) and whether anyone but the starter has spoken (`answered`); the contract walk now finds both by name. A reply count is the discussion's message counter less its removed messages and its topic cards, read from two small partial indexes, so a room read does not count every message of General.
- **Useful is one mark per member per message.** The same command again changes nothing; taking it back removes it; there is no negative mark. A member cannot mark their own message (`invalid_input`), a message that is not shown to them, or one whose author they blocked. A resident's answer can be marked, since the asker has no other way to say it helped.
- **Nobody is told who marked what.** A message carries only `usefulByYou`, to the person who marked it. An author sees no mark and no count. A mark on a removed message is not counted.
- **Erasure.** Account deletion deletes the person's marks and the marks on their messages. A Clear keeps them, as it keeps votes. The export lists the room and the time of each mark, never whose message it was.
- **Not evidence.** A mark is conversation. R-5's star rule must still count a human answer marked by someone other than its author, and never an AI answer.

Confirmed by: `rooms-asks.test.ts` ("a topic from any message" including the neutral title for a removed, blocked or deleted message, and the reply count; "Useful"), `topics.test.ts`, `useful.test.ts`, `titles.test.ts`, `rooms-erasure.test.ts` ("deletes a person's marks of Useful", "renames a topic that was titled from a deleted person's message"), the contract's allow-list test.

## D-169 · 2026-10-31 · The welcome is a template, and "while you were away" is Halley's, one call per 12-hour window (R-3, #489)

**Builds on** D-161, D-162 and T3 question 15. R3 gives these to the Keeper; T3 gives the room two residents, so the Explainer welcomes and the Skeptic catches people up.

- **The welcome** is a template filled from the room's name and the meaning of its topic. It is Nova's, labelled, and part of the member's own room read until they have read the AI notice. It makes no model call and writes no row.
- **When a return is owed a catch-up.** The member's device was last open at least 12 hours ago, at least 15 messages by other people or residents were shown meanwhile, the room has Halley and takes posts, and the member's AI level is not Off. Time and level are checked first, so the count of new messages is a query only for a long absence. A member who never opened the room is owed none. The numbers are R3's, kept as constants beside the router's caps to tune after two weeks of data.
- **One call per window per room.** A window is a 12-hour UTC block. The first member who qualifies in it claims it, and a unique key makes that exact under concurrency: their presence beat queues the one `rooms.summarise` job in the same transaction. Everyone else who returns in the window shares the same words. That is at most two calls per room per day, however many people return.
- **What it covers.** The room's newest 60 shown messages from every discussion, from the time the first member left (never more than a day back) to when it was claimed. The words are two to four short lines, at most 900 characters, with no names and no speaker numbers (the schema refuses "Member 2").
- **The same builder, with kept-out members left out.** It uses the context builder of D-161. A kept-out member's messages are dropped, not shown as placeholders, so nothing says that something was said. Removed messages are absent. With fewer than five readable messages left there is no call.
- **No topic title is read.** Each message carries the place it was said in: "General" or "a topic". A title is someone's words, and a cached summary can outlive the message a title was made from (a removal, a block, a deleted account), so the summary never reads one. The cost is that two topics in one window are not told apart by name.
- **Paid by the room scope** as the `ambient` role `rooms.ambient-away`, and needs `KS_REASONING_ROOM_ENABLED`. It has its own ledger row in `rooms.summaries` (trigger `member_returned`, decided by `window_open`, with role, model and attempt) and writes no `ai_turns` row, so it never counts against the unprompted caps. A nothing-to-add answer, a failure or a refusal shows nothing, and the window is not tried again.
- **Stale means erased.** A message it read removed by a moderator, a member who wrote in it turning keep-out on, or an account that wrote in it deleted: its words are erased, and a summary still being written is never stored. Only the messages it read matter, because it reads nothing else. The window is not rewritten. A summary may paraphrase a blocked person's words to the blocker; it names no one and quotes no one.
- **Where the member left off** is `rooms.read_positions`: one private row per member per room, moved by their own presence beats, never sent to anyone. It goes when they leave, are removed or delete their account. A Clear keeps it, like the lease.
- **What the member sees.** A card at the top of General from Halley: "While you were away, since 22:10", the words, and "Got it". While it is being written it says so. It is shown for a day after it was written, and not after. A Quieter member sees one line and opens it. A member whose AI is Off sees none and costs nothing.
- **Not built:** the design's "Catch me up in order" and "Jump to the answer", and the Keeper's topic-spin nudge (B3), which needs a message only two people see.

Confirmed by: `rooms-away.test.ts` (one call per window under concurrency and in the next window, kept-out and removed text absent from the request, erased on removal, keep-out and deletion, nothing queued when residents are off), `rooms-asks.test.ts` ("the welcome": no call, no row), `rooms-erasure.test.ts` (a queued summary ends with its job, positions and summaries through a Clear and a deletion), `away.test.ts`.

## D-183 · 2026-10-31 · On the web, a pull to a new topic is judged from the page at the touch (S-1c follow-up, #472)

**Amends** D-134 ("Vertical reading never navigates": a pull counts when it starts at the end).

- **The race.** The reader keeps its own record of the scroll position from scroll reports. On the web a report is throttled and the last one comes only when scrolling stops, so for a moment after the text reaches its end the record still says it is not there. A pull that began in that moment was read as ordinary reading and asked for nothing.
- **The rule.** When a finger lands on the text, the web asks the scrolling element under it where it is now (at its end within 4 px, and only if the text scrolls at all). The same rule, `scrolledToEnd`, serves the reader's own record. A pull that begins in the middle of the text is still reading.
- **Native** is unchanged: the pull is the scroll view's own `onScrollEndDrag`, which carries live values.

Confirmed by: `current-stop.test.ts` ("whether the text is scrolled to its end"), `stage-reader-smoke.spec.ts` ("a pull that starts at the end is a new topic even when the scroll report is late"; "a pull up past the end is a new topic; the same pull in the middle of the text is reading"). Shown failing with the touch reading the reader's record: the late-report test failed 10 of 10.

## D-180 · 2026-10-30 · Play is its own module, behind a wall in both directions (S-2, #473)

**Builds on** D-063, D-064 and D-082.

- **The module.** `modules/play` owns the calls a person locks and what they earned: `play.calls` (one row per person, Scroll revision and call stop) and `play.scroll_results` (calls locked, sparks, combo). It is in the erasure registry and the export. Sparks and combo belong to one Scroll revision; no schema sums them across Scrolls or ranks people (D-064).
- **The wall is two allow-lists, not a list of the modules to fear.** `play-wall-only-roots-import-play`: only `play` itself, the apps' composition roots, tests and the erasure registry may import `modules/play/`. `play-wall-play-imports-three-public-surfaces`: `play` may import only the `public.ts` of `identity`, `content` and `experience`, plus infrastructure and contracts. A deny-list of `composer` (with Jev), `planner`, `evidence`, `personal-context`, `geography`, `scenes` and the reasoning engine would miss every other module and every later one, and a path through one that is not named: `planner` imports `supply`, so `planner` to `supply` to `play` would pass. The allow-lists close all of those. A third check, `play/wall.test.ts`, stops any production file outside `modules/play/` naming `play.calls` or `play.scroll_results`, so no module can read a pick straight from the tables either.
- **What still counts as engagement.** Only that a call was played. Evidence takes the value-free interaction kind it already accepts; no kind, field or table carries the option picked, correctness, sparks or combo.
- **AGENTS.** The "scores" wording D-064 asked for is already in AGENTS.md (internal ranking, model or evidence scores; game points are play features), so sparks in the dock break no rule.
- **Not built.** The Final call, landmarks, the Orbit and the Universe overlay are S-3 and S-4. The Final call stays on the device without a spark.

Confirmed by: `tests/integration/modules/play.test.ts` (two people read the same Scroll, one calling right and one wrong: the same qualification, activity, Personal Context contribution and the same places formed; a different reading is not equal, so the comparison can fail), `pnpm boundaries:probe`, which writes one forbidden import from each of the seven walled modules (composer, jev, planner, evidence, personal-context, geography, scenes), the reasoning engine, `supply`, `social` and `rooms` into play, and one from play into each of the same eleven public surfaces (22 probes), then runs the same files again with the wall's rules loosened, and again loosened for `supply` alone: a probe that a loosened rule still rejects fails the run, and loosening one module frees only that module's probe. Shown failing by loosening each side: the run named the freed `supply` importer and the freed `geography` surface. Also `play/wall.test.ts` (the SQL scan finds play's own queries and a planted mention), `erasure.test.ts`.

## D-181 · 2026-10-30 · A lock is judged on the server, once, from the stored revision (S-2, #473)

- **The command.** `call.lock` carries the asset, the revision, the stop, the option and the recording generation. It never carries correctness. The server reads the call from the immutable revision (`content` `READ_SCROLL_CALLS`) and decides.
- **Who may lock.** Only a person with an activated exposure of that exact revision (`experience` `HAS_ACTIVATED_EXPOSURE`), and only a Call it of it. A revision, call or exposure the person does not have is one `NOT_FOUND`; an option the call does not have is `UNPROCESSABLE_REQUEST`. Nothing is stored for a refusal.
- **Idempotent, and the first lock stands.** An identical retry answers the stored lock with today's results. Another command locking the same call changes nothing and answers the first lock with `newlyLocked: false`. A row is never updated.
- **Sparks and combo.** One spark for each right call. The combo is the run of right calls in the order locked; a wrong call ends it and costs nothing. The device works the same rule out at once (`scroll-model` `tallyOf`), the server's answer wins, and a test checks the two agree.
- **Reading evidence is unchanged.** The reader still reports `revealed` once per call, by kind. The lock and the reveal are one tap and either counts as reading (D-128), so evidence has nothing new to learn.
- **The reveal.** A right pick shows "Called it!" and "+1 spark" (and "Combo ×n" from two in a row). A wrong pick shows "Plot twist!", what was called, the warm second look and, when it is the common guess, its reviewed source. Once the answer is shown only the option called and the right one stay, and the reader scrolls the reveal into view.

Confirmed by: `play.test.ts` (right and wrong pick, retry, a second lock, forged option, call and exposure refusals, Final call refused), `lock-call.test.ts`, `play.test.tsx`, `play-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-182 · 2026-10-30 · Paused recording, Clear and export for play (S-2, #473)

**Builds on** D-057, D-082 and D-083.

- **Paused.** The server stores nothing while recording is paused or the client's generation is stale: no row, no receipt, and an answer (`recorded: false`, a reason and the current generation) that says nothing about the call. A client whose policy says paused sends nothing at all. The screen says "Not saved while recording is paused." before the lock and in the reveal. Reading what was saved before the pause still works.
- **After resuming.** While paused the dock counts what the device just judged. When recording is on again the dock shows the server's numbers: a lock made while paused was never stored and, as D-083 says, does not appear later, so it is left out of the sparks and the combo (a combo of two is never shown over a lock the server did not hold). Its own reveal still says it was not saved.
- **On the device.** Play lives in memory for the account scope: it survives moving between Parts, not a reload, and sign-out, an account switch, a privacy reset and Clear end it. It is never written to the scope's storage, so a reload while paused starts clean, as "saves nothing" says.
- **Not reached.** A lock that the network did not carry keeps its command ID and is sent again after 1, 5 and 30 seconds, in the order made, while the app is open. The reveal says "Will save when you're back online." A lock the server refuses says "Couldn't save this one." and is not sent again.
- **Clear and deletion.** Both tables are universe-keyed and are erased. Play is third in the erasure order (after rooms and feedback), because nothing refers to its rows.
- **Export.** `play.scrolls`: per Scroll its title (if still reachable), sparks, combo and, for each call, the question, the option picked in words, whether it was right and when. No identifier and not the right answer.

Confirmed by: `play.test.ts` (paused stores nothing and writes no receipt, a stale generation is told to read the policy again, Clear erases, export in words with no identifier), `play-session.test.ts`, `erasure.test.ts`, `privacy-clear.test.ts`, `data-export.test.ts`, and the paused case of `play-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-108 · 2026-10-31 · A planet's world is laid out by `worldmap.v1` in `scene-core`, with pinned maths, and drawn as SVG (v3 U-4, #468)

D-078 said the generators live in `scene-core` with pinned maths and a generator version. This is how, and what the globe and the map are drawn from.

- **The inputs.** `buildGlobe(spec)` and `buildMap(spec)` take the planet's seed, its type, its look `variant` and its `siteCount`. Which regions have formed is not an input, so a cell keeps its shape and its name's place when a region forms. The globe (a 480-unit band that wraps) and the map (390 by 844 units) are two drawings of one planet: they use the same sites in the same order, with different noise.
- **How the world is cut.** Continents are placed from the seed. Each site's cell grows from a seed point on its continent, so a cell is the land nearest that point. Decorative continents have a point of their own and belong to no site. Region borders wander by noise. Coasts and outlines are marching squares, joined into loops and rounded off. A name's place is the middle of its cell, kept apart from every other name by the worst-case name width, not by the names themselves, and on the globe always inside the band.
- **Pinned maths.** Only add, subtract, multiply, divide, `Math.floor`, `Math.round`, `Math.sqrt` (correctly rounded) and `Math.imul` are used. Streams are FNV and Mulberry32 on integers. Noise is gradient noise on a seeded permutation, and wraps by whole lattice cells, so there is no `Math.sin` or `Math.cos` and no seam. Turning round a continent uses the 64-sample unit circle that `silhouette.v1` already pins (`unit-circle.ts`). A unit test refuses `Math.sin`, `Math.cos`, `Math.pow`, `Math.hypot`, `Math.log`, `**`, clocks and randomness in the generator's sources, and pins the numbers of three planets.
- **Land at every site.** Noise can leave a cell with no land or a speck. A cell, or a decorative continent, with less than 150 square units of land is given a rounded island of 12 to 15 units radius on its seed point (`island.ts`, from the pinned unit circle and a stream of its own), with its shallows, and the island joins the coast. A name on such an island stays put and the other name of a pair moves out of its way. This uses only the planet's own inputs, so it is the same for every formed set. A test builds 2,400 planets of 1 to 32 sites in both drawings and requires more than the floor of land in every cell.
- **The version.** The generator is `worldmap.v1`, which every look already names. Changing a constant, a size, a channel or the order of a draw is a new generator version, not an edit, as for `silhouette.v1`.
- **The cost.** A world is built after the screen's first frame and kept by the session (six at most), so it goes with the account scope. A world is 14,000 to 17,000 grid nodes and takes about 25 ms in V8 on a laptop.
- **Drawn as SVG.** The globe is the design's land band turning behind a circle (70 s a turn, the clouds 46 s, the moon about 56 s; a drag turns it; reduced motion stands still), not a textured sphere. D-073 listed "a planet" among the three.js views; the design draws U03 as SVG, like the map, so this **amends D-073** for the planet globe. The ring and the moon come from the look. No blur filter is used (`react-native-svg` filters are not driven on a device), so soft edges are layered flat shapes.
- **What is not known.** Nothing was run on Hermes or a phone. The geometry is arithmetic that every engine does exactly, so the loops should be the same numbers there, and the test pins them in V8; the drawing within one pixel on a device is not verified. Time to build a world on Hermes is not measured.
- **Removed.** The seed-picked lighthouse and harbour and the region pins of D-039. Landmarks come back as earned markers (D-089).

**Amends** D-073 (the planet globe is SVG) and D-039 ("Region pins ignore anchors", the pin layout, `composeRegions`). Confirmed by: `packages/scene-core/src/world/world.test.ts` (pinned digests and literal coordinates, no banned maths, every site has a cell, names apart, refusals), `planet-plan.test.ts`, `planet-views.test.tsx`, `e2e/universe-planet-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-109 · 2026-10-31 · Land not reached yet is outlines only, drawn on the map only (v3 U-4, #468)

D-078 said unreached land is grey, never labelled, counted or interactive. This is how the code keeps it.

- **Outlines and nothing else.** `chartWorld(world, regions)` splits a world into `charted` (a formed region: its id, site, outline and name's place) and `unreached` (an outline and a cloud). An unreached piece has no id, no site, no name and no count. Unformed sites and decorative continents are mixed in one list and cannot be told apart.
- **Never the unformed count.** A planet always has one to three decorative continents (two or three islands on an ocean world), and four spare sites that are never a region. So the grey on the map is never as many pieces as there are unformed sites, and a person cannot count what is left.
- **The map only.** The globe paints every continent in colour. Only formed regions get a dashed border and a name there.
- **Not in text, lists, search or the accessibility tree.** The drawing, its names and its grey are hidden from assistive technology. A formed region is one 48 px target named "Name, a region", in site order. The plain list shows the formed regions in the same order, and the list view, search and the outline read the descriptors, which hold nothing unreached. A grey shape has no press handler.
- **Without a look.** A planet that has no look (a friend's visit, a world not backfilled) is drawn plainly and keeps this rule. A friend's planet turns but does not land: a visit shows no regions.

**Builds on** D-078 and D-103. Confirmed by: `world.test.ts` (no id, site, name or count in `unreached`; the grey count differs from the unformed count for every number of formed regions, and adding a region leaves every other border and name's place bit-identical over many planets and orders), `universe-screen.test.tsx` (no string about unreached land anywhere in the tree, same names in the same order in the Simple drawing), `planet-views.test.tsx`, `e2e/universe-planet-smoke.spec.ts` (the accessibility snapshot names only the formed regions).
