## D-110 · 2026-10-31 · A planet opens as a globe, a tap lands on its map, and Back from an encounter lands on the map (v3 U-4, #468)

- **Three steps.** Focusing a planet shows its globe (U03). A tap on the globe, or Enter on its one button, lands on the map at planet level with nothing chosen (U04). Choosing a region on the map drops the camera over it and opens its card. A dragged-over click is not a tap: a press within 350 ms of the camera moving is ignored, on the globe and on the map.
- **Back is the hierarchy.** The Back pill on the map says the system, as the design does. A region steps up to its planet's map. Browser and hardware Back follow where the person has been, so they step back from the map to the globe. Landing writes nothing and reads nothing.
- **Remembered on the device.** `landed` is part of the camera in the account scope's storage, with the focus and frame, purged with the scope.
- **After an encounter.** Back from a region's encounter lands on the planet's map at planet level with the region marked "you were here", as the design draws it. It does not restore the region's own zoom.
- **A level tag.** The header shows PLANET or REGION in a small orange tag beside Back on the map. Its text is dark, not white as drawn: white on that orange fails WCAG AA.

**Amends** D-026 ("exact scoped camera return": the return is to the planet's map at planet level) and D-039 (region pins). Confirmed by: `universe-session.test.ts` (land, up, Back, memory, nothing but reads), `universe-screen.test.tsx`, `e2e/universe-planet-smoke.spec.ts` (drag does not land, tap does, only reads).

## D-111 · 2026-10-31 · Simple drawing is the whole tab as two plain lists, on by a setting or by itself when 3D fails (v3 U-5, #469)

D-073 said the Simple drawing is plain lists, switched on when WebGL is missing, the context is lost or memory is low. D-100 built the fallback inside the 3D stage. This says what it is on screen and what turns it on.

- **What it is.** The whole Universe tab becomes the two lists of `simple-views-finalized.html` ("Do not change"): "Your universe" lists each system as a heading with its planets as rows (how many countries each holds, or "You were here · <region>"), then the rooms; a planet lists its countries in the order its map names them, with the row where the person was in yellow with the rippling red dot. There is no 3D, glass or motion beyond that dot (none under reduced motion). The region card is solid, not glass. The names and order are the 3D drawing's own, because both read one index. Land not reached yet is never listed or counted (D-109).
- **Four automatic triggers.** WebGL missing or a context that cannot be created, a lost context, a frame that throws, and low memory. `SpaceView` reports the reason and the screen swaps in the lists, so the tab is never blank. Turning the switch off tries 3D again.
- **Low memory.** The system's memory warning (`AppState` `memoryWarning`, raised on iOS) and, on the web, `navigator.deviceMemory` of 1 GB or less, which opens the tab as the lists. Native has no device-memory reading the app can use, so Android relies on the warning alone. Nothing was driven on a phone.
- **The setting.** "Simple view" on the dial (phone) and "Simple drawing" in the desktop panel. It is remembered on this device with the camera and Lately (in the account scope's storage, purged with it) and sends `detail: low` on the scene read, as the old Low detail did. A failure turns the lists on for the screen's life only: it is not remembered, because the next visit may draw.
- **One planet that cannot be drawn** (its world is refused) shows that planet's list, as before, and leaves the rest of the tab in 3D.
- **A visit** gets the same lists with the friend's name, no rooms and no region lines (regions are never shared).
- **Deliberate differences from the file.** The switch is the app's cobalt switch, not the file's teal, so it is one control everywhere. Secondary text is 74 % ink, not 62 %: 62 % measures 4.18:1 on the paper and fails WCAG AA's 4.5:1. The Rooms count is the bare number, as the file prints it, and the row where the person was shows the rippling dot in place of the chevron. System headings are plain headings: pressing one would change nothing on this screen.
- **Truth is kept.** A hypothetical place says ", hypothetical" in its accessible name in the lists, search, the dial and the panel, and a hypothetical planet shows its Hypothetical tag beside its title on the Simple screen.

**Amends** D-100 ("a device-memory class check … is deferred") and D-106 (the dark `SpaceSimpleLists` fallback, now only the developer gallery's). Confirmed by: `universe-screen.test.tsx` (each of the four triggers, the switch back to 3D, the setting remembered, the finalized words and counts, same names in the same order, nothing about unreached land), `universe-session.test.ts` (`setSimple`, remembered and purged), `platform/low-memory-device.web.test.ts`, `a11y-smoke.spec.ts` (axe over both lists at 390 and 1440).

## D-112 · 2026-10-31 · The orbit dial replaces the List view, and the List view's other jobs go to the panel, Simple lists and search (v3 U-5, #469)

The designs replace the List view sheet with the orbit dial (`world-internals-05.html` O1 to O4). The sheet was also the screen-reader outline of every place and the way to reach a region. This says where each job went.

- **The dial** (phone and tablet; "List view" opens it). A wheel of systems (touch the row, turn it, let go to choose), the chosen system's big planet (the real globe, so a tap lands on its map), a dial of its planets, and the facts of each: planets, rooms and formed regions, "You're here" and "You were here". Filters: All, Lately (not quiet), With rooms and A–Z. Two searches: systems on the wheel (O2) and planets (O4, in this system or everywhere). It reads only what the Universe already holds; it fetches and writes nothing; it never lists or counts land not reached yet. The design's "3 uncharted" chip is not drawn (D-109).
- **Regions and rooms stay reachable.** A region is one Land away on the planet's map and in the Simple list; a room opens from its station, the Simple list, the panel and search. The outline of every place as one list is gone.
- **Search** now covers systems, planets, regions and rooms (rooms from the stations read, the person's own only). Best match first: the whole name, a name that starts with it, a word that starts with it, then inside a word; systems before planets before regions before rooms. Each result shows the letters that matched and its path in mono, "Region · Inference & serving · Machine learning" (the kind, then what contains it, as U08 draws). Enter or a tap flies to the place and Back restores the camera; a room opens its room. Search makes no request, keeps no query and writes nothing.
- **Desktop D02.** The panel is the same lists as the Simple view (title with counts, search, the picked planet's card docked, Lately and Simple drawing switches, systems with planets and rooms). On a system a tap on a planet picks it: it glows, and its card docks with Land (straight to its map) and Close. A visit does not pick: its planets only turn. Key hints, zoom buttons and the panel are drawn as D02 draws them.
- **Escape** closes what is open (story, sheet, card, pick), and with nothing open steps up one level, region to planet to system to map. The keyboard map lists the map's keys, Escape, the search field and the dial's wheel; a test refuses a key the map listens to and does not list.

**Amends** D-053 (the outline panel's rows and "the outline is the screen-reader view": the panel now lists systems, planets and rooms, and regions are on the planet's list) and the List-view line of D-039. Confirmed by: `dial-model.test.ts` (filters, search, no unreached land), `universe-screen.test.tsx` (the dial, both searches, the desktop panel, search with no request), `universe-search.test.ts`, `universe-lists.test.ts`, `keyboard-map.test.tsx`, `platform/universe-back.web.test.tsx`, `universe-space-smoke.spec.ts` (J3 over search, the dial and the Simple view).

## D-184 · 2026-10-31 · A v2 Scroll offers its Part and related moves wherever the server admitted them (S-1 follow-up, #472)

**Amends** D-134 ("One move, one path": a related move is now the way on when no next Part was admitted) and D-135 (`/scroll/<id>` had no moves).

- **The defect.** Opened at `/scroll/<id>`, the reader had no Part button, key or swipe, although the context read said the next Part was ready. The route drew no moves at all.
- **One source.** Both entry paths turn the exposure context into the same moves (`readerContinuations`): next Part, previous Part and related. Only a ready, admitted continuation is a move. Where none was admitted there is no button, the key and the swipe do nothing.
- **In Explore** a move is the encounter session's navigation with the displayed link, as before. **On `/scroll/<id>`** there is no encounter, so a move opens the address of the target the server named (its own exposure, like any address). The Part label is the context's count ("Part 1 of 3"); until the context arrives it is the Scroll's own number.
- **The way on.** The dock's primary button, the → key and a swipe left take the next Part. With none, they take the related move: the same `seriesNext ?? related` the Reel's edge tab and swipe use. The dock names the destination ("Part 2: <title>", "Related: <title>") and draws the related move dashed, as the Reel's tab does. The keyboard map says so. The end of the Scroll shows both, the related one as a dashed row.
- **Ask is Explore's.** "Ask about this" opens the request sheet, which belongs to the encounter. `/scroll/<id>` has no encounter, so it has no Ask. It keeps the sparks and •••.
- **v1 Scrolls** keep the article and its own end card.

Confirmed by: `continuation-moves.test.ts`, `stage-reader.test.tsx` (the related row), `reader-nav-smoke.spec.ts` (Part n → by button, key and finger from the address and from Explore; absent on the last Part and on a single Scroll; the related move alone and beside a Part). Shown failing before the fix: every address-route case failed with no Part button, no key move and no swipe.

## D-185 · 2026-10-31 · The reader's top bar wraps instead of hiding the title, and the end card follows the stage (S-1 follow-up, #472)

**Amends** D-133 (the top bar: "a phone has no room for the title") and D-134 (the end card's "The picture you built").

- **The defect.** At 390 px the title and "Part n" were drawn in one pixel and the Reel/Scroll switch was clipped ("Scro"). A Scroll with no stage said "The picture you built" over no picture.
- **The top bar.** Your Universe, then the title with its kicker and Part ("SEASONS · PART 1 OF 3", V3 A1 and G1), then the switch and Next ↑. Below tablet width Next is its arrow alone ("Next topic" for a screen reader), so at 390 px the bar is one line and the text keeps its height (a taller bar left no room to show a call's answer whole). When the title would be left under 84 px (320 to 360 px), the switch and Next wrap under the title, right aligned. The title is cut to two lines; its whole text stays in the heading for a screen reader. Nothing is clipped down to 320 px.
- **The switch.** Below tablet width it draws only "Reel" and "Scroll". "· unavailable" and "· preparing" stay in each option's name, and an unavailable one stays dimmed.
- **The Part chip** on the stage is gone: the top bar says it on every width.
- **The end card.** "The picture you built" is said only when the Scroll has a stage. A plain Scroll says "The short version" over the same recap.
- **A tap after a swipe is not changed.** A tap on a call option right after a synthetic touch swipe was often ignored in a hands-on run. It is the browser's: after a drag made of synthesised touch events, Chromium reports the next tap (pointer and touch events) but makes no click, on a page with no app code too. A swipe on the stage, outside the reader's responder and its scroll-settle, loses it equally. Nothing in the reader is changed for it.

Confirmed by: `stage-reader.test.tsx` (the end card with and without a stage), `reader-nav-smoke.spec.ts` (the bar at 320, 360 and 390 px: title, kicker and Part visible, no control leaves the screen or is cut off, axe clean; the end card both ways; a tap after a finger swipe, with a throwaway tap first for the browser's dropped one). Shown failing with the bar unable to wrap (the switch and Next left the screen at 320 px) and on the old bar (no title, a clipped switch).

## D-186 · 2026-10-31 · A Scroll that another screen covers is not being read (S-1 follow-up, #472)

**Builds on** D-135 (what the reader reports; activity is the article's own rule) and the product law that only an actual encounter creates consumption evidence.

- **The defect.** Moving to Part 2 on `/scroll/<id>` pushes a new screen, and the web and native stacks keep Part 1 mounted under it. Part 1's reader stayed active, so its recorder went on reporting active reading (`scroll.active_dwell`) for a Scroll the person had left. Two exposures were counted for the same minutes. The Scroll to Reel switch pushes the same way and had the same flaw.
- **The rule.** A Scroll route that is not focused is paused: the reader reports no activity, so its reading time stops. When the screen is focused again (Back) the time picks up from there. This is the Reel's rule, now the Scroll's, for both the Stage + Spine reader and the v1 article.
- **Unchanged.** Explore pauses a reader its overlays cover, as before.

Confirmed by: `reader-nav-smoke.spec.ts` ("stops when the next Part is opened over it, and picks up again on Back": Part 1 reports no reading past the moment it was left, resumes on Back, and Part 2 stops counting under it). Shown failing without the pause: Part 1 reported reading up to 16.7 s after it was left at 6 s.

## D-145 · 2026-10-31 · Talk is drawn on the New Universe's own palette, dark by default, with Paper a name away (v3 T-B, #482)

**Amends** D-001 and D-060 for Talk: T3's kit now governs how a conversation and a thread's messages look.

- **One palette.** `design-tokens` gets `universe` (the Universe file's sky, Paper, Ink, cobalt, focus, highlight, orange, red, glass and the chunky shadows, with the values copied from the file) and `talkThemes`, which gives each role of the T3 kit a value: ground, bubbles, quote strips, capsule, bar, field, Send, reaction pill, menu and the flash. There is no second token set.
- **Two themes, one set of roles.** `sky` (dark) is the default (T3 Q1). `paper` is defined with the same roles. `TalkThemeProvider` in `ui` takes the name, so the per-person setting in T-C is a prop and changes no component. A test fails if the two themes stop defining the same roles.
- **Contrast is tested.** Every text pair in both themes meets 4.5:1 and the focus ring 3:1 (WCAG 1.4.3 and 1.4.11).
- **What is drawn this way.** Bubbles, day capsules, the time under a run, the header and message bar of a conversation and of a thread, the card of a shared Reel or Scroll, the quote strip, the reaction pills and the menu. The Talk list, the thread's series panel and its sheets keep the v2.1 paper look for now. A thread's series panel and docked player stay on paper beside the dark chat, as T3 draws them.
- **Glass without blur.** The glass fill and its hairline edge are drawn everywhere; the blur only on the web, where `backdrop-filter` exists.

Confirmed by: `tokens.test.ts` ("Talk themes"), `talk.test.tsx` in `ui` (a theme switch changes the bubble), `talk-smoke.spec.ts` (screenshots at 390 x 844 and 1440 x 900).

## D-146 · 2026-10-31 · A reply is a flat quote of one earlier message of the same conversation (v3 T-B, #482)

**Amends** D-042 and D-046 ("Not built: reply").

- **One level.** A message may name the message it answers, and that is all: a reply to a reply quotes that reply, with no nesting (T3 Q4). Branch cards keep their own replies (T-F).
- **Same conversation only.** `replyTo` on a send is a message ID. The server looks it up inside the conversation under its lock, so a message of another conversation, an unknown ID and a removed message all answer `unavailable` and write nothing. The row keeps the original's sequence under a composite foreign key, so the database refuses a pointer into another conversation too.
- **The quote is read, not stored.** A page carries the original's author and its first 120 characters, read when the page is built, or `unavailable` once the original was removed. A client that holds the original prefers its own copy, so a removed original stops being quoted as soon as its tombstone arrives.
- **Same receipt as a send.** `replyTo` is part of the command's body: a retry replays the first answer, and the same command ID naming another original is refused (`command_key_reused`).
- **A removed original.** A reply waiting in the message box drops its chip as soon as the removal reaches the reader. One that was sent before the reader knew is refused for good (`unavailable`): its bubble says "Not sent" and offers "Send without the quote", which sends the same words as a plain message under a new command.
- **A reply is an ordinary message.** The export lists it with the person's own words (marked `wasAReply`), and account deletion tombstones it and clears the pointer.

Confirmed by: `social-replies-reactions.test.ts` ("stores a reply with the quote of its original", "refuses a reply to another conversation, to an unknown message and to a removed one", "reads a quote of a removed original as unavailable", "replies within the thread only"), `conversation-screen.test.tsx` ("drops the reply chip when the message it answers is removed meanwhile", "offers to send a refused reply without the quote") and `conversation-thread.test.ts` and `content-thread.test.ts` (a retry names the same original; a refused reply is sent again once, as a plain message). Shown failing with the conversation filter taken out of the lookup: three tests went red.

## D-147 · 2026-10-31 · Five reactions, set by a wanted state, shown as counts and names, never as a time or a score (v3 T-B, #482)

**Amends** D-042 and D-046 ("Not built: reactions"), and D-051's mask for blocks (see "Blocks").

- **Five fixed kinds.** Spark, Mind blown, Ha, Heart, Question (T3). The set is closed in the contract and in the table, and the client draws each kind itself: there is no emoji keyboard.
- **One per person, per kind, per message.** A person can hold several kinds on one message. The primary key is (conversation, message, person, kind), so the table cannot hold a duplicate and adding is idempotent by construction.
- **The command is a wanted state.** `react{messageId, kind, present}` puts the reaction there or takes it off. A repeat, whether a retry with the same command ID (a replay) or a new command ID, answers accepted with `changed: false` and writes nothing. Reacting needs the standing of sending (a member of an open conversation, a participant of a thread) and works on any message that is not removed, the person's own included.
- **The feed.** A change gives the message the conversation's next change number in the same transaction as the reaction and its receipt, and both inboxes hear a hint. The changes read sends the message again with its reactions. A reaction moves neither the message sequence nor the conversation's activity time, so the Talk list does not reorder and "unread" does not move.
- **What a reader sees.** For each kind on a message: how many people, whether the reader is one, and their names (T3: "count, tap to see who"). The pill carries the count; a tap lists the names, "You" first. No reaction time and no order of reacting is carried: the names come in a neutral order (display name, then person ID), so the order cannot be read as who reacted first. There is no total, leaderboard or notification of its own. Only people the reader may already see by name appear.
- **Blocks.** Someone the reader blocked is left out of the count and the names, for that reader only. Everyone else, the blocked person included, sees the reaction as before, so nothing tells them they were blocked. A block ends a direct conversation, so this matters in a thread, where a person the reader blocked may still be a member and their words are not masked (D-046). A reaction is therefore masked more strictly than a message. The brief asked for the same treatment as messages; this is the one place the two differ.
- **Erasure and export.** Deleting an account removes its reactions and every reaction on its messages; each message of someone else that loses one gets a new change number, so their catch-up replaces it and the count drops. Clear keeps reactions, as it keeps conversations (D-042). A person's own reactions are exported (kind, when, and whether on their own message or someone else's; the newest 2,000, with `reactionsGiven` and `reactionsTruncated` so a cut is said); other people's are not.
- **Not consumption evidence.** A reaction records nothing about what the reader has seen, opened or learned, and feeds no ranking.

Confirmed by: `social-replies-reactions.test.ts` ("adds and removes a reaction; a replay and a repeat under another command change nothing", "keeps one reaction per person and kind", "carries a reaction in the changes feed in order, and tells the other person's inbox", "refuses a reaction from a stranger, on another conversation's message, on a removed message and after the end", "leaves out the reaction of a person the reader blocked, for that reader only", "deleting an account removes its reactions", "exports a person's own reactions"), `account-deletion.test.ts` (the every-store check now fills `message_reactions`). Shown failing with the block mask taken out, with the held-state check removed, with the lookup left open and with the people ordered by when they reacted ("the order of the people behind a reaction").

## D-148 · 2026-10-31 · Reaching reply and react: swipe on touch, long press, a button and keys (v3 T-B, #482)

**Amends** D-053 (keyboard map) and D-055 (a long press on a bubble reports it).

- **Swipe right to reply, on touch only.** A bubble follows the finger to a limit, a reply arrow shows behind it, a tick marks 60 px and lifting the finger past 60 px asks to reply. A drag that is mostly down is scrolling and does nothing. A mouse never drags a bubble: only touch events are heard. Under reduced motion nothing slides, and the reply is asked for when the finger lifts past 60 px.
- **Long press (400 ms)** opens the menu: the chat dims, the message stays in view, the five reactions come first, then Reply, Copy and Report. Copy is offered when there are words to copy; Report only on another person's message. A thread's menu adds What if... and Another perspective, which replace the ⋯ sheet of X01.
- **The same without a gesture.** On the web every message has an options button ("Options for Ravi's message", shown on hover or focus, always reachable by keyboard). With it focused, R replies and E opens the menu. Esc in the message box cancels the reply. The menu key and Shift+F10 open the menu. A screen reader on a phone gets Reply, React, Copy, Report and "Go to the original message" as custom actions of a text bubble; a card keeps its options button on a phone too.
- **The quote jumps.** Pressing the quote strip scrolls to the original and flashes it for 1.2 s. If the original is older than what is loaded, up to five older pages are read first. Under reduced motion there is no slide and no flash, and a 2 px outline stays for 3 s.
- **Report moves into the menu.** The long press that opened the report sheet on another person's bubble (D-055) now opens the menu, whose Report row opens the same sheet. A thread's messages can be reported the same way.
- **After the end.** Once a conversation has ended the menu offers Copy and Report only: no reply, no reaction.
- **Keys listed.** The keyboard map has a "Talk messages" group.

Confirmed by: `conversation-screen.test.tsx` ("swipes right past 60 px to reply, and not for less, or when the finger goes down", "replies and opens the menu from the keyboard, ignoring keys with a modifier", "offers no reply or reaction once the other person has left"), `talk-smoke.spec.ts` (long press, quote jump and the 2 px outline with reduced motion), `keyboard-map.test.tsx`.

## D-149 · 2026-10-31 · "Watch together" in a conversation starts a thread for the same two people, with no shared playback (v3 T-B, #482)

**Amends** D-042 ("Not built: group threads"); settles T3 Q5 and the change map's row "Watch together in a DM".

- **A card has the button.** A Reel or Scroll shared in a conversation carries "Watch together" beside Open, while the conversation is open and the content is not withdrawn. It uses the existing `threads/create` command with the one friend and the card's exact revision, through the same sheet as Start a thread, so a retry makes the same thread.
- **The chat stays a chat.** Nothing is sent into the conversation and nothing is moved out of it. The other person gets an ordinary invitation and joins by answering it (mutual consent). Nobody's playback is shared, followed or synchronised, and nobody learns where the other is (D-046, D-065).
- **No evidence.** Starting a thread is not an encounter for either person.
- **Left for later.** T3 also draws a "Thread started from your chat" line in the conversation. It needs a system message and waits for a later slice.

Confirmed by: `conversation-screen.test.tsx` ("starts a thread from a shared card with Watch together, for the same two people, and leaves the chat as it is").

## D-166 · 2026-10-31 · Painted room backdrops: MiniMax's image model, a $1 cap in code, and a ledger of every attempt (R-4, #490)

**Amends** D-091, which held painted backdrops until an image provider and a budget were approved. On 31 Oct the person running the session on the owner's behalf approved both, in the #490 comment: MiniMax's image model, a still for each room, **$1 in total**, real calls only on the server.

- **What makes the picture.** `image-01` on `POST https://api.minimax.io/v1/image_generation`, one image of the wide band ratio (21:9), returned inline as base64 so no second request fetches an address. The transport is an adapter beside the text one (`reasoning/adapters/minimax/minimax-image.ts`): one request, a deadline, no retry, the key only in a header. A picture is never made on an HTTP path, inside a transaction (the guard refuses it) or from a client. There is no job: nothing that spends money runs by itself, so it is an operator command (`rooms backdrop`).
- **The price, and where it is from.** $0.0035 per image (`image-01`), read on 31 Oct 2026 at https://platform.minimax.io/docs/guides/pricing-paygo (the page shows no date). It is a constant with its source and the day it was read (`BACKDROP_PRICING`); changing it is a reviewed change. At that price $1 is 285 images, and the six library rooms need six.
- **Whether the plan covers it is not known.** The Token Plan FAQ says endpoints with pay-as-you-go pricing "deduct from your included M Plan quota at the corresponding endpoint price", and lists image models in every tier, but nothing says that a token-plan key (`sk-cp-`) may call this route. So the design holds either way: every attempt records the estimate from the published price, whether it is paid or drawn from a quota. Only the refusals MiniMax documents as coming before an image is made count nothing: provider status 1002 (rate limit), 1004 and 2049 (authentication), 1008 (balance), 1026 (blocked prompt) and 2013 (bad parameters), and HTTP 400, 401, 403, 404, 413, 422 and 429. The pricing page does not say a failed image is free, so everything else is **unknown**: any other non-zero status, HTTP 408, every 5xx and any other non-200 status, a timeout or reset, a success with no image, and `failed_count` above zero (images blocked by content safety after they were generated, which the same prompt would meet again). An unknown outcome is charged in full and blocks the room until an operator settles it.
- **The cap is code.** `BACKDROP_SPEND_CAP_MICRO_USD` is 1,000,000 millionths of a dollar. An attempt is refused when what every attempt now counts plus its own estimate would pass it; ending exactly on the cap is allowed. Raising it is a reviewed change, not a setting. Money is whole millionths of a dollar, so the arithmetic is exact.
- **One ledger row per attempt** (`rooms.backdrop_attempts`), in the states of a Reel order's ledger: `dispatched`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `not_sent`, `unknown`, `reconciled`, `written_off`. The row is written in the same transaction that took the spend lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`) and read the total, so two operators at once cannot both pass the cap; the provider call and the file write are outside every transaction. A refused attempt is recorded nowhere. Each row says what it counts now (`charge_micro_usd`): the whole estimate while dispatched, unknown or done, **nothing** for a conclusive refusal and for a call that never left. An image the provider made but that cannot be kept (not an allowed type, or the store failed) stays charged.
- **Unknown is never resent.** A timeout, a reset, a 502 or 504, a success with no image, or an attempt left `dispatched` past the request deadline plus two minutes is `unknown`. It keeps its charge and blocks any other request for that room. Only an operator who has read the provider's own record settles it: `backdrop-reconcile` (charged or not, with a reason) or `backdrop-write-off` (it stays charged). MiniMax offers no lookup, so nothing here looks it up.
- **Not the reasoning ledger.** An image is not a model call: no role, schema or tokens. The ledger is the rooms module's own, as `content.reel_attempts` is Content's for Reels.
- **No member text.** The prompt is built by code from the house style, the room's library topic label and the words of its kit (D-170), and is cut to plain letters, digits and a few marks. The house style bans text, letters, numbers, faces, people and logos, and (D-172) any signature, artist's mark, monogram, stamp or writing in the corners.

Confirmed by: `backdrop-spend.test.ts` (the arithmetic and each state's charge), `rooms-face.test.ts` ("the one-dollar cap": the attempt that would pass it is refused before it exists and asks the provider for nothing; the last image that fits, with the real constants, is made and the next is refused; two operators at once ask for only the images that fit; "what each outcome costs": a refusal and a call that never left cost nothing, an unknown outcome is never resent, counts against the cap, and is settled only by an operator), `minimax-image.test.ts`, `transaction-guard.test.ts` (an image request inside a transaction is refused). The cap tests were each made to fail by breaking the lock, the comparison, the cap and the unknown charge in turn.

## D-170 · 2026-10-31 · Every room has a face: a closed-vocabulary kit that a model proposes and code validates (R-4, #490)

**Builds on** D-051, D-090, D-162, D-163 and R3 D. **Amends** D-163: a room's face changes the residents' marks, not their names.

- **A kit is words, not markup.** Palette (`ground`, `accent`, `ink`, each one of 17 named swatches), emblem (a frame of six and a glyph of fourteen), a motif of eight, a motion of six, and the mark each resident wears (a glyph word, different for the two residents, which the schema checks). Nothing is free text, a colour code, an address or a piece of drawing. The vocabulary and its shape are the wire contract's (`room-identity.ts`); what each word looks like is the app's own (`features/rooms/identity/shapes.ts`).
- **Code judges contrast on the swatches' fixed colours.** The ink must read on the ground (WCAG ratio at least 4.5), the accent must stand out (at least 3), and the three are different swatches. A model that picks words cannot pass this by wording.
- **The `rooms.station-kit` role** runs on the nonpersonal `rooms` scope (D-162), as `interactive` (an operator asked, so it is never squeezed by the ambient share; the scope's own daily cap still bounds it), started by `rooms kit --room <slug>`. It reads the topic and its library meaning and nothing else, makes one call and at most one repair, and its answer is refused by the harness unless it passes the vocabulary and the contrast rule; a repair carries one fixed hint, never anything the model wrote. A kit that fails is stored nowhere. The run's ID is fixed by the room, the revision and a sequence number: a retried command answers from the ledger and never pays twice, an unknown outcome is never sent again, and a run that has ended (a failed proposal) is never reopened, so the next command for the room starts the next run.
- **Every room has a face from the start.** A room with no approved kit wears the default its ID gives it: a deterministic pick from eleven palettes that pass the rule and from the vocabulary, the same on every server and read, needing no model and no review. A stored kit is checked against today's vocabulary and contrast rule every time it is shown, and a kit that no longer passes shows the default instead. The default is never stored.
- **Residents keep their names.** T3's Nova and Halley win over R3's per-room names (D-060), so the kit's cast skins only choose the mark each resident wears: a glyph on a tile with square corners, the Explainer's in the accent and the Skeptic's in the ink. It is never a face, it is hidden from screen readers, and the AI label and name beside it still carry the meaning.
- **What the app draws.** The band (ground, faint motif and one slow loop of the six, opacity and transform only), the emblem, the residents' marks, the Talk row's emblem and, on the Universe map, the room's station: its ring in the kit's accent and its emblem as a sprite over the hub (the same outlines drawn into pixels), dimmed under Lately. The Simple views are a final design (U-5) and draw no emblem. The band's words and pills use the kit's ink. **Reduced motion is a still frame**: no loop starts and one that was running stops. A room whose server sent no face keeps the dark band it had.
- **Sent as `identity` on a room's summary** (optional on the wire, always sent by a current server), so a list, a preview, a station and the room carry the same face. No count, name or activity comes with it.

Confirmed by: `kit.test.ts` (the vocabulary, the contrast rule, the default for four hundred IDs, the fallback for a stored kit that no longer validates; the contrast test fails with the minimum set to 1), `rooms-face.test.ts` ("a room's kit": the default, one call from the topic alone with no member words or IDs in the request bytes, a palette that does not read is repaired once and then stored nowhere, words outside the vocabulary are refused, a failed proposal can be asked for again on a new run, an unknown outcome is never sent again, no write to a personal universe), `room-identity.test.ts`, `emblem-raster.test.ts`, `room-identity.test.tsx` (reduced motion is a still frame for each of the six, and the test fails when the check is removed), `build-scene.test.ts` (the emblem on the station), `rooms-smoke.spec.ts` (a dark and a light face with axe, both widths).

## D-171 · 2026-10-31 · An operator reviews a kit and a backdrop before any member sees it (R-4, #490)

**Settles** R3 question 17 (an operator queue first; moderators later) and the change map's "review" for the room's face.

- **Two states per proposal, one decision.** A kit and a painted backdrop each arrive `pending`; an operator approves or rejects it (`rooms kit-review`, `rooms backdrop-review`). Only `approved` is ever shown. Approving replaces the one shown so far, which becomes `superseded` in the same transaction, and partial unique indexes allow one pending and one approved row per room. Approving re-checks: a kit must still pass today's rules, and a picture's file must still be in the media store.
- **One at a time.** A second kit is not asked for while one waits, and a second picture is not asked for while one waits or while an attempt for that room is unresolved (D-166), so a result cannot pile up unreviewed.
- **The backdrop's address is its digest.** `GET /media/room/<sha256>` serves an approved picture to a signed-in session as the type it was stored with, and nothing else: a pending, rejected or replaced one is not found. The address is under `/media/*`, which the edge already sends to the API. It answers `private, no-cache` with an ETag: a device keeps the bytes but asks before each use, and the ask is checked against the approval and answered with a 304, so a rejection takes effect at the next use and a replacement (a new digest, so a new address) never meets a stale copy. Nothing is cached for a day. The picture is reviewed art with no text and no faces and holds nothing of any person. A room with no approved picture draws its kit alone.
- **How the operator sees what they approve.** `kit-list` prints the kit (words only). `backdrop-list` prints the picture's place under the media root, to be opened on the server; there is no panel, since an operator panel would be a screen of its own. `ops.room_faces` counts what waits and what is spent, and nothing else.
- **Not personal.** Rooms are not a person's data: no table here names an account, and who decided is the operator's login as `cli:<name>`. Neither a Clear nor an account deletion touches them, and the erasure registry has nothing to declare, as for `room_cast`. The backdrop's prompt holds no personal data either, so a copy at the provider needs no erasure.
- **Where it ends.** Not built: a panel in the Operator screen, review by a room's moderators, and a picture for each cast member.

Confirmed by: `rooms-face.test.ts` ("shows a kit … only once an operator approves it", "supersedes the kit shown so far", "serves a picture only after an operator approves it, and only the approved one", "waits for review", "will not approve one whose file is gone"), `room-backdrop.controller.test.ts` (approved only, signed in only, 304, HEAD, a size that is not the file's is not found), `operator-views.test.ts` (`ops.room_faces`), `rooms-smoke.spec.ts` (a backdrop is asked for only when one was approved).

## D-150 · 2026-10-31 · SEEN in a direct conversation is a mutual switch that only decides what a read returns (v3 T-C, #483)

**Builds on** D-066 (the owner's ruling). **Amends** D-042 ("Read state stays private … the other person sees only 'Sent'") for direct conversations. D-067 stands: nobody is ever shown that someone is typing.

- **One switch, per person, on by default.** `share_read_state` on `social.visibility_settings` (migration 0078), with its own `read_state_revision`, beside the audience and the availability choice. No row means on. It has its own command (`read-receipts.set`, a wanted state, so a repeat changes nothing) and its own read (`GET /v1/social/read-receipts`). The three settings never fence each other.
- **Mutual.** A direct conversation's snapshot and changes page carry `peerRead { throughSequence }` only while both people share and the conversation stands. In every other case the field is absent and gives no reason: they hid theirs, I hid mine, or they left look the same on the wire. A person who expects a read and sees none can still guess the other hid theirs, which is what a mutual switch means. A blocked pair gets `unavailable` as for any read. Both switches are read under the pair lock on each read, so nothing is cached and the answer is never older than that read.
- **A second position, strictly behind the first.** `conversation_members.read_through_sequence` still moves for every reader, for their own unread dot, whether or not they share it. SEEN uses a second number beside it, `shared_read_through_sequence` (migration 0078), that holds how far the person has read while their own switch was on. It moves in the same statement as the first and only while the switch is on, so it never passes it: it holds strictly less than the existing position, never more. It is the only position the other person is told. So what a person reads while SEEN is off is never shown, even after they turn it on again: they must read something new while it is on, and then it shows up to there. A repeated read of what they already read moves neither number. Reading a snapshot or a changes page writes nothing (a test compares the Social tables before and after a read). Existing direct conversations start with the shared position equal to the read position, because nobody could hide anything before this; threads keep 0. A first version of this decision returned the one position and said turning SEEN back on showed what was read while it was off. A review found that was false to the Off copy ("they won't see when you've read their messages"), and this replaces it.
- **A position, never a time.** `peerRead` is a message position. The app draws the time the message was sent, as T3 does ("13:15 · SEEN"). No read time is stored, sent or drawn.
- **The hint.** When a read moves the shared position while both people share, the other person's inbox head is bumped too (the same disposable hint as a message). When either hides, or the other person has left or deleted their account, only the reader's is, as before; nothing is created for a person who is gone. Turning the switch on or off tells nobody: the other person's open conversation follows at its next read (a hint, focus or the 20 s poll). The durable read is always right.
- **Not in threads or rooms** (D-066). A thread's snapshot has no field for it and its strict schema refuses one. Content seen markers (what a person has seen of a Reel or Scroll) stay private (AGENTS).
- **Clear, erasure, export.** Clear keeps the switch: it is a choice, like availability (D-047), not exploration history. Deleting the account deletes the row. The export says in words whether SEEN is on (`seenInDirectConversations`) and never anything about the other person.
- **In the app.** Under the person's own latest message the time reads "13:15 · Sent", or "13:15 · Seen" once the other person has read to it. Only the latest own message carries the word. The switch is in Account, Privacy, Sharing & visibility: "Show SEEN in direct conversations", with "If you hide yours, you won't see theirs."

Confirmed by: `social-seen.test.ts` ("is mutual: when either person hides theirs …", "tells the other person's inbox when a read moves while both share …", "shows nothing once the other person left, and nothing to a blocked person", "is never in a content thread …", "never shows what was read while the switch was off, even after it is turned on again", "shows nothing from a person who hid theirs before reading anything …", "still records a hidden reader's own position … and writes nothing more on a read", "tells only the reader's inbox once the other person left" and "… account is deleted", "starts every direct conversation with the shared position equal to the read position, and a thread member at 0", "keeps the switch and what was shared through Clear …", "exports the switch in words …"), `conversations.test.ts` and `sharing.test.ts` in `contracts`, `conversations.test.ts` (domain rule `visiblePeerRead`), `conversation-thread.test.ts`, `conversation-screen.test.tsx` ("SEEN under the person's latest message"), `visibility-panel.test.tsx`, `talk-seen-smoke.spec.ts`. Shown failing with the reader's switch ignored, with the other person's switch ignored, with the ended-conversation check removed, with the hint sent always or never, with the shared position recorded while hidden and with the peer's read position returned in place of the shared one.

## D-151 · 2026-10-31 · The Talk theme is a device choice, like text size and motion (v3 T-C, #483)

**Builds on** D-145 and D-057. Settles the change map's "per-person theme setting" and T3 Q1.

- **A device preference.** `talkTheme` is `sky` (dark, the default) or `paper`, kept with the other device choices under the one key, read once at the root above the account scope. It survives signing out, switching account and Clear, and nothing is sent to the server. It is chosen in Account, Accessibility & playback, under Talk.
- **Why the device and not the account.** It is a choice about how this screen reads (a bright room, a long conversation), like text size, so a person may want Paper on a phone in daylight and the sky on a laptop at night. A server setting would need a column, a command, an export line and an erasure line for a colour. D-057's rule holds: a setting that holds no account data lives with the device.
- **Applied once.** `PreferredTalkTheme` at the root gives `TalkThemeProvider` the name. No component reads the setting, as D-145 intended. A damaged or unknown stored value falls back to the sky.
- **What it covers.** What D-145 draws with the theme: bubbles, capsules, the header and message bar, shared cards, quote strips, reaction pills and the menu, in conversations and in a thread's chat. The Talk list, a thread's series panel and sheets keep their paper look, as D-145 says.
- **Left alone.** It does not follow the person to another device. If the owner wants that, it is one column and one command, added later.

Confirmed by: `device-preferences.test.ts` (the default, reading what was stored and falling back), `account-settings.test.tsx` ("starts Talk on the dark sky, offers Paper, and keeps the choice on this device only", "is the dark sky by default and Paper once chosen"), `talk-seen-smoke.spec.ts` ("Paper is chosen in Account, applies to the conversation at once and is kept on this device"; axe on the sky and on Paper at 390 × 844 and 1440 × 900).

## D-152 · 2026-10-31 · A party place is one forward-only row a member writes for themself, with a hide switch per thread (v3 T-D, #484)

**Builds on** D-065 (the owner's ruling). **Amends** D-046, "The server holds no position, selection or seen marker of any member", for this one coarse place. Everything else in D-046 stands: selection, folding and playheads stay on the device, and the J2 check holds for every answer except the places read.

- **One row per member and thread.** `social.thread_places(thread_id, account_id, part, state, day)` (migration 0080). `state` is `on` or `finished`. `day` is a `date`, stamped by the server from its own UTC clock, so no time of day can be stored or sent. The row follows the member's membership row.
- **Forward only.** Finishing a part sits between being on it and being on the next. Opening an earlier part, a part just finished, or the same place again changes nothing and keeps the stored day. So "all finished" never flips back, and a repeat is harmless.
- **Series only.** The part must be one of the thread's series parts, or the command is `invalid_input`. A thread on a single item has no places.
- **Written only by the member's own command.** `threads/places/set` takes a part and a state and has no field for whose place it is. The server never reads anyone's Experience to make a place, and a test shows a member who opened every part still has none.
- **No command receipt.** Place set and hide are idempotent by their state (a place only moves forward, hide is a wanted state), so they write no receipt. A receipt would keep the exact time of every report, and a receipt survives leave, hide and Clear. They therefore have no command ID, nothing to replay, and answers that carry the scope and what the command did, never a time. A first version kept receipts; a review found the time in `receipts.shared_commands` after a place was gone.
- **No clock time is kept anywhere.** The only time a place leaves is its `day` date. A test sets, hides and shows places at one distinctive instant, leaves and Clears, and finds that instant in no table of the database and no receipt row for a place command.
- **The switch is per thread, on by default off.** D-065 says places are shown by default, so "hide where I am" is false until the person turns it on. It lives on the member's own row (`conversation_members.place_hidden`), not in Account, so it sits where its effect is seen and covers one thread at a time (a person may share with friends and hide from a bigger group). The brief's line "hide is on by default" is the same switch read the other way round; D-065 governs.
- **Hiding deletes and blocks.** Turning it on deletes the person's place in the same transaction. While it is on, a place command is accepted, says `hidden` and writes nothing. Turning it off writes nothing either: the place comes back from the person's next report. The switch is not mutual: hiding yours does not hide the others' from you.
- **Who is shown to whom.** The places read is its own read, not part of the thread view, so the thread view and list keep no field for a place. It lists a member only when they take part now, have not hidden, and neither side has blocked the reader. A member who has none, hides, left, was removed, or is blocked looks the same: not listed. Order is by how far along, then by name, never by who arrived first. The reader's own place comes back with `you`.
- **Removed on leave, block, Clear and account deletion.** Leaving deletes the place. Ending the friendship that carried a member in (Remove, Block, a new friendship), or an inviter who leaves, deletes the places of the members whose standing it ended. Clear deletes every place of the person but keeps "hide where I am", which is a choice and not history. Deleting the account deletes them before the membership rows. The export lists the person's own places (thread name, part, state, day) and how many threads they hide in, and names no one else.
- **A change tells open threads.** A place that moves, and hiding, bump every taking-part member's inbox head and send the usual disposable thread hint (it carries nothing and is kept nowhere), so an open thread reads the places again. A repeat sends nothing. So a member who is online with the thread open can notice a change within seconds: this is the live party header of T3 Q6. Nothing durable keeps the time of that change, and the only thing stored is the `day`. Taken for the owner; listed for them to confirm.

Confirmed by: `social-places.test.ts` (shown by default with a part, a state and a day; no clock time or position on the wire and a date column; forward only and idempotent; a repeat that changes nothing; no receipt row and no clock time in any table after set, hide, leave and Clear; concurrent writes; a part the series lacks; a single-item thread; "hide where I am" deletes, writes nothing and returns from the next report; per thread; indistinguishable from never having opened a part; stranger, invitee, leaver and stale reference get nothing; leave, Remove and Block, both-way block mask, Clear, account deletion and an inviter's deletion remove; export; hints; table checks; no consumption evidence), `places.test.ts` (the pure rules), `threads.test.ts` in `contracts` (a datetime, a playhead, a branch, a selection, a command ID or a time in an answer does not parse), `account-deletion.test.ts` (the store is seeded and emptied). Shown failing with the hide filter, the standing filter, the block mask, forward-only, the write-while-hidden check, the leave delete, the Clear erase, the block delete and a receipt written for a place command each broken once.

## D-153 · 2026-10-31 · The thread's own player tells the place, and nothing else does (v3 T-D, #484)

**Builds on** D-065 and D-152. Settles which of the person's acts become a place, and what the thread draws.

- **What tells.** A Reel opened in the thread's player (they pressed Watch) is `on` that part. A Reel played to its last second is `finished`. For a Scroll, pressing Read this Scroll is `on`, and the reader's own "I've finished this part" at the foot of the Scroll is `finished`: a Scroll cannot tell it was read to the end, and a person saying so is more honest than a guess.
- **Recording pause stops what the player concludes.** Opening a Reel, playing it to the end and pressing Read this Scroll are automatic: they come from the person's own playing and reading, which Pause recording (D-057) keeps off the server, and D-083 says play saves nothing on the server while paused. So these send **nothing** unless recording is current and collecting, as the recorders already require; unknown, checking and paused all count as not collecting. What is played while paused is not kept even in memory, so it is not told later when recording resumes or when the person turns hiding off. The one thing that still sends is the explicit "I've finished this part" button: pressing it is a deliberate share, so it is sent while paused, and never while the person hides.
- **What never tells.** Choosing a part, opening the Series sheet, folding or docking the player, Full screen (which is ordinary Explore reading), the snippet viewer, and reading or playing the same content anywhere else. The design does not ask for Explore to set a place, so it does not.
- **Memory only.** The client keeps the furthest place the player reported while recording was collecting (or the person pressed the button) in memory. It sends it again after a lost report, and when the person turns hiding off, and only while recording is still collecting unless it was the button. It is never stored on the device or sent while they hide.
- **Wording.** "On Part 2", "Finished Part 1", "Finished all 3 parts", and "All finished" when everyone who shares has finished the last part. Never "watching" and never T3's "here now". A member list row says the place and its day ("On Part 2 · Sat 24 Oct"); the day is a date, shown as the date it is and never as a time or a "today". T3's "Ravi finished Part 1 · 13:02" is not built, as D-065 says.
- **What is drawn.** The header shows the part worlds with the face of each member who shares on the part they are on, and a line under it ("Ravi is on Part 2 · Asha and Kenji on Part 1"), only when someone else shows a place. The Series sheet has Parts and Members tabs; the parts carry their faces in words and pictures, and Members holds the list and the switch. On a desktop the panel has the same members section. A world is a numbered coloured tile until the parts have art, as T-A's tiles are.
- **Not built.** T3's chat-stream lines ("Ravi started Part 1 · 10:28", "All 4 finished Part 2 · 12:40") need a history of places, which the server does not keep. "Call it together" is T-E.

Confirmed by: `thread-places.test.ts` and `party-lines.test.ts` in `client-core`, `party.test.tsx`, `thread-links.test.ts`, `thread-party-smoke.spec.ts` (two people, each in their own browser: opening and finishing a part, the line, faces on the worlds and on the part rows, the Members tab, hiding and showing, choosing a part sends nothing, nothing is sent while recording is paused, "All finished"; axe at 390 × 844 and 1440 × 900). The paused cases are in `thread-places.test.ts` (paused, unknown, not kept for later, not sent on show, and the button while paused and while hidden). Shown failing with the pause gate removed.

## D-187 · 2026-10-31 · The Final call is a call: server-judged, a combo bonus, and a tilt to turn (S-3, #474)

**Builds on** D-064, D-126 and D-181. **Amends** D-180 ("The Final call stays on the device without a spark") and D-181 ("Final call refused").

- **A call like the others.** The Final call is locked with the same `call.lock` command and judged on the server from the stored revision, once. Only a stop that is not a call of the revision is `call_not_found`. Its reveal is the Call it reveal: "Called it!" or "Plot twist!" (D-064).
- **The combo bonus.** A right Final call that follows a run of two or more right calls earns two sparks, any other right call one, a wrong call none and ends the run. The design says "combo ×2 doubles the Final call"; its own examples disagree on where the bonus starts (page 6 shows "combo ready" after one right call, page 9 after two), so the rule starts at two. `COMBO_FOR_FINAL_BONUS` in `scroll-model` is the one number to change.
- **One rule, two copies that must agree.** The screen works the tally out with `afterLock` in `scroll-model`. The server has its own copy in `play/domain` (a domain file imports no package), and a test runs both over every run of up to six calls, with each call in turn as the Final call, and checks they agree. A Scroll has at most five sparks (three Call its and a doubled Final call), so migration 0077 widens the stored bound from four to five. A combo is still at most four.
- **Display only.** Sparks and the bonus are play features (D-063, D-064). They never reach Composer, Jev, Planner, Evidence, Personal Context, Geography or Scenes.
- **A tilt to turn.** The design gives the Seasons Final call a tilt slider on the picture (page 6, frame 9; page 9). The library plan can bind it: `finalCall: {kind: 'tilt'}` on a recipe becomes `binding: {kind: 'tilt'}` on the document's Final call, set by code. The reader turns Earth's axis from 0 to 90 degrees in whole degrees, starting at 23.4 ("today"), and the pinned picture and its text version follow at once. The kit's physics works out the Sun's latitude (`asin(sin(tilt) · sin(season))`) and each pin's distance from it (`physicsAtTilt`), and at 23.4 it agrees with `stagePhysics` in every month. The value lives on the screen only: nothing about it is stored, sent or reported.
- **What is not built.** No sin, cos or clamp formula kinds for v1 simulation blocks: the tilt is the kit's physics in code, not a formula in a document. No other binding (scale, sort, fork) until S-8.
- **Admission and the writer.** A bound Final call needs a stage whose picture at that stop changes when the tilt is turned, so a stage that ends on the Moon view or at an equinox (March or September, where the Sun is over the equator at every tilt) is refused (`final_call_binding_invalid`, with a repair hint). The writer prompt is `scroll-writer.v16`: for a bound recipe it asks for a "what if the axis were not tilted" Final call, tells the writer the reader can turn the tilt and asks it to end on a June or December picture. The Seasons library has not been regenerated yet, so no stored revision carries a binding until it is.

Confirmed by: `scroll-model` `play.test.ts`, `kit.test.ts` (tilt grid over every month and whole degree, agreement with `stagePhysics`), `validate.test.ts`, `assemble.test.ts`; `play/domain/results.test.ts` and `lock-call.test.ts`; `tests/integration/modules/play.test.ts` (the Final call judged on the server, doubled after a combo of two, once); `play.test.tsx` (the announcement, the tilt control, "+2 sparks"); `payoff-smoke.spec.ts` (the slider turns the picture and sends nothing).

## D-188 · 2026-10-31 · A Scroll's landmark is recipe data, awarded once by the Final call, gold or plain (S-3, #474)

**Builds on** D-063, D-082, D-083, D-084 and D-089.

- **What a landmark is.** A trophy of one Scroll revision, like "The Leaning Axis". Its key and name come from the library plan and are set into the document by code (`landmark: {key, name}`), never from the model. A recipe with none awards none, and so does every stored revision made before this: a missing landmark is safe, and S-6 adds the rest. The four Seasons recipes are named (`The Leaning Axis`, `The Turning Halves`, `The Near January`, `The Sideways Giant`). The first is the design's; the other three are authored here and a person should confirm them.
- **The finish.** Gold when the Final call was right when it was locked, plain otherwise (`landmarkTier`). A call is locked once, so "first try" is "right when locked". A wrong Final call still earns the landmark, in the plain finish, and the reveal says so.
- **Awarded once, in the Final call's own transaction.** `play.landmarks` (0077) has one row for each person and revision: key, the name they saw, the finish, the time. It is written beside the call and the tally with `on conflict do nothing`, so a retry, a second lock by another command and a replay award nothing again and never upgrade a plain one. It needs the activated exposure that every lock needs, so a revision the person has not opened and activated awards nothing.
- **Paused.** While recording is paused nothing is stored (D-083): no landmark row, and after recording resumes the screen does not show one that the server never held. The payoff says "Not saved while recording is paused."
- **Erased, exported, read.** Clear and account deletion erase it (D-082). The export lists each in words: name, finish, day and the Scroll's title if it is still readable, with no identifier and no key. `readResults` carries the landmark of one Scroll, and `POST /v1/play/landmarks/read` lists the person's own, newest first, for the Universe overlay (S-4).
- **No place is made or moved.** The row names no planet or system. S-4 attaches it where the Scroll's topic has formed (D-084) from the asset, and draws it as a marker (D-089). A landmark never makes a place form: gold and plain give Evidence, Personal Context and Geography the same input (D-063).

Confirmed by: `tests/integration/modules/play.test.ts` (gold for a right Final call, plain for a wrong one, once; refused without an activated exposure; nothing while paused; erased by Clear; exported in words; listed for its owner only; the wall test plays the Final call right for one person and wrong for another and finds the same qualification, activity, contribution and places), `lock-call.test.ts`, `library.test.ts` (which recipes name one), `privacy-clear.test.ts` and `data-export.test.ts` over the every-store fixture.

## D-189 · 2026-10-31 · The payoff ending: the picture, the score, the landmark and every way on at the same weight (S-3, #474)

**Amends** D-134 (the end was "a simple one", the payoff "a later slice") and D-185 (the end card).

- **What the end shows.** The stage keeps its last picture and the tray says "The picture you built" over the recap's three captions. Under them: the score of this Scroll (sparks, the best combo from two, how the Final call went), the landmark card (gold or plain, with its name), the peek at the next Part, then "Done for today" and "Something new" side by side at the same size, then another angle if one was admitted, then the "Small print" chip. A Final call still open says so and offers "Play the Final call" instead of a landmark.
- **Nothing moves by itself.** Every way on is a button, a key or a swipe the person makes. A test waits on the end and finds the same Scroll and no further request.
- **Done for today.** It opens "A good place to stop." in the tray with what was earned today and no streak or guilt. "Open your Universe" and "Close" (the same move as before) are there, and so is "Back to the end". The button that was pressed is gone, so focus moves to the panel, and "Back to the end" returns it to the start of the tray.
- **Small print opens where it stands.** The chip opens the limits aside inside the tray, and says whether it is open (`aria-expanded`). It used to jump up the text to the aside's stop, away from the end.
- **The peek is the next Part's title.** The context carries no teaser, so none is invented (D-072: only real numbers and text).
- **Honest about where the landmark is.** The card does not say it flew to the Universe: the overlay is S-4 (D-084, D-089). "Open your Universe" opens the Universe and promises nothing about it.
- **Reduced motion.** There is no flight to skip: the landmark card is drawn in its final state, and the picture follows the tilt at once. Haptics are not built (the app has no haptics module).
- **Words.** "You learned" and mastery words stay out (D-072). The card shows no points beyond the sparks, and no rank.

Confirmed by: `play.test.tsx` (the score row, gold and plain cards, the paused and waiting notes, the open Final call, Small print, Done for today), `stage-reader.test.tsx`, `payoff-smoke.spec.ts` (Seasons Part 1 to the end at 390 × 844 and 1440 × 900, 320 × 640, reduced motion, axe, no auto-advance, a reload that keeps the landmark, recording paused).

## D-194 · 2026-10-31 · Four more drawing kits give eight Earth & sky and Space recipes a picture (S-8a, #479)

**Amends** D-120 ("One kit exists in this version") and D-125 ("A new kit is a new id and a new decision"). **Builds on** D-124 and D-133.

- **Four kits, each its own id and its own closed words.** `tides.v1`, `greenhouse.v1`, `star.v1` and `transit.v1`. Earth & sky is unchanged. A kit is a few words the writer may choose and nothing else; code derives the rest (D-195).
- **A stage names its kit.** `Stage` is one union over the five kits, and every state of a stage holds that kit's words and no others. A stored Seasons document is unchanged: the same kit id, the same state shape, the same hash.
- **Eight recipes get a kit** (`stageKit` in the library plan, with a note in the file saying why the kit draws the subject):

| Kit             | Recipes                                                    | Why it draws the subject                                                                                                            |
| --------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `tides.v1`      | `tides`, `tides-spring-neap`                               | The Moon's pull, the two bulges and spring and neap alignment are what the briefs ask for.                                          |
| `greenhouse.v1` | `greenhouse`, `greenhouse-radiation`, `greenhouse-balance` | Sunlight in, infrared out, a layer that returns some, balance and the first moments of imbalance, and a layer that makes no energy. |
| `star.v1`       | `stars-fusion`, `stars-lifecycle`                          | Gravity against pressure, mass against lifetime, and the stages of a low-mass and a high-mass star without years.                   |
| `transit.v1`    | `exoplanet-transit`                                        | A planet crossing its star and the dip in the light, small against large. The wobble method is told in words only.                  |

- **Left without a kit, and why.** `tides-coasts` (basin shape and resonance are not a few words and cannot be derived honestly), `stars-colour-brightness` (a chart of two measures, a later block), `exoplanet-habitable-zone` (the zone's edges depend on a model, so it needs its own kit), `exoplanet-kinds` (a comparison of mass), `greenhouse-limits` (its subject is the audit of the blanket analogy, an argument about a comparison, which a picture of a layer cannot draw and could be taken to endorse), the three black-hole recipes (`star.v1` draws a massive star's end but not which remnant it leaves, so it cannot draw a black hole honestly) and `seasons-elsewhere` (a kit for other worlds, which D-125 left to S-8, is not built here).
- **Not marked `scrollV2` yet.** A recipe produces a v2 Scroll only when a person marks it (D-137), with its misconception and landmark. These eight are measured first: the runbook prints the spike line (`SKY_SPIKE_TOPICS_V2`), and the spike draws each with its own kit. Until a recipe is marked, production still writes its v1 Scroll.
- **The writer prompt is `scroll-writer.v17`.** The rules every stage shares stay in the prompt. What the picture draws, its words and what code decides come from the kit's own guide, so the prompt and the enums cannot drift. The Earth & sky text is unchanged.
- **The Final call binding stays Earth & sky's.** No new kit offers a control. A bound Final call on any other stage is `final_call_binding_invalid`.

Confirmed by: `scroll-model` `v2/kits/*.test.ts`, `producer/v2/kits-compile.test.ts`, `v2/kits-fixtures.test.ts`; `library.test.ts` (which recipe has which kit); `spike-v2/topics.test.ts` (the eight, with the runbook line), `spike-v2/run.test.ts` (the spike accepts a fixture answer drawn with each kit), `writer-v2.test.ts`; `kit-stage-smoke.spec.ts` (each kit's stage in the reader, axe at 390 × 844 and 1440 × 900).

## D-195 · 2026-10-31 · The physics of each new kit is derived by code from a few words (S-8a, #479)

**Builds on** D-124, which did this for the seasons, and D-194.

- **The rule for every kit.** The writer chooses words. Code settles them (a word the view does not draw is set aside), derives every physical fact, draws it and writes the text version from the same numbers. A state has no field in which a contradiction could be written, and each kit's tests compare its numbers with a computation the kit does not use.
- **Tides** (`view`, `moonAt`, `focus`). Each body raises a wave with two crests, the Moon's of height 1 and the Sun's 0.46 of that. The combined tide is the sum of the two waves, so new and full Moon give the sum (a spring tide, 1.46) and the quarters the difference (a neap tide, 0.54), with the crests along the line to the Moon. The Moon's pull is Newton's law at the near side, the centre and the far side, 60 Earth radii away. Test: the sum is sampled every 0.05 degrees round the planet and its tallest crest is compared; the law is written again as (D/(D-1))².
- **Greenhouse** (`view`, `layer`, `change`, `focus`). The one-layer model: a layer absorbs a share of the ground's infrared (none, 0.4 or 0.8, illustrative) and sends out as much as it absorbs, half up and half down. Settled, the ground sends up `absorbed / (1 - share / 2)`, which is more than it takes up, and exactly as much infrared leaves to space as the sunlight the ground takes up (the share reflected at once, about 0.3, never reaches the ground, so it is not what leaves). Just after the layer thickens the ground still sends up what it did, so less leaves than the ground takes up. The ground's warmth follows the fourth root of its emission. The layer lets sunlight through and the text says so, with a note that the real atmosphere does not. Test: the ground is warmed in small steps until it settles, and its first step after a thickening equals the imbalance.
- **Star** (`view`, `balance`, `mass`, `stage`, `focus`). Gravity and pressure equal hold the size, otherwise the star shrinks or swells. A main-sequence star lives for its fuel over its output: mass over mass to the power 3.5, so mass to the power -2.5; its radius goes as mass to the power 0.8. Masses are classes (half, one and ten Suns), never shown as figures. A low or Sun-like star ends as a white dwarf and only a high-mass star in a supernova, so the Sun cannot be drawn exploding. Test: lifetime from fuel and output, the order of sizes and lifetimes, and the ending for every mass.
- **Transit** (`view`, `planet`, `at`, `focus`). The planet is a disc of 0.1 or 0.2 of the star's width, crossing its middle. The share of the star it covers is the overlap of two discs, and the deepest dip is the square of the width, so twice the width is four times the depth. A planet blocks light from the first touch to the last, and is drawn in front only then. The dip is enlarged in the graph and the sizes are illustrative, and the text says so. Test: the overlap is added up strip by strip over thin slices of the two discs.
- **A focus is a promise.** The text version says "the picture draws the eye to ...", so a focus survives settling only for a part the view draws, and every such focus has a ring in the picture. The ring for the bulge facing the Moon sits on the Moon's side at every phase (the combined tide's axis is a line and has no side). Test: every settled state of every new kit is drawn in jest and a ring is found exactly when the focus is not `none`.
- **Text versions.** Each is built from the settled state and the same numbers, mentions only what the view draws and never gives a figure. Wording that would teach a wrong picture is tested out: no "lid", "blanket" or "traps" for the layer, no black hole or "explodes" for a Sun-like star, no coast, place or hour for the tides.

Confirmed by: `v2/kits/tides.test.ts`, `greenhouse.test.ts`, `star.test.ts` and `transit.test.ts` (each against its own independent computation); `v2/kits/kits.test.ts` (every state of every kit is settled once, described within the document's limit and accepted by the schema); `packages/ui` `kit-scenes.test.ts` (each picture's numbers agree with the physics) and `kit-focus.test.tsx` (a ring for every focus, and the bulge rings on the Moon's side).

## D-196 · 2026-10-31 · The writer's stage change is words by key, and the recipe's kit says which (S-8a, #479)

**Amends** D-120 (the stage as "closed word lists of the Earth & sky kit").

- **The shape.** `scroll-authoring.v2` still carries a stage change as a small object of words, but its keys and words depend on the recipe's kit, so the schema now holds only the shape (short words by key). The authoring rules check the keys and words against the kit (`stage_choice_invalid`, with a fixed repair hint) at the writer's item. The writer's prompt lists the kit's keys and words, from the same table.
- **Total over bad output.** Assembling ignores a key or word the kit does not have, so a refused answer never throws and its other findings are still reported. The validator of the finished document is unchanged: a stored state must be settled and its text must be the kit's own.
- **The first picture.** Every kit has a `view` key, and the first stop must set it (`stage_first_state_missing`).
- **No new reason for old kits.** An Earth & sky answer on an Earth & sky recipe is judged exactly as before. The old "unknown word" findings now arrive as `stage_choice_invalid`.

Confirmed by: `producer/v2/compile.test.ts` (an invented word, key and old word are refused at the item), `kits-compile.test.ts` (the words of Earth & sky and of the other kits are refused on a recipe of another kit; a stage on a plain recipe; a bound Final call on another kit), `writer-v2.test.ts`.

## D-190 · 2026-10-31 · Composer policy v1: a pull-up leaves the topic more often (S-5, #476)

After D-131, D-132 and D-136 a vertical move no longer offers the series or the related move, but 13 topics still led to their own neighbours: Intent (1 for any topic the source is on) weighs 0.35 and nothing counts a topic the person has just read. The policy is versioned data, so this is a new label and not an edit.

**Amends** D-034 (the vertical score). **Builds on** D-131, D-132 and D-136, which stay as they are.

- **Label.** `composer-policy.v1`. A decision replays under the policy it names (`policyOf(decision.policyVersion)`), and `persistComposerDecision` checks the saved bytes against that replay. Migration 0081 widens the pin of 0017 to v0 and v1. Every stored decision names v0 and replays byte for byte: 60 labelled fixture snapshots of every move are composed under v0 and compared with hashes taken from the code before this change.
- **v1 is off until its numbers are chosen.** The policy new decisions are made under is configuration: `KS_COMPOSER_POLICY`, `v0` or `v1`, default **`v0`**, any other value refused at start (`composerPolicy` in the API and the worker configuration; the pure `composeEncounter` also defaults to v0). Without it, a deploy would store v1 decisions made with numbers nobody has read against real inputs. Dev stayed `v0` until the numbers were chosen (below); the follow-up S-5b (#476) sets them and sets dev to `v1` in Ansible (`ks_v2_composer_policy`), and the live env file is changed by hand after the deploy. Replay does not read the setting: a stored decision names its own policy, and the command always runs both. Tested with each setting: the config parsers, the providers, and a navigation on real PostgreSQL that stores its decision under the label the setting names and replays it.
- **Numbers (vertical).** Intent 0.35 to 0.30. A new recent-topic penalty of 0.05. Interest 0.20, Format 0.15, Novelty 0.15, SemanticFit 0.15 and the repeated-explanation penalty 0.25 are unchanged. Horizontal moves are unchanged. Variant and return moves do not ask for the penalty.
- **The recent-topic share.** The newest three encounters within the 24 hours, weighted 3, 2 and 1 out of 6. An encounter counts when it shares a non-incidental topic with the candidate. A shorter or missing history counts as no repeat (neutral). It is in [0, 1] and is stored in the decision as the feature `recentTopic`. A v0 decision carries no such feature.
- **Where the topics come from.** The routing history now carries `topicIds` for each recent encounter, from the frozen attribution of its recording segment. The legacy lineage keeps no topics, so such an encounter is neutral.
- **How the numbers were chosen (2026-10-31, from the dev replay).** The first numbers were Intent 0.15 (the change map's figure) and a penalty of 0.25 (as heavy as a repeated idea), and they were provisional. The coordinator ran `composer replay-policy` on dev over the 1076 stored v0 decisions since 24 October, of which 640 were vertical moves with their source among the candidates and were replayed. The first numbers left the topic almost always: the share of first offers on the source's topic fell from 0.641 (v0) to 0.006, far beyond the fall of 10 to 30 points that was aimed at. A pull-up should usually bring something different, and swipe-left is what keeps the series, so the topic should leave often but not always. The choice is **Intent 0.30 and a penalty of 0.05**: first offers on the source's topic fall from 0.641 to 0.392, a drop of 25 points, and about 61 in 100 first offers move to another subject.

  Replay of 640 decisions (counts only; shares of first offers on the source's topic, and for the sweep also of first offers on another Jev subject and of all offers on the source's topic):

  | Policy (Intent / penalty)       | Same topic, first offer | Another subject, first offer | Same topic, all offers |
  | ------------------------------- | ----------------------- | ---------------------------- | ---------------------- |
  | stored, as served               | 0.711                   |                              |                        |
  | v0 (0.35 / none)                | 0.641                   |                              |                        |
  | 0.35 / 0.05                     | 0.534                   | 0.466                        | 0.343                  |
  | **0.30 / 0.05 (chosen)**        | **0.392**               | **0.608**                    | **0.286**              |
  | 0.30 / 0.07                     | 0.317                   |                              |                        |
  | 0.35 / 0.10                     | 0.372                   |                              |                        |
  | 0.25 / 0                        | 0.461                   |                              |                        |
  | 0.25 / 0.05                     | 0.258                   |                              |                        |
  | 0.15 / 0.25 (the first numbers) | 0.006                   |                              |                        |

  Sticky format was kept in all 640 decisions under every policy, so the format rule is not what moves these numbers. The replay names the series and format the person was in as today's assembly would, so it compares weights and not the rules of D-131 and D-132. The penalty is small on purpose: 0.07 with the same Intent already takes the share to 0.317.

- **The replay command.** `composer replay-policy --since <date> [--limit <n>] [--intent-weight <x>] [--recent-topic-penalty <x>] [--sweep]` re-runs the retained vertical decisions since a date, newest first, under v0 and v1, on the same inputs.
