A stored input is first named as today's assembly would name it (the series and format the person is in, the topics of recent encounters, all taken from candidates it already holds), so the report compares weights and not the rules of D-131 and D-132. It prints counts and shares only: first offers on the source's topic, first offers in its series, sticky format kept or fallen back, and first offers on another Jev subject (each a possible Jev call under D-058). It names no person, session, asset, title or text. `--sweep` replays v1 over a grid of Intent weights and penalties.
- **Changing the numbers.** The numbers of v1 were edited in place by S-5b (#476), and no `composer-policy.v2` was needed: no world had ever run `v1` (dev ran `v0` throughout, and the default is `v0`), so no decision named v1 and none can stop replaying. From the first v1 decision stored, new numbers need `composer-policy.v2` and a migration that widens the pin.
- **The Planner's lift is smaller under v1.** A plan adds `0.15 x agreement` to the Intent feature of a candidate that ties for the best Intent, and Intent is then weighed. The most a plan can add to a vertical score falls from 0.15 x 0.35 = 0.0525 to 0.15 x 0.30 = 0.045 (and from 0.02625 to 0.0225 for a topic match). With the chosen weight that is a small fall: Intent, which is the source's topic, counts a little less, and so does a plan that rides on it. A plan still only breaks a tie among the best Intent matches and never overtakes a better match. `PLAN_LIFT` is unchanged.
- **Also in v1.** The Format feature reads the play-level cell (D-192) and exploration spreads its picks over the play levels of the Scrolls in the pool (D-192).
- **Reels and Scrolls stay equal in exploration.** A Reel has no play, so it has no play level here, and it is not pooled with the plain Scrolls. The draw takes a member of the pool uniformly, as v0 does, and only when that member is a Scroll replaces it by a play level present among the pool's Scrolls and then a Scroll of it. So the share of Reels and of Scrolls in an exploration is exactly what the pool holds, with or without stage and game Scrolls, and a Reel is never squeezed out by them. The recorded probability is exact for both. (A first version drew a level first over the whole pool and put Reels with the plain Scrolls, which would have given Reels a smaller share as soon as stage and game Scrolls appeared.) Tested by a property (the Reels of any pool hold exactly their share), by the exact share of every member of a small pool, and by the recorded probability against the observed frequency of 4000 seeds (below).
- **Verified.** Pure and property tests (features in [0, 1], the same inputs give the same decision hash, the label replays), the 60 frozen v0 hashes, the exact cost of the topic the person is on (the Intent given up plus its share of the penalty), a case that stays on the topic under both policies and one that stays under v0 and leaves under v1, the setting tests above, the wiring tests of the API and the worker (each builds its compose provider from its own configuration token), and the replay with its skipped cases and its output holding no id, title or text. The probability a decision records for its first offer is compared with how often that offer was first over 4000 seeds, with a goodness of fit over every member of the pool, for Reels only, one level, Scrolls of mixed levels and Reels with Scrolls of mixed levels, under v0 and under v1; the share of each member is also checked against its exact value. Each fails when the rule is removed, and replacing a Scroll's share by a uniform one turns the mixed-level cases and the exact-share test red.

## D-191 · 2026-10-31 · activity.v1: a continuation credits its source only when the source was read (S-5, #476)

V3 lets a person swipe to Part 2 from anywhere, at once. `activity.v0` gives the exposure a deliberate series or related move leaves a flat +1.5 (H), more than a fully read q of 0.6. Parts 1, 2 and 3 swiped through in seconds would add about 4.5 to the topic's interest without any reading.

**Amends** the activity rule of the blueprint (§8, "Positive activity and separate channels"). Nothing else in it changes.

- **The rule.** H counts only when the exposure the move left has coverage of at least 0.5 in a measured reading, the bar a reading must also clear to qualify. With no measured reading there is nothing to clear it with, so no credit. The sum, its cap of 3 and the other channels are unchanged.
- **Which coverage.** That of the reading source behind each channel (the legacy and the session source each use their own), from `evidence.encounter_evidence`. Nothing new is read from the observations. A reading that arrives after the move re-projects the exposure through the existing qualification revision, and the credit appears then.
- **Version.** New revisions are `activity.v1` (migration 0082 widens the pin of 0019) and name it in their input vector. Old revisions stay `activity.v0` and are not rewritten. A credit already committed in an exposure's frozen base (the recording credit carried from an earlier generation) stays: a base is never rewritten. The coverage certificate that lets formation count a qualified reading accepts both labels.
- **What the navigation records.** `experience.navigation_transitions` keeps `activity.v0`. It records the fact that a deliberate move happened, which has not changed. The gate is applied when the credit is worked out.
- **Old exposures re-project, and that is intended.** The input vector of a revision now names `activityPolicy`, so every exposure's vector differs from the one stored under v0. At its next trigger (a new qualification revision, a Like or Save change that links to it, or an explicit reconcile pass) the exposure is projected again under v1. A swipe-through credit that v0 gave and that is not in the exposure's committed base is then dropped: its contribution leaves `topic_contributions` and, by the same same-time replacement, `topic_context`, and the topic's interest falls by what the swipe had added. The revision v0 wrote stays as it was. This corrects interest that came from no reading, which is the purpose of the change. It is not a bug and not a migration: nothing is rewritten at deploy, it happens as exposures are touched.
- **Effect.** A swipe through three parts with no reading adds 0 (it was 4.5). A part read through and then left still earns its 1.5.
- **Verified.** Pure and property tests of the gate under both rules (monotone in coverage; the swipe-through sum is 4.5 under v0 and 0 under v1), and an integration test on real PostgreSQL that swipes from a Scroll with no reading, with too little, and with enough. It fails with the gate removed.

## D-192 · 2026-10-31 · Format is learned per play level, from reading only (S-5, #476)

V3 Scrolls differ a lot in how much they ask of the reader, so a person who reads essays and one who plays calls would blur into one Format per topic and kind.

**Amends** D-034 (the Format feature) and the Evidence topic-format counts. **Builds on** D-063, which stands.

- **The level.** Content derives `traits.playLevel` from the stored document, the same for every reader: `plain` (every v1 Scroll, every Reel, and a v2 Scroll with no stage and no call), `stage` (a stage, or a single call) and `game` (at least two calls, one of them the Final call). It is read when a candidate is read and is not stored, because a revision is immutable. The same traits carry the reading minutes, the public text of the myth and the series size, which only Jev uses (D-193).
- **The cells.** `evidence.topic_format_cells` keeps measured and qualified counts per (topic, kind, level) beside the (topic, kind) counts that `topic_context` keeps over every level, which are the parent. Migration 0082 starts every existing parent count as a plain cell, because every encounter before it was read as plain. `topic_contributions.play_level` remembers the level an exposure's counts went to, so a correction moves them from one cell to another. A process from before the migration counts the parent and not the cell; counts never go below zero.
- **Reading only.** A qualified response is `q >= 0.6`, as it always was. Correctness, the option picked, sparks and tiers are not inputs, and a person who plays and gets everything wrong is not penalised. Three guards hold the wall (D-063): the dependency rules keep `play` away from every module that ranks, plans or remembers; the snapshot schema and the allow-list that Personal Context copies cells through take four fields and refuse a fifth; and a test reads one Scroll twice with a right pick and a wrong one and finds the same cells.
- **The feature.** For a candidate of level L: `(Q + m * prior) / (O + m)`, with `Q` and `O` the cell's counts, prior strength `m = 4`, and `prior = (1 + Q_rest) / (2 + O_rest)`, the rate of the person's **other** levels: the (topic, kind) parent less this cell. The parent counts every level, this one included, so shrinking toward the whole parent would count the cell's encounters twice and push the value past what the evidence says (six qualified in one level only would read 0.95, where the parent alone says 0.875). Taking the cell out of its own prior does not: that case reads 0.8, between even odds and the parent. A cell with no encounters equals its parent, a cell with few stays near the other levels, and it moves to its own rate as encounters accumulate. It is in [0, 1] and between the prior and the cell's own rate. A person with history in one level only is shrunk toward even odds for that level and reads the parent for the others. Unknown history stays neutral (0.5). Policy v0 reads the parent only. A process from before migration 0082 counts the parent and not the cell, so the rest is never taken below zero.
- **Why m = 4.** A topic rarely has more than a handful of encounters per level. At 4 a cell needs about four of its own to move halfway to its own rate, so one game Scroll does not decide a person's taste: two people with the same other levels who each read one game Scroll, one qualified and one not, are 0.2 apart; with eight each, two thirds apart.
- **Exploration.** Under v1 a Scroll drawn by the exploration branch is replaced by a draw of a play level present among the pool's Scrolls and then a Scroll of it, so a catalogue with few game Scrolls still explores them. Reels are not a play level and keep the share of the pool (D-190). The recorded probability is the exact one.
- **Not built.** The change map's weak per-person prior for a cold cell (a share of game Scrolls qualified over the last encounters). A cold cell reads its topic's parent.
- **Verified.** Pure and property tests (the fit is in [0, 1]; a cell with few encounters is near its parent; two people with the same parent diverge only as encounters accumulate; exploration of a lone game Scroll is about half of explorations under v1 and about 1 in 21 under v0), unit tests of the traits for every document shape, and integration tests on real PostgreSQL for the migration (old counts become plain cells), the cell counts of a real reading, a correction between levels, and the wall (a right and a wrong pick give the same cells).

## D-193 · 2026-10-31 · jev.2 describes an item by its public facts, and never by play (S-5, #476)

The writer of V3 can make two Scrolls that bust the same myth, and Jev could not tell: it saw only a title, a summary and facets.

**Amends** D-034 (what Jev is given). **Builds on** D-058 and D-063.

- **What it is given.** For each item: the kind, how much play it has (in words), the reading minutes, `Series: part n of m` when the series is known, and the public text of the myth it corrects. The structural lines come first and the summary takes what is left of 900 characters, so a long summary never cuts the myth.
- **What it is told.** These describe the item and are not criteria. A candidate that corrects the myth the current step corrects has high redundancy. A candidate in the current step's series is never offered (D-131), so series membership earns nothing.
- **Never play.** Which option anyone picked, whether it was right, sparks and tiers are not in the context, and the context has no field for them. A test searches the context for them. Code still owns the play-level feature (D-192); Jev keeps its three criteria and their weights.
- **Version.** `jev.2`. A stored assessment made under `jev.1` is not reused, so the first preparation after the deploy asks again.
- **Call rate.** The bound of D-058 is unchanged: at most one call per subject (language and primary topic) per ten minutes, within the background share of the budgets. A pull-up that leaves the topic more often makes more subjects, so more calls are possible. `composer replay-policy` counts the first offers that move to another subject, which is the upper bound on new calls per move. If the rate matters on dev, the knob is the refresh wait, not the context.
- **Verified.** Unit tests of the description (the facts, a plain Scroll, a Reel, a long summary, no total), of the instructions and of the version. One real `jev.2` run on dev, checking the `model` field, is listed in the PR for the coordinator.

## D-172 · 2026-10-31 · Two more emblem glyphs (globe, cell), a kit role that knows what each glyph means, and no signatures in backdrops (R-4b, #490)

**Amends** D-170 (the glyph vocabulary grows from fourteen to sixteen, and the kit role is `station-kit.2`) and D-166 (the house style of a painted backdrop).

- **What a first run on dev showed.** The kit model gave Earth the ringed planet three times, so Earth and Space shared an emblem. It gave Human body the centre circle, which is a pitch, and its backdrop scene said "a pitch with a centre circle". Two real History backdrops each carried a scribbled signature in a corner.
- **Two glyphs.** `globe` is the Earth seen from space, with flat continents cut out of a disc. `cell` is a living cell: a round membrane, a nucleus with its nucleolus and two organelles. Their backdrop scenes are "the Earth seen from space" and "a living cell". They are drawn once in `shapes.ts`, so the emblem, a resident's mark and the station sprite all have them. Both are drawn at 0.90 (globe) and 0.88 (cell) of their first size, the largest at which they stay inside the face of every frame, the diamond's being the smallest; at full size 10.8% (globe) and 17.0% (cell) of their accent pixels lay over the diamond's border.
- **Known gap, not fixed here.** Some older glyphs also cross a frame's border in the narrow frames: in the diamond, 20.8% of the wave's accent pixels, 16.1% of the hourglass's and 12.8% of the mountain's (measured by rasterising at 240 pixels against the inner face). The existing raster test only checks the outermost two pixels of the image, so it does not see this. Scaling them is a later change to the first fourteen drawings.
- **The vocabulary only grows.** New words go at the end of the list. A stored kit that names an older word stays valid, and so does the wire shape; no migration, because the database holds a kit as JSON and checks no glyph. A room with no approved kit wears `defaultKit`, which still picks from the first fourteen glyphs (`DEFAULT_GLYPHS`), so adding words never changes the look of such a room. A default has no topic, so it never needs the new two.
- **`station-kit.2`.** The role now lists every glyph with a line on what it stands for (`GLYPH_MEANINGS`, complete by type): `ringed-planet` for space and astronomy, `globe` for Earth and its processes, `cell` for the human body and biology, `centre-circle` for sport only, `book` for history and reading, and so on. It tells the model to choose the glyph whose meaning is closest to this room's topic. The model sees one room, so the role cannot ask it to differ from another; the lines are what keep Earth and Space apart. The example answer in the instructions uses a neutral glyph (`gear`), not one the model had over-picked. The version is recorded on every proposed kit. Code still validates the closed vocabulary; the lines only guide the choice. Kits already stored under `station-kit.1` stay as they are until an operator asks for a new one.
- **No signatures.** The house style now says: "No signature, no artist's mark, no monogram, no stamp, nothing written in the corners." It is a prompt, so it lowers the chance but cannot promise it; the operator's review (D-171) is still what keeps one out.

Confirmed by: `room-identity.test.ts` (the new glyphs and the first fourteen are accepted, in the same order), `backdrop-prompt.test.ts` (the ban and the two scenes; the test fails with the ban removed), `kit-role.test.ts` (every glyph has a meaning and the instructions carry it; the test fails with one meaning blank), `kit.test.ts` (a default never wears the new glyphs), `emblem-raster.test.ts` (each glyph is drawn and none alike; the globe and the cell, drawn against each frame's inner face at 160 pixels, have no accent pixel over a border, and the test fails at their unscaled size), `rooms-face.test.ts` (the prompt sent carries the signature ban).

## D-197 · 2026-10-31 · A served v2 Scroll can be made again, three takes to a production version (S-6a, #477)

**Builds on** D-129 (takes) and D-138 (the review gate). **Amends** D-129, where a take could follow only a rejection.

- **The gap.** A v2 recipe whose first take was released could not be produced again. A take above 1 was refused unless every earlier take had been rejected, so a Scroll with a wrong answer found after release, or a better writer, had no way back in.
- **The command.** `content regenerate --only <keys> [--dry-run]` sends the next take of each named recipe whose v2 Scroll is served. It is the same `content.produce-scroll` job as any other: the writer, the validator, one repair, the answer check (D-198) and the review gate. The new take is a new revision of the same asset, stored **held**. The served revision stays served until `content release`. `--only` is required, so regeneration is never a sweep of the library.
- **When it sends.** The recipe must be marked v2 and have a served v2 Scroll (`available`). A running take is sent again to resume it. Everything else is skipped with the reason: `not_v2` (v1 recipes and orders publish with no read, so they are never regenerated), `not_published` (`produce-library` makes the first one), `held` (decide the waiting revision first, whichever version made it), `spent`, `stopped` (an attempt of unknown outcome: reconcile it first). `production-status` shows the same words as `regeneration`, with `takesLeft`.
- **The rule in code.** A take above 1 of a v2 recipe may follow a take that was rejected or whose Scroll was released, never one still held and never one of unknown outcome. Other recipes are as before.
- **The budget is per production version.** A recipe has at most three takes to a version, so a recipe cannot loop paid production: a fourth take is refused, and a take above 3 never reaches the table. A take sends at most six requests (D-198), so a version costs a recipe at most 18.
- **A writer bump gives fresh takes, because it bumps the version.** I counted per production version, not per writer version recorded on each job, so no column or migration is needed: the job's identity already includes the production version, and the status already reads only the current version's jobs. The cost is a rule: whenever the v2 writer's instructions or the job's steps change, `SCROLL_PRODUCTION_V2_VERSION` changes with them. `versions.test.ts` pairs the two, so a writer bump without it fails. This slice moves the version from `scroll-production.v15` to `v17`: the answer check joined the job (D-198) and the writer moved to `scroll-writer.v17` (D-196). There is no v16, because writer v16 never ran with the check. The v15 jobs stay as history and do not count, so the four Seasons recipes have three fresh takes each.
- **Not done.** Nothing releases or rejects for you, and nothing regenerates by itself. Quartermaster still never writes a v2 recipe (D-138).

Confirmed by: `wave-tooling.test.ts` on real PostgreSQL (a served recipe is `available` with two takes left and a v1 or unpublished one is not; the new take is a held revision 2 while revision 1 stays served; a held revision blocks the next take; take 3 after a release; a rejected third take leaves the takes `spent`; a fourth take, and the third asked for again, send nothing; three rejected takes of an earlier version leave three fresh ones; a v1 recipe is never regenerated), `serving.test.ts` (the standing and the plan), `versions.test.ts` (the pair), and `release/review-operator.test.ts` (`regenerate` and `production-status` from the built release). Shown failing when a take could not follow a release, when a fourth take was allowed, and when the writer run was not named by the job.

## D-198 · 2026-10-31 · A second model checks that each call's answer key agrees with the call, and code decides (S-6a, #477)

**Builds on** D-123 item 3, D-124 and D-138. **Amends** D-129, whose bound was two model requests a take.

- **The gap.** Two of the four Seasons first drafts had a wrong answer key that no validator saw (#472). A call whose marked answer contradicts its own explanation is well formed.
- **The role.** `content.scroll-answer-check` (`scroll-answer-check.v1`) runs in the production job **after** the writer's answer has passed the validator and compiled, for a v2 library recipe only (the Scrolls that go through the review gate). It is shown, for each call and the Final call: the stop before it, the question, the lettered options, the marked letter, the explanation and the twist. It is shown no source passage: it judges whether the parts agree with each other, not whether the facts are right. That stays the operator's read. It replies `{"calls": [{"id", "consistent", "problem"?}]}`, one entry per call in order, `problem` exactly where `consistent` is false (8 to 300 characters), and nothing else. The shape is closed: a missing, repeated or unknown call, a stray field or a problem on a good call is refused as an invalid reply.
- **Code decides.** The model only proposes.
  - **All consistent.** The Scroll is stored held, with the verdicts on the job.
  - **A call flagged on the first draft.** The writer gets **one** repair, in a run of its own, with each flagged call named by its question, the checker's problem, and its own draft, as data. That repair is the writer's one repair: if the harness already repaired the draft for the validator, there is none left and the job ends rejected (`answer_check_flagged`). The repair has no repair of its own: if the validator refuses it, the job ends rejected (`check_repair_invalid_output`).
  - **The repaired draft is checked in turn.** All consistent: stored held. Still flagged: the job ends rejected (`answer_check_flagged`), nothing is stored and the take is spent.
  - **The checker gave nothing usable** (failed, refused, an attempt of unknown outcome, or a reply still invalid after the harness's one repair): the Scroll is stored held and marked **unchecked**, with the reason. Nothing is auto-released, because every v2 library Scroll is held anyway, and the operator is told to read every call. An attempt of unknown outcome stays in the ledger for `reasoning unknown`; it is never resent.
  - **Budget or quota spent before the check.** The job pauses and stays open, like the writer's. The writer's kept answer is read from the ledger on resume, so it is not paid for again.
- **The bound.** At most **six** paid requests a take: two writer requests (the draft and one repair, from the validator or from the check) and the checker once for each of the two drafts, each with at most one repair of an unusable reply. `REQUEST_BOUND_V2_PER_TOPIC` is 6; `requestBound` in a result and `content regenerate` print it. A v1 recipe and an order stay at two.
- **No model call in a transaction or on HTTP.** The checker runs in the worker, outside every transaction, through the production harness: every request is an attempt in the ledger and counts against the production caps. Budget class `interactive`, because the job waits on it. It is a role of its own, so its failure brake and role cap never touch the writer's.
- **The claim outlasts the worst take.** A take can chain six requests one after the other, so the job's claim in `content.produce-scroll` is no longer 900 s. `PRODUCE_SCROLL_CLAIM_SECONDS` is the request bound (6) times the largest request timeout the transport accepts (300 s, `MINIMAX_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MAX_MS`) plus 300 s for the research fetch and the short database steps: 2,100 s. It is derived, so raising the largest accepted timeout raises the claim, and a test pins it. A claim lost to a worker that died is retried after that time, not before. The migrate command applies the new time to the queue (`updateQueue`), and it holds for jobs sent after that; a job already queued keeps the 900 s it was sent with.
- **The runs.** The checker has a run for each draft, and the repair is a writer run, all derived from the job (`stableUuid`), so a redelivery asks the ledger instead of paying again. All of them close in the transaction that stores or ends the job. When the repair's answer is stored, the job names that run as its writer run, so the provenance, the receipts and the lineage check agree on who wrote the Scroll. The job is renamed inside the publication, after the publication has been accepted, so a publication that is refused (a revoked scope, a run that is no longer open) leaves the job exactly as it was and the retry answers from the ledger. The superseded first draft's receipt stays on the job with its run id.
- **Where the operator sees it.** The verdicts are receipts on the private job (`answer_check`, with the model's short problems: an operator-only note, never a reader's). A flagged call keeps what the checker was shown of it: its question, its options in the order a reader sees them, which option the key marks, and its explanation. It keeps nothing else of the draft (no other call, no stop), because a flagged first draft is not stored once the writer repairs it, and a refused take stores no draft at all, yet the operator needs to read what was flagged. `review-show` prints each flagged call under the header (what the first draft was flagged for, or "NOT CHECKED"), and `production-status` shows the latest take's check (`passed`, `repaired`, `flagged`, `unchecked`) with those calls as `check.flags[].call` and `check.firstDraftFlags[].call`, so a refused take can be understood. `review-list` carries `answerCheck` with the ids and the problems but not the text of the calls: a list holds no body text. A revision made before the check says it was not run.
- **Limits.** The checker is a model's opinion about agreement. It can miss a wrong key that the explanation repeats, and it can flag a right one: that costs a take, of which a recipe has three. It is a help for the read, never a substitute.
- **Production version.** The job's steps changed, so `SCROLL_PRODUCTION_V2_VERSION` is `scroll-production.v17` (D-197).

Confirmed by: `answer-check.test.ts` (what the checker is shown and not shown, the closed answer shape, the repair's role and items, reading the receipts back), `answer-check.test.ts` of the domain (the summary), `review-text.test.ts`, and `wave-tooling.test.ts` on real PostgreSQL (consistent passes with one writer request and one check; a flagged call is repaired once with the problem as its hint, the repaired draft is checked and stored, the job names the repair run and publication still attributes it, and all four runs close; flagged twice is rejected with nothing stored, two writer requests and two checks; a draft the validator already repaired is rejected at once; a refused repair ends the job; a checker that is unknown, failed or invalid leaves the Scroll held and unchecked, shown in the list and the text, never released; a spent quota pauses and the resume sends no second writer request). A refused publication after a repair leaves the job naming its own writer run and the retry stores the repair; a flagged call keeps its question, options, marked answer and explanation (and nothing else of the draft) on the job, in `review-show` and in `production-status`, and a list shows none of it; the claim is six requests at the largest timeout plus a margin (`versions.test.ts`). Shown failing when a flag was ignored, when a second repair was given, when an unusable check counted as a pass, when the repair's run was not named by the job, when the job was renamed before publication was decided, when the flagged call was dropped, when the claim went back to 900 s, and when the check's runs were left open.

## D-199 · 2026-10-31 · The first waves: thirty-five recipes become plain v2 Scrolls with calls, no myth, and a landmark (S-6a, #477)

**Builds on** D-125, D-137 and D-188. **Amends** D-138 ("Once every recipe is v2 ...") only in that Create now writes no recipe of these four systems.

- **Which recipes.** Every recipe of Human body, Computing, Machine learning and History, because none has a drawing kit (D-125): 9 + 9 + 12 + 5 = 35. Each is a `plain` v2 Scroll: stops and calls, no picture. A library test pins the list and that none has a kit, a Final call control or a myth.
- **Left for later.** The eight recipes S-8a gave a kit (D-194: tides, tides-spring-neap, greenhouse, greenhouse-radiation, greenhouse-balance, stars-fusion, stars-lifecycle, exoplanet-transit) are marked after their spike is read, as D-194 says. The Earth and Space recipes with no kit (tides-coasts, greenhouse-limits, stars-colour-brightness, exoplanet-habitable-zone, exoplanet-kinds and the three black-hole recipes) are not in the four systems named for this slice and are not marked here. A series must be marked whole (below), so the tides series (tides, tides-spring-neap, tides-coasts) and the greenhouse series (greenhouse-radiation, greenhouse-balance, greenhouse-limits) go together with their plain parts. Marking either group is a small edit each.
- **Series are whole.** Every series in the plan is wholly marked or not marked at all (a test says so), so `admit-continuity` can admit each whole. The seven series of these four systems are marked together, and the Seasons series already was. The Earth and Space series stay unmarked together until their recipes are.
- **Misconception: none for all thirty-five.** On 31 Oct I fetched every recipe's pages through the product's fetcher (35 recipes, 34 pages, a pool of 11 to 29 passages each) and searched the passages the writer would be offered for a statement that people hold a wrong idea: misconception, myth, common or popular belief, "often thought", "mistakenly", "contrary to", confusion, "misnomer", "in fact". Nothing states a belief as common. The nearest hits are not about a belief: "constant time does not mean independent of the problem size" (Time complexity), "revolution is a misnomer" (a historians' argument, Industrial Revolution), "gradient descent should not be confused with local search", "the term adaptive is problematic" (a naming remark). A myth needs a reviewed source that says people hold it (D-129), and the spike's placeholder myths (for example "the heart squeezes all four chambers at once") are stated as a belief by no page, so none could be reviewed. A Scroll with no myth is a narrative Scroll with calls; its first call has no common guess. A recipe may get a myth later, one at a time, with a source a person has read.
- **Landmarks.** Each recipe names one: short, plain, a thing the Scroll is about, never a claim about the reader ("mastery", "expert", "perfect" are refused by a test). Keys and names are unique across the library (a test). They are authored here and a person should confirm them.
- **Consequences.** Create commissions only v1 recipes, so a person's ask for a planet of these four systems is answered from the library as it stands and starts no production until the waves are released (D-138). The v1 Scrolls of these recipes keep serving until a v2 revision is released. The recipes' production version is `scroll-production.v17`, and `production-status` lists all thirty-five as `not_started` until a wave runs. The spike now runs these recipes exactly as the plan marks them.

| Recipe                     | System           | Page the choice was checked against             | Misconception | Landmark (key)                                    |
| -------------------------- | ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------- | ------------- | ------------------------------------------------- |

## D-155 · 2026-10-31 · "Call it together" reveals to those who locked, once the party has finished or two days have passed (v3 T-E, #485)

**Builds on** D-065 and D-152. It settles the reveal rule that the design ("after the party watched") and the change map ("waits until every member who shares their place has finished") leave open, and what happens to a member who never picks or never finishes.

- **A call is the first Call it of a part's Scroll.** Only a series thread has calls, and only a part whose Scroll has one; a Reel, a Scroll without a call and a single-item thread have none. The Final call is another moment (D-126, D-187).
- **Hidden until you lock.** Before they lock, a member sees the question, the options and how many have called ("2 of 4 have called"), never who. After they lock they see their own pick. Nobody sees another member's pick, or the right answer, before the reveal.
- **The reveal.** For a reader who has locked, the call is revealed when every member they can see who shares where they are has finished that part, or on the second UTC day after the first lock, whichever comes first, and only once someone has locked. Then the reader sees every pick they may see, who was right, why, and a right call earns a spark (shown on the card, not stored).
- **Nobody holds the others for ever.** The reveal never waits for a pick: a member who does not call is not waited for. A member who shares a place but never finishes holds the others for two days at most. A member who hides where they are, has no place, left, was removed, or is blocked either way with the reader is not waited for. If nobody shares a place, only the clock opens it. The two days are counted in calendar days (the pick stores a `date`, like a place, D-152), so a call opens between one and two days after the first lock. `REVEAL_AFTER_DAYS` in `social/domain/calls.ts` is the one number to change.
- **Calling is a guess made before the part.** A lock is refused (`closed`) once the call has been revealed, or once the member's own shared place says they are on that part or past it. A member who never locked sees "revealed without you" and no picks. A member who hides where they are can still read the Scroll first and then lock: the server reads nobody's Experience to stop it (that would make a place by another road), and a spark is display only.
- **A block hides in either direction.** The pick, the count and the progress of a member with a block either way are left out of the reader's view and do not hold the reader's reveal. The reveal can therefore come at different moments for two readers only because of a block.
- **A wrong call is a plot twist** (D-064): the warm second look, no spark, no minus, no count of misses.
- **A reveal that has happened stays, for a reader no block touches.** The first time a read or a lock finds a call revealed for the whole thread (the same rule with nobody masked by a block), the fact is recorded in `social.thread_call_reveals` (migration 0083: a thread and the call; a row's existence means "revealed", with no day, no person and no pick; it is declared in the erasure registry as kept with the thread). From then on the call is revealed to every member who locked and closed to every member who did not, whoever leaves, clears or joins afterwards, and the clock no longer matters. Before this, the earliest locker leaving or clearing moved the two-day clock back and hid the picks again. A read may write that one row, once, to a row of its own, and so may a lock that ends as "stands" or "closed", because the row records a fact about the thread that any read would record. A reveal that nobody read before things changed was never shown, so it does not count.
- **A block keeps the live rule.** The record is honoured only for a reader with no block, in either direction, with a member who takes part. A reader with one keeps the live rule, which leaves the blocked member out. Honouring the record for them would let the blocked member's lock day and progress move their card: it would turn a call they had not locked from open to closed (so they could no longer call), or reveal a call they had locked because of a day the blocked member locked on, and they could learn of the blocked member's moves by polling. So a blocked reader's card can go back to "locked" if the live conditions move back. That is accepted. Tests show that a blocked member's earlier lock, or a lock that opened the clock, changes neither the blocker's card nor their ability to call, that the blocker's cards are the same whether or not the blocked member called, and that breaking the fix turns them red.
- **A member who hides, or never reports a place, does not hold the reveal.** A member who hides where they are has no place to wait for, and a member who never reports one is the same to the server. So the others' picks, and that member's own, show at the pace of the members who share. The member's own call stays hidden from them until they have locked, as for everyone, but they may have read the part later than the rest. Taken for the owner; named in the PR.
- **Not built.** Calls in a single-item thread, on a Reel, or on the Final call; and no time of a lock or reveal is shown (the design's "REVEALED 12:41" is words here, as D-065 says).

Confirmed by: `calls.test.ts` (the pure rule: counts only before locking, the wait for those who share, hide, leave, removal and block, the clock, the day a call opens, no pick for a reader who did not lock, closing, ordering), `social-calls.test.ts` (the same over real PostgreSQL through the use cases, and a reveal that stays after the earliest locker leaves or clears, after a lower place is shown again, and for a member who never locked; and a block never lets another member move a reader's card), `thread-calls-smoke.spec.ts` (two browsers and a third member: a pick hidden until the party finished, the reveal, a member who hides, nothing sent while recording is paused; axe at 390 × 844 and 1440 × 900). Shown failing with the block mask, the wait for those still on their way, the record of the reveal and the block exception to it each broken once.

## D-156 · 2026-10-31 · A pick is stored in Social behind a wall like play's, and is judged when it is read (v3 T-E, #485)

**Builds on** D-063, D-082, D-083 and D-180. **Amends** D-180 ("Rooms and threads will share it later") by recording where thread play lives and how it is walled.

- **Where.** `social.thread_calls` (migration 0083): thread, account, asset, revision, stop, the option locked and the UTC day, one row per member and call, never updated (the first lock stands). A second table, `social.thread_call_reveals`, holds that a call revealed for a thread (D-155): a key and nothing else, no day, no person and no pick; it has a foreign key to the thread and is declared in the erasure registry as kept with the thread. It lives in Social, not in `play`: a lock needs the thread's lock, the member's standing, the places that decide the reveal and the block between two members, and leave, an ended friendship, Clear and deletion must remove it where Social removes a place. Play may import only identity, content and experience and nothing may import play (D-180), so a lock in `play` would have needed Social's thread frame, membership and every deletion path reached through the composition roots.
- **What is stored.** The option and the day. Not whether it was right: the right answer is read from the immutable revision when a reveal is built (content's `READ_SCROLL_PLAY_FACTS`, which now also gives a call's words), so no play result is kept beyond the pick. No receipt and no command ID: a lock is idempotent by its key (the first lock stands, a repeat answers `stands`), and a receipt would keep the exact time of the lock after leave and Clear (as D-152 found for places). A test finds no receipt row and no clock time in any table.
- **The wall.** The same two-sided guarantee as play's, built for where the table is. (1) `thread-pick-wall-only-listed-modules-import-social` in `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` is an allow-list: only Social, `feedback`, `rooms`, `scenarios` (which use its people, friend and thread reads, none of which carries a pick) and the erasure registry may import Social, so a module that ranks, plans or forms places, and a path through one, cannot reach it. (2) `social/wall.test.ts`: no production file outside Social names `social.thread_calls`, and no migration but Social's own (found by name) or operations script names it in SQL, so a view or a function over the picks cannot be added from outside. (3) `social-calls.test.ts` fingerprints every table outside Social and the receipts before and after locks, a reveal, reads, a leave and an export, and finds them equal; a reading (an exposure) does change the fingerprint, so the check can fail. `pnpm boundaries:probe` writes one forbidden import from each walled module, the reasoning engine, supply, play, identity, content and experience, and runs them again with the rule loosened.
- **Sparks.** A right call is one spark, shown on the reveal and not stored. It is not added to `play.scroll_results` (that is the reading of one Scroll revision, D-064) and there is no combo across thread calls.
- **Recording paused** (D-083). A pick is a play result, so while recording is paused nothing is stored and the card says so; the lock carries the recording generation as `call.lock` does. Reading a call works while paused. A client that holds a stale generation reads the policy again and sends the same pick once.
- **Removed** on leave, on an ended friendship that carried the member in, on Clear and on account deletion, in the same places as party places. **Exported** as the person's own pick in words (thread name, the question and the option picked, and the day), and not whether it was right.
- **A lock tells open threads.** A new lock sends the usual disposable thread hint to every member, so "2 of 4 have called" moves within seconds. A repeat sends nothing.

Confirmed by: `social-calls.test.ts` (visibility, reveal, removal on leave, a removed friendship and an inviter's deletion, Clear, account deletion, export, paused and stale generation, no receipt, no clock time, the table shape, and the wall), `wall.test.ts`, `pnpm boundaries:probe` (13 Social probes, and each fails when the rule is loosened), `account-deletion.test.ts` and `data-export.test.ts` (the store is seeded, emptied and listed). Shown failing with the block mask, the leave delete, the recording gate, the Clear erase, the friendship delete, the inviter delete, the account erase and the wait for those still on their way each broken once.

## D-157 · 2026-10-31 · No live tally of picks: a reveal's common guess is the Scroll's own reviewed note (v3 T-E, #485)

**Builds on** D-126, D-155 and D-156. **Amends** the change map's row "aggregate room or thread calls feed a Scroll's 'common guess' only past 30 calls, with no per-person data", which this does not build.

- **What the card says.** At a reveal, a member whose own pick was the Scroll's authored common guess sees the note the Scroll reader shows for it: "The common guess. Source: <label>." It comes from the revision's own `commonGuess`, set by code from the recipe, and only when its source is marked reviewed (admission requires it; the thread read applies the same rule again). Otherwise the card says nothing about a common guess. No percent, count or tally of anyone's picks is kept or shown, in a thread or across threads.
- **Why not a live tally.** A first version counted every pick of a call across all threads and showed the leading option and a percent past 30 calls. A count threshold cannot make that safe:
  - It counted rows, not people. One account can lock in thirty threads (no thread cap, no place needed), reach the threshold alone, and then read any other lock from how the pair (option, percent) moves.
  - It counted picks whose own thread had not revealed yet, and picks of members the reader has blocked.
  - A leave, a Clear or a deletion moves the numbers, which also tells.
  - A small group does what one account does. Making it safe would need distinct people, a count only of revealed picks, a cap per account and thread, and noise: a subsystem for one line of text. A person's hidden pick is more important than that line.
- **What stays authored.** The reader's common guess (D-126) is the only common guess. It is reviewed by a person before the Scroll is served, and says nothing about how people in threads played.
- **Removed.** The cross-thread count (`countsOf`, its index) and the 30-call threshold. The contract's reveal carries an optional `guessSource` text and nothing numeric.
- **T3.** The design shows no common guess in the thread card, only the R3 question 16 leaning ("Call it together results feed Scrolls' common guess: yes, past 30 calls"). The difference is recorded in `docs/v2/design/fidelity.md`. Taken for the owner.

Confirmed by: `social-calls.test.ts` (one account locking in thirty other threads leaves the card byte for byte the same, with no percent or "most called" line; a reviewed authored guess shows to the member who picked it and to no one else; an unreviewed one shows nothing), `threads.test.ts` in `contracts` (a percent, a count, a `commonGuess` object or a person does not parse), `call-together.test.tsx` (the note is worded as the reader words it, and no figure appears). Shown failing with the review check removed, the pick check removed and the stored authored guess shown to everyone.

## D-173 · 2026-10-31 · A painted backdrop asks for full bleed, since dev suggests marks sit in a paper margin (R-4c, #490)

**Amends** D-166 and D-172 (the house style of a painted backdrop).

- **What dev showed.** After D-172's ban, six backdrops were made on dev, one per room. Four of the six still carried a signature or an edition number. In three of the four (Space, Computing, Human body) the mark sat in a cream paper margin below or around the print. The History one sat on a cream ground that covers the whole picture, so no margin can be told apart there. Of the two clean images, Earth runs to every edge; Machine learning is cream all over.
- **The change.** The house style asks for "wide panoramic composition, full bleed: the picture runs to every edge, with no paper border, margin or mount". It no longer says "band", which reads as a strip on a sheet; the 21:9 shape is sent as the image ratio. The ban on signatures stays. This is a prompt, so it lowers the chance of a mark and cannot promise it; the operator's review (D-171) keeps a mark out.
- **Not yet measured.** Whether full bleed removes the margin marks. The coordinator re-makes the four rejected backdrops after deploy and records the result on #490. A cream ground or cream ink may still invite a mark in a corner; if so, the ink words are the next thing to look at.

Confirmed by: `backdrop-prompt.test.ts` (the prompt asks for full bleed and no paper border, and never says "band").

## D-174 · 2026-10-31 · A room earns a star from three acts, three a day, never from an AI, and nobody owns it (R-5, #491)

**Builds on** D-064, D-068 and R3 E ("Game feel in rooms"). **Implements** the star rule of the change map: a different person gives Useful, a replay earns no star, an AI never earns, at most three a day per room.

- **The acts today.** (1) A person's words in a room's Today channel, or in a question topic by someone other than the person who asked it, that another member marked Useful. (2) A debate that closed by vote with at least two different voters. (3) The first right tap on the day's Spot the flaw. The fourth act of R3, a finished Launch, comes with R-6. There is no replay yet, so no replay can earn.
- **Never a star.** A resident's words, a mark on one's own message, a card, General chat, a debate or a share topic, a debate with one voter, and a second right tap on the same Spot the flaw.
- **Once per act.** A star is unique per room, act and source (the message, the debate or the ritual item). A retry, two people finishing together, and a mark taken back and given again earn one star. The unique row is the guard, so the award is exact without a job.
- **Three a day.** At most three stars per room per UTC day (`KS_ROOMS_STARS_PER_DAY`, 1 to 50, default 3). An act that meets a full day earns nothing and is not remembered. A Useful mark can earn on a later day (taken back and given again, since the unique row was never written); a debate closes once and a Spot the flaw is only today's, so those two lose their star when the day is full. A dev world may raise the number so a journey can fill a constellation in one day.
- **In the act's transaction.** The star is written in the transaction of the Useful mark, the tap or the debate's close, under a per-room advisory lock taken last in the lock order (personal root, own account, room root, discussion rows, sky lock). A room read never writes a star.
- **Nobody owns a star.** A star holds an act, a source id, a UTC day and the constellation it counts toward. It holds no account, no message text and no title, so taking a mark back, leaving a room, a Clear and an account deletion never remove a star. What a person can take back is their own votes and taps (D-176).
- **Not evidence.** A star, a tap and a vote are play results and conversation. They are never read by Composer, Jev, Planner, Evidence, Personal Context, Geography or Scenes (D-063). No rooms command writes to those modules, and a test counts the rows of their schemas around every act.

Confirmed by: `rooms-sky.test.ts` ("what earns a star": the acts, the refusals, the cap and the next day, two at once; "a debate closed by vote"; "Spot the flaw"; "writes nothing to a person's own universe"), `sky.test.ts`. The cap, the AI refusal and the member-only sky were each made to fail by breaking the rule.

## D-175 · 2026-10-31 · Seven stars finish a constellation; the room names it by a 24-hour vote that the next member command settles (R-5, #491)

**Builds on** R3 E, R3 question 11 ("a lit station changes the room cosmetically: yes"), D-090 and D-174. Updated after review: more than one constellation without a name.

- **Seven.** The seventh star finishes a constellation and offers three names. The next star starts the next constellation. A room that has finished one keeps its station lit (`finished` of one or more), whatever it is making next. Inactivity changes activity, never existence: nothing ever dims a finished constellation.
- **Three names from code.** The names come from fixed lists by library system (eighteen each, twenty-four in the shared list), from a place the room and the constellation's number choose. A constellation is not offered a name another constellation of the room holds, nor one a constellation still without a name is offered, so naming one never takes a name from another's list. Only when fewer than three names are free does a repeat appear: free names first, then names only offered elsewhere, then held ones. No model writes a name and no member types one, so a name needs no moderation.
- **The vote.** Every current member may choose one name and change it until the vote closes, 24 hours after the seventh star. Counts show only to a member who has voted, as for a poll, and never say who chose what.
- **Two without a name.** A room may finish a second constellation inside the first one's day. Each keeps its own 24 hours. A vote goes to the earliest constellation with no name whose vote is still open, so the second is voted on once the first's day has ended. **The vote names its constellation**: the command carries the number the voter's sheet showed (`naming.constellation`) and is refused unless that is the one open, so a tap from an out-of-date sheet never counts for another constellation's name at the same place; the screen reads the sky again. When a vote has closed, the next member command names every closed one, oldest first, each by its own votes, reading what is due again under the sky lock. A name an earlier constellation of the room already holds is passed over while a free one is left; when all three are held (the list is used up) the plain vote decides and a repeat is allowed. (The other way, holding the next constellation's stars back while a vote is open, was refused: it would make a room's good work wait on a name.)
- **The rule.** The name with the most votes wins, a tie goes to the earlier name in the list, and no votes at all go to the first. Anyone can compute it from the counts.
- **No job settles it.** A vote that has closed, and a debate whose time has passed (D-176), are settled by the first member command in that room after the moment: a presence beat, which an open room sends every 25 seconds, or a vote. Each settlement is guarded by the row it changes, so two commands at once settle once. A room nobody opens settles nothing, which costs nobody anything. A vote after the close is refused. The room reads a debate as closed from its close time even before the star lands.
- **Why not a cron.** A job needs a person's authority to take the room lock (D-164), and a deleted starter would strand the room. A member's own command already holds the lock and the authority.

Confirmed by: `rooms-sky.test.ts` ("a constellation and its name": finish at seven, three names, a changed vote, the name by votes after a day, no votes, a tie, the second constellation, the station on two maps; "more than one constellation without a name": the second finishes inside the first day, a vote for any other than the open one is refused and counts for none, no name is offered twice at once, all three names held leaves the plain vote to decide, both named oldest first), `sky.test.ts` (the naming rule, the candidate lists, the plain vote when all three are held), the client-core and component tests (the vote names its constellation) and `rooms-sky-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-176 · 2026-10-31 · The room's sky is members' to read, and the count ban of D-051 is lifted for stars and a few tallies (R-5, #491)

**Amends** D-051 ("no member, count, online or activity field" on a station), as D-069 already did for faces and the member total. **Builds on** D-090 and D-160.

- **Who reads it.** `sky` is on a member's own view of a room and on the station of a member or moderator on their own map. An outsider's or an invitee's station and a room's preview carry none, so a room's activity is not shown to people who have not joined it. The sky read is a member's only, and every other caller gets `unavailable`.
- **What it says.** Stars lit of seven, how many constellations are finished, whether the latest is being named or is named, and its name. A star that is tapped shows an act and a UTC day. It names no person, message, title or time of day, so a block changes nothing in it and there is nothing to mask.
- **The allow-list.** The new numbers are named here and in the contract's comments (none of their names is one the allow-list test looks for): `sky.lit`, `sky.of`, `sky.finished`, a debate side's `votes` (only after the reader voted or it closed), a name vote's counts (only after the reader voted), a Today question's `messagesToday` (people's messages in Today since UTC midnight), the sky's `starsPerDay` and a Spot the flaw's `found` (only after the reader tapped). Each is a real count. With no data there is no number.
- **Friend visit.** The visit copy still holds systems and planets only. A room's sky is never added to it.
- **Counts and choices.** In a room of two or three members a tally can let a voter infer another's choice (with two voters and a result of 1 to 1, each knows the other chose the other side). Polls already have this property, and this accepts it the same way: counts are shown only to someone who voted or after the close, and never with a name.
- **A tap is stored only while recording is on.** A tap on Spot the flaw is a play result the server keeps, so it carries the device's recording generation like a call in a Scroll. While recording is paused, or the generation is out of date, the server stores nothing, keeps the key and answers `not_saved`; the screen says so plainly and the tap can be sent again once recording is back on (D-083, D-182). This is stricter than a call, which works on the device and is not saved: here the key is on the server, so a paused person cannot play it.
- **What a person takes back.** Leaving or being removed deletes their votes (names, debates) and taps. An account deletion deletes the same and exports them first. A Clear erases their Spot the flaw taps, which are a play result (D-082), and keeps their votes, which are conversation (D-042), as it keeps poll votes.
- **Debates.** A topic tagged Debate carries two different sides of up to 60 characters and a close time from 1 hour to 7 days. A vote may change until then. A tie has no winner. Only counts are shown, never which side a person chose.
- **Today's items.** A day's question is a template over a library Scroll's title and summary (no model call). A Spot the flaw is a passage of the same Scroll with one sentence altered: code chooses the sentence and keeps the original as the key, and the model writes only the altered sentence, which code checks. Both wait for an operator's approval, as D-070 requires, and are made by a nightly job or an operator command, never while someone waits.

Confirmed by: `rooms-sky.test.ts` ("Spot the flaw and recording": paused, stale and replay; "who can read the sky": an outsider, a stranger and a person who left; a station shows an outsider none; the same sky for someone who blocked a member; no person in a star; "what a person takes back": leave, Clear, deletion, export) and `room-sky.test.ts` (a sky with no place for a person).

## D-201 · 2026-10-31 · Two kits draw the heart and the nerves, and six Human body recipes name them (S-8b, #479)

**Amends** D-125 ("A new kit is a new id and a new decision") and D-194 (five kits). **Builds on** D-124.

- **Two new kits.** `circulation.v1` and `nerve.v1`. Each is its own id with its own closed words. A stage names one kit, as before (D-194).
- **Six recipes get a kit** (`stageKit` in the library plan, with a note in the file saying why the kit draws the subject):

| Kit              | Recipes              | Why it draws the subject                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ---------------- | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `circulation.v1` | `cardiac-cycle`      | The beat in order (the two atria, then the two ventricles), four valves that open and shut with the pressure behind them, and the right side to the lungs and the left to the body. The brief asks for exactly these.                       |
| `circulation.v1` | `blood-pressure`     | Pressure falling along the loop, the fall across a stretch as flow times resistance, and narrower or wider arterioles. The brief asks the reader to change the flow and the resistance and see the pressure respond, with relative numbers. |
| `circulation.v1` | `oxygen-transport`   | The S-shaped curve, haemoglobin loading in the lungs and giving some oxygen up in resting and in working tissue. The brief asks to compare resting and active tissue on an illustrative curve.                                              |
| `nerve.v1`       | `action-potential`   | Rest, threshold, rising, peak, falling and the undershoot, with the channels, the all-or-none rule and the spike running one way. The brief asks to step through rest, rising, falling and recovery.                                        |
| `nerve.v1`       | `synapse-signalling` | The packets, the gap, the receptors, and an exciting and an inhibiting connection, with no chemical named. The brief asks to compare the two kinds of connection.                                                                           |
| `nerve.v1`       | `reflex-arc`         | Sensor, sensory neuron, spinal cord, motor neuron, muscle, and the news still on its way to the brain when the muscle responds.                                                                                                             |

- **Each series is drawn whole.** The heart series (the three `circulation.v1` recipes) and the nerves series (the three `nerve.v1` recipes) each hold their three recipes, so D-199's rule that a series is marked whole still holds when they are marked.
- **Left without a kit, and why.** `innate-immunity`, `adaptive-immunity` and `immune-memory`. No kit draws cells, antibodies or a first and a second exposure, and a picture of "the immune system" drawn with another kit would teach a false subject (D-125). They stay plain Scrolls until a kit of their own exists.
- **The kit draws only what a recipe is about.** `circulation.v1` has no disease, no heart rate and no pressure figure, and `nerve.v1` has no voltage figure, speed, time or named chemical. A brief that asks for one of those is answered in words and calls.
- **Marked as stage Scrolls.** D-199 marked these six as plain v2 Scrolls with calls, no myth and a landmark, because no kit drew them. Now that one does, each is a `stage` Scroll. The misconception (none), the landmark and the series are D-199's and are unchanged, so the other twenty-nine of the thirty-five stay plain. The runbook prints the spike line for the six (`BODY_SPIKE_TOPICS_V2`, `--spike-id body-kits-v18`); it runs them as the plan marks them, so read it before the first wave.

Confirmed by: `library.test.ts` (which recipe has which kit, that these kits go only to Human body recipes, and that the immune recipes have none), `spike-v2/topics.test.ts` (the six, with the runbook line), `spike-v2/run.test.ts` (the spike accepts an answer drawn with each kit), `writer-v2.test.ts`, `kits-compile.test.ts`, `kit-stage-smoke.spec.ts` (each kit's stage in the reader, axe at 390 × 844 and 1440 × 900).

## D-202 · 2026-10-31 · The physiology each new kit derives by code, and what it leaves out (S-8b, #479)

**Builds on** D-195, which set the rule for every kit: the writer chooses words, code settles them, derives every physical fact, draws it and writes the text from the same numbers.

- **Circulation: the heartbeat.** The valves are not chosen. A valve is open when the pressure behind it is above the pressure ahead of it, and the pressures at each point of the beat are the shape of the textbook's Wiggers diagram, relative to the aorta's highest and never shown as figures; the picture shows the left side's, and the right side runs lower in step. So the valves between the atria and the ventricles are open while the ventricles fill and the atria squeeze, all four valves are shut while the ventricles tense and while they relax, and only the valves to the arteries are open while blood is pushed out. The two atria squeeze together and then the two ventricles; no state has all four chambers squeezing. The ventricles hold their volume while they tense and while they relax with every valve shut. Test: the textbook's valve table for each point, and the rule written again.
- **Circulation: pressure along the loop.** The pressure across a stretch is the flow times its resistance (the fluid form of Ohm's law) and the four stretches are in series, so the pressure at the start of the loop is the flow times the whole resistance and the pressure at the end of the veins is 0. The resistance of the arterioles goes as one over the radius to the fourth power (Poiseuille), so arterioles at four fifths of their width resist about two and a half times as much, and for the same flow the pressure before them is higher and the pressure after them is not. The usual shares of the resistance (0.10, 0.50, 0.25, 0.15 for the large arteries, arterioles, capillaries and veins) are illustrative and in the shape of the textbook. Test: Poiseuille's law with its constants, the shares, the sum of the falls, and Ohm's law the other way round.
- **Circulation: oxygen.** The share of haemoglobin carrying oxygen follows the Hill equation with a steepness of 2.7 and half loading at 26.8 mmHg. The three places (lungs, resting tissue, working muscle) sit at 100, 40 and 20 mmHg, so the blood leaves the lungs nearly full, gives up a small share at rest and most of its load in working muscle. The pressures are used only to place a point and are never drawn or described. Test: Severinghaus' fit, a different formula, agrees within 0.03 from 20 to 120 mmHg; the anchor points; half loading at 26.8; symmetry on a log scale; the slope is far steeper at the muscle than in the lungs. Below 20 mmHg the Hill equation reads lower than the measured curve (about 0.07 against 0.10 to 0.13 at 10 mmHg). The picture draws that end too, so the lowest few per cent of the axis is a little low. No place the picture marks is below 20 mmHg.
- **Nerve: the membrane.** The voltage, the channels and everything that follows from them come from the Hodgkin-Huxley model of the membrane with fourth-order Runge-Kutta (`membrane.ts`). It is the squid axon at a cool temperature: a human neuron has other channels and numbers, and the same sequence. Code finds the weakest push that fires the cell and the threshold (the weakest sudden lift that fires it) by bisection, takes the six points of the spike from the run, and classes the channels from the gates (ready, opening, open, shutting, inactive; shut, open, closing). A weak push stays under the threshold and the two stronger give a spike of the same height, so the all-or-none rule is a result and not a statement. Whether a fresh push would fire the cell is found by running it. It does not from the peak until the membrane has recovered, which is what the picture and the text say. Test: a forward-Euler integration twenty times finer written from the paper's equations (the peak within 1 mV and 0.1 ms, the undershoot within 1 mV), the voltage never past the sodium or below the potassium reversal voltage, the textbook state of each channel at each point, the push just above and just below the threshold.
- **Nerve: the axon.** The voltage along the axon at one instant is the spike's time course read backwards: what has passed is behind the peak and what is to come is ahead. So the spike keeps its size and shape wherever it is. The stretch behind the peak where a fresh push would not fire the membrane is drawn as recovering and is why the signal runs on one way. The length of the spike against the axon is not to scale, and the text says so.
- **Nerve: the synapse.** The next cell's voltage moves by a small pull toward the voltage at which the ions that flow would stop, 0 mV for the excitatory channel and -90 mV for the inhibitory one, so an excitatory connection moves it toward the threshold and an inhibitory one away from it, and one excitatory input alone stays under the threshold. The size of the change is illustrative and the text says a cell adds up many inputs. Its course is the textbook's alpha function. Whether a connection excites or inhibits depends on the receptor, and no chemical is named.
- **Nerve: the reflex.** The order is physiology and the numbers are counts of links (a stretch of nerve fibre or a synapse), never times: the muscle is reached after four and the brain after at least six, with a longer climb. The muscle responds while the news is still on its way up, and the brain is told after. The picture shows the brain taking part and never says a reflex needs no brain.
- **What the pictures exaggerate.** Pressures, the shares of resistance and the sizes of a synaptic change are relative or illustrative and say so. In the heart view the pressure bars draw the low pressures (the atrium's and the ventricle's at rest, about 0.1 to 0.3 of the aorta's highest) two to four times as tall as the measured ratio, so the valves can be compared; the text says so and the picture is labelled "low ones drawn larger". The sodium channels' start of opening is a few thousandths of their most, too little to see, so the picture draws it as a dashed arrow and a slightly wider gap and keeps the real share for the solid arrow. The channels sketch is drawn against each channel's most open moment in the spike. The spike's length against the axon is not to scale. No figure, voltage, time or speed is ever drawn or written.
- **What the pictures leave out, and say so.** The pulse (pressure graph); acidity and warmth, which shift the oxygen curve (a label on the oxygen view and a clause in its text); the calcium that enters the sending cell between the spike and the release (a clause in the synapse text and the guide); and the relay neuron most reflexes use between the sensory and the motor neuron (a clause in the reflex text and the guide). Carbon dioxide is not named: it shifts the curve with acidity.
- **The colours of the blood.** Red is blood rich in oxygen and blue is blood poor in it, a drawing convention (blood is red either way). Every view that colours blood (heart, circuits, pressure, oxygen) says so in its text, from one sentence in code, and carries the same words as a label.
- **Cost.** A nerve scene works out only its own view's numbers. The membrane model runs once, the first time any view but the reflex is drawn: about 120 ms in Node on the development machine. The axon view adds its recovering-stretch probes the first time, about 50 ms; the all-or-none pushes and a spike's channels are a few milliseconds; the reflex and the synapse after the model are free. Every later state is free.
- **Text versions.** Each is built from the settled state and the same numbers, mentions only what the picture draws, and is tested for no digit, no unit and no named chemical. A statement the picture does not draw is not made: the fresh push that would not fire the cell is a label on the picture.

Confirmed by: `v2/kits/circulation.test.ts` and `nerve.test.ts` (each against its own independent computation, listed above), `v2/kits/kits.test.ts` (every state settled once, described within the document's limit, accepted by the schema), `packages/ui` `kit-scenes.test.ts` (each picture's numbers agree with the physics) `kit-focus.test.tsx` (a ring for every focus, over every state) and `kit-claims-body.test.tsx` (each sentence of the text beside its mark in the picture and each mark beside its sentence, in every state of both kits, and every label a claim of a row or a plain name; the heart, circuits, pressure and oxygen views say the colour convention in text and picture together). Each rule was broken once and a test went red: a valve held shut against the pressures, resistance as the cube of the radius, a straight oxygen curve, arterioles no longer the largest resistance, a wrong potassium conductance, an inhibitory channel that excites, the brain told before the muscle, a weak push that fires, shifted channel classes, a focus with no ring in each kit, a valve drawn open against the pressures, and no recovering stretch drawn. The claims test was shown failing when the dashed opening arrow was removed and when the convention label was removed from a view.

## D-203 · 2026-10-31 · The writer's prompt is `scroll-writer.v18`, with a guide for each new kit (S-8b, #479)

**Amends** D-194, which made the prompt `scroll-writer.v17`. **Builds on** D-196.

- **What changed.** The prompt version is `scroll-writer.v18`. The rules every picture shares, and the guide of each earlier kit, are unchanged, so Earth & sky and the four kits of D-194 give the same instructions as under v17. The only new text is the guide of the two new kits, read from the kit's own definition, so the prompt and the words cannot drift.
- **What each guide says.** First what the picture draws and that the writer chooses only words from the listed keys, and that the words are claims the picture makes. Second what each view and each word means, in the order of the beat, the spike, the synapse and the reflex, and which keys a view ignores. Third what the picture never draws and the writer must never say: for `circulation.v1` no heart rate, no pressure figure, no disease, no advice, never that blood is blue, never all four chambers at once; for `nerve.v1` no voltage, speed, time or named chemical, never that a stronger push makes a bigger spike, never that a nerve is a wire carrying electricity, never one chemical as a "happiness chemical", never that a reflex has no brain or is a decision.
- **Existing documents are unchanged.** No stored Scroll is rewritten. A stage of either kit is stored with the kit's id and its own words, and its text is the kit's own (D-196).
- **The production version is `scroll-production.v18`.** The writer changed, so the version moves with it, as D-197 requires (`versions.test.ts` pairs them). Every v2 recipe has fresh takes at v18.
- **The spike.** `content writer-spike-v2 --topics cardiac-cycle,blood-pressure,oxygen-transport,action-potential,synapse-signalling,reflex-arc --concurrency 2 --spike-id body-kits-v18`, at most two requests for each of the six. The runbook says what to read: `variant` is `stage` and `stageStates` at least 3 for all six, no `stage_choice_invalid` after the repair, and in each accepted document the picture must match what each stop says.

Confirmed by: `writer-v2.test.ts` (the version, that a recipe of each new kit gets its own guide and no other kit's, that the keys and words are listed, and the length), `spike-v2/run.test.ts` and `tests/integration/modules/writer-spike-v2.test.ts` (the version in the spike line).
