## D-158 · 2026-10-31 · A thread makes five branches a day, counted by the UTC day (v3 T-F, #486)

**Builds** D-076 (T3 Q8). **Amends** D-049, "`KS_SUPPLY_SCENARIO_THREAD_CAP` per thread over its life (default 6; dev 6)".

- **Rule.** Quartermaster refuses a branch when the thread has had five orders since 00:00 UTC. It counts its own ledger (`supply.scenario_orders`), so a removed branch still counts. What-ifs and perspectives count together: both cost a made-to-order Scroll. A reused branch (the same idea asked twice) makes no order and does not count.
- **A new variable.** `KS_SUPPLY_SCENARIO_THREAD_DAILY_CAP`, default 5 (1 to 100), read by the API. The old `KS_SUPPLY_SCENARIO_THREAD_CAP` is not read any more, so a world that still sets it to 6 (dev does) gets five a day without being edited. The world's daily cap `KS_SUPPLY_SCENARIO_DAILY_CAP` is unchanged.
- **What bounds the spend now.** The old ceiling of six branches over a thread's whole life is gone: a thread can make five every day, for ever. A thread's five a day bounds one thread; the only bound on the whole is the world's daily cap (`KS_SUPPLY_SCENARIO_DAILY_CAP`, 6 on dev, 0 outside `local` unless set), together with the asker's own allowance and the reasoning harness's budgets. Each branch costs a made-to-order Scroll and, for a what-if, a short answer, so raising the world cap raises both.
- **The sixth is refused honestly.** The command answers `budget_unavailable` (HTTP 429 `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS`) and writes no node, no order and no job. The sheet says "This thread has made its five branches for today" and when it comes back ("the day changes at midnight UTC") when its own cards show five from today; otherwise it names both limits (the thread's five and KnowScroll's own for the day). The client counts the cards it already has; the server decides.
- **Verified.** `scenario-orders.test.ts` (five then refused, the next UTC day, another thread unaffected), `processes.test.ts` (the default, the new name, the old name ignored), `scenario-answers.test.ts` (five and the sixth against real PostgreSQL, nothing written; yesterday's orders do not count), client-core `scenario-answers.test.ts` (`threadMadeAllToday`) and `thread-whatif-smoke.spec.ts` (the sixth refused in a browser).

## D-159 · 2026-10-31 · The card's checks: every claim cites, every figure is a source's, the assumption line is mandatory (v3 T-F, #486)

**Builds** D-211. The rules are `answerRefusals` in `scenarios/domain/answer-card.ts`; a refusal reaches the writer's one repair as a fixed hint.

- **Structure.** One JSON object: an assumption line (12 to 200 characters), two or three claims of 20 to 300 characters that each cite one to three passages by handle, and up to three figures with their own citations. Two claims make an answer of at least 41 characters, which is what the contract's answer needs (40), and three make at most 902 of its 1000: a card that passes the writer's rules is always one a list can carry. Plain text, no web address, no unknown field. A card with no assumption line is never shown.
- **Citations are real.** A handle must be one the code offered. A claim is judged against the passages it cites, not those of another claim.
- **Digits are sourced.** A digit in a claim must be a number a passage that claim cites states, or one the branch itself states (its title, changes, kept lines and premises). Every quantity that follows from the premise is written in words ("about eight times"). The assumption line may use only the branch's own figures. `4,096` and `4096` are one figure. This is the Scroll's rule ("digits only where a cited passage states them", D-049) with the branch's own premise added, because a premise is stated by the member, not claimed about the world.
- **Figures are a source's.** Each entry of `numbers` is a label and a value. The label is words: a digit in it is refused, so no number hides beside its value. The value is one number with at most one short unit ("2 m", "200 milliseconds", "37%") and nothing else: no prose, no second number. And it appears, written exactly so, in a passage the figure cites, with no letter or digit stuck to either end (`150` is not in `1500` or `150,000`). When no passage gives a figure the list is empty, and the card draws no figures row. The card never computes a number.
- **A stored card that does not serve reads as no short answer.** The worker never stores a card the contract would refuse, and a row that no longer parses (a limit moved since) is read as "no short answer" instead of failing the thread's list for every member.
- **No copying.** A claim may not repeat nine or more words in a row of a passage it cites (the Scroll's copy check).
- **It reads as reasoning.** At least one claim carries a word such as "would", "could", "might" or "likely", so the answer is not a flat statement of what happens.
- **No repeats.** Two claims may not say the same thing.
- **Code decides.** The writer proposes; a draft that breaks a rule after the one repair ends as "no short answer", never a lesser card. Nothing is shown unchecked (T3 Q9: "never on unchecked text").
- **Verified.** `answer-card.test.ts`: each rule is broken once and the test goes red (an offered handle, an unstated digit, a premise figure, a copied run, a missing marker, a repeat, a figure that is not a number, a missing assumption), and `scenario-answers.test.ts` runs a repaired card and a card that stays refused through the harness.

## D-211 · 2026-10-31 · A what-if has a short answer card, written by a worker in the asker's allowance (v3 T-F, #486)

**Amends** D-049 (a branch is one made-to-order Scroll) and D-006 i. **Builds on** D-150 (nothing about a member's position on the wire).

- **What it is.** When a member chooses a what-if, the thread shows a card about 30 seconds later: the assumption line, what changed and what stays, a short answer and any figure a source states, labelled "What-if · made by KnowScroll". It opens into the branch's full Scroll once that exists. Another perspective has no short answer: T3 draws only the what-if.
- **Where it lives.** On the branch's own row (`scenarios.nodes`: `answer_state` of `making`, `ready` or `unavailable`, the checked card, when it settled). It is shared work of the thread exactly as the node is and it names no person, so Clear keeps it, and account deletion needs nothing more than the link the node already clears. The export lists the answer with the branches the person started.
- **How it is made.** `choose` sends the job `scenarios.write-answer` (IDs only) in the same transaction as the node, the order and the receipt. The worker, on the asker's own scope, reads the branch's checked premise, the public subject and the claims the thread's Scroll cites (`READ_SCROLL_EVIDENCE`), asks the role `scenarios.answer-card` (`answer-card.1`, one call and one repair, interactive class, so the asker's allowance pays), checks the answer with D-159 and stores it. The run is the branch's ID, so a redelivery never pays twice and an attempt of unknown outcome is never resent. No model call runs in a transaction or on the HTTP path. The writer sees the branch's premise (its changes and kept lines, which may be a member's own typed sentences, as for the Scroll writer, D-049), the thread root's public subject and the cited claims. It never sees the request text, a message, a name or another thread.
- **The wait is honest.** `making` says "about 30 s" and "keep chatting"; after 90 seconds it says it is taking longer than usual; there is no step list, because no step is tracked. The minute sweep gives up on an answer after 12 minutes (a paid attempt at the largest request deadline and its one repair can take 10, and the job's claim lasts 670 s, so a paid slow answer is not dropped) and the card says there is no short answer (the branch and its Scroll carry on). A late answer finds the wait ended and is dropped. A starter who left the thread, or a branch removed meanwhile, gets nothing stored. With fewer than two claims to cite no model is asked.
- **The card does not move the branch.** The answer arriving does not change the branch's `revision`, so someone about to remove it or branch from it is not told the branch is stale because words arrived. Removing a branch clears its answer.
- **The status label stays.** D-006 a allows only Hypothetical (or Speculative, D-056) as a status label on a branch. The card keeps it beside "What-if · made by KnowScroll", on the making card and the ready card alike.
- **Another member's card says nothing about them.** The card carries no field for where anyone is, what they have open or whether anyone read it; "Opened by you" stays the caller's own history (D-049).
- **Branches made before this** have no short answer and are drawn as they were. Nothing is back-filled.
- **Verified.** `scenario-answers.test.ts` against real PostgreSQL with a labelled fixture model; `answer-card.test.ts`; `what-if-card.test.tsx`; `thread-whatif-smoke.spec.ts` at 390 by 844 and 1440 by 900 with axe. **Not yet run with the real model:** the coordinator runs it on dev and checks the served `model` is `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` (the ledger's attempt for the run, which is the branch's ID, records it).

## D-212 · 2026-10-31 · The card cites the claims of the Scroll the thread rests on, and shows the checked premise as it is (v3 T-F, #486)

**Builds on** D-049 and D-211.

- **What it may cite.** The claims recorded in the provenance of the thread root's Scroll (`READ_SCROLL_EVIDENCE`): the passages of public sources a published Scroll already rests on, from the same Scrolls and under the same review gate as the pages a what-if is researched from (D-138). Nothing is fetched and no page is named by a person or a model. At most 24, as `[e1]`, `[e2]`.
- **Changed and kept are not the writer's.** T3's JSON has "changed" and "kept". The card shows the branch's own `changes` and `kept` statements, which the Planner proposed and code checked (`planStep`), so a card cannot say a branch keeps something it does not. The writer returns the assumption line, the claims and the figures.
- **Citations are stored, not shown.** The card stores each claim's citations as claim IDs of the Scroll's provenance, for an operator; the thread is served the assumption, the claims joined as one answer and the figures. Consumer UI shows no sources panel.
- **Verified.** `scenario-answers.test.ts` (the stored claim IDs; none in what a member reads; a Scroll that records one claim gets no model call).

## D-213 · 2026-10-31 · What T3 draws on the card that is not built yet (v3 T-F, #486)

- **Reply in branch** and "2 replies in this branch" need a scope on messages for each branch, which Social owns and the Scenarios README already lists as not built. The card has no reply button, and no reply is drawn under it.
- **Call it first** needs an authored call in the branch's Scroll. A made-to-order Scroll has none, and T3 allows it only on a branch that passed its checks (Q9). Not drawn.
- **The Branches tab** groups every what-if under the part it came from. A branch continues the thread's root or another branch, never a part (D-049, "a branch under a specific part: not built"), so there is nothing to group by. The thread's branch map (X04) stays the list of branches.
- **The step list** ("Read Part 2, Set the assumption, Checking the numbers") would show progress no code tracks. The wait is one honest line and a bar.
- **Branch again** is offered once the branch's Scroll exists (the branch must be ready to continue), not as soon as the short answer is.

## D-204 · 2026-10-31 · Two more kits give five Space recipes a picture, and one recipe stays plain (S-8c, #479)

**Amends** D-194 (which left these recipes without a kit). **Builds on** D-125.

- **Two kits, each its own id.** `starlight.v1` draws the light of a star and what it makes possible; `black-hole.v1` draws how a black hole forms, what its edge is and how we know. `star.v1` is unchanged. Its words are what a writer chooses for fusion and a star's life; new views need new keys (a distance, an orbit), and a new key would change the shape of every stored `star.v1` state. A new id leaves those states, and the stars series, as they are (D-125: a new kit is a new id).
- **Five recipes get a kit** (`stageKit` in the library plan, each with a note saying why):

| Kit             | Recipe                     | Why it draws the subject                                                                                                                                |
| --------------- | -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `starlight.v1`  | `stars-colour-brightness`  | Colour from temperature, output from size and temperature together, dimming with distance and the chart of stars are the four views the brief asks for. |
| `starlight.v1`  | `exoplanet-habitable-zone` | The zones of three stars of different output, and a planet's orbit across them. The brief's "move a planet outward" is a choice of orbit.               |
| `black-hole.v1` | `black-hole-formation`     | A massive star's core collapsing, beside a Sun-like star that leaves a white dwarf. Supermassive holes are told in words only.                          |
| `black-hole.v1` | `black-hole-horizon`       | Two holes to scale with their horizons, the light near an edge, and the same pull from far away. Spaghettification is told in words only.               |
| `black-hole.v1` | `black-hole-evidence`      | Four clues: orbits round an unseen mass, a glowing disc, the ripples of a merger and the shadow. The brief's lensing is the shadow.                     |

- **`exoplanet-kinds` stays plain, and why.** Its subject is what the name "super-Earth" does not say: composition, temperature, orbit, habitability. A kit could draw only a ruler of masses, and the masses of the Solar System's planets and of known exoplanets are real data, which is a chart block (S-8d), not a few words. A picture of a planet would teach a surface the name does not give (D-125).
- **Not marked `scrollV2`.** Naming a kit is not marking the recipe (D-194). Series stay wholly marked or not at all, since none of these recipes is marked: the stars series (`stars-fusion`, `stars-lifecycle`) keeps `star.v1` and the black-hole recipes are in no series.
- **Writer prompt `scroll-writer.v19`.** The runbook prints the spike line for the five recipes (`SPACE_SPIKE_TOPICS_V2`, `--spike-id space-kits-v19`, at most ten requests). The habitable-zone recipe now runs as a stage Scroll in the first spike's twenty.

Confirmed by: `library.test.ts` (which recipe has which kit; `exoplanet-kinds` and `seasons-elsewhere` stay plain), `spike-v2/topics.test.ts` (the five, with the runbook line), `run.test.ts` and `writer-v2.test.ts` (a recorded answer drawn with each new kit is accepted), `kits-fixtures.test.ts` (the CI fixtures show every view), `kit-stage-smoke.spec.ts` (each kit's stage in the reader, axe at 390 × 844 and 1440 × 900).

## D-205 · 2026-10-31 · The physics of the two Space kits is derived by code, and each picture says what it exaggerates (S-8c, #479)

**Builds on** D-195.

- **Starlight.** A star's colour is its glow's colour: Planck's spectrum weighed by the eye (CIE 1931, an analytic fit) and turned to sRGB. Where the glow is strongest is Wien's law. Output is the radius squared times the temperature (against the Sun's) to the fourth. The three main-sequence stars take size and output from the same mass power laws as `star.v1` (radius as mass to 0.8, output as mass to 3.5), and their temperature follows from them. The red giant and the white dwarf are typical values. Dimming is one over the distance squared. The zone's distances are the square root of the output over the limiting starlight (0.356 and 1.107 of Earth's, the conservative limits of Kopparapu and others, 2013, applied to every star as an illustration).
- **Black holes.** The horizon is 2GM over c squared, so it goes as the mass (about 3 km for each Sun); the speed to escape reaches the speed of light there. Orbits are Kepler's: the period goes as the radius to 3/2, and every orbit round one mass gives the same mass. The disc's temperature goes as the radius to minus 3/4, from a million-degree inner edge at three horizon radii. The shadow is three root three over two horizon radii. The merger's chirp is Peters's: separation as the time to merger to the 1/4, frequency to minus 3/8, strength as the frequency to 2/3.
- **Tests that do not use the same formula.** The luminosity and the zone edges are recomputed in SI units from the Stefan-Boltzmann constant and the Sun's radius (the solar constant comes out at 1361 W/m²). Planck's curve is searched for its tallest point and compared with Wien. A surface at 6504 K must look white. The horizon is checked against Newton's escape speed, the orbits against periods and speeds in SI, the shadow against the photon sphere, and the chirp's frequency against Kepler's. The picture's pixels are checked against the same numbers: a planet lies inside a zone's band exactly where the verdict says so.
- **Exaggerations and simplifications, labelled in the picture and in the text version.** Colours are strengthened (the real colours are paler): each channel against the largest, cubed, so a hue never turns (a red giant stays red-orange, the Sun cream, a white dwarf blue), and every view that draws a star's colour says so. Sizes and the bars of output are on stretched scales. Orbits are drawn as circles and the merger's graph is "not to scale"; so is the formation view. The disc's glow is false colour, because X-rays cannot be seen. The black hole and the star of the pull picture are not to scale. The zone's edges are illustrative, and the zone view and its text version say so.
- **What is never drawn or said.** A Sun-like star never becomes a black hole. A black hole is not said to suck in everything. The horizon is a boundary, never a surface. Nothing is drawn at the centre, and the centre is not said to be understood. The shadow is never the horizon. Each of the four clues says it is one clue and does not prove a black hole alone. A zone is never said to hold water or life. Colour alone never gives a star's age.
- **Words and picture agree.** Two new tests: `kit-layout.test.tsx` checks that no label runs past the frame or reaches the phone's TEXT pill (the lower right corner at 390 px) and that no bar of the output view crosses a label, in every state; and `kit-claims.test.tsx` renders every state of both kits and checks that each claim of the text version has its label in the picture and each such label has its claim. It found one disagreement while it was written (the merger graph was "not to scale" in the picture only).

Confirmed by: `v2/kits/spectrum.test.ts`, `starlight.test.ts` and `black-hole.test.ts`; `kits.test.ts` (every state of both kits is settled, described within 600 characters and accepted by the schema); `packages/ui` `starlight-scene.test.ts`, `black-hole-scene.test.ts`, `kit-focus.test.tsx` (a ring for every focus of every state), `kit-claims.test.tsx` and `kit-layout.test.tsx`.

## D-206 · 2026-10-31 · The writer's guide for the two Space kits names what to say and what never to (S-8c, #479)

**Builds on** D-196.

- **The same shape as every kit.** Each kit has a `writerGuide`: what the picture draws, the views and their words, and what code decides. The prompt takes it as the kit's own lines, so prompt and words cannot drift. The words of `starlight.v1` are `view`, `star`, `distance`, `orbit` and `focus`; those of `black-hole.v1` are `view`, `star`, `stage`, `hole`, `merger` and `focus`. A key a view does not use is ignored. In `zone` a giant or a white dwarf is shown as a star like the Sun, because only three stars have a zone.
- **What the writer is told never to say.** Starlight: that a planet in the zone has water, an atmosphere or life ("could have liquid water" is the claim); that colour alone gives a star's age; that every main-sequence star is alike. Black holes: that a Sun-like star will become one; that one sucks in everything; that the horizon is a surface; anything about the centre but that it is not understood; that the shadow is the horizon; that one clue proves a black hole. The picture draws no named star, no real planet and no year.
- **The Sun looks white from space.** The guide tells the writer never to call it a yellow star, and to give no figure for the strengthened colours or the zone edges.
- **What the picture leaves to words.** Supermassive black holes and how they grew, spaghettification, lensing apart from the shadow, the wobble method (as before) and kinds of planet.
- **Two rules for every Scroll, from the first real spike of the S-8a kits.** The writer made a call outside its recipe's pages and got it wrong (stars-fusion: more heavy elements "shorter main-sequence life"; at the same mass it is longer). The answer check (D-198) cannot catch a call whose answer and explanation agree with each other. So the prompt now says that every call, the Final call and every explanation state only what the recipe's pages support, and that a fact the pages do not state is never the subject of a call. It also says to give an effect the size the pages give it, never "slightly" or "a little" (spring tides: the Sun adds about half as much as the Moon). A test checks that both sentences reach every recipe's instructions. Nothing can check the rule on a draft; the spike and the read still do.
- **Prompt `scroll-writer.v19`.** S-8b took v18 for the heart and nerve guides (D-203). The Space kit guides and the two rules above are new in v19, and `scroll-production.v19` goes with it (D-197). The other kits' text is unchanged, and a test keeps each kit's guide out of another kit's prompt.

Confirmed by: `writer-v2.test.ts` (each recipe's instructions hold its kit's guide and no other kit's, and list its keys and words), `kits-compile.test.ts` (the words of another kit are refused), `spike-v2/run.test.ts`.

## D-177 · 2026-10-31 · The room band draws its painted backdrop a quarter larger, so a signature in a corner never shows (R-4d, #490)

**Builds on** D-170 and D-171 (how a room’s face and its backdrop are shown and reviewed). It follows D-172 and D-173, which asked the model not to sign.

- **What dev showed.** Ten real backdrops from the MiniMax image model, made under the signature ban (D-172) and then the full-bleed prompt (D-173). Full bleed did not help: all four made under it still carried a mark. Eight of the ten carried a signature, an edition number or a scribble. Every mark sat within a tenth of an edge, mostly in a bottom corner or a paper margin; the subject always sat in the middle. A prompt does not stop it.
- **The change.** The band draws the backdrop with `BACKDROP_ZOOM` 1.25 inside a view that clips, so the outer tenth of the picture on every side stays outside the band at every width. The picture is already shown under a wash of the ground (80 % in `room-band.tsx`), as a background behind the room's name. The prompt keeps both earlier asks, which cost nothing.
- **Review.** The operator still approves every backdrop (D-171). A picture whose mark reaches past its outer tenth is rejected.
- **Cost.** No new image is needed for the change: the four held backdrops on dev are reviewed again with the crop in mind after it deploys, including the D04 rail, where only the middle of the picture shows.

Confirmed by: `room-identity.test.tsx` (the backdrop is drawn at 1.25 inside a clipping view).

## D-214 · 2026-11-01 · A landmark stands on the planet of its Scroll's topic, joined by the client from two reads, and waits in a tray until then (v3 S-4, #475)

**Builds on** D-063, D-084, D-089, D-180 and D-188.

- **The join.** `play` and Geography never see each other (D-180). A landmark names a Scroll revision; `play` adds that Scroll's primary catalogue topic from Content's `READ_ENCOUNTER_TOPICS` (`topicId`, a public id, absent when the Scroll can no longer be read). Geography answers a second, separate read, `POST /v1/universe/places/concepts/read`, with the topic of each established factual planet. The client sets a landmark on the planet whose topic matches, if that planet is on its map now. Matching by label was ruled out: a label can change and is not an identity.
- **The tray.** A landmark whose planet is not on the map waits. The Universe says "Your landmark is waiting" with its name and finish, and the person can open the list. When the planet forms, the next read of Geography moves the landmark onto it with nothing else changed. A landmark never makes a planet form, and a gold one and a plain one are placed the same way (D-063).
- **Drawn as a small pennant.** `landmarkSpots` in `scene-core` picks the places: land that has formed first, then any land, clear of every name and of each other, ranked by a fixed hash of the planet's seed, variant and place, using only the world generator's pinned maths. The first `n` places of a longer list are the same as a list of `n`, so a landmark does not move when another is earned. A region forming can move a landmark only onto a place that now outranks its old one. At most twelve are painted on a planet; the list holds all.
- **Listed in words.** The same collection is a plain list in the landmarks sheet, the desktop panel and the Simple drawing, each row naming its finish and where it stands ("Gold finish · On Earth & sky" or "Waiting for its planet"). The drawing is hidden from assistive technology, so the list is how a screen reader meets it.
- **Never on a friend's universe.** The person's own session reads the collection. A friend's map session holds an empty one, and the surface ignores any other. A landmark is a play result, private to its owner (D-065, D-110).
- **A deliberate difference from the Simple file.** `simple-views-finalized.html` is "Do not change" (D-111). The Simple drawing now also carries a collection section above its places (the Orbit line, the resume button and the landmark rows), because a person on the Simple drawing, or a screen reader, has no other way to meet a landmark. Nothing in the file's own lists or rows changes, and the section is absent when there is nothing to say.
- **Not built.** The design's dashed "next landmark" and "2 of 3 seen" would need the count of landmarks not yet earned, which the library does not publish for a person. No cloud thinning and no ring around the star (D-087, D-088).

Confirmed by: `tests/integration/modules/play.test.ts` (a landmark lists its Scroll's topic; before any planet exists the planets read is empty and the landmark waits; after formation the topic names the same planet for a gold landmark and a plain one, and the same places formed for both), `geography.controller.test.ts`, `universe-collection.test.ts` of client-core (`placeLandmarks`), `collection-view.test.ts`, `collection-screen.test.tsx` (tray, sheet, painted marker, Simple list, a friend's universe shows none), `landmark-spots.test.ts` (clear of names, spaced, prefix-stable, no banned maths), `e2e/universe-collection-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-215 · 2026-11-01 · The weekly Orbit counts the days of the week on which a call was locked, in the person's time zone, and is only text (v3 S-4, #475)

**Builds on** D-064, D-083, D-087 and D-182.

- **What a day is.** A day on which the person locked at least one call (a Call it or the Final call) of a Scroll. The design says "3 days a week of anything"; today play is the only thing that earns one. Reading alone does not, so Orbit and reading evidence stay apart.
- **What a week is.** Monday to Sunday in the person's own time zone, which the device sends (`timeZone`, an IANA name). A name the database does not know is read as UTC, never refused. The read counts distinct days from this week's Monday to today. There is no table and no migration: the calls already say when they were locked.
- **Three days close it.** `ORBIT_DAYS_TO_CLOSE` is 3 in the contract, and the server's copy in `play/domain` is tested against it. More days that week still read "3 of 3 · closed".
- **No loss, no streak.** Nothing looks at an earlier week. A missed week removes no landmark, no spark and nothing the person owns, and the next Monday starts at zero. The words never say streak or lose, and a missed week is named only to say that nothing goes away: the one reassuring line is the design's own ("Missed a week? Nothing here goes away. Orbits just start fresh on Monday.").
- **Said only once there is something to say.** The Universe prints the Orbit once the person has played this week or holds a landmark; before that it stays quiet.
- **Paused and Clear.** A call locked while recording is paused was never stored (D-083), so its day is not counted. Clear and account deletion erase the calls (D-182), so the Orbit starts over with them.
- **Text, not a ring.** D-087 stands: the Orbit shows in the Universe's strip, sheet, panel and Simple list, never on the map.

Confirmed by: `orbit.test.ts` (Monday weeks across a month, a year and a leap day; distinct days; closes at three; a missed week starts at zero; a day after today is ignored), `tests/integration/modules/play.test.ts` (one day for two calls, the calendar count over three days, twenty days ago leaves the landmark, two zones fourteen and eleven hours apart, an unknown zone, one person's own days, nothing while paused, a read that writes nothing, Clear), `universe-collection.test.ts`, `collection-screen.test.tsx`.

## D-216 · 2026-11-01 · "Resume Part 2 of 3" is the next unopened Part of a series the person started, read from their own openings only (v3 S-4, #475)

**Builds on** D-059, D-131 and D-184.

- **What it offers.** From the person's own activated Scroll openings of the last 30 days (the newest eight), the first whose series has a next Part that is admitted, not hidden and not yet opened by them. The chip says "Resume Part 2 of 3"; the Scroll's title is its accessible description. `part` and `total` are counted from the admitted series, never declared (D-059). The design's chip names the series ("Resume Seasons · Part 2"); the series has no display name in the data, so none is invented.
- **What "unfinished" means here.** The server cannot know how far into a Scroll a person got: that is the device's private position, and play results are walled off. So "unfinished" means a series with a Part still to open, and a Scroll that stands alone offers nothing.
- **Own state only.** `POST /v1/experience/resume/read` reads the caller's `experience.exposures` and the admitted series. It reads no thread, no place of another member and nothing from Social or play, so it cannot reveal anyone's position (D-065). A test has one person read to Part 3 and finds another person's chip unmoved.
- **A read.** It opens, writes and enqueues nothing. Tapping the chip opens the Part by its address (`/scroll/<id>`, a `direct` open), like any link.
- **Where it shows.** A chip at the top of the phone Universe, a button in the desktop panel and the Simple list. Not on a friend's universe.

Confirmed by: `tests/integration/modules/universe-resume.test.ts` (nothing before any reading; Part 2 then Part 3 then nothing, counted from the series; another person's reading changes nothing; a long absence offers nothing; the read writes no exposure, receipt or revision), `experience.controller.test.ts`, `collection-screen.test.tsx` (the chip opens the Part's address, and is absent when there is none), `e2e/universe-collection-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-217 · 2026-10-31 · The kit recipes of Earth & sky and Space become v2 Scrolls: fifteen recipes, no myth, a landmark each (S-6b, #477)

**Builds on** D-125, D-137, D-188, D-194, D-199 and D-204. **Amends** D-199 only where it left these recipes "for later". It also amends D-138 in effect: Create writes only v1 recipes, and now one recipe is left that is (`exoplanet-kinds`).

- **Which recipes.** Every Earth & sky and Space recipe a drawing kit draws (13), and the two plain parts of the series they sit in (`tides-coasts`, `greenhouse-limits`): 15. The thirteen are `stage` Scrolls with their recipe's kit (`stageKit`); the two are `plain`. With the Seasons recipes and the six heart and nerve recipes this makes 22 stage Scrolls, and with the 35 recipes of D-199 54 of the 55 library recipes are v2. A library test pins the list and that the variant follows the kit.
- **`exoplanet-kinds` stays unmarked.** It has no kit (D-204) and is in no series, so no rule asks for it. It is the only v1 recipe left, so an ask for the Exoplanets topic is the only one Create can still commission. A person may mark it as a plain Scroll later; that is a separate choice.
- **Series are whole.** The tides series (`tides`, `tides-spring-neap`, `tides-coasts`), the greenhouse series (`greenhouse-radiation`, `greenhouse-balance`, `greenhouse-limits`) and the stars series (`stars-fusion`, `stars-lifecycle`) are each marked entirely. The test of D-199 still holds. `greenhouse`, `stars-colour-brightness` and the other kit recipes are in no series; their related moves point inside their own system's wave.
- **Misconception: none for all fifteen.** On 31 Oct I fetched each recipe's pages through the product's fetcher (20 pages, a pool of 7 to 27 passages each) and read the passages the writer would be offered for a statement that people hold a wrong idea, the same search as D-199 (misconception, myth, "commonly believed", "often thought", "mistakenly", "contrary to", "in fact", "misnomer", "so-called", and a wider read for "not a", "instead of", "often called"). No page states a belief as common. The nearest hits are not beliefs:
  - NOAA, _What are spring and neap tides?_ says "spring tide is a common historical term that has nothing to do with the season of spring". It explains a name; it does not say people take it for the season. The brief still tells the writer to say so, in a stop, not as a myth.
  - Wikipedia, _Main sequence_: "In fact, a higher metallicity leads to a higher opacity" (physics, not a belief).
  - NASA, _Anatomy_: "These rings are really multiple, highly distorted images of the accretion disk" (a description).
  - NASA, _How We Find and Characterize_: "small planets are very common" (a finding about planets).
  - NASA, _Black Holes_, in the `black-hole-evidence` pool: "Cosmic vacuum cleaners. Black holes don’t suck in other matter." The page lists it among things black holes are not, so it is a correction of a popular picture, but it does not say how many people hold it. A person may review this page as the source for a myth later.
  - NASA Space Place, _What Is a Black Hole?_: "it sucks in everything, even light" (in the `black-hole-horizon` pool). The writer's guide for the black-hole kit forbids that picture (D-206). The page is a source the writer is offered, so the reader of the Space wave checks the held `black-hole-horizon` Scroll for it before release.
    The spike's placeholder myths for these recipes (the Moon makes one bulge, spring tides happen in spring, the Sun burns like a fire, a planet in the habitable zone has life, and others) are stated as a belief by no page, so none could have a reviewed source (D-129). A recipe may get a myth later, one at a time, with a source a person has read; the first candidate is the spring tide name, if a person finds a source that says people hold it. The spike's own table of those placeholders reached no marked recipe, so it is deleted; the spike runs a marked recipe as the plan decides and an unmarked one (`exoplanet-kinds`) plain, with no misconception.
- **Landmarks.** One each, short (at most five words), plain, a thing the Scroll is about, never a claim about the reader. Keys and names are unique across the library and a test refuses mastery wording. They are authored here and a person should confirm them.
- **Consequences.** Create no longer commissions any recipe of these topics; the v1 Scrolls keep serving until a v2 revision is released (as in D-199). `production-status` lists the fifteen as `not_started` until a wave runs. The production version stays `scroll-production.v19` (no writer change, no migration).
- **The waves.** Two waves, each holding its series whole: Earth & sky (7) and Space (8). The commands are in the runbook.

| Recipe                     | System      | Variant (kit)           | Page the choice was checked against                                     | Misconception | Landmark (key)                              |
| -------------------------- | ----------- | ----------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| `tides`                    | Earth & sky | stage (`tides.v1`)      | NOAA, Gravity, Inertia, and the Two Bulges; Tidal Variations            | none          | The Two Bulges (`the-two-bulges`)           |
| `tides-spring-neap`        | Earth & sky | stage (`tides.v1`)      | NOAA, What are spring and neap tides?; What are tides?                  | none          | The Added Pulls (`the-added-pulls`)         |

| `greenhouse-radiation`     | Earth & sky | stage (`greenhouse.v1`) | NASA, The Earth's Radiation Budget; What is the greenhouse effect?      | none          | The Returning Glow (`the-returning-glow`)   |

Confirmed by: `library.test.ts` (the v2 recipes are the Seasons four, the 35 of D-199 and these fifteen, and nothing else; each of the fifteen is `stage` exactly where a kit draws it and `plain` for `tides-coasts` and `greenhouse-limits`, has no myth and no Final call control, and a landmark with no mastery wording; every series is wholly marked or not at all; landmarks are unique), `topics.test.ts` of the spike (the Space recipes run as the plan marks them, and `exoplanet-kinds` is the recipe left to the spike's defaults), `versions.test.ts` (`exoplanet-kinds` is the v1 example). The integration files that used a kit recipe as their v1 example now name it with `markRecipesV1`. Shown failing when a recipe of a series was left unmarked (the list, the series and the landmark tests), when a plain recipe was given the `stage` variant, when a landmark name claimed mastery and when two landmarks shared a name.

## D-218 · 2026-10-31 · An ask order is a made-to-order Scroll with its own caps, and it never goes through the review gate (v3 S-7, #478)

**Builds on** D-049 (a branch is a made-to-order Scroll) and D-129 (takes). **Delivers** the path D-138 left to S-7 ("a person's question becomes a made-to-order Scroll").

- **What it is.** A person's question about the Scroll they are reading becomes a short Scroll made for them alone. It uses the same machinery as a thread's branch: `content.made_to_order_scrolls` (mode `question`), the same `content.produce-scroll` job, takes and stop rules. The writer has its own instructions (`question-writer.v1`): answer exactly the question from the cited passages, about 230 to 340 words in prose only, a minute to a minute and a half, and end with a limit callout, which code checks (the last block must be the limit callout). The brief holds the question as data, on one quoted line whatever the person typed, the Scroll's title and the words of the stop they asked from.
- **Research is chosen by code.** The pages are those the source Scroll's own provenance records (`READ_SCROLL_SOURCE_PAGES`, the same read a what-if uses). A person or a model never names a page. If the source records none, no order is made and the request closes `could_not_prepare`.
- **It is nobody's but the asker's.** The Scroll is attributed to no topic and has no coverage, so it adds no planet and no progress. It is never offered in Explore and cannot be shared as a card. Content opens it only to the person whose request made it (`READ_ASK_ORDER_AUDIENCE`, an account, not a system actor), through the same audience check as a branch's Scroll.
- **D-138 and v2.** An ask order writes a v1 document and publishes as every order does, with no operator read. The gate exists so that nobody is served a Scroll an operator has not read; an ask order is served to nobody else, and a person cannot wait for a read. Every mechanical check still holds: citations to allowlisted pages, the copy limit, digits only where a passage states them, and the reading length (one to two minutes). A v2 ask order (stops, calls, a stage) is not built; it would need the gate to be skipped for a private audience, which this decision does not decide. Create still writes only v1 library recipes.
- **Two caps.** `KS_SUPPLY_ASK_DAILY_CAP` orders per UTC day for the world (default 20 in `local`, 0 elsewhere, so a world turns it on) and `KS_SUPPLY_ASK_PERSON_DAILY_CAP` per person (default 5). The world's count is read from `supply.ask_orders`, which names no person and no words and so survives erasure. The person's count is read from their own requests, so Clear resets it; the world cap still bounds the spend. A full cap closes the request `budget_unavailable` ("We can't prepare new explanations right now"). The open-asks limit of ten and the three-takes-per-order limit still apply. The caps are counted apart from `KS_SUPPLY_CREATE_DAILY_CAP` and the scenario caps.
- **No automatic second take.** A refused take closes the request `could_not_prepare` and sends nothing on its own. The person's Try again (E07, D-054) sends the next take of the same order, at most three takes in all, so one ask costs at most three writer takes (two paid requests each) however often it is pressed. An attempt of unknown outcome stops the order's takes and is never resent.
- **Not built.** Reel asks (no production path), a v2 ask order, and asking from a passage (D2) or as a wager (D3).

Confirmed by: `supply-ask.test.ts` on real PostgreSQL with labelled fixture models (the order, the brief, the pages, the ledger, the one job; produced, bound and opened by the asker alone and not by another person or a system actor; both caps and the cap of zero; a refused take, Try again, the third take and no fourth; a cancel closes the Scroll), `writer.test.ts` (the question instructions, the one-to-two-minute target and its repair hint, a short prose answer accepted and the same refused for a library recipe), `ask-order.test.ts`, `processes.test.ts`. Shown failing when the audience let a system actor open the Scroll, when the person's cap was not counted, when a cancelled request kept its Scroll open, and when a refused take sent the next one on its own.

## D-230 · 2026-10-31 · Where a question asked from a Scroll goes, and what a model may decide (v3 S-7, #478)

**Builds on** D-218 and D-037. The model proposes a place; code accepts only what the schema allows and does everything else.

- **The anchor.** The ask names one stop of the exact immutable revision the person is reading. The server reads that revision (`READ_SCROLL_ASK_FACTS`) and refuses a stop that is not in it, a revision the review gate holds and a document that is not a v2 Scroll, all as `ASSET_NOT_FOUND`. The stop's words reach the writer as context and never enter a shared key.
- **A question the Scroll suggests.** If the words are exactly one of the Scroll's own authored `askSuggestions`, the ask is about that Scroll by construction. The order is made in the create transaction and no model is called.
- **Never the Scroll being read.** The rule sits in the read every way in uses (`inventory`), so placing, polling the waiter and Try again all skip the explanation the question was asked from.
- **A question the person typed.** It waits as `matching`, and a worker makes one call (one repair) on the personal harness, role `supply.ask-scope` (`ask-scope.1`). The model sees the question, the Scroll's title, the stop's words and the catalogue, and answers `here`, `catalogue` or `none`. The schema refuses a topic beside `here` or `none`, `catalogue` without an exact catalogue topic, and a facet the topic lacks, so an invented id never reaches a brief.
- **What code does with it.** `here` makes an ask order. `catalogue` is handled as a free-text ask is: Reuse, then Join or Create on the library, under the library's own cap, but never binding to the explanation the question was asked from. `none` closes `unsupported_request`. A failure, a refusal or an unknown outcome closes `could_not_prepare` and is never resent (the run is the request's ID).
- **What "served from the library" means.** A question that clearly names another catalogue meaning is served from that meaning's library Scroll when one exists. A question about the Scroll being read is not answered by another Scroll on the topic, because it asks for something specific: it gets an ask order.
- **Change.** "Something new" sends the words as a free-text topic ask with no source, which is the E04 path. The E04 sheet stays for Reels and v1 Scrolls, which have no stops and no suggestions.

Confirmed by: `supply-ask.test.ts` (here, catalogue, none, an invented topic, an unknown outcome never resent; the Scroll being read never returned when placed, polled or tried again; a stop that is not in the Scroll, a v1 Scroll and a held revision read as nothing), `ask-scope.test.ts`, `ask-order.test.ts` (typed words stay on one quoted line), `request-session.test.ts`, `ask-sheet.test.tsx`.

## D-231 · 2026-10-31 · The words of a question are private, an ask is refused while recording is paused, and an ask is never evidence (v3 S-7, #478)

**Builds on** D-057, D-082 and D-083. **Decides** D-083 for this slice, as the brief asked.

- **Private.** The words live in the person's own request row. Clear and account deletion erase it, and the export lists it with their requests. No other person, room or thread ever sees it.
- **The order's copy.** The words also sit in the order's brief, which the writer needs. Erasing the request first sets that brief empty (the one write supply makes into Content's table), so a take that arrives later finds no order to write and is skipped, and the Scroll opens to nobody because the request that opened it is gone. The ledger names no one and stays.
- **What erasure does not reach, and for how long.** Three copies outlive Clear and account deletion, because none of them belongs to a person in the production scope. All are unlinked from the person, and the privacy copy has to say so (D-021).
  - **The writer's request bytes** (the brief, with the question): up to 48 hours (`KS_REASONING_REQUEST_RETENTION_HOURS`), as for a what-if premise (D-049) and an unprompted room turn (D-162).
  - **A refused writer answer:** up to 30 days (`REJECTED_ANSWER_RETENTION_DAYS`), kept for the operator when a take is refused. It can echo the question, because the writer is writing about it.
  - **The Scroll the answer became:** its title and text stay in Content, unlinked from the person and open to nobody. It answers the question from public pages and may restate it. It is not deleted, because Content's revisions are immutable.
- **Refused while recording is paused.** An ask from here is tied to what the person has read: it names the Scroll they have open and the stop they are on, and the scope step runs on their personal harness. So it follows D-083: nothing is stored on the server. The device checks the policy first and says so; the server fences with the recording generation (`RECORDING_PAUSED`, 409, and a stale generation is `RECORDING_POLICY_STALE`, with no receipt). A "Something new" ask carries no source and keeps E04's rule.
- **Never evidence.** Making the request, placing it, producing the Scroll and binding it touch no evidence, no exposure, Composer, Planner, Personal Context or Geography. The Scroll made is a normal Scroll that creates evidence when it is read, like any other. The puzzle's lock belongs to the play layer (D-063, D-180) and reports no reading interaction.
- **A cancel closes the Scroll.** A cancelled request is no longer the audience, so a Scroll that finishes later is open to nobody.

Confirmed by: `supply-ask.test.ts` (nothing in exposures, observations or actions changes; paused and stale refused with no request and no job; export; erasure and Clear scrub the brief, close the Scroll and skip a later take; cancel), `request-session.test.ts` (paused on the device and from the server), `ask-smoke.spec.ts` (paused: no request is sent). Shown failing when the erasure left the brief and when the fence was skipped.

## D-232 · 2026-10-31 · The wait is honest: a card to leave, a time only when measured, a puzzle from the Scroll's own calls (v3 S-7, #478)

**Builds on** D-054 (E07 states) and D-218.

- **What is said.** "Making it now. We'll let you know here when it's ready" with the question quoted, a card the person can leave ("Keep reading") or cancel, and the existing ready notice and Requested list. D1 says "in your Seasons thread", but there is no solo thread, so nothing says so.
- **A time only when measured.** The server sends `typicalSeconds` on a preparing request that is an ask order (not one still being placed, and not one the library answers): the median of how long the last 20 finished ask orders took (`ready_at` minus `created_at` in the ledger, set once when the Scroll is first seen admitted), and only from five of them. The sheet then says "usually about N min" (a minute when under 90 seconds). With fewer than five it says nothing.
- **The puzzle.** An unplayed Call it of the Scroll the person asked from: the latest one at or before their stop, else the next one ahead, never the Final call, and none when every Call it was played. One tap on an option locks it through the play layer. A right pick shows "Called it!" and its spark, a wrong one "Plot twist!" (D-064). While recording is paused it works on the device and says it is not saved (D-083).
- **Keys and size.** A tap on a suggested question sends it at once. Keys 1 to 3 pick one while the question box is empty (the box takes focus when the sheet opens); `?` and A open the sheet and Esc closes it. The counter appears from 240 of 280 characters. Reel is not offered.

Confirmed by: `ask-sheet.test.tsx`, `ask-puzzle.test.tsx`, `puzzle-pick.test.ts`, `ask-copy.test.ts`, `ask-smoke.spec.ts` at 390 by 844 and 1440 by 900 with axe, and `supply-ask.test.ts` (no time before five, then the median of 100, 110, 120, 130 and 600 is 120). **Not yet run with the real model:** the five real ask orders (accuracy, a minute to a minute and a half) belong to the coordinator on dev.

## D-114 · 2026-10-31 · The first open belongs to the account: one conditional update claims it, and it plays only where it can be shown (v3 U-6, #470)

D-092 and the design say the first-open animation plays once in the lifetime of the account, on any device, and that the flag lives on the account and is set when the animation starts. This is how that is built.

- **One column, one update.** `identity.accounts.universe_first_opened_at` (migration 0086) is set by `POST /v1/account/universe/first-open`, a conditional update (`where universe_first_opened_at is null`). The call answers `{ claimed: true }` for exactly one request in the life of the account, whichever device asks first, and `false` for every later one. Eight racing calls give exactly one `true`. It writes only that column: no evidence, no personal data, no universe revision, no pg-boss work. It takes no personal root, because nothing personal changes. A suspended account claims nothing.
- **Set when it starts.** The client claims just before the picture begins, so a crash or a closed app never plays it again. Skip, Back, Escape, a hidden app and finishing all leave it set. A request that gets no answer within 4 s (the timer is cleared as soon as the answer comes) is treated as "seen" and remembers nothing: the opening is never shown twice, and a held tab is worse than a missed opening.
- **Claimed only once the picture can be drawn.** The picture builds its own WebGL context first, and only then is the account asked. A device whose context fails leaves the flag alone, so another device can still play it.
- **Not claimed where it cannot play.** Under reduced motion, with Simple drawing, on the desktop layout (the design has no desktop animations), on a low-memory device (D-111) and where WebGL is missing, the flag is left alone, so a phone that can show it still can.
- **A device remembers "seen".** Either answer is kept in the account scope's storage, so a returning account is not asked, and does not build the picture, at every cold start. It goes with the scope when the person signs out. The Android Back button is held for the opening only while the Universe tab is in front.
- **Clear keeps it, deletion removes it.** The column is on the account row, which Clear does not touch and account deletion deletes. The data export says in words whether the opening was shown (`universeOpeningShown`), never when.
- **Where it runs.** The Universe tab, on its first visit, before anything else. The real screen is not drawn until the picture is nearly over (1.2 s before it is shown), so one 3D scene runs at a time for almost all of it.

Confirmed by: `tests/integration/modules/account-first-open.test.ts` (once, on any device, eight at once, another account, a suspended or unknown one, only the flag written), `data-export.test.ts`, `packages/client-core/src/first-open.test.ts` (no answer means seen), `animation/step-animations.test.tsx` (plays once, claims once, none under reduced motion, nothing claimed when the context fails, not asked again once seen), `account-deletion.test.ts` (the export reads the flag, Clear keeps it, deleting the account removes the row; it fails if the export says a constant), `e2e/universe-animations-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-115 · 2026-10-31 · First open ends where the empty state would rise in, then cross-fades to the real map (v3 U-6, #470)

**Builds on** D-092, which this makes exact. The design draws the opening ending on U01 Empty and does not say what happens for an account that already has places (the first people to meet it will, since every dev account has been used before).

- **The real screen is never rebuilt.** The gate keeps `children` in one place in the tree and only covers them. The real screen starts drawing beneath the picture at 19.0 s, and when the picture goes it is that same screen with that same 3D canvas, so the first frame of the real sky is the last frame of the picture, with no blink. A test counts the renderers: the picture's is disposed at the end and the real one is not.
- **An account with no places** plays all 21.2 s. The empty screen (title, line, Go to Explore) rises over the last picture from 19.6 s, as one piece rather than four, and the picture is then taken away to show the same screen on its own sky. It never says "starts quiet" about an account that has places.
- **An account with places, or whose map cannot be drawn yet,** plays until 19.6 s, the moment the empty state would rise in, then cross-fades to the real screen in 0.3 s. The real screen starts drawing beneath the picture at 19.0 s. It never claims the account is empty.
- **The picture is the whole of it.** One three.js scene on one straight path of 2,765 units: dust that falls in along five arms and builds the star (14,000 grains), sparkles (2,600), a tremble and a squeeze, a flash and two shock rings, 14 things passed (asteroids, a lush planet with a moon, four galaxies, a ringed gas giant, a pink nebula, two systems), 1,500 star streaks and a smooth stop at U01 Empty's camera. The camera is not given a breathing drift at the end, so the first frame of the real sky is the last frame of the picture.
- **Not as drawn.** The star is the approved sun, not the design's painted ball with convection cells, and the asteroids share one painted rock. Neither changes a beat or a timing.

Taken for the owner; named in the PR for #462. Confirmed by: `animation.test.ts` (it ends exactly on U01 Empty's camera at rest, the star builds and bursts, Skip is there until just before arrival, the empty state rises last), `e2e/universe-animations-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-116 · 2026-10-31 · The three steps down are pure functions of one time value, and a tap cannot interrupt one (v3 U-6, #470)

**Builds on** D-093 (going back up is a 0.3 s cross-fade) and D-107 (the levels are distances of one camera).

- **Which steps.** The whole map to a system, a system to one of its own planets, a planet's globe to its map at planet level. The same animation for a friend's universe (system and planet; a visit has no map). A search or a dial jump across levels goes straight to its destination.
- **Every step up is a 0.3 s cross-fade** (D-093): a planet to its system, a planet's map to its globe, a system to the whole map. The screen being left stays on screen, fading out over the one entered (the planet views are laid over each other; for a system to the whole map the 3D camera's own 0.3 s fly is the cross-fade), while the chrome of both cross-fades. Taps are ignored for those 0.3 s. A longer jump up goes straight to its destination.
- **One `t`.** An animation player advances one value `t` by the time between the stamps `requestAnimationFrame` gives it, clamped to 0.1 s a frame and accepted at most every 33 ms. Every value on screen is a pure function of it: the camera, the rocket, the globe laid over the planet, the circle that opens, the chrome. The 3D scene is told the player's clock and draws at the 30 fps cap and the 1.5 pixel ratio like everything else in the tab. Overlays are `Animated.Value`s written from the clock, so a frame re-renders nothing.
- **One scene, not two.** The design draws the overview and the system as two scenes and cross-fades them. This tab has one scene and one camera, so there is no hand-over: the camera swings on a sphere round the sun, as in the design, and the rocket's distances are the design's divided by its overview-to-system scale (2.18).
- **The globe rides on the planet.** Each frame the renderer reports the planet's projected centre and radius, and the planet view's own globe is placed there, scaled by that radius over its own, blurred and fading in. The camera's last pose puts the planet where that globe is drawn for the stage's real size, so the first frame of the planet view is the last frame of the glide.
- **Rings and a moon turn into the planet view's.** For a planet that has either, the 3D ring narrows from 1.4 / 2.4 radii to 1.42 / 1.56 and turns to the planet view's tilt, the moon leaves its orbit for the planet view's place on the near side, and both fade out only once they match.
- **The world opens from the globe.** The live map is moved and clipped by a circle that opens until it fills the screen, so the last frame is the landed screen; its names and clouds come in last.
- **Taps are ignored; Back is not.** While one plays, the session's gate refuses taps (focus, land, select, search, the camera). Back, Up and Escape first jump the animation to its end frame and then go back as normal. Reduced motion, Simple drawing and the desktop layout play none. The tab bar is not part of the screen and is never touched.
- **The glide ends on the planet view's sky.** Over the last 0.45 s of the glide the 3D canvas (neighbours, haze, limb) fades out, revealing the starfield the planet view draws, so the last frame is the planet view with only its globe on it. The clouds of the landed map drift in over 0.5 s after landing.
- **Not as drawn.** The overview's names vanish at the start instead of fading over 0.4 s; the destination header rises as one piece. A friend's universe derives its type, rings, moon, hue and haze on the visitor's device from the public ids (never the owner's): every type is used once before any repeats.

**Amends** D-106 (a friend's visit no longer draws the plain look) and D-107 (a fly down into a system is the 5.3 s entering animation; a step up is still 0.3 s). Confirmed by: `animation.test.ts` (each animation starts and ends on the screens it joins, the planet lands within a pixel of the globe at three stage sizes, the player's cap and clamp), `step-animations.test.tsx` (steps up, the layers), `visitor-scene.test.ts`, `e2e/universe-animations-smoke.spec.ts` (at a phone's density of 3 the cap and the 1.5 pixel ratio hold through the first open, the glide into a system, into a planet and the world opening out).

## D-178 · 2026-10-31 · The Launch is a scored room activity with its own rules: the server's clock, a score, a podium for one Launch (R-6, #492)

**Implements** D-070. **Amends** D-051 on R03, "never scored", for the Launch only: a moderator's poll or quiz is as it was.

- **A kind of its own.** The Launch has its own tables (`rooms.launches`, `launch_players`, `launch_answers`, migration 0088) and its own use cases. It is not a row of `rooms.activities`, so nothing a poll or a quiz promises is changed.
- **Five questions, one clock.** A member starts one. A lobby of 20 seconds follows, then five rounds: a question open for 8 seconds, then its answer shown for 5. Every time is the start plus a fixed number of seconds, so the server's clock alone says what is open. A read carries the time left in milliseconds, never a time of day, and a device counts it with a steady clock, so a fast, slow or mis-set clock changes nothing. An answer says the round and the option and has no field for a time: the server measures from the question's opening to the moment it took the answer. An answer after the close, or for a round that is not the open one, is refused.
- **The score.** One point for each right answer. A tie goes to the less total time over the right answers, then to who joined first, so the order is the same on every device. A wrong answer or none costs nothing: it is not a point, and its time is not held against anyone. The first answer to a round stands.
- **The podium** is the first three people with at least one right answer, for this Launch only. Nobody below third is listed and nobody is listed for scoring nothing. It is read for 24 hours and then it is gone. There is no total, level or rank across Launches or weeks.
- **No tick job.** A phase is worked out when it is read, from the stored start and the clock. The Launch is marked finished by the first member command after its end (a presence beat, a start, or the device's own "it is over"), guarded by the row it changes, as D-175 settles a constellation's name. A room nobody opens settles nothing.
- **Finished is when it ended, not when it was noticed.** A Launch that ended on Monday and is settled by a beat on Thursday is finished on Monday: its podium is read for a day from Monday's end (so it is already gone), its star counts against Monday's day and its answers are kept seven days from Monday. A settlement is bookkeeping, not an event of the game.
- **Starting.** A current member of an open room, with two people having the room open (R3), an approved deck (D-179), and the room's rest since the last Launch over (two hours, `KS_ROOMS_LAUNCH_GAP_MINUTES`). The room is locked FOR UPDATE, so two starts make one Launch.

Confirmed by: `launch.test.ts` (the phases at each millisecond of the edge, the timing rule, the score, the tie, the podium), `rooms-launch.test.ts` ("a game on the server clock", "tie", "accepts an answer only while its question is open", "starting one").

## D-179 · 2026-10-31 · Launch questions are library Scroll calls that code chooses and an operator approves, and no model is asked (R-6, #492)

**Builds on** D-070, D-138 and D-176. **Amends** D-070 only in that the nightly batch makes no model call for a deck.

- **The source.** A `call_it` or `final_call` of an admitted library Scroll under the room's topic, with its options, right answer and reason exactly as its authors wrote them. Those calls passed the review gate (D-138) and the second model's key check (D-198) when the Scroll was made. So a Launch never shows unreviewed model text, and a Scroll made for a thread is never used (D-049).
- **The deck.** Five questions, from different Scrolls where the library allows and at most two from one, preferring calls this room was not asked in the last 14 days. The reason keeps its first whole sentences up to 280 characters. A library with fewer than five usable calls makes no deck: nothing is made up. It is a `launch` item of `rooms.rituals`, drafted by the night's batch beside the day's question and Spot the flaw and by `rooms rituals`, and it costs no call.
- **Approval.** An operator approves a deck. Approving re-reads each cited Scroll's exact revision and refuses with `citation_changed` unless every question still says what the deck says. A deck is played once (`launches.deck_ritual_id` is unique) and is offered for today and the day before.
- **Starting checks again.** The deck is frozen onto the Launch, and the start refuses unless every cited revision is still the current one anyone may be served. It reads through the caller's own scope, so no network wait runs inside the transaction.
- **Never generated live.** No model is called on a Launch's path, and its queue holds no job.

Confirmed by: `launch-deck.test.ts`, `rooms-launch-deck.test.ts` (no model call, approval, a corrected Scroll), `content-library-calls.test.ts` (a real admitted v2 Scroll; held and withdrawn ones are not read), `rooms-launch.test.ts` (a deck whose Scroll is gone does not start).

## D-219 · 2026-10-31 · The host is Pixel: labelled AI, template lines, no cast row, no message (R-6, #492)

**Amends** D-163, which gave the Quizmaster a place in the cast table when the Launch arrived.

- **Why not a cast resident.** A resident speaks as messages and every message is an `ai_turns` row. The host never posts: it says short lines on the stage, drawn from the state of the game ("Question three. Go.", "Called it!"). So it has no turn to log, no cap to decide, no job and no model.
- **Always labelled.** The view carries the host as `{ name: 'Pixel', label: 'AI · Quizmaster' }`, fixed by the contract. The screen draws the name, a square mark and the label; a screen reader hears "AI, Quizmaster". No line speaks of a wrong answer.
- **An AI never plays.** A player is an account, and the contract's faces and podium entries hold only a public person. An AI has no place to stand on a podium, in a count or among the people who called a question.
- **The ledger rule still holds.** A Launch writes no `ai_turns` row, no AI message and queues nothing; a test counts them around a whole game.

Confirmed by: `rooms-launch.test.ts` (nothing queued, no turn, around five rounds), `room-launch.test.ts` (the host and the faces), `launch.test.tsx` (the label on the stage and the start sheet), `client-core/room-launch.test.ts` (the lines).

## D-220 · 2026-10-31 · A Launch answer is a play result: walled from evidence and ranking, refused while recording is paused, erased by Clear, exported as the person's own (R-6, #492)

**Builds on** D-063, D-082, D-083 and D-182.

- **The wall.** An answer and its score reach no evidence, ranking, planning or formation. Rooms writes to none of those schemas (`write-boundary.test.ts`, and the run-time row counts around a whole game). Two checks keep the tables inside Rooms: an import rule (`launch-wall-only-listed-modules-import-rooms`, an allow-list with probes) and a scan that no production file outside Rooms names a Launch table (`launch-wall.test.ts`).
- **Paused.** Starting, joining and answering carry the device's recording generation. While recording is paused, or the generation is out of date, nothing is stored, no receipt is written, and the answer is `not_saved` with its reason. This is stricter than a call in a Scroll, which plays on the device: a round does not wait and the key is on the server, so a paused person cannot play a Launch. The stage and the start sheet say so before any tap, from the person's own recording policy (kept fresh while the screen is open), and offer no Start, Join or answer to press.
- **Erased.** A Clear deletes the person's place and answers (play data, D-082). Leaving or being removed deletes them too, and so does deleting the account. A star the Launch earned has no account and stays. The players and answers of a Launch are also deleted seven days after it ended, by the first member command in the room after that (a presence beat, a start or a settlement), so a room that stops playing does not keep them; the Launch row stays. The count is from the end of the play time, not from when anyone settled it.
- **Exported.** The person's file lists each Launch they played to its end: the room, the day, how many questions they got right and how many they answered. Never what they picked and never another person. A Launch still running is not listed.

Confirmed by: `rooms-launch.test.ts` (paused, stale, Clear, deletion and export, leave and removal, seven days, the personal-schema row counts), `launch-wall.test.ts`, `pnpm boundaries:probe`, the erasure inventory checks.

## D-221 · 2026-10-31 · A finished Launch earns the room one star when two people answered (R-6, #492)

**Implements** the fourth act D-174 left for R-6.

- **The act.** `launch_finished`, once per Launch (the star is unique by room, act and the Launch). It is awarded in the transaction that marks the Launch finished, under the Launch row held FOR UPDATE and then the room's sky lock, last, as everywhere. That wait means the finished Launch holds every answer accepted before its end. The star carries the moment the play time ended and counts against that day's three, even when a later command settles it; a constellation it finishes starts its name vote from the settlement, when members can first choose.
- **Why two.** A Launch played by one person is not something the room did together, so it earns nothing (the same reason a debate needs two voters). It still has a podium and a share card.
- **The day's three.** The star counts against the room's three a day (D-174). A full day earns nothing and is not remembered; the Launch says so by showing no star line.
- **Never an AI, never a replay.** The host has no account. When R-7 adds a replay it earns sparks, never a star.

Confirmed by: `rooms-launch.test.ts` ("the star": one for two players, none for one, none from a full day, none for the same Launch twice or from two commands at once), `domain/launch.test.ts`.

## D-222 · 2026-10-31 · What a Launch shows, and to whom (R-6, #492)

**Builds on** D-160 and D-176.

- **Members only.** Every Launch read and command answers `unavailable` to a stranger, an invitee and a person who left. A room's preview says nothing of one. The room's own view carries a word for it (ready, starting, on, just finished) and a member's or moderator's station carries `live`; an outsider's station and a friend's visit carry neither.
- **While a question is open** a reader gets its words, the time left and their own answer. The contract has no field there for the key, the reason, a count or a name, so no client can show one.
- **When it closes** the reader gets the key, the reason, how many answered, how many were right and the faces of those who were right. Nobody is named as wrong; the count and the faces let a reader work it out for themselves, as R3 draws it.
- **Blocks.** For a reader who blocked someone, that person is left out of the counts and the faces, and keeps their place on the podium as a place without a name, so the order is true and nobody moves up. The blocked person and everyone else see the room as before.
- **The numbers.** The allow-list names them: a reveal's `locked` and `right`, the podium's `played` and each score, and `players` in the lobby. Each is a real count; with no data there is no number.
- **The share card** is the person's own squares, one for each question, with no question and no answer, so it cannot spoil a Launch. Sharing it into General is a button, and posts an ordinary message.

Confirmed by: `rooms-launch.test.ts` ("who can take part", "draws a person the reader blocked", "says on a member's station"), `room-launch.test.ts`, `launch.test.tsx`.

## D-223 · 2026-10-31 · Real time: the server's clock decides, hints are disposable, a read recovers everything (R-6, #492)

**Builds on** D-160.

- **The screen reads** when the room's hint arrives (a start, a join, an end), on focus, on a 20-second poll, and by itself the moment the phase it shows has ended. Between reads it counts down the milliseconds the last read gave, so it is never ahead of the server and a lost hint costs a few seconds, not a wrong answer.
- **No hint says what was answered.** A start, a join and a settlement announce the room. A first answer by someone who had not joined puts them in the Launch and announces that, like a join; a later answer by someone already in announces nothing. A hint names the room and nothing else, and an open question has no field for who has answered or how many, so a re-read learns nothing before it closes.
- **A device says the Launch is over** once (`finish`) when its read finds it so. The server refuses while its own clock says it is not, so a fast clock cannot end one early. It settles once whoever asks.
- **The podium opens by itself** only for a person who watched that Launch go by in this session; arriving later shows a Podium chip and nothing pops up. Digit keys pick an option on the web (R3: "keys 1-4"; a call has two or three options), and the timer is a ring and the words "8 s": the ring steps and never needs motion.

Confirmed by: `client-core/room-launch.test.ts` (time left, the next read, the one `finish`, the retry as the same command), `campfire.test.tsx` (the podium), `e2e/rooms-launch-smoke.spec.ts` (two people in two browser contexts, five rounds, at 390 and 1440, axe).

## D-224 · 2026-10-31 · While a Launch runs the stage takes the top of the phone; the conversation keeps 68 percent the rest of the time (R-6, #492)

**Amends** D-140 ("the conversation keeps over 60 percent of a phone") for the time a Launch runs. **Builds on** the R-5 measurement of the Now strip.

- **The stage sits above General**, not in its list, so the question stays on screen while the chat below it scrolls as the crowd (R3 E 1, A2). The Launch chip is a chip of the Now strip, which scrolls with the messages, so a room with no Launch running keeps 68 percent of a 390 × 844 phone for its messages, as before (measured in `rooms-launch-smoke.spec.ts`).
- **While a Launch runs** the messages have 18 percent of that phone (154 px: the band, the stage with three options, the Now strip, the AI bar and the composer share the rest). The design draws the same trade (a question card, then two or three crowd messages). It lasts about a minute and a half, once a night, and a person who wants the room back can scroll the stage's place or wait for the podium. The 60 percent rule is for a conversation that is not interrupted by a timed game.
- **On a desktop** the stage keeps the reading measure, centred above the conversation, and the band's "Launch live" tag sits in the rail.

Confirmed by: `e2e/rooms-launch-smoke.spec.ts` (both numbers are printed by the run).

## D-234 · 2026-10-31 · The thread's reader draws a v2 Scroll as the Stage + Spine reader, and its foot is the end tray (v3 dev check, #570)

**Builds on** D-153 and D-133. Fixes a gap: the thread's own reader opened every Scroll as a v1 article, so a v2 Scroll, which is what a series holds now, showed "This needs an app update" and a person could not reach the finish or the reveal.

- **Same reader.** `/threads/<id>/read/<assetId>` opens the Scroll with `stage: true` and draws it with `StageReaderSurface`, the surface `/scroll/<id>` and Explore use, with the same recorder, so what is read here is an ordinary encounter. The snippet viewer and a branch's reader still open v1 documents only.
- **The finish sits in the end tray.** "I've finished this part" is drawn after the score and before the ways on, where a v1 article draws it at its foot. It is the same button and the same rule: sent while recording is paused, never while the person hides where they are.
- **No Part or related move from here.** The reader offers Back to thread, Done for today and Your Universe. The next Part is chosen in the thread, whose player is the only thing that tells a place (D-153); a move to `/scroll/<id>` would leave the player and tell nothing.
- **Not built.** Sharing a passage of a v2 Scroll back into the thread. A snippet names a v1 block; a stop is not one (D-135).

Confirmed by: `e2e/thread-scroll-smoke.spec.ts` (two people in a thread of three v2 Scrolls: Read opens the Scroll, the finish tells the thread, a locked call reveals once both have finished; a desktop; axe at 390 × 844 and 1440 × 900) and the end tray test in `stage-reader.test.tsx`. Shown failing with the reader opened without `stage`.

## D-235 · 2026-10-31 · A Scroll's place "on" is told once the Scroll has opened, not when Read is pressed (v3 dev check, #570)

**Amends** D-153, which made pressing Read this Scroll the `on` of a Scroll. A place only moves forward and is never taken back (D-152), so a place sent by a tap that then failed to open the Scroll could not be undone: others saw "is on Part 3" for a part nobody had opened, and the person's "Call it first" for the earlier parts closed.

- **The reader tells it.** The thread's reader mounts a small component only after the Scroll's exposure is open and its document is ready, and that component reports `on` for the part. Pressing Read, in the dock or in the open player, tells nothing; it only opens the reader.
- **Same gate as before.** The report is automatic, so the controller sends nothing while recording is paused or not known, and nothing while the person hides where they are (D-153). The finish button is unchanged.
- **A Reel is unchanged.** A Reel's `on` already waited for its document to open.

Confirmed by: `thread-encounter.test.tsx` (Read, in the dock and in the open player, tells nothing), `e2e/thread-scroll-smoke.spec.ts` (a Scroll that does not open tells the thread nothing and stores no place; an opened one tells `on`; paused tells nothing while the button still shares the finish). Shown failing with the tap reporting again.

## D-233 · 2026-10-31 · A thread's call stays with the revision its members called on, and says when the Scroll was updated (v3 dev check, #570)

**Amends** D-155 ("a call is the first Call it of a part's Scroll") and **builds on** D-156 (a pick is stored against an asset, a revision and a stop). Fixes a loss found on dev: when a part's Scroll got a new revision and the series was declared again with it, the thread's call moved to the new revision's question, the three picks stayed in the table against the old one, and the card showed a different question and "0 of 3 in" with no reveal and no word of why.

- **A pick belongs to the revision it was locked on.** A call is one question for the whole party, so while any member the reader may count has a pick on an earlier revision of the part's Scroll, the call is about the earliest such revision: its question, its options, its right answer and its reveal. The picks, the count and the reveal rule are unchanged, and a member who has not called yet is asked the same question the others answered, and their pick is stored on that revision.
- **Fresh only where nothing was locked.** When nobody the reader may count has a pick on an earlier revision, the call is about the series' current revision, as before, and its picks go to that revision. So a new revision never takes a pick away, and a thread that had not started calling gets the new question.
