- **The person is told.** A call whose question is older than the Scroll carries `updated: 'held'`, and the card says "Part N has been updated since your thread started calling. The call keeps the question your thread called on, so everyone answers the same one." in an open, locked and revealed call alike.
- **When the earlier revision cannot be read.** If the revision the picks are on was held or rejected by review, its question cannot be shown. The card then asks the current question afresh (`updated: 'restarted'`: "Part N has been updated, and the question your thread called on can't be shown any more. This call asks the new question afresh."). The old picks are not deleted; they are kept with the member's row, shown to nobody, and leave, Clear and deletion remove them as before.
- **A block moves nothing.** Only the picks the reader may count choose the revision, so a member the reader has a block with cannot make the reader's card ask a different question from the one the reader would see without them (D-155).
- **A lock names the question it was made on.** `LockThreadCall` carries the `assetRevision` of the card the person pressed on. The option is an index into that question's options, so the server stores the pick only if the call is still about that revision, and otherwise answers `changed`, stores nothing and the card is read again with a plain line ("This call changed. The question was updated while you were choosing, so nothing was locked."). Neither `stands` (the caller already locked) nor `closed` (the call is closed to them) fits, so `changed` is its own answer. This closes the window where the only earlier locker clears or leaves between the card and the lock, which moves the call to another revision.
- **Nothing else changes.** No migration: the key already holds the revision. The export names a pick under the question it was made on.

Confirmed by: `social-calls.test.ts` ("a part whose Scroll gets a new revision": the question, picks, count and reveal stay with revision 1 while revision 2 has a different question and answer; the only earlier locker clears between the card and the lock and the pick lands nowhere, and a revision the call was never about stores nothing; a member who had not called is asked the old question and locks on it; a thread with no picks gets the new question with no note; the export names the question the pick was made on; a rejected earlier revision restarts the call and says so; a block moves nothing), `calls.test.ts` (the pure choice of revision), `threads.test.ts` in `contracts`, `call-words.test.ts` and `call-together.test.tsx` (the words). Shown failing with the choice of revision reduced to the current one.

## D-236 · 2026-10-31 · The Useful mark that earns a star may come from the person who asked (clarifies D-174, #570)

**Clarifies** D-174 and D-168. No code changes. A dev check read D-174 as "the Useful mark must come from someone other than the question's asker" and found that the asker marking someone else's answer to their own question earned a star. That is the rule as written.

- **Two different people, each from the author.** In a question topic the star's words must be by someone other than the person who asked the question (`startedByAccountId` is not the author), and the mark must come from someone other than the words' author (D-168: nobody marks their own). Neither says anything about the giver and the asker. The asker is the person the answer was for, and the one most able to say it helped, so their mark is the main case. D-168 says the same for an AI's answer ("the asker has no other way to say it helped"), where the mark is allowed and earns no star.
- **What still earns nothing.** The asker's own later words in their question, marked by anyone. A mark on one's own message (refused). A resident's words. General chat, a debate and a share.
- **Collusion.** Two accounts can still ask, answer and mark each other. The daily cap of three and one star per act and source are what bound that; a rule about the asker would not (the answerer could just as well mark a third account's words). R3 says "a different person gives Useful", meaning different from the author.

Confirmed by: `rooms-sky.test.ts` ("counts the asker's own Useful on someone else's answer, and nobody's Useful on the asker's own words"). Shown failing when a mark by the asker was refused.

## D-237 · 2026-10-31 · An AI reply is an ordinary message with a colour edge, still labelled (v3 dev check, #570)

**Amends** the v2.1 rule that an AI contribution is drawn with a dotted outline, a square corner and "A suggestion. Nothing is made unless someone chooses it." R3 (C1) says: "Drop the dotted 'suggestion' box. An AI reply is a message in the conversation, not a proposal waiting for approval. The label and the Challenge button keep it honest", and "Character colour as a 4px edge. No dotted box, no footnote."

- **What a reply looks like.** The same bubble as a person's, with a thick left edge in the resident's colour: the Explainer's is the room's accent and the Skeptic's its ink, the colours of their marks (D-170). A room without a face has an ink edge.
- **Still labelled.** The resident's name, the AI label, the kind of contribution and the reason it spoke stay above the bubble, and a screen reader hears "AI, Skeptic". The edge is colour only and is never what says it is an AI.
- **The buttons stay.** Challenge and Other side stay under a reply, since asking is how a person pushes back. The footnote is gone: nothing about an AI reply is a proposal.
- **Left as it was.** The "while you were away" card is a card, not a reply, and R3 does not draw it as a message; it keeps its dotted outline.

Confirmed by: `room-messages.test.tsx` (no footnote, no dotted style, the label and the buttons stay; the edge is the resident's colour in a room with a face), `room-identity.test.tsx` (the colour of each resident).

## D-238 · 2026-10-31 · A sheet closes before the screen it opens, and on the web waits for its own Back (v3 dev check, #570)

**Builds on** D-006 k (a sheet adds one transient history entry and a Back removes it). That Back is asynchronous, so a screen opened in the same moment is popped with it: the address changes and the old screen stays. Found on dev three ways: "Visit a friend's universe" left Friends showing, "Open thread" in the Share sheet on `/scroll/<id>` did nothing, and a topic picked in the room menu opened behind the menu.

- **One helper.** `closeThen(close, run)` marks the Back as pending, closes the sheet, and runs the navigation once the Back has landed (or after 400 ms if it never reports). On native it closes and runs.
- **Where it is used.** Visit a friend's universe, Open conversation and Open thread in the Share sheet, and a topic picked from the room menu's Topics or Pins, which closes the menu first. The older local copies in the room menu, the branch sheets and Explore keep working as they are.

Confirmed by: `close-then.web.test.ts` (the order, and the fallback), `friends-smoke.spec.ts` (Visit reaches `/visit/<handle>` and Friends is not the screen shown; shown failing with the helper replaced by close-then-navigate), `campfire.test.tsx` (picking a topic closes the menu first; shown failing without it), `rooms-smoke.spec.ts` (the topic opens and no sheet is left).

## D-239 · 2026-10-31 · Who is here ends with the lease: a minute after the last beat, and at once when the device says away (v3 dev check, #570)

**Builds on** D-069 and D-160. States the rule a dev check asked for, with no change to the numbers. A dev check saw "3 here" for "minutes" after two members closed their browsers. Measured again on dev with two killed browsers: the header went from 3 to none between 70 and 75 seconds, which is the rule below.

- **The lease.** A face is a lease the member's own device renews every 25 seconds (`ROOM_PRESENCE_BEAT_MS`) and the server reads only while it is under 60 seconds old (`ROOM_PRESENCE_LEASE_MS`). A device that leaves, hides the app or is covered by another screen says `away`, which deletes the lease at once.
- **What the others see.** An open room reads again every 20 seconds, on a hint, and on focus. So a person who says `away` is gone from another member's header at its next read, and one who just disappears (a closed browser, a lost connection) within the lease and one read: at most about 80 seconds, usually less.
- **A hidden tab.** A tab that is not visible does not poll; it reads again when it is shown. Nobody is looking at it, so nothing stale is seen.
- **Not changed.** The lease is not shortened: a shorter one would drop a face for a single lost beat, and a face that comes and goes is worse than one that stays a minute. A closed tab's `away` is not guaranteed on the web (the request may be cut off), which is why the lease exists.

Confirmed by: the measurement above (dev, 31 Oct), and the existing presence tests for the lease and `away` (unchanged).

## D-240 · 2026-10-31 · How a person finds a room: the design is silent, and nothing lists rooms to browse (v3 dev check, #570)

**Records, builds nothing.** A dev check with a new account could not reach the Space room. `/rooms/space` said "This room isn't available". `space` is not a room's address: a room's address carries its ID, which is shown nowhere, so the answer is the same as for a room that does not exist.

- **What reaches a room today.** The room's station on the person's own Universe map (it appears beside a system of their own, so a person has to have formed that system by reading), a room row in Talk once they are a member, an invitation, or a link that carries the room's ID. A preview before joining is read from the station or the link.
- **What the design says.** R1 and R3 draw a room from the inside, and draw the station on the map (R3 p-04, p-09, "Enter room"). Neither draws a list of rooms to browse or search.
- **What this means for a new account.** It meets a room after it has read about the room's topic. That is the shape the designs give ("rooms follow your own universe"), but it makes a room unreachable from Explore alone. If the owner wants a directory, it is a new design (a list of the rooms of systems the person has formed, a "Rooms near you" row, or a share link), not a fix.
- **Seeded rooms.** An operator seeds one room per library system. Nothing links the seed to a person until their map has the system.

Recorded as a fidelity row ("R1, R3, finding a room") and in the PR. No code changes.

## D-207 · 2026-11-01 · A read stop may carry one play block and a guide line, and `scroll-document.v2` gains them without changing for any Scroll that has none (v3 S-8d, #479)

**Builds on** D-120, D-121 and D-124.

- **Where a block lives.** A read stop has two optional members: `interactive` (a sort, a fork, a scale or a growing chart) and `guide` (a line from the Scroll's character). They are not new stop kinds, so every rule that counts calls, finds the Final call or binds a facet to a read stop still holds. The stop's text is the lead-in the reader sees first.
- **The schema is the same.** The document stays `scroll-document.v2` and the limits stay `scroll-limits.v2`: the members are optional and strict, and a document without them has the same bytes, measures and hash as before. The server serves v2 only to a reader that declares it (D-129), and this reader and these documents ship together.
- **The budget.** At most two play blocks in a Scroll (the limit that has been a constant since D-121, `otherInteractives`), besides at most four calls and one stage. A sort has three to five items, a scale three to six points, a chart three to eight, a fork exactly two paths of at most 40 words. A guide speaks at most twice, in at most 25 words, with two stops at least between lines.
- **The reading estimate.** A block's words count like a stop's (a fork counts its longer path, as a call counts the longer one), and each block adds ten seconds, like a call. A read stop that holds a block asks for the time its words, the block's included, take to read (D-128).
- **Who chooses.** A person, per recipe: `blocks` (each used once) and `guide` (the character) on the recipe's `scrollV2` plan. The writer is told which to write and may add none: a block the recipe does not name, a second of one it does, and a named block that is missing are all refused, and the one repair can fix them. The guide's character is the recipe's; the writer gives the pose and the line.
- **Code owns what the model could get wrong.** The writer lists a sort's items in the right order; code shuffles them by a stable seed, never leaving them in the right order, and keeps the order as the answer. Code sorts a scale's points, finds "you are here" and derives each zoom view from the points (each point from the second on is a wider view). It writes every id, the text versions, the measures and the hash. Nothing is model-written markup, layout or a coordinate.
- **Shapes.** `sort {items, order, explain}`, `fork {prompt, branches[2]}`, `scale {axis, unit, points[], here?}`, `chart {shape, unit, points[]}`, `guide {character, pose, line}`. The design's `scene` is not here (D-210).
- **Not as drawn.** A sort is moved with arrow buttons on each card, not by dragging. A chart grows as the reader steps through its points, not as the stops go by. Both stay usable by keyboard and screen reader.

Confirmed by: `producer/v2/blocks-compile.test.ts` (each block compiles; the sort is never shown in order; unnamed and missing blocks; guide limits), `v2/blocks.test.ts` (admission of each block, the budget, the reading estimate, the sort check), `v2/blocks-fixtures.test.ts`.

## D-208 · 2026-11-01 · A scale or a chart shows only figures that a cited passage states, written as the passage writes them (v3 S-8d, #479)

**Builds on** D-120 (a figure in reader text must be in a passage the item cites).

- **Why a rule of its own.** A chart is a picture of numbers, and a made-up number in a picture looks like a fact. The writer must never fill in a point, round a figure or convert a unit.
- **The figure is text.** The writer gives each value as the passage writes it (`"8.3"`, `"1,000"`), with no word or unit; the unit is its own field. A text that is not a figure is refused (`block_value_invalid`). A bare number is read the same way.
- **The existing check does the work.** Every value, label and unit is reader text of the item, so each figure must appear in a passage the item cites (`authoring_number_not_in_evidence`). A figure that only another item cites does not count.
- **"8.3 million".** A passage often writes a figure with a word after it. The writer gives that word as `magnitude` (thousand, million, billion, trillion) and code multiplies. The passage is read the way a person reads it: thousands separators are dropped on both sides ("1,400" is 1400), and the word may follow after a space or a hyphen ("8.3-million"). With a word, a cited passage must write that figure with exactly that word. Without one, some cited passage must write the figure bare, so a chart cannot show a million times too little. A figure written both ways ("17 million people" and "17 sheep") may be given either way.
- **Ways of writing a figure a value cannot state are refused.** A figure the passage scales with `8.3m`, `8.3bn`, `crore`, `lakh` or similar is refused, with a word or without one, because none of the four magnitudes names it. A figure that is one end of a range ("8 to 10 million", "8–10") is accepted only when the item itself states the other end, and then with the word that follows the range. Exponent notation (`1.5e-7`) is refused, as a text or as a JSON number. A figure a passage writes as a power of ten (`1.5×10−7`, `1.5 × 10^7`, `1.5x10⁷`, `1.5·10-7`, `1.5*10^7`, `1.5e-7`, `1.5E+30`, superscript exponents) is a scaled figure too: it is refused, and so are the "10" and the exponent as figures of their own.
- **The unit's words come from the passages.** Every meaningful word of an item's `unit` (joining words and plurals set aside) must appear in a passage the item cites, so "light years" over a passage that says kilometres is refused, and so is "million km" when the passage says "million kilometres" and the figure carries the magnitude. The writer is told to write the unit in the passage's words and to put "million" in `magnitude`.
- **Limits that stay.** The rule proves that a figure is in a cited passage, not that it means what its label says. Every Scroll is still read by a person before release (D-138), and the review text shows each figure and each answer key. A unit made of the passage's own words that still means something else is not caught by code.

Confirmed by: `producer/v2/blocks-compile.test.ts` (an invented figure, a figure the item does not cite, a value written with words, a wrong or a missing magnitude, the sheep case, a value with its word as an exact number, and, each shown failing against the first matcher, thousands commas, `8.3m`, `8.3bn`, `crore`, `8.3-million`, range ends, exponents and a unit the passages never use), `adapters/review-text.test.ts`.

## D-209 · 2026-11-01 · Play blocks are played on the device and record nothing, so they stay behind the play wall (v3 S-8d, #479)

**Builds on** D-063, D-083 and D-180.

- **Nothing is sent, stored or counted.** A sort's order, a fork's path, a scale's zoom and a chart's steps are state in the block on screen. No interaction kind, event field, table, command or export names them: `INTERACTION_KINDS_V2` still has only the call kinds `locked` and `revealed`, and the play schema still has only calls, Scroll results and landmarks.
- **Reading is unchanged.** A stop with a block counts as read by being visible for the time its words take (D-128). Playing the block neither adds to that nor is needed for it. Composer, Jev, Planner, Evidence, Personal Context and the Universe cannot see how a block was played.
- **Recording paused.** There is nothing to refuse, because nothing is recorded. The blocks work the same while recording is paused and say nothing about saving.
- **No sparks yet.** A sort could be a small Final call (design page 7), but a spark is judged on the server from the stored revision (D-181), which needs a second call kind, its own stored shape and a migration. That is a later slice; until then a sort says "In order!" and no more.
- **Text version.** The reader's text version (T key) and each block's own switch swap a block for its words, derived from its data: a sort lists its items and offers the order, a fork shows both paths, a scale and a chart list their points. Nothing in these blocks moves by itself, so reduced motion has nothing to remove; the end-to-end test runs every block under reduced motion too.

Confirmed by: `ui/src/scroll/play-blocks/play-blocks.test.tsx` (playing each block calls no observer; the text version of each), `play/wall.test.ts` (the play schema holds exactly calls, Scroll results and landmarks, and the check finds a planted table), `adapters/reading-facts-v2.test.ts` (a block's stop is read by being visible, and the only interaction kinds accepted are the calls'), `e2e/blocks-smoke.spec.ts` (no request while a block is played; axe at 390 and 1440, also under reduced motion).

## D-210 · 2026-11-01 · Which blocks ship, the writer is `scroll-writer.v20`, and the first spike (v3 S-8d, #479)

**Builds on** D-196, D-197 and D-203.

- **Shipped:** sort, fork, scale, growing chart and guide. Each is built, played on the device, has a text version, and is filled by the writer from JSON.
- **Deferred: scene** (a picture with numbered hotspots). A scene needs a drawing whose parts a hotspot can name. The nine kits draw their parts as one picture and expose no anchors, so hotspots would be coordinates the writer chose, or anchors drawn for one picture that no recipe is about. Both are the model-drawn geometry D-124 rules out. It returns with a kit built to be tapped.
- **Deferred: dragging a sort, pinch on a scale, a slider on a chart, sparks for a sort, and the rest of the page-8 kit** (landmarks and people as drawings, thermometers, map-lite). None is needed for a block to work, and each is a slice of its own.
- **Writer `scroll-writer.v20`, production `scroll-production.v20`.** For a recipe that names them, the prompt adds a section for each block, with the rules of D-208, and a guide section. A recipe that names none gets the v19 text unchanged. The pair is in `versions.test.ts`, so every recipe has three fresh takes (D-197).
- **Marking waits for a reader that parses `interactive`.** Marking a recipe for blocks needs every installed reader to parse a read stop's `interactive` and `guide`. The web reader ships with the server. A native build made before this change would refuse such a Scroll, so mark only after a native build with this reader exists (there are no native users today).
- **Nothing is marked.** The plan holds no block or guide on any recipe. The spike asks for them with `--blocks <recipe>=<sort|fork|scale|chart|ray>[+...]`, and a person marks a recipe after reading it.
- **The first spike: four recipes, one block each.** `hybrid-sorting` a sort (the steps of Timsort), `industrial-causes` a chart (the population figures of its page), `black-hole-formation` a scale (the masses its page states), `seasons-elsewhere` a fork between Mars and Uranus with Ray as the guide. Each is one write and at most one repair, as always.

Confirmed by: `writer-v2.test.ts` (each block's rules reach the prompt only for a recipe that names it; a plain recipe's text is unchanged; the role refuses an answer without the recipe's block), `versions.test.ts`, `spike-v2/topics.test.ts` (`--blocks`, the four recipes, none marked), `domain/library/library.test.ts`.

## D-241 · 2026-11-01 · A replay plays a finished Launch again against the answers kept for seven days, and stores nothing (R-7, #493)

**Builds on** D-070 ("Replays are labelled and use real recorded answers"), D-178, D-220, D-221 ("a replay earns sparks, never a star") and D-222.

- **What can be replayed.** The room's newest Launch whose play time has ended and whose answers are still kept: seven days from the end, the same moment the purge counts from (D-220). The read does not wait for the purge, so a Launch leaves the replay the second its seven days end. It is offered to a member who did not answer in it, and only while a person they have not blocked did. A person who played has seen the questions and their own answer is among the recorded ones, so they are not offered it. A Scroll withdrawn or corrected since the deck was frozen takes the replay away, as it stops a start.
- **Labelled on every screen.** A black banner says "Replay. These answers were given on 26 Oct by the 5 people who played. This one is just for you: nothing is saved, and the podium and the room's star don't change." The night is the Launch's UTC day, never "last night", which a UTC day cannot promise. The host is Pixel, labelled AI, with template lines; no model is called.
- **Real answers, and no more than D-222 allows.** The questions go out without their key; the key comes with each answer. The figures are the recorded ones: how many answered, how many were right and the faces of those who were. Nobody is named as wrong. No winner is named: the podium is read for a day (D-178), and a replay is read for seven.
- **No time and no order of speed.** A replay says nothing of how long anyone took or who was quicker: no "you'd have been 2nd fastest", and the read takes no time and no choice. It is stateless and unlimited, so a read that took a time could be swept from 0 to 8 seconds and the answer would step at each recorded time; faces drawn quickest first would say the same. The faces are in person order, and the cut at twelve is taken in that order. D-222 allows the key, the counts and the faces of those who were right, and nothing finer.
- **Nothing about the replaying person is stored.** No table, no receipt, no row. So a paused recording, a Clear, leaving and account deletion have nothing of theirs to refuse or erase, and the export has nothing to add. The device counts the eight seconds of a question for its own ring and sends nothing of them.
- **No star and no sparks.** A replay earns the room nothing (D-221). Sparks are the play module's (D-180) and rooms may not reach them, so the design's "your sparks count" is not built; the screen says the podium and the star do not change.
- **It shrinks with the rows it reads.** A player who leaves, clears or deletes their account takes their answers out of every later replay. With nobody left there is nothing to replay. A person the reader blocked is left out of the counts and the faces for that reader only.

Confirmed by: `domain/replay.test.ts` (the window, who is offered it, the figures), `rooms-quiet.test.ts` ("the replay of a finished Launch": offered and not offered, the key and the faces, the seven days to the second, a player who leaves, a block, a withdrawn Scroll, row counts that do not move and no star; no field that comes from a time, and the faces by person ID with the cut at twelve whoever was quicker), `room-quiet.test.ts` (no place for a key, a pick, a time or a rank; a read takes the question and nothing else), `client-core/room-replay.test.ts`, `quiet-room.test.tsx`, `e2e/rooms-quiet-smoke.spec.ts` (the banner in every state, no Launch command).

## D-242 · 2026-11-01 · A left question is a topic that stays open for people until a person answers, and its asker is told once (R-7, #493)

**Builds on** D-167, D-168 and D-174. It uses the asked turn, the Question tag and the star rule as they are.

- **One command.** `rooms.question-leave` makes a topic tagged Question that begins with the member's words (at most 300; its title is their first 80 characters), leaves a card for it in General and, when they ask, queues one asked turn. It is one transaction, so a question never exists without its first message.
- **Its title has the protections of any title made from a message (D-168).** The question's first message is the topic's origin and its title is marked as made from it, so the asker's words are read as a title only while that message is shown and not blocked by the reader, and become the neutral title when a moderator removes the message or its author deletes their account. The discussion is made first, then the message, then the origin is set, because the origin is a foreign key to the message. A resident reads the question once: the first message is not added again as the origin and the title, which is the same words, is left out.
- **Open is read, not stored.** A question is open while no person but its asker has spoken in it. A resident's answer never closes it (the AI has no account). A moderator's removal of the only answer reopens it, and so does its author deleting their account. The summary of such a question carries `waiting`, and a member's view carries how many questions wait: a real count, with no field when none does. A question whose asker's account is gone waits for no one in either.
- **A resident answers only if asked.** The asker may switch on "Ask Nova to answer now" (on by default): an asked turn of kind `about` for the Explainer, inside the asked bounds of D-068 (10 an hour for a person, 80 a day for a room). When the bounds are full the question is still left and the answer says `resting`. When the asker keeps their messages out of what AI reads it says `kept_out` and asks nobody. The command never schedules the unprompted router, so no resident answers a question its asker did not ask them to, and a room nobody writes in still makes no call.
- **The notice.** Two columns on the discussion: `notify_answer` (the asker's own switch, on by default) and `answer_noticed_at`. The notice itself is read: the first shown message of a person other than the asker whom the asker has not blocked, so a blocked person's answer tells the asker nothing and an answer that is hidden takes its notice back. A card above the room says "Lena answered your question · 2 days later" with "Read it" and "Got it", and the room's own hint makes an open room read it in seconds. It is in the room only: no push exists. The Talk list does not show it yet.
- **The star is the existing one.** D-174's first act, as D-236 clarifies it, counts a person's answer in a question topic that someone other than its author marked Useful, and the asker's own mark is the main case. A resident's answer earns nothing.
- **Erasure.** A Clear and leaving keep a question and its words as they keep any conversation (D-042). The asker's switch and the marker of the answer they noticed are theirs, so a Clear, leaving the room, being removed and deleting the account each turn the switch off and clear the marker, as they delete a place and votes; the topic stays for the others. The export file lists the switch and the marker of each question the person left. A replay holds nothing to erase.
- **Recording paused.** A question is conversation, like any message, so a paused recording (D-083, which is about play and reading) does not refuse it. A replay stores nothing, so there is nothing for a pause to refuse there either.
- **Not built from the design.** "Make it a topic" (a left question is one already), the "FLAIR · FIRST ANSWER" mark (flair is not built) and the Keeper's "+1 star" card (the sky chip shows the star).

Confirmed by: `rooms-quiet.test.ts` ("a question left for people": the topic, the card and one asked turn; no unprompted check; resting and kept out; the same command again; a resident does not close it; the notice, the switch and a block; a removed answer; the star; refusals; no write to a person's own universe), `contracts/room-quiet.test.ts`, `client-core/room-questions.test.ts`, `quiet-room.test.tsx`, `campfire.test.tsx`, `e2e/rooms-quiet-smoke.spec.ts` (two browsers: the question, the answer, the notice).

## D-243 · 2026-11-01 · The quiet room says only what is real: a template offer from the host, and a pill that counts people (R-7, #493)

**Builds on** D-069, D-081 ("Welcome, Launch lines, quiet-room offers are templates"), D-160 and D-219. **Amends** D-160 on one word of `lastMessageAt`.

- **The offer is a card, not a turn.** R3 F draws "Quiet evening. Want a 2-minute warm-up?" as a message from Ray. Here it is made on the device from what the server read, in the host's name (Pixel, labelled AI). It is not a message and not a resident's turn, and has no `ai_turns` row, no job and no model call. It is shown only when a real replay exists and no person has written for 30 minutes, at the end of the conversation where R3 draws it. "Not now" puts it away for that room and night, in the account scope's own storage, so it goes with the scope when the account switches, the person signs out or a Clear changes the epoch. It says nothing of people who are not here.
- **Not built:** "three quick calls on what this room talked about this week". A quiz made from chat would be generated while someone waits, which D-070 forbids; the offer leads to a deck an operator approved.
- **The quiet pill counts people, and says so.** `lastMessageAt` is now the newest shown message **a person** wrote, and the pill reads "Quiet right now · last person wrote 3 h ago" (R3 draws "last message"), or "no one has written yet" when no person has. A resident's words do not count, so an AI answering a question does not make an empty room look busy (D-069: the cast is never counted). A person's own message still counts, as before.
- **An idle room makes no call.** Opening, reading, replaying and being offered make none; a test counts the queues around all of them.

Confirmed by: `campfire.test.tsx` ("the quiet room": the offer, once, the chip, none while people talk), `client-core/room-questions.test.ts` (when the offer is made), `rooms-quiet.test.ts` ("the quiet pill", and the queue counts around reads), `rooms-campfire.test.ts` (the person-message cases, unchanged).

## D-244 · 2026-11-01 · Simpler is the stop's authored text; Quick answer waits for its worker job (v3 S-9, #480)

**Builds on** D-085 and D-086, which stand unchanged. Quick answer is built in S-9b as D-086 says (an HTTP command, then pg-boss, then MiniMax, with a daily cap, labelled "Quick answer · not a checked Scroll", reportable, never evidence and never in the Universe).

- **Simpler.** A read stop shows its own `simple` text in place of its words, in a yellow box named "SIMPLER VERSION", with Undo. Only the stop's own text is swapped: a guide's line and a play block on the stop stay (D-207). The button under each stop, the ••• item "Simpler (this screen)" and the "Simpler" item of the selection menu all do this. It is the text written and checked with the Scroll (D-085), so it is instant and free and calls no model.
- **The menu.** On the web a small toolbar with "Simpler" and "Ask" sits above selected words in a read stop (a page cannot add items to the browser's own menu). Until S-9b, "Ask" opens the Ask sheet (D-218) at that stop with "Tell me more about “the words”" as a draft the person can change. No card is drawn between the two. A call, the Final call and the end are never offered the menu, because their words could give the answer away before the person has locked it in. A phone's own selection menu cannot be extended, so on a phone Simpler is on each stop and in ••• (planned: native selection items).

Confirmed by: `simpler.test.tsx`, `selection-menu.web.test.tsx`, `e2e/simpler-smoke.spec.ts` at 390 by 844 and 1440 by 900 with axe (Ask opens the sheet with the draft and sends nothing until Send; a call offers no menu).

## D-245 · 2026-11-01 · Challenge a friend is a card in a direct conversation, and each pick stays hidden until both have called (v3 S-9, #480)

**Builds on** D-063, D-083, D-155 to D-157 and D-180. It settles the challenge snippet's shape, which the change map leaves open.

- **The card.** A content message in a direct conversation can name one call of the exact Scroll revision it shows: its Final call, else its first Call it. Migration 0090 adds `social.messages.challenge_stop` (the stop id, only on a content message). The question and options are read from that immutable revision when the card is shown. The send carries the stop and no pick.
- **The picks.** Each person locks their own pick with `conversations.lockChallenge` (message, option, recording generation). It is stored in `social.thread_calls`, keyed by the direct conversation, the revision and the stop. That table's key is a conversation and its member, so Clear, account deletion, export and the wall around picks (D-156) already cover it. The sender locks right after the card goes, as a second command, so no pick is in the send's receipt. The first pick stands. No receipt, no time finer than a day.
- **What each person sees.** Before they call: the question and the options, and nothing of the friend, not even whether the friend has called. After they call and before the friend does: their own pick only. When both have called: both picks, the right answer, why, and the Scroll's warm second look after a wrong pick. The right answer is never stored and never sent before then. A right call is a spark, shown and not stored. A wrong one is a plot twist (D-064).
- **Privacy.** A blocked or ended friendship closes the conversation, so neither person can read or lock (`unavailable`, as for every other message). A deleted friend's pick is erased and the other person's card stays "locked". A deleted sender's message becomes a tombstone and clears `challenge_stop`. The export lists a person's own pick in words with `challengeFromConversation`, never the friend's and never whether it was right.
- **The wire says nothing early.** A pick made before the friend has called moves nothing on the friend's side: no new change number for the message and no hint to their inbox. A pick that reveals moves both. Otherwise an observer could learn that the friend has called.
- **Recording paused** (D-083). The card is sent, the lock stores nothing and says so (`recording_paused`, or `recording_generation_stale` once, then the same pick is sent again).
- **A Clear after the reveal.** Clear erases the person's side of a challenge by design: their card is open again, the friend's card goes back to waiting, and a new call shows as their call; nothing is scored, so this is accepted.
- **Not evidence, not ranking.** Sending, locking and reading a challenge add no row outside Social. Picks never reach Composer, Jev, Planner, Evidence, Personal Context, Geography or Scenes: the thread wall rule already allows only Social, feedback, rooms and scenarios to import Social.
- **Once per call.** A second challenge for the same call in the same conversation shows the same card state, because the picks are keyed by the call.
- **Not built.** A challenge in a thread or by link, a challenge on a Reel, and a wager (D3).

Confirmed by: `social-challenges.test.ts` (real PostgreSQL: the card with no pick or answer, hidden until both have called, the first pick stands, a stranger, a plain card and another conversation are all `unavailable`, a block, recording paused and a stale generation, Clear, deletion of either person, export, no receipt, and the same tables outside Social before and after), `challenges.test.ts`, `challenge-client.test.ts`, `e2e/challenge-smoke.spec.ts` (two browsers at 390 by 844 and 1440 by 900 with axe). Shown failing with the reveal rule and the recording gate each broken once.

## D-246 · 2026-11-01 · A Reel has the same two top buttons a Scroll has: Your Universe and Next topic (v3 S-9, #480)

**Builds on** the V3 question 16 and D-185.

- **Phone.** The Reel's top bar gains Your Universe at the left and Next topic at the right, in the same places as the Scroll reader's bar, for when a swipe does not work. The format switch is drawn without its state words so the bar stays one line at 390 px and 320 px. Next topic is the same vertical move as a swipe up, and Your Universe opens the Universe tab.
- **Desktop.** The Reel card's top row gains Your Universe before Previous subject. Next subject stays below the card as it was.
- **Nothing else changes.** Reel and Scroll keep equal standing: neither gets a control the other lacks. A v1 Scroll's article keeps Back.

Confirmed by: `e2e/simpler-smoke.spec.ts` (the order of the buttons, 44 px targets, Next topic sends the vertical intent, Your Universe opens `/universe`, nothing clipped at 320 px, axe at 390 by 844 and 1440 by 900).

## D-247 · 2026-11-01 · What the room's residents remember is one short text, written again and never added to, and erased when what it read goes (R-8, #494)

**Builds on** D-161 (a summary or memory must use the keep-out rule and be marked stale in the change that makes it), D-169 (the summary's window and its stale mark) and T3 question 11 (moderators edit what a resident remembers; anyone may ask for their messages to be forgotten). **Amends** the design, which draws "what Nova remembers" for each resident: the room has one memory, shared by its residents.

- **One text per room.** `rooms.room_memory` holds at most 600 characters, two to four short lines, with no name and no speaker number. The two residents read it and each profile shows the same words, and says so. One text halves the calls and cannot say two things about one room.
- **Written from what is retained.** The newest 60 shown messages that people wrote in any discussion of the room (no card, no topic title, no resident's words). Fewer than 8 readable messages are not enough, so a quiet room makes no call. The first text is written then, again once 20 readable messages have followed what it read, and again after it went stale. Each time it replaces the old words: nothing is appended.
- **The keep-out rule.** A member who keeps their messages out is not in the window at all, so their words are not in any request. It does not use the placeholder builder of D-161, because it runs under the room's scope with no person; the rule is the same and `rooms-memory.test.ts` reads the request bytes.
- **Stale means erased.** A moderator removing a message the text read, a member it read turning keep-out on, and a deleted account that wrote in what it read each erase its words (`stale`) in the same transaction, before a deleted account's messages become tombstones. The next refresh writes it again from what is left. A refresh whose call was in flight when something it read was removed stores nothing and stays counted. That holds however the two interleave: the store takes the room's row exclusively first and counts the messages and member rows it read under a share lock, a removal and a keep-out take the room's row shared before they touch a message, and a deletion takes it shared for every room its person wrote in before it erases anything. A removal in flight is waited for and what it committed is what is counted; one that comes later waits for the store and then erases what it stored.
- **Written in a resident's voice, and naming no one.** The text has no "I", "me" or "my" and claims no experience (the guard of a find's note, D-249). It names no member: no speaker number, and none of the room's members' display names, any word of one of three letters or more, or handles, as whole words in any case. The names are read when the call is made, for the one repair, and again when the words are stored. A word of a name that is also a common word makes a text that uses it unwritable, which costs a refresh and never a name. Only the text a resident wrote is checked; a moderator's words are theirs (D-249).
- **A call that wrote nothing waits a day.** A refresh that was declined, or that failed after the provider was reached, is not asked again for 24 hours, so a room whose words the model will not summarise cannot spend a call each heartbeat.
- **A refresh is an AI turn.** It has an `ai_turns` row (`memory_refresh`, no discussion, no message) naming its trigger and the cap that decided it. It counts toward the room's unprompted caps (8 an hour, 40 a day) and never toward the ratio to people's messages.
- **A moderator's text stands.** Written by a moderator, it reads no message, so no removal erases it and no refresh replaces it, until a moderator clears it (D-249).
- **A block changes nothing in it.** As for D-169, the words are shared by everyone in the room and name no one, so they may paraphrase a blocked person's words to the person who blocked them. It reads only people's text, so a moderator removing a resident's note or a card does not erase it.

Confirmed by: `memory.test.ts` (when it is due), `rooms-memory.test.ts` (the window and its bytes: kept-out and removed words absent; once per slot; stale on removal, keep-out and deletion and not for a message it did not read; not stored when something it read was removed during the call, and not when a hide, a keep-out or a deletion is open in another transaction and commits just after the check; a deletion that begins while the text is stored erases it; the voice guard and the names, including a member renamed during the call; the day's rest; the cap). Staleness on each of the three causes, each lock and each guard was shown failing by dropping it.

## D-248 · 2026-11-01 · Residents post finds on a heartbeat: code picks the asset, the model writes only the note, three a day, six hours' rest (R-8, #494)

**Builds on** D-068 (caps, ambient class, quiet refusals), D-081 (code decides, a model only proposes), D-162 (the room scope pays) and T3 (a heartbeat of about three a day, one post per resident per room every 6 hours, each post a find or a source). **Amends** D-051's "an AI message has an asker": a find has none, and no person is its actor.

- **A rhythm, not a poke.** `rooms.feed-heartbeat` runs at the start of each 8-hour UTC slot (cron `7 0,8,16 * * *`), three times a day, and looks once at each open room that has a resident and a member whose AI level is not Off. It runs under the nonpersonal `rooms` scope in the room scope's own transactions, with the room row locked, so no member's universe is its authority and a Clear or a deletion of one person cannot stop it. It is off until `KS_ROOMS_FEED_ENABLED=1` and needs `KS_REASONING_ROOM_ENABLED=1`.
- **Code picks the asset.** From the admitted library assets under the room's topic (Reels and Scrolls: the current revision, not held, not made for a thread, with a summary), the newest one the room has not seen. The room has seen an asset when a find or a counted find turn names it or a member shared a card of it. The model is not asked to choose and cannot post about anything else.
- **A Reel and its Scroll are one find.** An asset counts as seen when any asset of the same explanation (`explanation_unit_id`) has been seen in the room, so the twin of a find, or of a card a member shared, is not offered as a second find. The library column is the only one rooms reads.
- **The model writes only the note.** One or two sentences, at most 280 characters, from the asset's title and summary and what the room remembers. It reads no member's words. It may answer "nothing to add". Code checks the note again where it is stored (D-249) and that the asset is still served; a post that links nothing is not made, and a foreign key and a check keep one from existing.
- **Who posts.** A resident whose rest is over: the one who posted longest ago, the Explainer first. Each resident rests 6 hours in each room after a find or a call about one.
- **Caps.** At most three finds per room per UTC day (the slot makes it three; the cap is the belt), and every find counts toward the room's 8 an hour and 40 a day. A cap refusal is a quiet `ai_turns` row (`decided_by` `feed_day`, `room_hour` or `room_day`): no call, no post, no message, and the asset is offered again at the next look.
- **Zero calls.** A look that finds no listener, no new asset, every resident resting or a closed room records nothing and asks no model. A look is one turn per room and slot, so a retried or repeated job pays nothing twice; its run ID is fixed by (room, kind, slot).
- **An asset gets one call per room.** A find that was declined, or failed after the provider was reached, is counted and the asset is not offered to that room again, so an outage cannot make a room pay for one asset every look. The library is finite: when nothing is new the feed runs dry and says so, and posts nothing to fill the silence.

Confirmed by: `feed.test.ts` (the pick, the rest, the caps, the slot, the decision for each drop), `rooms-feed.test.ts` (an idle room, a room nobody listens to, a paused room, a library with nothing new, a link to a real asset, the ledger row, a Reel and a Scroll, the dry library, a card already shared, the twin of a find or of a card, the rest, the day cap and the hour cap, a retry, the asset withdrawn during the call, no write to a person's universe, the database refusing a find with no asset). Each was shown failing by breaking its rule.

## D-249 · 2026-11-01 · A find is a discussion about its asset with the resident's note first, told in an editorial voice, and a moderator's words are audited (R-8, #494)

**Builds on** D-237 (an AI message is an ordinary, labelled message), D-174 (an AI never earns a star), D-068 and T3 (agents cannot vote, no karma). **Amends** T3's sample note ("changed how I picture dark matter"), which claims an experience a resident cannot have.

- **A find is a discussion.** `kind = 'find'`, rooted at the asset (a content thread, so the Reel or Scroll opens from it), with no starter and the resident's note as its first message (`ai_kind = 'find'`, `trigger = 'feed_find'`). People reply in it like any thread. It is not in the topics list or the room's discussions; it is in the Feed, newest first, at most 30. One room never has two finds about one asset.
- **A reader's AI level shapes the feed.** Lively reads the note, Quieter reads the asset with the note held back (it is read in the discussion), Off is listed no find at all and told why. The Now strip says who found something and how long ago, for readers who can see a find.
- **Editorial voice.** A note says what the find shows or what to check. It has no "I", "me" or "my" (a Roman numeral such as "Type I" is not the pronoun), no claim to have watched, read, remembered or been changed, no life story, no link and no claim to be a person. A note that fails gets one fixed hint and, failing again, is not posted. The words are checked in the answer schema and again where the note is stored.
- **Agents cannot vote, message or count.** A resident has no account, so it has no vote, Useful mark, poll answer, message to send, lease or place; a Useful mark in a find earns no star; a find lists no person and no count of people. The feed draws no vote, no follow and no "online".
- **Moderator words are audited and stand.** A moderator may replace what the residents remember or clear it (D-247). Each is a `rooms.memory_edits` row with who, what, when and the SHA-256 of the words; an earlier text is not kept, and the row stays when the moderator's account goes (the link is cleared, the edit stays a fact about the room). The words are plain, bounded, link-free text. The same command again changes nothing.
- **Not built.** The design's people's posts, Hot/New/Top, votes, "+ Post", a resident's "interests" and profile history, Mute on the profile (the room's AI level is the member's mute) and introductions are not here: the Feed lists only residents' finds.

Confirmed by: `feed.test.ts` (the voice, with the design's own sample), `rooms-feed.test.ts` (a note that claims an experience is never posted, the repair, the three levels, a stranger, the thread and the topic lists, a removed note, a withdrawn asset, no star and no account), `rooms-memory.test.ts` (a moderator's replacement, its audit row, replay, refusals, a clear and a rewrite, a deleted moderator), `feed.test.tsx` and `rooms-feed-smoke.spec.ts` (at 390 and 1440 with axe; the AI label is read as "AI, Explainer" and the mark is hidden from a screen reader).

## D-250 · 2026-11-01 · An ask order's answer is read against the passages it cites before the person is served (v3 S-7b, #580)

**Builds on** D-198 (a second model checks, code decides), D-218 and D-231. **Amends** D-218, where an ask order was served after the writer's mechanical checks alone, and D-129's bound for an order (two requests a take).

- **The gap.** The coordinator read the first five real ask orders on dev. One said a place near a pole can see the Sun nearly overhead (it never can). One said decoder blocks use cross-attention with causal masking (the mask is on the self-attention). One was true but never gave the reason it was asked for. The validator proves that a digit comes from a cited passage and that nine words are not copied. It cannot see a sentence about a cause or a direction that says more than its passage. Nobody reads an ask order before the person who asked, so a checker model holds the line that a person holds for a library Scroll, and code holds the checker to what code can see.
- **The role.** `content.ask-support-check` (`ask-support-check.v2`) runs in the production job, after the writer's answer passed the validator and compiled, for the answer to a question only (mode `question`). It is shown two items: the passages the answer cites (`[e1]`...) and the answer's sentences, numbered. The title, the dek, each heading and a callout's title are sentences too (a title can state a fact: "Why the equator barely notices the tilt"), and rest on everything the answer cites; every other sentence rests on the passages its own block cites. It is shown no question, no brief and no stop, because support is judged by the passages alone: its request holds none of the person's words.
- **What code checks.** The reply has one verdict for each sentence, in order. A `supported` verdict carries a `quote`. Code refuses the verdict unless the quote
  - has three or more words, counting letters and numbers (a dash or a mark is not a word);
  - is found, word for word (case, punctuation and typography aside), in a passage that sentence cites; and
  - is about that sentence: it carries at least two of the sentence's key words (its uncommon words of three letters or more, compared by their first five letters; all of them when the sentence has fewer than two).

  An `unsupported` verdict carries a `kind` from a fixed list (`not_stated`, `contradicted`, `goes_beyond`) and a short `problem`. A `no_claim` verdict is accepted only for a question, a plain admission of a gap ("the pages do not...", "this cannot..."), or a sentence with no figure that shares no key word with the passages it cites (a label or a transition). A figure written in words ("twenty-one") counts as a figure. A reply in which more than half the sentences are `no_claim` is refused. Each refusal has a fixed hint for the harness's one repair, and a reply that stays invalid is no usable check.
- **What code cannot check.** Whether the quoted words support the sentence. That is the checker model's judgement, and it can be wrong in both directions. A model can quote real words of a cited passage that share key words with a false sentence and call it supported: if the passage says the Sun is never directly overhead near the poles, "the Sun is never directly overhead" carries two key words of "The Sun is nearly overhead at the pole in summer" and passes the rules. What code does is take away the cheap ways to be wrong: no verdict without real, relevant words, no skipping of the hard sentences as "no fact", no quote that is about something else. How well the checker catches the sentences that matter is measured on dev with `content ask-check` (D-252) before anything relies on it.
- **Code decides.** All supported: the Scroll is published. An unsupported sentence on a first draft that the validator had not already repaired earns the writer **one** repair, in a run of its own: the writer's instructions plus a revision notice, and as data the unsupported sentences (located by their own words, each with the fixed hint of its kind and the checker's note) and its draft. The repair has no repair of its own. The repaired draft is checked in turn: any unsupported sentence ends the job rejected (`support_check_failed`). A first draft the validator already repaired has none left and ends at once. A refused repair ends the job (`support_repair_*`).
- **Never an unchecked answer.** A check that gave nothing usable (failed, refused, or invalid after the harness's one repair) ends the job rejected (`support_check_<code>`). This differs from D-198, where an unchecked library Scroll waits held for the operator's read: no one reads an ask order, so it is not served. An attempt of unknown outcome ends the job `unknown` and is never resent, as for the writer. A spent budget or quota pauses the job, and the writer's kept answer is read from the ledger on resume.
- **What the person sees.** The existing honest state (E07, "We couldn't make a good answer from what we have", `could_not_prepare`) with Try again. Each Try again is the next take, three at most (D-218), so one ask costs at most three takes however often it is pressed.
- **The bound.** At most **six** paid requests a take (`REQUEST_BOUND_ASK_ORDER`): the writer once and one repair (the validator's or the check's), and the check once for each of the two drafts, each with at most one repair of an unusable reply. The check runs inside the job's claim, which is already sized for six requests (`PRODUCE_SCROLL_CLAIM_SECONDS`). `requestBoundOf(key, mode)` answers six for an order that answers a question and two for the others; the job reports the bound once it has read the order.
- **Runs and receipts.** The check's runs and the repair's run have ids derived from the job (`stableUuid`), so a redelivery asks the ledger. The job closes them in the transaction that stores or ends it, and names the repair's run as its writer run when its answer is the one stored. The receipts (`support_check`) keep sentence numbers and kinds, never a sentence or the checker's words: the job outlives an erasure of the question, and an answer can echo it (D-231).
- **The writer is told.** `question-writer.v3` adds rules to the question rules. When the question asks why, give the reason the passages themselves give; if they state what happens but not why, say what they state and then say in one sentence that the pages do not give the reason. If the passages do not hold the answer at all, say so in one sentence at the start. Every sentence that states a fact must rest on a passage cited in that block, and each one is checked, the title, the headings and the callout's title included. `versions.test.ts` pairs the writer's version with the checker's, so neither can change alone. `SCROLL_PRODUCTION_VERSION` stays `scroll-production.v14`: an order has a job of its own, and that version also belongs to v1 library recipes and what-ifs, whose takes must not start again.
- **Limits.** Besides what code cannot check: the checker can flag a good sentence, which costs a take (a person has three). It does not prove that the passages are right: they are public pages chosen by code. Figures stay proved by the validator against the cited passages (D-208).

Confirmed by: `ask-support.test.ts` and `domain/ask-support.test.ts` (the sentences, including the title, the headings and the callout's title; what the checker is shown and not shown; every rule of the reply with the cases that got through the first version: the false sentence marked supported with the quote "the Sun is", an unrelated quote on every sentence, exactly half the sentences marked as stating no fact, the quote "Sun — is" counted as three words, a figure in words; the fixed hints; the repair role), `versions.test.ts` (the pair, the bound of six for an answer to a question and two for any other order), and `supply-ask.test.ts` on real PostgreSQL with a labelled fixture model whose replies are built from the passages (supported: one writer request and one check, the question never sent to the checker; an unsupported sentence and an unsupported title each repaired once with the fixed hint and then served, with all four runs closed and no sentence text in the receipts; still unsupported after the repair: rejected, nothing stored, the person's request closed as could-not-prepare; a draft the validator repaired ends at once; a refused repair ends the job; a failed, malformed, invented-quote, unrelated-quote or half-no-claim reply refuses; an unknown check is never resent and Try again starts nothing; a spent quota pauses and resumes without a second writer request). Shown failing when the check was not run, when an unusable check was published, when a finding was ignored, when a quote did not have to be in the passages, when it did not have to be about the sentence, when no_claim was unbounded, when a lazy reply was believed, when a validator-repaired draft earned a repair, when a receipt kept the checker's words, when the bound stayed two, and when the question writer's version changed alone.

## D-251 · 2026-11-01 · A library Scroll answers a question only when the checker finds that it does; otherwise an order is made (v3 S-7b, #580)

**Builds on** D-230 and D-250. **Amends** D-230, "What 'served from the library' means" and "`catalogue` is handled as a free-text ask is: Reuse, then Join or Create".

- **The gap.** On dev, a question about why carbon dioxide is treated as the main driver of warming when water vapour is the stronger greenhouse gas was answered from the library with "When Sunlight Becomes Heat". That Scroll mentions neither gas nor feedback. A Scroll on the right subject is not an answer to the question.
- **The rule.** When the scope step places a question asked from a Scroll on another catalogue subject, code lists up to three admitted library Scrolls of that subject (the Scroll being read is never one) and reads their prose from shared content. The personal harness runs `supply.ask-library` (`ask-library.2`), one call and one repair, outside every transaction. The reply is `{scroll, quote}`: a handle and a quote copied from that Scroll, or neither. Code accepts a Scroll only when the handle is a candidate, the quote has three or more words (letters and numbers; a dash is not a word), the words are in that Scroll's text, and they are about the question: they carry at least two of the question's key words (its uncommon words, compared by their first five letters; all of them when it has fewer than two). The checker's word alone offers nothing. Whether the quoted words answer the question is the checker's judgement, which code cannot check (D-250) and the replay measures (D-252). Choosing none is a good answer, and the instructions say so.
- **Otherwise an order.** If no Scroll is offered (none answers, the subject has none, or the check gave no usable reply), code makes an ask order for the question, within the caps of D-218, with the research pages of the Scroll being read as always. Its writer says in one sentence when those pages do not hold the answer (D-250). If the caps are full, the request closes `budget_unavailable`, the honest state. A library Scroll that answers costs no order and is served even when the day's ask orders are used up.
- **The library is not started for a question.** A question no longer joins or creates the shared work of a library recipe: a Scroll made for a subject is not known to answer a question about it, and it would reach the person unchecked. A free-text topic ask (E04) is unchanged. A question that was already waiting on a library group when this ships finishes as it did.
- **Failures.** A reply that stays unusable after the one repair, or a provider failure, offers nothing, so the person gets an answer made for them, which is checked itself. A spent personal budget closes the request `budget_unavailable`. An attempt of unknown outcome closes it `could_not_prepare` and is never resent, as the scope step's is (D-230).
- **Runs.** One personal transaction closes one personal run, so the library check's run is closed in a transaction of its own before the verdict is applied together with the scope step's run. A process that dies between the two finds the library run closed on redelivery and offers nothing (an order is made); the scope step's answer is never lost.
- **Limit.** When the question belongs to a subject the Scroll being read does not rest on, the order rests on that Scroll's pages and says honestly what they do not cover. Choosing the subject's own pages for such an order is left for a later decision.

Confirmed by: `ask-library-role.test.ts` (the handle, the quote, each refusal and each outcome), and `supply-ask.test.ts` on real PostgreSQL with labelled fixture models (a Scroll offered only with words of it that are about the question; none answers; a quote that is not in the Scroll, a quote that is in the Scroll but not about the question, an unknown handle, a missing quote and a wrong shape each offer nothing after one repair and make an order; a subject with no library Scroll asks no model; no shared work and no Create cap are used; the Scroll being read is not a candidate; a full cap is an honest unavailable while an answering Scroll is still served; an unknown outcome is never resent; the check's run is closed or left open as stated). Shown failing when a quote did not have to be in the Scroll, when it did not have to be about the question, when a question could start the library again, and when both runs were closed in one transaction.

## D-252 · 2026-11-01 · The "usually" time counts only orders made under the check, and an operator can replay the check over stored answers (v3 S-7b, #580)

**Builds on** D-232 and D-250. **Amends** D-232, "A time only when measured".

- **The time includes the check.** An ask order is ready when its Scroll is admitted, and that now comes after the support check and any repair. `supply.ask_orders.support_checked` (migration 0092, default false) is set when an order is made, and the "usually" time reads only such orders, so an order made before the check, which did not wait for it, is not counted. The time is the median of the last 20 finished checked orders and still appears only from five of them: measured, never guessed. After this ships the sheet shows no time until five checked orders have finished.
- **No new cap.** The check is inside the order's request bound (D-250), so the world's and the person's daily caps of D-218 are unchanged, and the check has no cap of its own.
- **The replay is how the checker's judgement is measured.** Code checks that each verdict quotes real words of a passage the sentence cites and that the words are about the sentence (D-250). Whether those words support the sentence is the checker model's judgement, and nothing in the repository can say how good it is. `content ask-check (--asset <id,...> | --recent <n>) --out <file>` runs the same checker, in a run of its own through the production harness (every call is an attempt in the ledger), over stored ask answers: the authored answer the job kept and the passages rebuilt from the research it froze. The coordinator reads the verdicts on the answers whose faults are known (#580) and on good ones, and decides from them whether the checker is too lenient (a known fault passes) or too strict (good sentences are flagged, so people see "could not prepare"), before relying on it. A change to the checker's instructions is a new `ask-support-check` version, paired with the writer's.
- **What the replay touches.** It publishes, stores and changes nothing. It reads no question, because the check does not use one. Standard output holds one line for each answer with counts and codes only (sentences, supported, unsupported, no claim, requests). The sentences, the passages each cites and the verdicts go to the file named by `--out`, which only its owner reads, because it holds the answers' words: delete it after reading (the runbook says so). It refuses to run on a model other than `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` (D-031), stops at a spent budget or quota and at an attempt of unknown outcome, and reads at most 20 answers.

Confirmed by: `supply-ask.test.ts` (no time from four checked orders, none when the fifth is an unchecked one, then the median of the checked five), `ask-support-replay.test.ts` on real PostgreSQL with a labelled fixture model (a stored answer checked again with the flagged sentence in the detail and counts only on the line, nothing in the content tables or the job changed, the replay's run closed; an asset that is not an ask answer is not replayed and asks the model nothing; the newest answers, newest first, and no other kind of order; an unusable reply reports unchecked with no detail; a spent quota stops it; another model is refused), and `ask-check-operator.test.ts` (the options are refused before anything is touched). Shown failing when the time counted unchecked orders.

## D-253 · 2026-11-01 · A Quick answer is a capped worker job, asked to rest only on the words the reader has reached and shown only after a support check (v3 S-9b, #480)

**Builds on** D-086, which stands, and **amends** D-244's "Until S-9b" line (below). **Builds** the live Quick answer D-086 describes, in the shape D-218 gave ask orders: the HTTP side stores, a worker thinks.

- **The path.** "Ask" on selected words sends `POST /v1/quick-answers`. One transaction stores the row, writes one row in a ledger that names no person, sends one pg-boss job (IDs only) and bumps the revision. The worker's `supply.answer-quick-answer` consumer makes the writer's call (one repair) on the personal harness, role `supply.quick-answer` (`quick-answer.1`, `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash`), then the support check below, all outside any transaction, then settles the row in a second one. The client gets the disposable scope hint and polls the status (first after 1.5 s, then every 3 s). No model call is made on HTTP or inside a transaction.
- **What the model is offered.** The read stops of that exact revision from the start up to and including the stop the words were selected in, and the plainer words of that stop (the reader may have selected inside them), nothing after. A call, its options and its explanation, a later stop, a guide's line and a play block are never offered, so a model cannot copy from what the reader has not reached. The words must be from that stop (or its plainer words), the stop must be a read stop, and the person must have opened that revision. What the model does with them is checked below, not assumed.
- **The stop is the client's claim, and that is accepted.** The server does not know which stop the person is on. It checks that the stop is a read stop of the exact revision they opened and that the words are from it, and it offers the passages up to that stop. A client that names a later stop gets an answer that rests on more of the Scroll. That is accepted because the device already holds the whole document (a Scroll is served whole), so nothing is revealed that the person's own app could not show. The rule keeps an honest reader from a spoiler; it is not a lock.
- **What code checks, and what the checker model judges.** Code checks, before anything is shown: every citation is a passage that was offered; a covered answer cites one to three; every digit in the answer or the question is in a cited passage, the selected words or the person's own question; the text is plain sentences (no Markdown, list or link); the question carries at least two of the key words of the selected words, the person's own question and the cited passages together (all of them when there are fewer), which keeps it near the passage but does not stop it adding words of its own; and the support checker's reply is valid only if it gives one verdict for every sentence in order (D-263: a verdict that breaks a rule then makes only its sentence unsupported: a `supported` verdict needs at least three words that are in a passage that sentence cites and are about it, and `no_claim` is allowed only where code can see the sentence states no fact). The checker model judges the one thing code cannot: whether the quoted words state what each sentence says, so an answer that brings outside knowledge in plain words with no digit is found by it, not by code. That is a model's judgement and not a proof. It is the same check an ask order's answer goes through (D-250), and it is the only check of that.
- **The label is part of the wire.** The answered status carries `label: "Quick answer · not a checked Scroll"` and every card draws it, with "Make it a full Scroll" (the S-7 Ask sheet, with the answer's question as its draft), "Ask a follow-up", Report and Close. The follow-up is another Quick answer and counts against the caps.
- **Caps, off by default.** `KS_SUPPLY_QUICK_ANSWER_DAILY_CAP` for the world per UTC day, counted from the ledger so Clear and deletion cannot reset it (default 20 in `local`, 0 elsewhere, so a world turns it on), and `KS_SUPPLY_QUICK_ANSWER_PERSON_DAILY_CAP` per person (default 10, counted from their own rows, so Clear resets it and the world cap still bounds the spend). At most three wait at once. A full day, a person's own limit and a full queue are an honest `unavailable` outcome with no row and no receipt, and the card says so in plain words. A card waits at most four minutes; a late answer is dropped.
- **Never evidence, never the Universe.** Asking and answering write no exposure, observation or action and call no module that ranks, plans, remembers or forms places. `supply/quick-answer-wall.test.ts` allow-lists the files that may name a Quick answer, as the play wall does.
- **The support check, and it fails closed.** After the writer's answer passes code's rules, a second model (S-7b's checker, D-250, under its own role ID `supply.quick-answer-support` so its failure brake and budget are not the writer's or an ask order's) reads each sentence against the passages the answer cites. All clear: the card is shown. An unsupported sentence earns one rewrite, told which sentence by a fixed hint of its kind (never the checker's words), and the rewrite is checked once more; not cleared twice, the card closes as "We couldn't answer that one" and shows nothing. A check that failed, was refused, ran out of budget or gave a reply code refuses also shows nothing. The answering step cannot be built without a checker (it throws), and an exception from the check stores nothing and leaves the delivery to be retried: no checker, no card. Every attempt goes through the personal harness and its ledger under the person's own runs named from the answer's ID, so a redelivery never pays twice. At most six paid attempts for one Quick answer (the writer and its repair, the check and its repair, one rewrite, one re-check), so the job's claim is 1,950 s, and the daily caps bound the spend at six times the cap.
- **A card that says it cannot answer shows no model text.** When the passages do not cover the question the writer says so and cites nothing; the card then shows a sentence code owns ("What you have read so far does not answer that. A full Scroll would.") under the person's own typed question, or their selected words cut short. Nothing is checked, because nothing the model wrote is shown.
- **The menu changes as D-244 said it would.** "Ask" on selected words now asks for a Quick answer. The Ask sheet (D-218) is reached by "Make it a full Scroll" and by the dock's Ask, so nothing is lost. A phone's own selection menu still cannot be extended, so a phone has no Quick answer until native selection items exist (D-244).
- **Reel.** Not offered: the selection menu is a Scroll's.

Confirmed by: `quick-answer.test.ts` (each rule broken once), `quick-answer-role.test.ts`, `quick-answers.test.ts`, `quick-answer-wall.test.ts`, `tests/integration/modules/quick-answers.test.ts` (no model on the ask, the model's request holds no stop after the reader's and no call, caps, no evidence), `quick-answer-session.test.ts`, `quick-answer-card.test.tsx` and `e2e/quick-answer-smoke.spec.ts` at 390 by 844 and 1440 by 900 with axe. Shown failing when an unoffered citation, an unstated figure, markup or an off-topic question was allowed, when a paused ask was stored, when a late answer was shown, when an unchecked answer or an unsupported one was shown, when the model's words were shown for a question not covered, when the checker was left out of the build, when the second check got a repair, when the ledger row was left out, and when a later stop was offered. **Not yet run with the real model:** the coordinator's dev run (accuracy, latency, how often `covered` is false) is the first.

## D-254 · 2026-11-01 · A Quick answer is refused while recording is paused, and for now it also does not run for a person who has paused and resumed (v3 S-9b, #480)

**Builds on** D-083 and D-231, and **decides** D-083 for this slice, as the brief asked.

- **Refused while paused.** A Quick answer is not evidence, but it names the Scroll the person has open, the stop they are on and the words they selected, and the server keeps them for a day. Pause recording means the server keeps no record of reading (D-083), so the command is refused as an ask from here is (D-231): the device checks the policy first and the card says "off while recording is paused"; the server fences with the recording generation (`RECORDING_PAUSED`, 409; a stale generation is `RECORDING_POLICY_STALE`) and stores nothing. "Make it a full Scroll" is not offered on that card, because it is refused too.
- **A known limit, shared with S-7.** The personal harness runs only at the first recording generation. A person who paused and resumed has a later generation, so their Quick answer is stored and then closes as "We couldn't answer that one", with no model call, as an ask's scope step does (supply README). Fixing it means the recording-aware harness accepting a `supply` purpose; that is not in this slice. `quick-answers.test.ts` pins it.

Confirmed by: the integration test (paused and stale refused with no row, no ledger row and no job; a paused-and-resumed person gets an honest "could not answer" and no model call), `quick-answers.test.ts`, `quick-answer-session.test.ts` (nothing sent while paused) and `e2e/quick-answer-smoke.spec.ts`.

## D-255 · 2026-11-01 · The words of a Quick answer are cleared as soon as they are used, the answer is kept a day, and a report keeps what it said (v3 S-9b, #480)

**Builds on** D-231 and D-055.

- **Cleared when used.** The words the person selected and the question they typed are in the row only while it waits. Settling the answer, closing the card ("Close" erases at once) and a failure all clear them in the same statement. Only what the card shows (the question it answers and the answer) stays, and only for one day (`expires_at`).
- **A job that never ran does not keep the words.** A job that arrives after the wait limit settles the answer as unavailable and clears the words without asking a model. The purge does the same for an answer still waiting after the limit, whether or not any job ever ran (a stopped worker, a lost job). The three-at-once limit counts only answers younger than the limit, so a row nobody waits for never blocks the person's next ask.
- **A day, then gone.** An answer past its day reads as not found whether or not the purge has run. The scheduled job `supply.purge-quick-answers` deletes expired rows every ten minutes, in small batches that skip rows another transaction holds. A day or more is what keeps the person's daily count exact, because it is counted from these rows.
- **Clear, deletion and export.** Clear and account deletion erase the rows and the queued job IDs at once. The export lists the answers still kept, in the person's words. The ledger names no one and stays.
- **What erasure does not reach.** The model's request bytes (the passages, the selected words and the question) are the personal harness's protected bytes, erased with the person and otherwise kept up to `KS_REASONING_REQUEST_RETENTION_HOURS` (48 hours), as for an ask's scope step (D-231). A refused model answer is kept for the operator up to 30 days (`REJECTED_ANSWER_RETENTION_DAYS`) and can echo the question. The privacy copy has to say so (D-021).
- **Report.** "Report" opens the content-error form (P03) about the Scroll revision, at the stop, with the Quick answer's ID. The server reads the question and answer from the reporter's own kept copy and stores them in `feedback.reports.quick_answer`, because the answer is gone a day later and the safety team could not otherwise read what was wrong. Only the reporter's own, answered, still-kept answer about that revision is accepted; anything else is a missing target. A Report keeps the question and the answer until the reporter's account is deleted, and Clear keeps it (a report is a message to us, D-055); the form says so before it is sent. The reporter's export lists it with their report. Nobody is told, and the report feeds nothing.

Confirmed by: `tests/integration/modules/quick-answers.test.ts` (the words are null after the answer, after a dismiss, and for a failure; the purge removes only expired rows; an expired answer reads as not found; export; erasure and Clear remove rows and the queued job; the ledger keeps counting after erasure; a report keeps the words, refuses another person's answer and outlives a dismissed one), `file-report.test.ts`, `quick-answers.test.ts`. Shown failing when the settle left the words, when erasure left the rows, and when a report did not check the revision.
