## D-256 · 2026-11-01 · A Watch together session keeps a Reel and a start time; each device works out its own position (R-10, #496)

**Implements** D-075. **Amends** the blueprint's §17 line 1152, "There is no synchronised co-watching or shared playback cursor in the initial version", for a room's Watch together only. The blueprint text is not edited; this entry is the amendment.

- **What the server keeps.** `rooms.watch_sessions` holds the library Reel and its revision, its title and length, the member who shared it, when the first frame is due and, once it is over, when it stopped. `rooms.watch_viewers` holds who joined. No column holds a position, a pause or a time of a person's own, and a test lists the columns. A read says how long ago the first frame was due, on the server's clock; a device counts on from that with a steady clock, so a fast, slow or mis-set device clock changes nothing, as in D-178.
- **The clock.** The first frame is due five seconds after the start command, so people who have the room open can load the Reel. The session ends when the Reel's length has passed. There is no job: the first member command in the room after the end (a presence beat) marks it stopped **at the moment it ended**, deletes its viewers, and deletes sessions that ended seven days ago. The member who shared it, or a moderator, can stop it for everyone. A room has one session at a time (a partial unique index) and at most 20 a day.
- **Where a position lives.** The device compares its own player with the clock and moves the player only when it is more than a second and a half off. Pause is the person's own: nothing is sent, and Play brings them back to where the room is. The line "0:41 / 1:10 · synced for everyone" and the bar are the clock's, never a person's place. No request, answer or hint has a field for a position.
- **Which Reels.** Library Reels the room's topic rests on: the current revision of each admitted Reel whose primary topic is the room's (a system covers its planets), not a fixture and not under review, read through Content's public `ReadLibraryReels`. No Reel is ordered or made for this.
- **Hints are disposable.** A start, a join, a leave, a stop and a settlement announce the room, and the hint names nothing else. A device that heard none of them reads the whole truth again: the session, whether it joined, and the time.

Confirmed by: `domain/watch.test.ts` (the phases at each millisecond of the edge), `room-watch.test.ts` (contracts: no field for a position), `rooms-watch.test.ts` ("what the server keeps", "the clock", "a device that lost its hints"), `client-core/room-watch.test.ts` (the clock, the drift rule), `e2e/rooms-watch-smoke.spec.ts` (both players on the clock; a dropped connection recovers from one read).

## D-257 · 2026-11-01 · The stage shows "N watching" only from three people who joined, names no one, and holds one thing at a time (R-10, #496)

**Builds on** D-069, D-160, D-222 and D-224. The allow-list of D-069 gains no entry: `watching` is not a count of who is here.

- **Members only.** Every read and command answers `unavailable` to a stranger, an invitee and a person who left. A room's preview says nothing of a session. A member's or moderator's station says `live` while one is on, as for a Launch (D-222), and never which of the two, who or how many.
- **"N watching".** A number is sent only from three people. It counts people who **joined** and still have the room open (a lapsed lease is not watching, D-239); someone who only has the room open is not counted. The reader counts when they joined. A person the reader blocked is left out for that reader only. An AI cannot join, so it is never counted. Below three there is no number at all.
- **No names.** R3 A2 draws faces beside "4 watching". They are not built: two faces would tell a person who is watching, which a count from three exists to prevent. The member who shared the session is named ("Shared by Lena") while they are still a member the reader has not blocked, as a message's author is.
- **One thing on the stage.** A Reel cannot start while a Launch is in its lobby or running, and a Launch refuses with `stage_busy` while a Reel is on. A Reel also needs two people with the room open: it is for watching together.
- **Stage mode.** While a session is on, the stage sits above General at 42 percent of a phone's height (260 to 380 px), edge to edge under the band as R3 A2 draws it, and General keeps the rest as the crowd, as D-224 does for a Launch. Measured at 390 × 844 the messages have 206 px (24 percent) while a Reel is on (a Launch leaves 18 percent), and 574 px (68 percent) when nothing is on. The band says "LIVE · REEL". R3 itself picks "A1 turns into stage mode only while something is live", so a room with nothing on its stage keeps its 68 percent. "Nothing on stage right now", with what was last on the stage and the two offers (a Reel, a quiz), is a sheet from the Now strip, not a screen of its own.

Confirmed by: `rooms-watch.test.ts` ("N watching", "the stage is one thing at a time", "who can take part"), `watch.test.tsx` and `campfire.test.tsx` (the number, the chip, the band, the sheet), `e2e/rooms-watch-smoke.spec.ts` (two and three people at 390 by 844 and 1440 by 900 with axe; both measures).

## D-258 · 2026-11-01 · A join is kept in the room and taken back like a play result; the watch itself is the person's own encounter (R-10, #496)

**Builds on** D-063, D-082, D-083 and D-220.

- **Paused.** Starting and joining are kept in the room (who joined, who shared it), so each carries the recording generation and is refused with `not_saved` while recording is paused or the device is out of date. Nothing is stored and no receipt is written. The stage and the sheet say so before any tap. A paused person cannot join at all, rather than watching uncounted: the stricter rule, like the Launch's (D-220). Leaving and stopping remove what is kept, so they always work.
- **Taken back.** A Clear deletes the person's place among the viewers and empties the link that says they shared a session. So does leaving or being removed from the room, and so does deleting the account. Viewers also go when a session ends or is stopped. The person's file lists the sessions they shared and the ones they joined that are still held, by room and day, never a position and never another person.
- **Evidence is the person's own.** A session writes nothing to evidence, experience, geography, composer or planning, for anyone. Only after a person taps Join (or starts one) does their device open the Reel through the ordinary open, grant and activation with the ordinary recorder, as an encounter of their own. Leaving ends it; joining again is a new one. Showing a session, being counted in one or being invited to one creates nothing. A device that has not joined opens nothing: it counts as joined only when its own Join (or Start) was accepted, never because the server says the person is in, so the same person's second device, or a room reopened while their place still stands, shows Join and opens nothing.
- **A wall.** No file outside Rooms names a Watch table (`watch-wall.test.ts`), so who watched with a room can reach no ranking, planning or formation.

Confirmed by: `rooms-watch.test.ts` ("recording paused", "what a person can take back", "writes nothing for anyone in the personal universe"), `watch-wall.test.ts`, `watch.test.tsx` (an unjoined device opens no encounter), `e2e/rooms-watch-smoke.spec.ts` (each person's observations name only their own exposure).

## D-263 · 2026-11-01 · The ask support check works on real model output: marks are matched after normalising, a verdict that breaks a rule fails one sentence, titles may state no fact, and a long answer is checked in batches (v3 S-7c, #591)

**Builds on** D-250 to D-252 and D-253. **Amends** D-250 ("What code checks", "Code decides", "The bound", "The writer is told"), D-252 ("The replay is how the checker's judgement is measured") and D-253 ("What code checks, and what the checker model judges"; the Quick answer's writer and wait).

- **The gap.** The replay on dev (1 Nov, `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash`, `ask-support-check.v2`) checked one of five real answers. The other four were refused whole, twice each, on `quote_not_about_sentence` (many sentences), `quote_not_in_cited_passages`, `no_claim_without_ground` (the title, a heading, the limits lines) and `truncated` (21 sentences in one reply). The one answer that was checked showed a sound checker: it flagged the false "sunlight aimed nearly overhead" at a pole and the equator claims no cited page makes. So the checker judged well and code's rules threw its reply away for one bad verdict.
- **Marks and spacing.** Every quote rule (the quote is in the passage, has enough words, is about the sentence; the same helpers serve the library check of D-251) compares words after `wordsOf`: Unicode compatibility form, lower case, curly and straight quotes and apostrophes alike, every dash and space (non-breaking, thin, repeated, line breaks) as a break, and the separators of a figure removed (`4,096` is `4096`). A quote may leave words out with an ellipsis (`…`, `...`, `[...]`): each part must stand in the passage, in order, and each part has at least two words (a lone word between two marks proves nothing). The quote still has three words in all.
- **One verdict, one sentence.** The shape of the reply still refuses it whole, with the harness's one repair: not valid JSON or cut off, a verdict that is not well formed, a sentence skipped, repeated or reordered. Every other rule is now applied to the verdict of one sentence (`judgeVerdicts`). A verdict that breaks one makes that sentence `unsupported` (kind `not_stated`, a fixed note, the rule's code kept: `quote_too_short`, `quote_not_in_cited_passages`, `quote_not_about_sentence`, `no_claim_without_ground`, `too_many_no_claim`), and every other verdict stands. This stays fail closed: a sentence the checker could not show a passage for is not supported. It earns the writer the same one repair as any unsupported sentence, with the fixed hint of its kind and the rule's fixed note (never the checker's quote), and a sentence that stands on the repaired draft ends the job (`support_check_failed`). Receipts and the replay keep the code with the sentence number and kind, never the words (D-231). The credibility rule (more than half the sentences marked "no fact" refuses the reply) becomes: when more than half of a request's sentences are marked "no fact" on the weakest ground below (sharing no key word with their passages), those sentences are set aside; a question, a gap or a label that code can see does not count toward it.
- **Titles, headings and gap lines.** The title, each heading and each callout's title are labels (`SentenceUnderCheck.label`, set from their place in the document). A label with no figure may be marked "no fact" with no other ground, even when it shares words with its passages; one with a figure, or one the checker judges supported or unsupported, is judged like any sentence. A plain admission of a gap is accepted as "no fact" only when the sentence *begins* with it, from a small fixed list (`namesTheGap`): "The pages do not say...", "This answer cannot tell/show/determine/choose...", "It also cannot...", "Nothing in the pages...", "That detail remains unspecified here". A sentence that states something besides a gap ("This explains the pattern, but it does not give the height") is judged, which tightens D-250, where the phrase could stand anywhere. The checker model is told all of this (`ask-support-check.v3`), and the question writer is told to begin an admission of a gap with the admission and to put nothing else in that sentence (`question-writer.v4`). The writer's v3 rule that a title is checked "like any sentence" changes: a title that only names what follows needs no passage; one that states a fact is still checked.
- **Batches.** A draft is checked a batch of at most ten sentences at a time (`ASK_SUPPORT_BATCH_SENTENCES`), in order, one request and one run each (`askSupportRunId(job, draft, batch)`), each with only the passages its sentences cite. A reply of ten verdicts does not reach the output ceiling the way one of twenty-one did. The first batch with no usable reply makes the whole check unusable and the later batches are not asked. The harness repairs one unusable reply in the whole check, wherever it happens (`allowRepair` is false for the batches after one that was repaired), so a draft costs its batches plus one. A draft of more than four batches (40 sentences; the real answers had 21) is not checked: `support_check_answer_too_long`, with no request. A spent budget or quota between two batches pauses the job, and the batches already answered are read from the ledger on resume.
- **The bound and the claim.** `REQUEST_BOUND_ASK_ORDER` is twelve, from six: the writer once and one repair, and for each of the two drafts its four batches at most and one repair (`2 + 2 x (4 + 1)`). The claim of `content.produce-scroll` follows the reasoning README's rule for a job that calls `propose` (each request: 10 s preflight, up to 300 s, and 10 s of scope locks), plus 180 s for the research and the database steps: `12 x 320 + 180 = 4,020` s (it was `6 x 300 + 300 = 2,100`). A library recipe still sends six requests, but its job now also holds a claim of 4,020 s, because the queue has one policy; a worker that dies mid-take is retried after that long. Two drafts of a typical 21-sentence answer cost 2 x 3 requests for the check.
- **The Quick answer** (D-253) takes the same checker and the same rules: its quotes are matched after normalising, a verdict that breaks a rule makes that sentence unsupported (so a bad quote earns the rewrite instead of ending the card), and the checker role is the same. It is checked in one request, which keeps its bound at six paid attempts and its claim at 1,950 s: the writer's answer is held to ten sentences (`too_many_sentences`, with a fixed hint for the writer's one repair, `quick-answer.2`), and the check refuses without a request an answer longer than one request carries. Beyond that, no paid step of a Quick answer starts once the person's wait is over (`waitEndsAt`): the answer would be dropped when it arrived. D-253 said the question "cannot bring a subject of its own"; what code checks is that the question carries at least two of the key words of the selected words, the person's own question and the cited passages together (all of them when there are fewer). A question can still add words of its own. Only the answer's sentences are read against the passages.
- **Versions.** `ask-support-check.v3` is paired with `question-writer.v4` (`versions.test.ts`); `SCROLL_PRODUCTION_VERSION` is unchanged for the reason in D-250.
- **Limits.** Code still cannot tell whether the quoted words support the sentence (D-250). Setting a sentence aside costs a writer repair when the fault was only the checker's quote; the replay's `codes` count says how often that happens, and a high count is a reason to change the checker's instructions, not to loosen a rule. A gap line that the list does not know is judged by the model, which may call it unsupported.
- **The replay** prints the batches and, for each rule code, how many sentences it set aside (`codes`), on the standard output with counts only; the file keeps each sentence's code.

Confirmed by: `domain/ask-support.test.ts` (marks, spacing, ellipsis and figure separators; the real limits lines of the 1 Nov answers accepted as gaps and the mixed sentences not; labels; each rule setting aside its sentence alone; the credibility rule; the batches), `production/ask-support.test.ts` (the shape refused whole, each rule per sentence, the equator answer of the 1 Nov replay as a fixture), `ask-support-step.test.ts` (ten sentences to a request, a run each, one repair in the whole check, stop at the first unusable batch, pause between batches, too long), `versions.test.ts` (the bound of twelve, the claim, the pair), `supply-ask.test.ts` on real PostgreSQL (one bad quote: one sentence unsupported and the rest kept, repaired once with the fixed note and served; rules that set aside every sentence reject with `support_check_failed` and not `invalid_output`; labels and a limits line as "no fact"; 23 sentences in three requests; one repair in the whole check; a pause between two batches that does not pay for the first batch again), `ask-support-replay.test.ts` (batches, `codes`, the detail's codes), and for the Quick answer `quick-answer-pipeline.test.ts` (no paid step after the wait), `quick-answer-role.test.ts`, `quick-answer-support.test.ts`, `quick-answers.test.ts`. Each rule was broken once and its test went red (ellipsis, figure separators, labels, anchored gaps, the per-sentence judgement, the credibility rule, the batch size, one repair per check, stop at the first unusable batch, the wait-limit stops, the Quick answer's sentence limit).
